# Project agent memory

This file is the project's committed home for project-intrinsic agent knowledge: build, test, release, architecture, and sharp-edge notes that should travel with the code.

- Add durable project-specific notes here as they are discovered through real work.

## Current state: MVP Step 1 — notebook document + save pipeline

Built on the SvelteKit ↔ Jupyter kernel bridge. Full product/tech spec lives outside the repo at `firstmate/data/cellar-spec.md` (read §3 for the versioning model); nbdev field-policy detail at `firstmate/data/nbdev-study-n8/report.md`. See `README.md` for run/verify details.

- **Two runtimes.** Node (SvelteKit) + a Python sidecar (Jupyter kernel service). Both are booted by the CLI launcher `bin/cellar.js` (one command → both servers up → browser opens, workspace = cwd). Distributed as an **npm package** (`package.json` `files`=`build/`+`bin/`+`src/lib/server/venv.js`; `prepack`/`prepublishOnly` run `vite build`); launcher defaults to the prod build (`node build/index.js`), `--dev` uses Vite. `build/`/`.venv/` gitignored. A root **`Makefile`** wraps these mechanics for convenience (`make setup` = install+build+`chmod +x bin/cellar.js`+`npm link`; `make update` = `git pull`+install+build; plus `make build`/`run`/`dev`; bare `make` prints help) - it invents no new scripts, just the real npm/launcher commands. `bin/cellar.js` is committed **executable** (git mode 100755 - the shebang'd, sole `package.json` `bin` entry); preserve that bit if you ever regenerate it, or `npm link` yields a non-runnable `cellar` symlink (the `make setup` `chmod` is a belt-and-suspenders re-set).
- **Per-repo launcher + venv binding (`bin/cellar.js` + `src/lib/server/venv.js`).** `cellar` in any repo binds the kernel to the **project's** venv. Resolution (first match): `--venv`/`CELLAR_VENV` → `$VIRTUAL_ENV` → `<ws>/.venv` → create. All venv create/installs go through **`uv`** (`uv venv`, `uv pip install`) — hard requirement, fails fast if absent (no `python -m venv` fallback). Ensures `ipykernel` in the project venv; **confirms before** create/install on a TTY (`--yes`/`-y`/`$CI`/non-TTY auto-approve; `--python` = bind arbitrary interpreter, no create/install). `venv.js` uses node builtins only so it's importable by both the launcher (`../src/lib/server/venv.js`) and the SvelteKit server (`$lib`). Cellar's heavy Jupyter host env (`jupyter-server`) lives in a private `~/.cellar/host-venv`, created/cached first run, kept separate from project venvs.
- **Kernel bridge = the de-risked core.** `src/lib/server/kernel.js` connects to Jupyter over REST+WebSocket using `@jupyterlab/services` (spec §2). Node 18+ global `fetch`/`WebSocket` are passed into `ServerConnection.makeSettings` — no polyfill. `execute()` emits real nbformat output objects so the same shape streams live to the browser and is persisted. `rebindKernel()` (added) tears down + restarts the kernel so it re-reads a rewritten kernelspec onto a new interpreter (a plain `restart()` reuses the original argv and would NOT switch venvs). **Startup injection (`initKernel`):** every kernel silently runs `%matplotlib inline` on fresh start **and** after `restartKernel()` (restart clears the namespace + backend), so matplotlib figures emit `image/png` even when the project's default backend isn't inline (e.g. `MPLBACKEND=agg`/macosx); guarded by try/except so it's a harmless no-op when matplotlib is absent.
- **Run watchdog = liveness PROBE, never silence (`kernel.ts` `execute()`).** `await future.done` is unbounded (a stalled websocket / an undelivered `execute_reply` / a dropped autorestart wedges the queue slot forever), so a watchdog guards it - but **silence is NOT death, and must never abort a run.** A Spark query (`sql.ts` compiles to `spark.sql(...).limit(N).toPandas()`), a big pandas op, or untraced training is ONE blocking call emitting NOTHING until it returns; the old abort-on-idle-window watchdog could not tell a 20-minute cluster job from a dead kernel and killed the user's real work ("Kernel went unresponsive (no activity for 900s)" on a healthy kernel). So `CELLAR_KERNEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` is now a **probe interval, not a deadline** (default **30s**, was 15min - a probe is cheap and no longer destructive, so a wedged notebook recovers in one window (proven death) to three (`SUSPECT_STRIKES`), ~30-90s, instead of waiting 15min for the watchdog's one shot). Expiry only runs `probeKernelLiveness`; **only its verdict may abort**. Any kernel traffic still re-arms, so a cell that emits output is never probed at all. **`CELLAR_KERNEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS=0` disables the per-run watchdog entirely** (`kernelIdleTimeoutMs` maps an explicit `0` to the `WATCHDOG_DISABLED` sentinel; `armWatchdog` never arms → never probes, never aborts, run relies only on the force-abort path), matching the culler's `CELLAR_KERNEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT=0`; empty/unset/non-numeric/negative still fall back to 30s via `envMs`, and the probe TIMEOUT knob keeps rejecting non-positive values as typos.
  - **The one out-of-band signal is the sidecar's REST view - `GET /api/kernels/<id>` (`KernelAPI.getKernelModel`).** Empirically verified against a real sidecar running a blocking `time.sleep`: it answers in **~3-5ms with `execution_state:'busy'` while the shell channel is blocked**, because it is the Jupyter SERVER's own bookkeeping. **What does NOT work, and why (do not retry these):** a `kernel_info_request` - or ANY `execute`, **`{internal:true}` included** (that bypasses Cellar's run queue, NOT the busy kernel) - rides the **shell channel** and QUEUES behind the running cell, so it answers only once the cell ends and would read "unresponsive" for exactly the workload this protects; and `getKernelInfo()`/`listKernels()` report the **local** `connection.status`, which a stalled websocket freezes at `busy` forever. A gone kernel surfaces as `getKernelModel` resolving **`undefined`** (it resolves the 404, it does not throw); a throw means the sidecar itself was unreachable.
  - **Abort rule:** Gone-from-server (`undefined`) or `execution_state:'dead'` ⇒ **proven dead**, abort immediately (not a race that resolves) - the REST verdict outranks any socket state, since a kernel the server no longer has is dead whatever the socket says. Else the server still HAS the kernel, so the verdict turns on whether we can still HEAR it, and **exactly two readings may accrue a strike**: (a) `connectionStatus==='disconnected'` ⇒ **suspect** - @jupyterlab exhausted its 7 retries, so the reply can never arrive whatever the kernel's state; (b) `connectionStatus==='connected'` **and** `execution_state!=='busy'` ⇒ **suspect** - we are connected, so we would have heard the reply, and the kernel is not running our cell: the genuine lost-reply case, the transport failure this exists for. Connected **and** `busy` ⇒ **alive**, re-arm (repeats indefinitely - a 3-hour job survives). Any other socket state - in practice **`'connecting'`**, @jupyterlab mid-reconnect - ⇒ **unknown/inconclusive, re-arm, NEVER a strike, regardless of `execution_state`**. That last rule is load-bearing in BOTH of the blip's shapes: `busy` is proof the kernel works, and `idle` does **not** mean the reply was lost - a cell that FINISHED mid-blip leaves the kernel idle with its `execute_reply`/iopub **buffered** for replay (`buffer_offline_messages`, default on), so convicting there would destroy a result already computed and report a loss that is about to arrive. Either way it would abort exactly the silent multi-hour Spark job this exists to protect. A truly dead socket is not spared, only slower: @jupyterlab exhausts its retries (~120s) into `'disconnected'`, which convicts via (a)'s strike path - so dead-socket recovery costs **~120s + the strike window (~90s at the default) ≈ 210s, delayed but never lost** - the explicitly accepted price. A suspect verdict aborts only after `SUSPECT_STRIKES` (3) **consecutive** readings, like `parent-watch`'s CONFIRM_STRIKES. Probe threw **or timed out** ⇒ the one reading with no view of the kernel at all, so it turns entirely on the socket (`probeFailureVerdict`): with the socket `'connected'`/`'connecting'` it is **inconclusive, NEVER abort** - the instance-reaper's stance (`pidReapDecision`): killing REQUIRES a positive match, because the costs are asymmetric (a false abort destroys an hour-old cluster job; a false re-arm only delays recovery of a notebook the user can restart by hand). That is what keeps a silent Spark query safe through a flaky sidecar: its socket is connected, so failing probes alone can never convict it. **EXCEPT** a failed/timed-out probe **and** `connectionStatus==='disconnected'` ⇒ **suspect** (the third strike-accruing reading, converting through the normal strike path): the websocket rides the SAME sidecar, so HTTP failing and the socket having exhausted its retries are two INDEPENDENT, CORROBORATING signals that we cannot hear this kernel by ANY route - a positive match, which UPHOLDS the doctrine rather than weakening it. Without that carve-out an unconditional `unknown` reset the strikes forever, so a **persistently** wedged sidecar (HTTP black-holed while the TCP socket stays up) wedged the queue slot PERMANENTLY - the exact failure the watchdog exists to clear, and one the old 15-minute abort did free. The inconclusive stance is for a **transient** failure only. Its message obeys the same honesty rule and is the strictest case: having lost BOTH routes we know LESS about the cell than any other path, so it says only that the kernel is unreachable by any route and the run was abandoned - never that the cell stopped. (Deliberately NOT implemented: preserving strikes across `unknown`, which would let a purely transient flake accumulate toward conviction.) Aborts keep the same `KernelExecuteAborted`/`'idle_watchdog'` reason + single-release path, but the message now names the real cause, not "no activity for Ns". **Each verdict carries its OWN complete sentence** (`ProbeResult.message`) - never a shared prefix in front of a fragment, because the paths know different things: gone/dead and connected-but-not-busy may say the kernel is no longer running the cell (the server lost it / we are connected so we would have heard the reply), but **`'disconnected'` may NOT** - without the socket we cannot see whether the cell is still running, and on a healthy kernel it very likely is, so it says only that the connection was lost and the run abandoned. Asserting a cause a reading did not establish is the exact defect ("no activity for 900s") this whole watchdog exists to retire; don't reintroduce it on the one path least able to know. A kernel PROCESS death is not this probe's case: jupyter **autorestarts** it and `abortActiveRuns(…,'kernel_autorestart')` already settles the run. Covered by `tests/unit/kernel-execute-watchdog.test.ts` + `tests/e2e/kernel-watchdog-probe.spec.ts`.
  - **Dead-socket self-heal = `refreshKernelConnection(nb)` (rung 1 of the reconnect ladder).** Convicting a `disconnected` socket **frees the run's slot but does NOT repair the transport**: the dead `KernelConnection` stayed in the `kernels` map, the PROCESS was still in the server's running list (so `reconcileCulledKernels` never dropped it), and `getKernel` handed that same dead socket to the next run - whose messages sat in @jupyterlab's `_pendingMessages` awaiting a reconnect whose 7 retries were spent, so **every later run silently wedged, was convicted again, and the notebook was broken until a manual restart** (the `cellar-dead-connection-refresh-x8` bug). The fix is a transport-only refresh built on `@jupyterlab/services`' **`KernelConnection.reconnect()`** (*"refreshes the connection to an existing kernel … does not restart the kernel"*): it rebuilds the websocket to the SAME kernel id and re-flushes pending messages, so the **namespace and any running cell survive**. **A plain `teardownKernel` is the WRONG fix** and must never replace this - a `disconnected` socket does NOT prove the process is dead; on a healthy kernel the user's cell is very likely still executing (the multi-hour Spark job the watchdog exists to protect), and teardown would destroy exactly that work. So teardown is gated behind the watchdog's own **"gone-from-server ⇒ proven dead"** rule: `refreshKernelConnection` first runs `probeKernelLiveness`, and only a **`dead`** verdict (server no longer has the kernel, or reports it dead) falls through to `teardownKernel` (rung 3) + lazy restart; a `disconnected` socket with a **present** model is `suspect`, not `dead`, so it reconnects the socket. On a successful refresh there is **NO epoch bump** (the session never changed), so `connectionStatus()`/`ran_this_session`/`spark`/`w` stay valid. **Idempotent + single-flight** per notebook (`NotebookKernel.refreshInFlight`, mirroring `livenessInFlight`), so the **two triggers converge on ONE reconnect - keep the reconnect model single, never add a second path**: (1) the watchdog conviction fires it fire-and-forget when `connectionStatus==='disconnected'` and changes the abort message from "Restart the kernel to recover" to "the connection is being refreshed; re-run the cell"; (2) **`execute()` defensively refreshes before dispatching a run** whose cached connection reads `disconnected` (this is what actually closes "every later run hangs"). The reconnect handshake is bounded by `reconnectTimeoutMs()` (**15s**, `CELLAR_KERNEL_RECONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS`); a timeout is non-fatal (`reconnect_timeout`) since a later run retries. Rungs 2 (session expiry, `reconnectTo`) and 3 (process restart, `reconnectAfterKernelRestart`) live in `databricks.ts` and are untouched. Covered by `tests/unit/kernel-connection-refresh.test.ts` + the self-heal case in `kernel-execute-watchdog.test.ts`.
  - **The probe MUST always settle, or it disarms the watchdog it drives.** `armWatchdog()` only re-arms *after* a probe settles, so a probe that never resolves is strictly worse than one that aborts wrongly: the watchdog stops cycling for the rest of the run and the wedged queue slot it exists to free stays wedged forever. `fetch` has **no default request timeout**, so a sidecar that accepts the connection and then says nothing (black-holed socket, wedged jupyter-server) hits exactly that. So the probe threads an `AbortSignal` through `makeSettings(signal)` (`ServerConnection.ISettings.init`, which @jupyterlab merges into the request as `{...settings.init, ...init}` - note it **replaces** the library's default `init`, hence `cache`/`credentials` are restated there) and races it against `probeTimeoutMs()` (**10s**, `CELLAR_KERNEL_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`), so the hung request is **cancelled**, not merely ignored and left leaking a socket. That controller is **hoisted into `execute()`**, so the run's `finally` also cancels a probe still in flight when the run settles (its verdict is already discarded by the settled/aborted guard) rather than letting a hung GET hold its socket for the rest of the timeout. The timeout is a **fixed constant, deliberately NOT derived from `idleMs`** - tests drive that window to 40ms and would time out every probe. At the **30s default window** it cannot cause overlapping probes: `armWatchdog()` re-arms only after a probe settles, and the only other arming path (`resetWatchdog`, on kernel traffic) cannot fire a second probe inside a window longer than the timeout. Probes are **not** sequential by construction, though - drive the window below the probe timeout and traffic mid-probe can start a second one; that is tolerable (and deliberately carries no overlap guard) because a verdict only ever re-arms or accrues a strike. A timed-out probe is `unknown` (re-arm, strikes reset) while the socket is still connected/reconnecting, **never** an abort - a hung sidecar alone proves nothing about the kernel, and the point is only that the watchdog keeps CYCLING so it can still convict on a later real dead/suspect verdict (or once the socket itself gives up: see the corroborated carve-out in the abort rule above).
- **`%restart_python` magic + comm subshells (`src/lib/server/controlMagic.ts`).** `initKernel` also injects (via `runSilent`, every start + restart) a `%restart_python` line magic (Databricks-style fresh-namespace restart). It's a **managed** restart, not a self-kill: the magic prints "Restarting Python kernel..." and opens a `cellar.control` comm with `{op:'restart'}`; `kernel.ts` `registerControlComm` catches it and runs `restartKernel(nbPath)` for THAT notebook only (epoch bumps → cells read not-run-this-session; widgets/queue cleared; `sys.path` project-root re-applied; sidebar card kept). The restart is **deferred until the kernel is next idle** (`whenIdle`) because the `comm_open` lands mid-run and tearing the process down before the cell's `execute_reply` would reject that run's future. **Load-bearing:** `getKernel` sets `kernel.commsOverSubshells = CommsOverSubshells.Disabled` before any comm opens. @jupyterlab/services' default under ipykernel 7 is `PerCommTarget`, which issues a `create_subshell_request` per comm target; `restart()` cancels that future and @jupyterlab rejects it with NO awaiter → an **unhandled rejection that crashes the Node app**. `%restart_python` (comm-then-restart) hit this every time, and a widget open racing a restart is the same latent trap; Cellar serializes runs so subshells buy nothing. Covered by `tests/unit/control-magic.test.ts`. **`%run` is IPython's built-in** and works unchanged: the kernel's cwd is WORKSPACE (sidecar spawn), so `%run helper.py` / `%run subdir/x.py` resolve workspace-relative; no Cellar code needed.
- **Packaging + self-update (`cellar --version` / `cellar --update`).** Both flags are handled at the top of `bin/cellar.js` (right after `mcp`, before normal arg parsing) so they never boot a server, and delegate to `src/lib/server/selfupdate.js` (node-builtins only, in `package.json` `files`). `--version` prints package.json version + git short-sha/branch/date/dirty (of `REPO`, the launcher's own dir) + detected install method. `--update` is **install-method aware** (`detectInstall`): Homebrew (running path under `brew --prefix`/a `/Cellar/cellar/` keg, or `brew list cellar` succeeds) → `brew update && brew upgrade --fetch-HEAD cellar` (the `--fetch-HEAD` is load-bearing — a HEAD keg won't move without it); else a git clone → `git pull --ff-only` + `npm ci`/`npm install` + `npm run build` (the Makefile `update` steps). Cellar tracks **latest** (git `main`), not semver. **Homebrew tap:** a HEAD-only formula (canonical copy `packaging/homebrew/cellar.rb`; installable copy in the private tap repo `fbereilh/homebrew-cellar` at `Formula/cellar.rb` — keep in sync) `depends_on "node"`+`"uv"`, `npm ci && npm run build`, installs into `libexec`, `bin/cellar` wrapper runs `node libexec/bin/cellar.js`. Audits clean under `brew audit --strict --formula` / `brew style`. Private repo → `brew install --HEAD` needs the user's git creds.
- **CHANGELOG.md is git-cliff-generated - never hand-edit it.** Produced from the git history (Conventional Commits + tags) by **git-cliff** (a `devDependency`; config `cliff.toml`, grouping commits by type into one dated section per release tag). Regenerate with **`make changelog`** (= `npm run changelog` = `bash scripts/gen-changelog.sh`); output is deterministic. The script runs git-cliff in **two passes** because a post-v0.1.0 rewrite of `main` orphaned the `v0.1.0` tag (its commit is no longer an ancestor of `HEAD`, so one walk folds the initial dev into v0.2.0): pass 1 emits the mainline releases from `merge-base(v0.1.0,HEAD)..HEAD`, pass 2 walks the `v0.1.0` tag directly (correct date), and awk strips pass 2's duplicate header before concatenating. Cutting a release = `npm version` → `make changelog` → commit → `git push --follow-tags` (see `RELEASING.md`); regeneration is a **manual** release step by design, not a CI commit-back (which would need branch-protection-clearing write creds and can't include a tag's changelog in its own tagged commit).
- **no-mistakes test step runs `npm test` (vitest), committed in `.no-mistakes.yaml`.** The per-repo `.no-mistakes.yaml` (`commands.test: 'npm test'`) makes the gate's test step run the real vitest suite (`vitest run`, non-watch, ~6s) deterministically as a baseline, so it no longer falls back to the "no test command configured, asking agent to run tests..." agent path (that fallback agent runs the suite fine but the process exits status 1 - a harness artifact the gate miscounts as a test failure, forcing manual gate-skips). **no-mistakes loads repo commands from the DEFAULT branch, not the pushed branch** (a security gate against a PR injecting commands), so a change to this file only takes effect for gate runs once it has merged to `main`. The same file also sets `test.evidence.store_in_repo: false` to keep evidence out of the repo (the default, made explicit).
- **`npm run test:e2e` BUILDS FIRST and runs at `workers: 2` - a stale build used to masquerade as real failures.** The e2e harness boots the REAL launcher **without `--dev`** (`tests/e2e/harness.ts`), so every spec exercises the production build `build/index.js`. `test:e2e` had no build dependency and the launcher guarded only a **missing** build, never a **stale** one, so the suite silently validated code that was never compiled: measured at 11 false failures burning **901s of a 1434s (23.9 min) run** - one spec file swung **853s/10-fail stale → 14s/11-pass fresh**. Worse than slow: a green run certified uncompiled code, a red run sent you chasing failures that did not exist. Two layers now, and they share **one** staleness rule in `src/lib/server/build-freshness.js` (node-builtins only, so `bin/cellar.js` can import it like `venv.js`; it is in `package.json` `files`) - do not add a second: (1) the **`pretest:e2e`** hook (`scripts/ensure-build.js`) rebuilds **only when stale**, so an already-fresh re-run costs ~100ms, not a 45s `vite build`; (2) `bin/cellar.js` **`assertUsableBuild()`** refuses a stale build outright, placed at the **top of `main()`** - before venv resolve/create and the Jupyter sidecar - so a build we will not serve costs no boot (`CELLAR_SKIP_BUILD_CHECK=1` overrides; `--dev` skips it). Staleness is **mtime-based** (`src/` + `static/` + root `package.json`/`vite.config.js`/`svelte.config.js`/`tsconfig.json` vs `build/index.js`), deliberately: no build system, no hash pass, no state file, and every legitimate change - an edit, a `git pull`, a `git checkout` - moves an mtime forward. **A packaged install must classify `unknown`, never `stale`**, and that CANNOT key off the absence of `src/` — `files` ships `src/lib/server/*.js` and Docker/Homebrew copy source too, so the source tree is present in real installs. So `isSourceCheckout(repo)` gates the mtime comparison on a POSITIVE signal: a `build/build-info.json` `source` of `release`/`env` is a stamped packaged build (Homebrew stable tarball / release automation) → NOT a checkout; otherwise a real checkout is proven by a `.git` at the repo root (a dir, or a worktree pointer file — dev clones, CI, and the e2e harness all have one). Anything ambiguous falls through to `unknown` → launch, so a shipped app can never false-refuse to start; only a proven source checkout runs the real mtime comparison. Concurrency: **`workers: process.env.CI ? 1 : 2`** with `fullyParallel: false` kept - tests INSIDE a file share one launcher/workspace/kernel and must stay ordered, while `workers` is *file*-level concurrency, which is safe because each spec gets its own `mkdtemp` workspace, dynamic ports, and `--new` (so concurrent launchers never reap each other). **Do NOT raise it past 2**: 4 was *not* faster (the makespan is bound by a few long spec files) and broke four timing-sensitive real-kernel specs (`kernel-status-ui`, `kernel-watchdog-probe`, `interrupt-clears-queue`, `databricks-header-pill`); more concurrency needs those split into their own `workers: 1` project first. Full suite: **~2.5 min green** (was 6.1 min serial, 23.9 min stale). The unit suite (~6s, 92% of its parallel floor) and the CI/no-mistakes gates were already correct and are untouched - **e2e is deliberately absent from both** (CI has no kernel runtime). Timing evidence: `data/cellar-test-timing-scout-t7/report.md`. Covered by `tests/unit/build-freshness.test.ts`.
- **Kernelspec = the venv lever.** The launcher writes a per-run `python3` kernelspec (`writeKernelspec`) whose `argv[0]` is the project python, into a temp `JUPYTER_PATH` dir handed to the sidecar; `startNew({name:'python3'})` then launches the project interpreter — `kernel.js` unchanged. The dir is passed to the app as `CELLAR_KERNELSPEC_DIR` so the runtime venv control can rewrite it.
- **Sidecar cwd must equal `root_dir` (= WORKSPACE).** `bin/cellar.js` spawns the Jupyter sidecar with `cwd: WORKSPACE`, not Cellar's `REPO`. Because `startNew({name:'python3'})` passes no notebook path, `jupyter_server` doesn't derive a kernel cwd from `root_dir` and the kernel inherits the sidecar's process cwd — so anchoring it at WORKSPACE is what makes `os.getcwd()`, relative reads/writes, and repo-root walks resolve in the user's project (else they resolve in / write into Cellar's install dir). Everything else in that spawn (host python, `JUPYTER_PATH`) is already an absolute path, so it stays REPO-independent. The old follow-up here (pass a path to `startNew` so a kernel roots somewhere other than the sidecar's cwd) SHIPPED, once kernels became per-notebook and the ambiguity that blocked it went away — see **Notebook code roots** below. The sidecar cwd rule is unchanged and still what a notebook declaring NO root gets.
- **Notebook code roots — one instance, several checkouts of one repo (`src/lib/notebookRoot.ts` + `src/lib/server/notebookRoot.ts` + `notebook-root-actions.ts`).** A notebook may declare `metadata.cellar.root = "roots/pr-482"` (workspace-relative, in the allowlisted `cellar` namespace so it round-trips clean-on-save with zero git diff); absent/empty = the workspace root, which is the DEFAULT and byte-for-byte today's behavior. The use case is a review notebook pointed at the branch under review, re-run after a fix to see which findings clear. **A root changes EXACTLY two bindings, both already parameterised on a path** — the kernel PROCESS's cwd (`getKernel` sends the root as the `path` field of the kernel-start request; jupyter_server resolves it under its `root_dir` = the workspace via `cwd_for_path`) and the `sys.path` entry `initKernel` injects (`projectRootAddCode`). **Everything else stays WORKSPACE-scoped and must not be "extended" into a root**: the file tree, git status/decorations/blame, search, checkpoints, the UI-state store, `.cellar/` runtime + harness config, the instance registry, and the INTERPRETER (one venv per instance, unaffected). Kernels were already per notebook, so two notebooks on two roots are already two processes with two namespaces — this only changes where each one's code comes from.
  - **Roots live INSIDE the workspace, and that is load-bearing, not a convenience:** the workspace stays the single `root_dir` and the single security boundary, so `resolveInWorkspace` and the notebook path guard are UNCHANGED (do not widen either). A root outside is refused **lexically** - an absolute path and a `..` traversal are refused by name, while a root that is a SYMLINK out of the workspace resolves, since the guard does no `realpathSync` and `statSync` follows links. That narrowing is **accepted, and must not be "fixed" in `notebookRoot.ts`**: the same lexical guard backs the file tree and `/api/fs/file`, so such a symlink already reads outside everywhere else, and realpathing the root alone would refuse legitimate setups (a macOS `mkdtemp` workspace is `/tmp` -> `/private/tmp`); the exposure is bounded to a hand-edited `metadata.cellar.root` or an MCP call (the picker cannot enumerate a symlink, whose dirent is not `isDirectory()`) over a kernel that runs arbitrary user code anyway. Reasoning lives at the seam, in that module's header. The conventional home is a visible `roots/` directory (`ROOTS_DIR`) — deliberately not `.roots/`, since `buildTree` skips dot-prefixed entries and the review files must be browsable; a root may sit anywhere inside the workspace, `roots/` is just what `list_roots` and the picker enumerate.
  - **A declared-but-unusable root REFUSES the kernel start; it never degrades.** `cwd_for_path` walks a non-directory UP to `root_dir`, so accepting a stale/misspelled root would silently run the notebook in the WORKSPACE while it claimed to run in the tree under review — precisely the failure this exists to prevent. `resolveRootDir` therefore checks existence + directory-ness and throws with the path and the fix; `getKernel` resolves BEFORE registering anything, so a refusal leaves no half-built Map entry. `getNotebookRoot` likewise returns an unusable hand-edited value VERBATIM rather than null, so it reaches the resolver instead of being read as "no root".
  - **Changing a root FREES the kernel (teardown), it does not `restart()` it.** A process's cwd is fixed at spawn and `restart()` reuses the same argv+cwd, so a restart would put `sys.path` and the real cwd in disagreement. `setNotebookRootAndRestart` (in `notebook-root-actions.ts`, the ONE place that pairs the declaration with the kernel — `jupytext-actions.ts` precedent) resolves first, persists, then `rebindKernel(nb)`; teardown bumps the epoch and publishes `kernel:shutdown`, so the cells honestly read "not run this session" and the result reports `namespace_cleared`. An UNCHANGED declaration (compared by MEANING via `sameRoot`) is a genuine no-op — no write, no teardown. For the same reason `NotebookKernel.codeRoot` records the root the PROCESS was started at (the `databricksRuntime` precedent) and `initKernel` re-injects THAT, never the document's current value.
  - **A `.py` (jupytext / Databricks source) notebook REFUSES a root outright, and its picker is not rendered — the same refuse-never-degrade rule, applied to PERSISTENCE.** `persist` writes a `.py` notebook back through jupytext / the Databricks converter, which rebuilds the file from its CELLS: no notebook-level metadata survives, so a declaration would live only in memory and be gone on the next reload, after which the notebook would silently run at the workspace root while still claiming the tree under review. So `setNotebookRoot` throws `textNotebookRootError` (naming `.py` and the fix) — at that ONE writer, so no surface can route around it — and `setNotebookRootAndRestart` checks it BEFORE resolving the directory, so the message names the real reason rather than reporting a perfectly good directory as the problem. **CLEARING (`''`/null) stays allowed everywhere**: it can only remove state, never strand it. The two halves must agree, so `NotebookView.isPy` (from `doc.jpFormat`) rides to the browser and gates `showRootBar` — a notebook that cannot hold a root is offered no control for one. Covered by `tests/unit/notebook-root-py.test.ts`.
  - **Surfaces:** `POST/GET /api/notebooks/root` + the `root-bar` picker in `Notebook.svelte` (rendered only when the workspace HAS roots or the notebook declares one AND the notebook is not `.py`, so a workspace that never adopts them shows no new chrome), the `notebook:root` SSE event, and MCP `list_roots` / `use_notebook(name, root)` / `get_notebook_map`'s top-level `root`. INSTRUCTIONS clause 8 carries the agent-facing rule. **The `use_notebook` COMPOSITION lives in the tool handler (so `svc.useNotebook` stays synchronous) and its ORDER is load-bearing**: the root is resolved FIRST (`svc.assertRootUsable`, the same resolver the write path runs — never a second validation rule), because the open-or-create and the session pin are irreversible by the time the write could throw, and a mistyped root used to leave a freshly created `untitled.ipynb` pinned behind its own error. **`root` WITHOUT `name` re-roots the session's ALREADY-PINNED notebook** (`svc.setRootOnWorkingNotebook`) rather than falling through `useNotebook`'s `untitled` default, which silently repointed the session of an agent trying to move the notebook it was working in; with nothing pinned that is an error naming the ambiguity, never an invented notebook. **The picker fetches the root list on MOUNT and on `notebook:root` only, never on every `load()`** — `load()` is also the reconnect / seq-gap / output-append resync backstop and the list costs a git read per root on the process carrying the kernel websockets and the SSE fan-out, while it only decides whether a picker is OFFERED; the fetch is generation-guarded (the `statusSeq`/`kernelReqSeq` convention) so an older reply cannot restore a stale list, and a change made HERE refetches explicitly, since this tab suppresses its own `notebook:root` echo by `originId`. The bar states the COST before the click (changing a root restarts the kernel and clears variables), matching the Databricks Runtime card, and its feedback line is self-dismissing. Covered by `tests/unit/notebook-root.test.ts` (rules + refusals + clean round-trip), `kernel-root.test.ts` (the request field and the injected `sys.path`, in BOTH directions — a no-root notebook must send NO `path` at all), `notebook-root-restart.test.ts` (the teardown pairing), `notebook-root-py.test.ts`, `mcp-notebook-root.test.ts` (incl. the two handler-ordering rules over the REAL registration), and `tests/e2e/notebook-roots.spec.ts` (the headline against real git worktrees + real kernels: two notebooks importing their own copy of one module name, concurrently).
  - **Stated limitation (deliberate, first cut):** git status/decorations/blame run from the WORKSPACE root, and a nested worktree is a separate checkout, so decorations for files inside a root may be wrong or absent. The ONE surface that reads a root's OWN git state is the sidebar's **Git** section (see the shell section below), which reports the commit each notebook's kernel is rooted at; everything else stays workspace-scoped. Making them root-aware is a separate task. The file tree also lists every root's files, so a repo with several same-repo worktrees shows its files several times; scoping the tree to the active notebook's root is a documented follow-up, not a defect.
- **Runtime venv control.** Settings → "Python environment" (`src/lib/Settings.svelte`) → `src/routes/api/venv` (GET info / POST `{path,create}`) → `src/lib/server/venv-bind.js`: resolves/creates the venv via uv, ensures ipykernel, rewrites the kernelspec, updates in-process `CELLAR_PROJECT_VENV`, then `rebindKernel()`. Confirm-before-create is enforced in the UI.
- **Notebook documents (server-owned, multi-doc).** `src/lib/server/notebook.js` holds in-memory notebooks keyed by absolute path in a `docs` Map — the default `notebook.ipynb` **and** any other workspace `.ipynb` opened live. Every exported op takes an optional trailing `nb` (workspace-relative or absolute path); omitting it targets the **active** notebook (`activePath`). `setActiveNotebook(nb)`/`getActiveNotebookPath()` track which notebook the agent-facing (MCP) tools default to; the UI POSTs `/api/notebooks` to follow tab focus. **Loading never writes to disk** (`loadDoc`): the default notebook is materialized *in memory* if missing so callers get a valid shape (SSR seeds the shell from `getDefaultNotebook()`), but no `.ipynb` is written on a bare startup/open — so opening Cellar in a folder drops **no** uninvited file. Every notebook (default included) persists only on a genuine mutation (add/edit/run/…) or an explicit `createNotebook`, which materializes the file (and the UI's empty-state "New notebook" button → `POST /api/notebooks {create:true}` → `createNotebook`); an arbitrary opened `.ipynb` must exist and is likewise NOT rewritten until mutated (no surprise git diff on open). Startup with no `notebook.ipynb` shows the shell's empty state (`+page.svelte`, `data-testid=empty-state`). Cellar owns cell IDs (uniqueness enforced **per document** on load via `enforceUniqueIds`; ids may legitimately repeat across notebooks — do NOT assume global uniqueness).
- **Persistence + clean-on-save.** `src/lib/server/ipynb.js` (de)serializes canonical doc ↔ real nbformat 4.5 with **deterministic** output (fixed key order, 1-space indent) so identical re-runs produce zero git diff. `src/lib/server/clean.js` is the nbdev field-policy port: nulls all `execution_count`, deny-by-default metadata allowlist (cell keeps only the `cellar` namespace; notebook keeps only `kernelspec` — drops `language_info`/`widgets`), normalizes `kernelspec.display_name`→`name`, scrubs `<… at 0x…>` memory addresses. Must stay **idempotent**. **Writes are atomic + serialized (the notebook is the user's PRIMARY data):** `writeNotebook` snapshots the cleaned JSON synchronously, then persists via `src/lib/server/atomic-write.ts` `atomicWriteFileSync` — write a **unique** temp *in the target's own dir* (a cross-device `/tmp` rename is NOT atomic), `fsync`, then `renameSync` over the target; on any error the temp is removed and the original is left untouched, so a crash/disk-full/SIGKILL mid-write can never truncate the `.ipynb` (a reader sees only the complete old or complete new file). Persist stays **synchronous** (the on-disk file must exist the instant persist returns — the unload flush and the git-blame/diff paths read it right after writing), so `src/lib/server/write-lock.ts` `serializeWriteSync(resolvedPath, fn)` runs inline when the path is idle (the normal case — a synchronous critical section can't be interrupted) and only queues behind an in-flight **async** writer; `withPathLock` is the async `Map<path,Promise>` tail-chain for that case, with the map entry deleted when a path's chain drains (never grows). Unique temp names mean even two racing writes to the same path each own a private temp — neither observes the other's half-written bytes; last complete rename wins. `.py` (jupytext) writes go through the external jupytext writer (`writePyNotebook`), unchanged. Covered by `tests/unit/atomic-write.test.ts`.
- **API.** `+page.server.js` load returns the default notebook + seeds the sidebar. `src/routes/api/notebooks` (`GET ?path=` loads a live doc's cells; `POST {path}` sets the active notebook, or `POST {path,create:true}` materializes it on disk via `createNotebook`). Cell ops under `src/routes/api/cells/…` (`POST` add, `PATCH` source/type, `DELETE`, `POST …/run` streams NDJSON + persists, `…/move`, `…/clear`) all carry an `nb` path (body field, or `?nb=` query for clear/delete) so they mutate the addressed notebook, not just the active one.
- **Cell types - all THREE nbformat types are real, `sql` is the one LOGICAL type, and `nbCellType` is the single mapping between them.** On disk a cell is nbformat `code`, `markdown` or **`raw`**; the type the UI toggle and the MCP tools speak adds `sql`, which is a `code` cell tagged `metadata.cellar.language='sql'`. **The reasoning INVERTS between `sql` and `raw`, and that inversion is the design** (`src/lib/cellLanguage.ts`'s header): `sql` is a metadata tag precisely BECAUSE nbformat does not define it and an invented `cell_type` would break jupytext/nbconvert/Jupyter, while `raw` **is** one of nbformat 4.5's three, so encoding it as a tagged code cell would be that same interop breakage with the sign flipped - Quarto reads `"cell_type": "raw"` to find frontmatter and nbconvert/nbdev route raw cells by it, so a tagged code cell would be executed or rendered by every downstream tool. Cellar already wrote raw cells to disk long before it could author one (`deserialize` passes a foreign `cell_type` through verbatim), so this widened a type to match data that was already there - **no migration, and no persistence change at all**: `serialize`/`cleanCell` key on `cell_type === 'code'`, so a raw cell round-trips with no `outputs`/`execution_count`, idempotently. Markdown and raw carry source + no outputs; clean-on-save + UUID ids apply the same. `addCell(afterId, cellType)` / `setCellType(id, type)` (PATCH `cell_type`) / the `POST /api/cells/bulk` `type` op / MCP's four `cell_type` enums all speak the four logical types; converting AWAY from a Python code cell clears outputs and drops the imports role and the nbdev export flag (nothing that reads as executable may sit on a cell holding no Python); the `hide_input` override is deliberately KEPT, since `$lib/hideInput` reads it only for a code cell - it is already inert on markdown/raw, so dropping it would only lose a report-view choice across a there-and-back conversion.
  - **`nbCellType(logical)` is the ONE logical→nbformat mapping** (`cellLanguage.ts`, beside `isRawCell`/`isSqlCell`/`logicalCellType`/`isLogicalCellType`). It replaced **four** hand-written copies of `cellType === 'markdown' ? 'markdown' : 'code'` - a shorthand that reads every third type as code, which is exactly how a raw cell got silently retyped by whichever copy was not updated. Never re-inline it. **`isLogicalCellType(rawCell, 'code')` must stay FALSE** (it requires the nbformat type AND the logical type to match): if it flips, the bulk retype silently stops converting a raw cell while the single-cell setter still does - the divergence that predicate exists to close, pinned by `tests/unit/raw-cell-type.test.ts`'s truth table and the `raw`→code case in `bulk-cell-ops.test.ts`. The one place a foreign/stored type is still checked by hand is `replaceCells`' `NB_CELL_TYPES`, which **validates** a checkpoint snapshot's type rather than coercing it - the old coercion silently turned a restored Quarto frontmatter cell into code.
  - **Every run entry point refuses a non-code cell, and the check sits at EACH DOOR - `executeCellRun` is deliberately NOT the gate.** There are three (`POST /api/cells/[id]/run`, MCP's `run_cell`, and `jupytext-actions.ts`'s `runAllCells`, which filters to `cell_type === 'code'` before calling the core), because each owes the caller a different refusal SHAPE - this route's terminal `run:refused` NDJSON frame, MCP's `status:'skipped'` result - which the shared core has no way to speak. So a new run path must bring its own refusal; it does not inherit one. MCP's `run_cell` always did (`service.ts`, and a raw cell gets a note NAMING raw rather than the generic "not a code cell", so an agent is not left guessing); `POST /api/cells/[id]/run` did not, so it handed markdown/raw source straight to the kernel - the `SyntaxError` landed in the cell's in-memory `outputs` where the UI showed it, then vanished on reload because `serialize` drops outputs for a non-code cell. It now refuses **before `enqueueRun`** (so a cell that can never execute never takes a queue slot) with a terminal `run:refused` NDJSON frame at HTTP 200 - the route's own idiom for `run:duplicate`/`run:cancelled`, and one the client's reader ignores, so no client change was needed for correctness. **The submitted source is still SAVED first**: refusing to run an edit is not refusing to keep it. `LiveNotebook.runCell` guards the same shape client-side to spare the round trip; the authoritative rule stays on the server.
  - **UI: a raw cell is inert and shows it.** `Cell.svelte` derives `isRaw`/`isRunnable` and every execution-shaped affordance gates on `isRunnable`, **never on `!isMarkdown`** - that shorthand is what rendered a Run button on frontmatter. No Run, no Run-above, no Clear, no copy-output, no output block, no ⋮ actions menu, no stale/not-run badges; it keeps drag, collapse, insert/move/delete, copy-INPUT, the id chip and the type toggle (which is how you leave raw). `langFor()` returns `[]` (no Lezer grammar ⇒ plain text) and `staticLang` is `'plain'` (`staticHighlight.ts`), so the static and live renders match pixel-for-pixel. A neutral `raw-badge` (ghost, unlike the tinted `sql-badge` - raw is the ABSENCE of an execution mode) says the cell is deliberate. Command-mode **`r`** and the palette's "Change cell(s) to raw" convert; there is deliberately **no "+ Raw" insert button** (the type menu covers a once-per-notebook type). `applyCellTypeLocally` + the remote `cell:type` handler mirror the server's drops for the `clampMoveIndex` reason - `cell:type` carries no metadata, so a client half that skipped them would keep drawing a badge the server has already stripped.
  - **HTML export renders a raw cell as a muted `<pre>`** (`export-html.ts` `renderRawCell`, `.raw-cell`), deliberately - the export shows what the notebook CONTAINS - and **emphatically NOT as raw HTML**, which would make a shared report an XSS surface; the source is escaped exactly like a code cell's and takes no highlighting. (nbconvert instead OMITS a raw cell whose mimetype is not `text/html`; Cellar shows it so nothing silently disappears.) An empty one is dropped, and report view keeps it - a raw cell is not code.
  - **A raw cell keeps its `raw_mimetype`/`format` metadata through a clean** (`ALLOWED_RAW_CELL_METADATA`, applied in `cleanCell` **only** when `cell_type === 'raw'`): nbformat defines `format` on a raw cell and Jupyter's "Raw NBConvert Format" toolbar writes `raw_mimetype`, and both name the output formats the cell belongs to, so dropping them silently repurposed it. Deliberately **NOT** a widening of `ALLOWED_CELL_METADATA` - a code/markdown cell's foreign metadata is still dropped, so the deny-by-default policy the zero-git-diff clean rests on is untouched. Keep the list narrow: a key belongs there only if nbformat defines it ON a raw cell.
  - **A `.py` TEXT notebook REFUSES a raw cell, at the doc-layer WRITERS - refuse, never degrade.** That same jupytext coercion (below) applies to a LIVE `.py` document: `persist` rebuilds the file from its cells, so a raw cell declared on one lived only in memory while disk held a `code` cell, and after a reload the frontmatter sat in a cell with a Run button - the exact failure a first-class raw type exists to prevent, and worse from MARKDOWN, whose prose lost its markers on the way too. So `assertCanHoldRaw` throws `RawCellTypeError` (`$lib/cellLanguage`, which owns the ONE message; the `textNotebookRootError` precedent, for the identical rebuilt-from-cells reason) at the four writers that can put `raw` into a document - `setCellType`, `setCellTypes` and **both creators, `addCell` and `addCellAt`**, the CREATE path being how the convert guard is otherwise routed around (`addCellAt`'s only caller passes `'code'`, so it is guarded to make the every-writer claim true by construction rather than by that caller's argument) - so the type menu, the `r` chord, the palette, the REST routes and every MCP add/convert tool are covered by ONE rule. Nothing is written: the batch and `addCells` refuse BEFORE their first write (and before the agent checkpoint), so a refused call mints no snapshot and reports no `changed` a client could read as a legitimate skip. **Only `raw`, and only on a `.py` doc** - every other conversion there, and every raw cell in an `.ipynb`, is untouched. Surfaced as a 400 `{reason:'raw-in-py-notebook', message}` by the three cell routes and as a named MCP refusal (`setType`'s `{ok,refused}`; `rawRefusable` wraps the three add tools, whose service layer THROWS rather than returning a union - a union return broke `addCells`' inferred shape across ~20 test files); INSTRUCTIONS clause 11 carries the agent-facing rule. The BROWSER mirrors it optimistically (the `clampMoveIndex` pairing): `Cell.svelte` filters Raw out of the type menu when `isPy` (the flag that already gates `showRootBar`, now threaded to `Cell`), `LiveNotebook`'s `refuseUnsupportedType` says so on the shell's transient `onNotice` line for the keystroke paths (which send no request that could fail), the cross-notebook PASTE refuses a raw entry for the whole paste, and a refusal the tab did not predict is still noticed + resynced - the route emits no `cell:type`, and this tab would echo-suppress it anyway. Covered by `tests/unit/raw-cell-py-notebook.test.ts` (every writer, the routes, the MCP surface, the `.ipynb` non-regression, plus source guards on both client halves).
  - **Every route that accepts a `cell_type` VALIDATES it against `$lib/cellLanguage`'s `LOGICAL_CELL_TYPES`/`isLogicalCellTypeName`** - `PATCH /api/cells/[id]`, `POST /api/cells` and the bulk route, which now share that ONE vocabulary rather than each keeping a copy (the list grew by one when `raw` landed, and three hand-maintained copies is how it grows by a fifth in only one of them). Not a style point: `nbCellType` maps anything it does not recognize onto `code`, so a malformed value ('RAW', a trailing space) silently converted the target into a RUNNABLE Python cell - harmless while every non-markdown type was code, a real downgrade of a cell holding frontmatter now. An out-of-vocabulary value is a 400 `bad-cell-type` (the bulk route's existing shape) and changes nothing; an ABSENT `cellType` on the add route still means `code`. The client `addCell` no longer reads `cell` out of a refusal body (a bare `TypeError` that said nothing and left the model unreconciled) - it notices, reloads and throws, which is the "an insert can reject" contract `undoDelete` already handles. Covered by `tests/unit/cell-type-route-validation.test.ts`.
  - **Two known limits, documented rather than fixed.** (1) `jupytext.ts` coerces `cell_type` to markdown|code on BOTH read and write, so a raw cell does not survive a `.py` round trip ("Save as .py" a Quarto notebook and the frontmatter comes back as code) - Cellar's Databricks/percent converter has no raw marker to speak (jupytext itself uses `# %% [raw]`), so carrying it is a separate task with its own format questions; do not "fix" it by widening the coercion (the LIVE-editing half of this is refused outright - see above). (2) Raw source is still SEARCHABLE and copy-input still works, deliberately. Covered by `tests/unit/raw-cell-type.test.ts` (model + persistence + checkpoint restore), `raw-cell-mcp.test.ts` (the agent surface, incl. a source guard that all four enums carry `'raw'`), `raw-cell-run-refused.test.ts` (the HTTP refusal, no kernel) and `tests/e2e/raw-cell.spec.ts` (real browser + real kernel: inert render, `Mod-Enter` producing nothing while the code cell below proves the kernel is live, Run all skipping it, and a code↔raw round trip on disk).
- **UI.** `src/lib/Cell.svelte` = one cell — CodeMirror editor (language via a Compartment: python / markdown). **Editor theming is centralized in `src/lib/editorTheme.js`, which exports one constant extension `EDITOR_THEME`** shared by `Cell.svelte` and `FileTab.svelte`. It is deliberately **theme-independent**: every color is a `--cellar-cm-*` custom property resolved in `app.css` by `light-dark()` against the **resolved `color-scheme`** daisyUI sets on `<html data-theme>` (never a theme-name allowlist), so a theme toggle is a plain CSS repaint that dispatches **nothing** into any editor. **Do not reintroduce a theme `Compartment`**: swapping a theme through one makes CodeMirror re-mount the single shared `<style>` element it keeps in `document.head`, invalidating document-wide style, and doing that once per mounted editor turned a toggle into N full-document style+layout recalcs (~16ms each → ~800ms of blocked main thread at 61 cells; now ~23ms flat). The consequence: the editor never sets CodeMirror's `dark` flag, so `EDITOR_THEME` must itself supply every color the library's `&light`/`&dark` base rules would have picked (panels, tooltips, buttons, text fields = the search panel + autocomplete popup) — add a token there, not a `dark:` option. The live editor palettes are Atom "One Light" (retuned for WCAG AA) on the grey editor surface and One Dark ported verbatim (the `@codemirror/theme-one-dark` dep is gone); the HTML export keeps the older pygments "default" light scheme. Palette provenance + license notices live in `THIRD-PARTY.md`. `light-dark()` takes colors only, so the few non-color values that differ by scheme (syntax font weight/style) key off `<html data-color-scheme>`, an attribute `applyTheme()` in `+page.svelte` derives from that same resolved `color-scheme`. There is no `theme` prop on `LiveNotebook`/`Notebook`/`Cell`/`FileTab`. `Cell.svelte` also carries the per-cell toolbar and renders nbformat outputs. **Copy input / copy output** (`data-testid=copy-input`/`copy-output`, beside the run/clear controls; the text rule lives in `src/lib/copyCell.ts`): both read the cell MODEL - `currentSource()` and `cell.outputs` - never a rendered node, so they work for a cell windowing has never mounted. The rule is *copy gives you, as text, what the cell SHOWS*, so `copyCell.ts`'s mime priority mirrors `renderOutput` below and a picture / plotly chart / live widget contributes NOTHING (an image-only cell's button is therefore disabled rather than copying matplotlib's `<Figure …>` placeholder repr). **A DataFrame copies the TABLE the grid shows, not pandas' elided repr**: the structured payload outranks `text/plain`, and a SAVED one - whose structured MIME clean-on-save strips - is recognized through the SAME `parsePandasDataFrameHtml` (`$lib/dataframeHtml`) `renderOutput` re-parses it with, never a second parser, so a live and a re-opened DataFrame copy the same shape. **ALL `text/html` outranks `text/plain`, not only the DataFrame shape** - html is what `renderOutput` SHOWS (in a sandboxed iframe) and IPython attaches a placeholder `text/plain` repr to almost every rich object, so preferring the repr pasted `<folium.folium.Map …>` for a map the cell renders in full; html that parses as a DataFrame copies its table, any other rich html is tag-stripped to a tab-separated table, never pasted as markup, and an all-script bundle (folium, Bokeh, Altair, plotly's html renderer) strips to nothing and so DISABLES the button - the image/plotly outcome, never a placeholder (that parse is browser-only, so outside a DOM a saved DataFrame repr falls through to the tag-strip rather than to `text/plain`, and `parsePandasDataFrameHtml` answers a bundle that never says "dataframe" by a regex scan rather than a full DOM parse it would only reject). **A `text/html` payload over `MAX_HTML_COPY_CHARS` (512 KiB) is refused outright**, measured off the nbformat line ARRAY so an oversized bundle is never even joined into one buffer: that decision runs inside the `$derived` on the cell-mount path AND during SSR, on the process carrying the kernel websockets and the SSE fan-out, and what exceeds it is an inline JS bundle (Bokeh `INLINE`, plotly `include_plotlyjs=True`) that is all script and would disable the button anyway - the same cheap-refusal spirit as `dataframeHtml.ts`'s `/dataframe/i` pre-check, with pandas' own repr (60x20 by default, ~25 KiB) three orders of magnitude clear of it. **Whitespace inside a `<pre>` is CONTENT, so those bodies are lifted out before the per-line tidy and put back after it** (`PRE_BLOCK` -> `preformattedText`, parked behind a NUL sentinel `decodeEntities` refuses to mint, so no payload can forge a slot): collapsing space runs and stripping indentation there destroyed the alignment of pygments-highlighted code, an aligned ASCII table or a `<pre>`-wrapped `to_string()`. The placeholder carries the trailing newline the `</pre>` it replaces would have emitted and no leading one, so block boundaries around a `<pre>` are unchanged. A **TRUNCATED** frame keeps pandas' own `[N rows x M columns]` footer, built in the ONE `dataframeTable` from `total_rows`/`total_cols` so the live and the saved payload copy it identically - that completeness signal is what the elided repr carried for free and what the grid shows as a truncation banner, and without it a 1M-row frame pastes 500 rows saying nothing; the totals are validated, never trusted (a missing or non-numeric one falls back to the counts present, so the footer can never read `[undefined rows …]`), and a non-truncated frame gets none. **A trailing TAB is a real, empty last column, so only ONE is ever trimmed** (`trimCopyText`, plus the per-line ` *\t? *$` in `htmlToPlainText` and its non-`.trim()` tail): a greedy trailing-whitespace trim pasted a row whose last cells are empty one column short of its siblings, i.e. ragged data in exactly the paste-a-table case this conversion exists for - text that is nothing BUT whitespace is still nothing to copy, which is what keeps a bare `print()` disabled. **The leading trim is `^\n+`, NOT `^\s+`**, for the same reason at the other end: a pandas Styler's header line opens with the tab of its blank index heading, and eating it left the header one column short of every row. **Whitespace touching a TABLE-STRUCTURE tag is absorbed first** (`TABLE_LAYOUT_WS`, before the tag strip): a jinja-templated Styler - the very case this path exists for, since it ships no `text/plain` and no `class="dataframe"` - puts a newline plus an indent between every cell, and those newlines survived the strip, so each cell landed on its own line and the whole frame pasted as ONE vertical column. Scoped to the table tags deliberately, never a blanket inter-tag collapse: a newline between two `<span>`s IS the line break in pygments-highlighted code. Both shapes (compact and pretty-printed) are pinned by test, since a fixture written with no whitespace between its tags cannot see either bug. **`copy-output` is disabled exactly when the copy would be EMPTY** - the enabled test reads the CONVERTED text, not the mere presence of a mime, so a bare `print()` (stream text `"\n"`) and an all-script Bokeh/Altair bundle disable it instead of admitting a click that copies nothing; `hasCopyableOutput(outs) === (copyOutputText(outs) !== '')` holds by construction because both read one per-output conversion memoized on output ELEMENT identity (a WeakMap, like `renderCache`), which is what keeps the `$derived` off a multi-MB regex+DOMParser pass on every ~40ms stream flush. The disabled REASON rides the `aria-label`, since a disabled control gets no pointer events and its `title` can never be hovered. The button is omitted entirely on a markdown cell; `copy-input` is always available. A MISSING value copies as a BLANK cell where the grid draws an italic `NaN` - the one **stated exception** to the shows-what-you-see rule (both payload sources reach `cellStr` as `null`), because a blank pastes into a spreadsheet as a real empty/NA cell while the literal `NaN` would paste as TEXT; the accepted cost is that a missing value and a genuinely empty-string cell copy identically, and live-vs-saved parity is untouched (one path, one string). `asText`/`stripAnsi`/`DataFramePayload` are shared with `$lib/search.ts` via `$lib/outputText.ts`, but the two PRIORITIES deliberately differ in TWO places (search returns the `<Figure …>` placeholder as searchable text where copy returns nothing; and search never touches `text/html` AT ALL, falling through to `text/plain`, where copy reads it both ways - so a SAVED DataFrame value the grid shows but the elided repr hid is copyable yet not findable, while a folium map is findable by its placeholder repr yet honestly not copyable - a PRE-EXISTING search-side gap, not a regression, whose fix would put a DOMParser parse and a multi-pass string conversion on the search hot path) - decide for both before changing either. A denied `navigator.clipboard.writeText` resolves to `false` and simply shows no confirmation. Covered by `tests/unit/copy-cell.test.ts` + `tests/e2e/copy-cell-io.spec.ts`. **Image outputs** (`image/png`/`jpeg`/`gif` base64, `image/svg+xml` URI-encoded) render as an `<img data-testid=output-image>` via `imageDataUrl()`; the image mime is **preferred over `text/plain`** so a matplotlib figure shows the plot, not its `<Figure … with N Axes>` repr (the kernel's inline-backend injection is what makes `image/png` present - see the Kernel bridge entry). **Double-click an image toggles fit-to-width ↔ natural pixel size** (JupyterLab behavior): in full size the `<img>` (`max-w-none`) is wrapped in an `overflow-auto` box (`data-testid=output-image-wrap`, capped `max-h-[80vh]`) so it scrolls within the output area instead of widening the notebook. The choice is **view-only** local `$state` keyed by output index (`fullImages` Set) - a re-run resets it, never touches the `.ipynb`. Because that wrapper owns its own scroll, a full-size image (like a DataFrame grid) is excluded from the outer output-scroll auto-contraction and hides that toggle (`ownsScroll = hasDataframe || hasFullImage`), so there is never a nested double-scroll. Markdown cells render to sanitized HTML (markdown-it `html:false` + DOMPurify, client-only via `browser` guard); double-click / pencil button → raw edit; Run/⌘Enter renders in place, Shift+Enter renders+advances. Type toggle is the far-right `python3`/`markdown` label. Per-notebook cell ops (add code/markdown, delete/reorder, single-kernel one-run-at-a-time; markdown "run" persists source only, no kernel) live in `src/lib/LiveNotebook.svelte`, which owns one notebook's cells + renders via `Notebook.svelte` (pure renderer). `Cell` seeds its editor doc once on mount and does NOT sync later `cell.source` changes into the editor — so `LiveNotebook` fetches authoritative cells on mount (never pre-seeds a stale snapshot). Editor source autosaves via debounced `PATCH`. Dev exposes `window.cellarViews[id]`. **Active cell** = `LiveNotebook` `activeId` (set on a Cell's `onfocusin`/`onpointerdown`, and on keyboard move); the active Cell gets a `border-primary` ring + a left accent bar (`data-active`), works in both themes. **Drag-to-reorder cells**: a per-cell grip handle (`data-testid=drag-handle`, `draggable`) + HTML5 DnD drop zones in `Notebook.svelte` → `onMoveToIndex` → the move route's `{toIndex}` → server `moveCellTo` (persists + git-clean like any move; keyboard ⌘/Ctrl+Shift+Arrow still works). **Scrollable outputs** (Jupyter "Enable Scrolling for Outputs"): a per-cell toggle contracts a tall output to a fixed-height scroll box; auto-applies above `SCROLL_THRESHOLD` px unless overridden. The choice persists in `metadata.cellar.output_scrolled` (undefined=auto / true / false) — round-trips through clean-on-save; note `cellView` in `notebook.js` now carries `metadata` so the browser sees it, set via PATCH `{scrolled}`. **Collapsible code editor** (sibling of scrollable-outputs, code cells only): the editor grows with content up to `EDITOR_MAX_PX` (576px = `max-h-[36rem]`), then a per-cell toggle (`data-testid=editor-collapse-toggle`, same style/placement as the outputs-scroll toggle) contracts it to that fixed scrollable box; auto-caps tall editors unless overridden (tri-state undefined=auto / true / false; a `ResizeObserver` on `editorEl` measures unclamped `scrollHeight`, covering content growth + the hidden→visible tab transition). **Both** expand toggles (this one and the outputs-scroll one) must clear the scroll box's vertical scrollbar, so neither is pinned at `right-1` — each measures its box's scrollbar width (`offsetWidth - clientWidth`, via `measureEditor` / a `ResizeObserver` `$effect`) and insets `style:right` by `scrollbarClearRight()` = `max(1rem, calc(0.25rem + Wpx))` (the measured term keeps a constant ~4px gap past a classic gutter of any width; the `1rem` floor covers macOS overlay scrollbars that measure 0 but paint over the edge on hover). Re-pinning to `right-1` reintroduces the overlap. Unlike `output_scrolled`, this choice is **runtime-only** like fold state — `LiveNotebook` `editorCollapsed` map persisted through the per-project UI-state store (`$lib/uiState`'s `getUi`/`setUi`, key `cellar-editor-collapsed:<absPath>`, i.e. the server-owned `.cellar/` JSON, NOT `localStorage` - there is deliberately no `localStorage` mirror, so it survives a relaunch on a new port), **never** written to the `.ipynb` (zero git-diff). Markdown edit views keep the original `max-h-96` cap. **Full-cell collapse** (`src/lib/cellCollapse.ts` = the pure rules; `LiveNotebook.svelte` owns the record, `Cell.svelte` draws the chevron + the collapsed render): a per-cell chevron (`data-testid=cell-collapse-toggle`, deliberately the same glyph + rotation as the heading fold-toggle, so the two collapse gestures read as one vocabulary) hides the cell's **input AND output**, leaving the toolbar row - which already carries the cell id, the type, the run controls and the run/stale badges - plus a one-line source preview (`data-testid=collapsed-preview`, `flex-1 min-w-0 truncate`, rendered ONLY while collapsed so the expanded layout is untouched), so a collapsed cell stays identifiable AND actionable; the chevron, or a click anywhere on the collapsed header that is not one of its own controls, expands it. **Orthogonal to its two neighbours and to heading folding, and that separation is the design**: `editorCollapsed` contracts only a TALL editor (output untouched), `hide_input` hides the input and KEEPS the output, a heading fold hides a RANGE of cells outright - a full collapse supersedes them only VISUALLY, and expanding restores exactly what they were. State is a `Record<id,true>` keyed by cell id, **never off a mounted node** (windowing means most cells have no DOM at all), persisted per notebook through the SAME UI store as `editorCollapsed` (`cellar-cell-collapsed:<absPath>`) - "hide this cell" is a durable intent, so it survives a reload - and never reaching the `.ipynb` (zero git diff). Expanding **DELETES** the entry rather than storing `false`, which is what doubles as the delete-path cleanup (`forgetCollapsed`, called from the local delete, the bulk delete and the `cell:deleted` event). **The two LOCAL deletes prune only once the server CONFIRMS** (inside `deleteCell`'s `res?.ok` branch and `deleteSelection`'s `if (await bulkOp(...))`, beside the undo group those already hold back for the same reason), because unlike its `setRawEdit` neighbour this record is PERSISTED: an optimistic prune the server then refuses (`would-empty-notebook`, via the stale client cell-count race) or that never lands (the `fetch(...).catch(() => null)` network path) has already written the state away for good - `load()` puts the cell back and `loadCellCollapsed()` re-reads the ALREADY-PRUNED record, so nothing restores it. A stale entry in the interim is harmless; the next load's `pruneCollapsedToCells` is the backstop. The `cell:deleted` event is a genuine server-confirmed delete and keeps pruning immediately. That covers the deletions a tab OBSERVES; the ones it cannot (a cell deleted while it was disconnected - the reconnect/seq-gap refetch - or a checkpoint restore) are caught by the load-time reconcile `retainCells`/`pruneCollapsedToCells`, which prunes the record to the cells the load actually returned, so **together** they are what makes "the persisted record can never leak an entry for a cell that is gone" true in general. Both are identity-preserving: a load with nothing to drop writes nothing. Two windowing couplings, both load-bearing: `estimateHeight(cell, collapsed)` returns the header-only `COLLAPSED_CELL_PX` (34 - the measured row) so a collapsed cell that has never been on screen reserves no phantom flow; and `focusEditorOrCell` focuses the CARD, because a `display:none` editor cannot take focus and a focus that silently went nowhere takes the modal keyboard with it. **Edit-intent (`enterEdit`) EXPANDS first** - asking to type in a cell is asking to see it - but a mere navigation or selection does **not**: landing on a collapsed cell (`j`/`k`, follow-running, the find bar) leaves it collapsed, the deliberate contrast with a FOLD, which must unfold because a `display:none` cell shows nothing at all. **The find bar is the deliberate, decided case of that rule**: a CONTENT match inside a collapsed cell is counted but not painted, left that way because a `hide_input` (report-view) cell's match is equally invisible today (so the two hide features stay consistent) and because a Ctrl+F sweep must never silently discard collapse state the user set by hand - do not "fix" it by expanding on a search jump or by dropping collapsed cells from the search count. **The ONE thing a collapsed cell still paints is a `field:'id'` match** (searching a cell by its handle - see the search-engine entry): its surface is the toolbar chip, which is exactly what a collapse keeps on screen, so that highlight is visible precisely where the content ones cannot be. The same rule governs the header click: `onHeaderClick` runs a click carrying a SELECTION modifier through the shared `pointerIntent` and **returns without expanding** (a Shift range / Cmd-Ctrl toggle landing on the header is a selection, not a disclosure) - necessary because `click` is NOT one of the compatibility mouse events `onCardPointerDown`'s `preventDefault` suppresses, so it arrives here regardless. The body blocks hide by one of two mechanisms, chosen per block rather than uniformly: `{#if}` where the content is purely derived (rendered markdown, the show-code bar, the "changed on server" banner), `display:none` where a re-mount would reset view state (the editor's CodeMirror cursor/undo, the output's DataFrame-grid page, an `HtmlOutput` iframe that would RELOAD). **That split is why `cellCollapsed` is a tracked dep of `Cell.svelte`'s search-highlight `$effect`**: the `{#if}` DROPS the rendered-markdown block, detaching the very nodes this cell's registered `cellar-search` Ranges point at, and nothing else changes on expand - so without it a re-expanded markdown cell painted no highlights while the find bar still counted its match (a code cell's editor/output survive `display:none`, so their Ranges stay valid and repaint on their own). While collapsed all three CONTENT surfaces CLEAR rather than keeping registry entries with nothing to paint - the id chip (`K_ID`) is the deliberate exception and keeps painting (both halves pinned by the collapse cases in `tests/e2e/find-highlight.spec.ts`). **EVERY block below the toolbar is guarded, the "changed on server" banner included** - that is what makes the header-only `COLLAPSED_CELL_PX` the window plans against TRUE, and it also stops the banner offering a Load button acting on an editor the user cannot see. It takes the `{#if}` form because its content is purely derived from `remoteChanged`/`pendingRemoteSource`, which are Cell-instance state the collapse never touches: **collapsing must NEVER call `clearRemoteStash`** (that would silently discard an agent's edit), so expanding shows the same banner and a windowed teardown still hands the marker back via `cell.remoteEdit`. **One accepted seam with FOLDING, deliberately not worked around**: the fold chevron and the "N cells hidden" chip live inside the rendered markdown, so collapsing a markdown cell whose heading is currently FOLDED hides them with the body and the folded range stays hidden with no in-notebook marker of why. It is cosmetic, arises only from deliberately folding AND collapsing the same cell, is recoverable three ways (expand the cell, the sidebar Outline chevron, `h`/`l`), and the collapsed header's preview still reads the heading itself (`## Setup`). Do NOT put a fold marker on the collapsed header (the minimal row is the point of the feature) and do NOT touch fold state on collapse in either direction - refusing to collapse, or auto-unfolding, means writing state the user never asked to change. Per the per-cell-affordance rule the chevron addresses ITS cell only, never the multi-selection, and there is deliberately no shortcut binding yet. Covered by `tests/unit/cell-collapse.test.ts` (the pure rules + the collapsed height estimate) + `tests/e2e/cell-collapse.spec.ts` (real browser at the windowed default: hide/round-trip, persistence in both directions, a modifier click selecting without expanding while a plain one still expands, a windowed-out collapsed cell staying collapsed and compact, running from a collapsed header, `j`/`k` landing without expanding, delete cleanup, a delete the server never confirms leaving the persisted entry INTACT, the remote-changed banner hidden while collapsed and its stash surviving both an expand and a windowed remount, and independence from editor-collapse + folding). Those tests share ONE workspace and ONE persisted collapse record and several select their target by SOURCE pattern, so a test that rewrites a source or leaves a cell collapsed must put it back (`restoreCell`) or it silently re-points a later test's selector at a different, possibly windowed-out cell. **Markdown tables in text/stream output** are parsed (header + `|---|` separator) and rendered as sanitized daisyUI `table table-zebra table-xs` (`renderOutputMarkdown` - the same markdown-it config + DOMPurify path as markdown cells, minus math: kernel output is data, so a `$` in it stays a `$`); non-table text is unchanged. **Collapsible headings** (Jupyter "Collapsible Headings"): **the foldable unit is a heading *occurrence*, not a cell.** A markdown cell may hold several headings (`# Intro` / `## Setup` / `### Details` in one cell is ordinary), so `src/lib/headings.js` splits each markdown source into segments at its heading lines (fence-aware) and addresses each heading by a **fold key**: the plain cell id for a cell's *leading* heading, `<cellId>#<segIndex>` for the rest. That is what lets **every** heading level carry its own chevron (`data-testid=fold-toggle`, rendered inline beside the heading in `Cell.svelte`'s rendered markdown, not in the toolbar) - keying folds by cell id could only ever fold the first heading of a cell. A heading's section runs until the next heading of the **same or higher** level, wherever it lives: later in the same cell, or in a following cell. Nested folds compose. `computeFolding(cells, foldedIds)` returns `{hidden, segs, counts}` - whole cells to drop (`Notebook.svelte` `class:hidden`s them), per-cell segment indices an *outer* fold hides inside a still-visible cell, and the whole-cell count per fold key. A folded heading keeps its own line, tinted with an ellipsis + "N cells hidden" chip, so a collapsed section reads as collapsed. Fold state is **runtime-only** (`LiveNotebook` `foldedIds`, persisted through the per-project UI-state store as `cellar-folds:<absPath>` - `$lib/uiState`'s `getUi`/`setUi`, i.e. the server-owned `.cellar/` JSON, NOT `localStorage`, so it survives a relaunch on a new port and is shared by every browser context opening that workspace, restored on load) so it never touches the `.ipynb` (zero git-diff) - the deliberate contrast with `output_scrolled`, which does round-trip through `metadata.cellar`. **`LiveNotebook` owns the one fold state and the sidebar Outline is a second *view* of it, never a second copy**: `onFoldsChange(path, foldedIds, folding)` publishes it up through `+page.svelte`, `onRegisterFolds(path, toggleFold)` lets an outline chevron call straight into the notebook's own `toggleFold`. Both directions therefore stay in sync by construction. (When reporting fold state upward, assign into the map key - `notebooksFolds[path] = …`; a `{...spread}` rebuild *reads* the state the reporting `$effect` writes, and Svelte loops forever.) `headings.js` also owns `withHeadingLevel(source, level)`, the write-side inverse of `headerLevel` that the `1`-`6` shortcuts use to (re)stamp a cell's leading heading. Note the deliberate divergence: MCP's server-side section tree (`service.js`) still treats a cell's first line as its only header, since agent-facing sections are addressed by cell id.
- **TeX math in rendered markdown (KaTeX; authoritative wiring: `src/lib/markdown.ts`).** `$…$` inline and `$$…$$` display (Jupyter's delimiters) typeset in **authored prose only**: notebook markdown cells and `.md` file previews. `markdown.ts` owns every markdown engine in the app and exposes exactly two renderers, both funnelling through ONE `sanitize` call site and the ONE `MARKDOWN_SANITIZE_CONFIG` - `renderMarkdown` (prose, math on) and `renderOutputMarkdown` (the markdown-table-in-KERNEL-OUTPUT path, math OFF). `md` itself is **not exported**, so no surface can reach `md.render` and bypass DOMPurify. **The two-renderer split is by CONTENT CLASS, not the renderer drift the consolidation removed:** kernel output is arbitrary data and a printed price column ("$5", "$1,200") is the everyday case, so $-scanning it would false-typeset the user's own DATA; rendering real `text/latex` output is a separate feature keyed on the output's MIME type (deliberately NOT $-scanning arbitrary cells). Beyond math the two are identical - same markdown-it config, same sanitizer, same call site - so do not re-fork a renderer per surface, and do not give the output one math. That is also why `Cell.svelte`'s `renderTable` calls `renderOutputMarkdown` instead of keeping its own markdown-it + DOMPurify pair. Engine is **KaTeX via `@vscode/markdown-it-katex`** (the plugin VS Code's own markdown preview uses — maintained, markdown-it v14) — KaTeX, **not MathJax**, because it typesets at PARSE time into a static HTML+MathML tree: no runtime script, which is exactly what lets math survive DOMPurify and Cellar's script-free rendering contract. The options object is spread into `katex.renderToString`, so KaTeX's own knobs ride along: `throwOnError:false` (an invalid formula renders KaTeX's inline red error node — one bad expression can never blank a cell or a preview), `trust:false` (refuses every command that could emit raw HTML or a URL — `\href`, `\url`, `\includegraphics`, `\html*` — which is load-bearing, not decoration), `strict:false`, and **`maxSize:10` (em)** - `trust:false` does NOT gate `\rule`/`\hspace`/`\genfrac` sizes, so at KaTeX's default of `Infinity` a downloaded notebook containing `$\rule{1em}{9999em}$` blows out the cell or preview around it; capped, it still renders. `maxExpand` is deliberately untouched (its default is 1000, NOT `Infinity`, so macro-expansion DoS is already bounded). **Currency stays prose:** the plugin's own delimiter rules reject a `$` pair that would open or close against a word character, so "it cost $5 and $10 total" is untouched (pinned by a test — `\(…\)`/`\[…\]` are NOT supported by this plugin, and `$`/`$$` is the required Jupyter-native set).
	- **KNOWN, ACCEPTED limitation - an unfenced shell prompt typesets.** Those same rules reject a closing `$` followed by a word character but NOT one preceded by whitespace, so a paragraph of `$ npm install` / `$ npm run build` lines parses the span between the two `$`s as inline math. **Jupyter behaves identically**: this is inherent to the `$` delimiter set the feature is required to use, a consequence of the requested delimiters rather than a bug in the wiring, so it is documented (here and in `markdown.ts`) rather than worked around - do not "fix" it by changing the delimiter rules. The workaround is the one a user reaches for anyway and is pinned by test: math is NOT parsed inside an inline code span (`` `$ npm install` ``) or a fenced block, which is where shell commands belong. The accepted behavior itself is pinned too, so changing it has to be deliberate.
	- **The DOMPurify allowlist is extended by exactly TWO tag names, and that is the whole security surface** (`MARKDOWN_SANITIZE_CONFIG`). DOMPurify's defaults already pass KaTeX's `<span class="katex">` tree (`class`/`style`/`aria-hidden` included) **and** its MathML — everything except `<semantics>`/`<annotation>`, the pair carrying the original TeX. Without them DOMPurify **UNWRAPS** the annotation rather than dropping it, so the raw TeX leaks into the MathML as text a screen reader reads aloud while the visible math still looks perfect: a silent regression, which is why the test asserts the ELEMENTS and their `encoding`, never just the rendered text.
