Both run entry points publish `run:start`/`run:output` (first frame of each stream element; subsequent growth rides `run:output-append` deltas — see the Streamed-output deltas entry)/`run:end`: the UI `/api/cells/[id]/run` route (`actor:'user'`, carries the caller's `originId`) and MCP `service.js` `runCell` (`actor:'agent'`; `runCells`/`runAll`/`runRange` funnel through it). **Canonical notebook id (the P0 gotcha):** events are tagged with the notebook's **absolute** path; `LiveNotebook` learns that id from its `GET /api/notebooks` load response (`body.notebook.path`) and filters SSE on it — the workspace-relative `path` prop is only for the REST API (server resolves it via `resolveNotebookPath`/`resolveAbs`). **Echo:** the initiating tab drops its own events by `originId` match (it renders that run from the `/run` NDJSON response), so a user run never double-renders; agent runs carry no `originId` so every tab renders them. **Backstop:** on `EventSource` (re)connect or a detected `seq` gap, the notebook refetches. Deferred to later phases (NOT built here): unified server-side run function, the busy/queue hang fix (run metadata landed later in P3, see below).
- **Follow the running cell (viewport).** `LiveNotebook`'s follow effect keys off **`runningId`** (the cell running in THIS notebook, whatever the actor - the user's own run, another tab's, or an agent's), so the viewed notebook follows its running cell however it was started. The load-bearing scope is the **`active`** guard: a run in a notebook the user is NOT looking at (an agent working a background notebook) never moves the viewport, which is what preserves the active/viewed-notebook decoupling (no background hijack). A **user opt-out** (`follow` prop, default on) gates the whole behavior; the shell (`+page.svelte`) owns it as a global viewer preference persisted via the per-project UI-state store (`getUi`/`setUi` `cellar-follow-running-cell`, NOT the `.ipynb`), toggled from the Navbar app-menu "View" section (`data-testid=toggle-follow-running-cell`, styled like the hide-all-code toggle). There is no `agentRunningId`: following any run in the viewed notebook is the intended behavior, and the `active` guard alone scopes it. Guards, in order: the `follow` preference must be on; the tab must be `active` (a hidden pane is `display:none`, so it has no geometry - the effect re-fires on activation and follows a still-running cell); the user must not have typed inside this notebook in the last 3s (`lastTypedAt`, a capture-phase keydown listener on the notebook root; focus alone is too coarse - a cell keeps editor focus long after typing stops, and following would silently never happen); the cell must not already be visible (follow-tail: "visible" = its **top** edge is on screen, since the run affordance lives there). A cell hidden by a folded heading is revealed first (`revealCell` drops exactly the folded headers whose own section contains it) - fold state is runtime-only, so this costs a re-fold, not a git diff. The scroll runs inside `untrack()`: it reads (and `revealCell` writes) fold state, which would otherwise re-fire the effect on every edit and loop. Cell lookup is scoped to `rootEl` because **cell ids repeat across notebooks** and every open notebook stays mounted. `rootEl` is the notebook's single subtree handle, shared with the modal keyboard: it must be a real, focusable box (`tabindex=-1`), **not** `display:contents` - the shell's `overflow-y-auto` pane is the scroll ancestor, so a plain wrapper stays layout-neutral. Its scroll helper (`scrollElementIntoView`, centers or top-pins a cell, called with `settle:'visible'` so it corrects the mount-drift but never chases a growing streaming cell) is deliberately distinct from the keyboard's smallest-movement writer `scrollElementIntoViewNearest` (`block:'nearest'`, the tail of `scrollCellIntoView`'s mount → land → reveal → release sequence): run-follow reframes the viewport, selection must not. Both go through `ensureCellMounted` first, so either can land on a cell virtualization has windowed out (see the virtualization entry's P4 note). Selection (`activeId`) is never touched; `Cell.svelte` marks the running cell with a `warning`-hued accent bar + ring (`data-running`, `data-testid=running-bar`) that outranks the `primary` selection accent, so "executing" and "selected" read as different things. Covered by `tests/e2e/follow-running-cell.spec.ts` (follow ON scrolls to the running cell, OFF stays put, the choice persists across reload, and a background notebook's run never hijacks the view).
- **Live structural sync (SSE, P2).** Agent-driven (and cross-tab) *structural* changes also reflect live with no reload. **Single chokepoint:** `notebook.js`'s mutation ops (`addCell`/`setSource`/`setCellType`/`deleteCell`/`deleteCells`/`clearOutputs`/`clearOutputsForCells`/`moveCell`/`moveCellTo`) `emit()` `cell:added`/`cell:edited`/`cell:type`/`cell:deleted`/`cell:cleared`/`cell:moved` (schema per scout report §3.3), tagged with the doc's absolute path + `seq` + an optional `originId` threaded through from the UI REST routes (MCP calls pass none → every tab renders). `LiveNotebook` `applyStructuralEvent` patches its `cells` in place (insert/remove/reorder/retype), gated by the same `originId` echo-suppression + `seq`-gap-refetch backstop as runs. **Editor-safety rule (report §3.4):** a `cell:edited` hands the new source to `Cell.svelte` via `cell.remoteEdit`; the Cell applies it to CodeMirror only when the cell is **not** focused / has no pending local edit — otherwise it shows a "Changed on server" affordance (`data-testid=remote-changed`) with a **Load** button, so remote edits never clobber active typing. Applying a remote edit sets `applyingRemote` so the editor's update listener doesn't echo it back as a local save. **The marker is the EDITOR's channel, never the model's, so `cell.source` must be written wherever no mounted Cell owns it** - with windowing on by default a far cell has no instance, and `cell.source` is what every non-editor reader falls back to: `runCodeIds` reads it for a bulk run and `/api/cells/[id]/run` PERSISTS whatever it is POSTed, so a stale value would run the pre-edit code AND write it over the agent's edit in the `.ipynb` (it also feeds `onCellsChange` → find bar / sidebar search / outline, so the edit would be unfindable until the cell scrolled back). Two write points, both gated on "nothing owns the editor": `applyStructuralEvent` writes the model when `cellApis[id]` is absent at delivery, and the Cell's teardown hand-back writes it alongside re-arming the marker - its `currentSource() === savedSource` guard already proves there is no local text to lose. Covered by `tests/e2e/virtualization-remote-edit-run.spec.ts`. **New/active notebooks surface live:** MCP `create_notebook(name?)` really creates (or opens) a workspace `.ipynb`, makes it active, and publishes `notebook:opened {nb,relPath,name}`; the shell (`+page.svelte`) subscribes and opens/focuses a tab via `openFilePermanent` (own-action echo dropped by `originId`). Because the agent's write ops default to `activePath` (the notebook the UI last focused / just created), agent edits land in the exact in-memory doc the open tab shows.
- **Single-instance-per-folder + reap (`bin/cellar.js` step 0 + `runtime.js` + `instances.js`).** A relaunch in a folder that already has a live cellar **takes over**: it **reaps the old instance and starts fresh**, rather than leaving the old server running with stale in-memory code. This is the fix for the pile-up: old `build/index.js` servers accumulate (a launcher killed uncleanly leaves its app **reparented to init**, still listening for hours; or a still-running instance after a code update keeps serving old MCP `instructions`/tool descriptions to agents that discover its port). Two servers also each persist `notebook.ipynb` from independent in-memory docs → last-writer-wins clobber, so exactly one at a time is enforced. **`.cellar/instance.lock`** is still an `O_EXCL` lockfile (`acquireInstanceLock`) claimed at the top of `main()`, atomically gating the folder so a rapid double-launch can't start two at once. The loser of the lock **no longer attaches** — it **reaps the owner** (`reapInstance` on the owner's registry entry when present, so its recorded app/jupyter child pids are killed explicitly, else a synthetic entry relying on the launcher's SIGTERM cascade), then re-acquires the lock (retry loop) and boots. `waitForInstanceUrl` is now unused/removed from the launcher. `--new`/`--force` skips the whole reap+lock block to run a deliberate second instance. `cleanup()` releases the lock (pid-guarded) only if we took it, and drops our registry entry.
  - **Global instance registry (`instances.js`, `~/.cellar/instances/<launcherPid>.json`).** Cross-workspace record of every instance (`launcherPid` + `appPid`/`jupyterPid` filled in after spawn, ports, workspace, `startedAt`, and a per-pid **`launcherStart`/`appStart`/`jupyterStart`** = real OS start time from `ps -o lstart=`). Lives in `$HOME` (not the project `.cellar/`) so it **survives the workspace dir being removed** — the deleted-worktree case where orphans pile up. Node builtins + global fetch only, so both the launcher and the SvelteKit server import it. `runtime.js` now **exports** `pidAlive`/`appPortResponds` for it. On launch (before the lock) the launcher also runs `pruneDeadInstances()` (drop registry files with no live process) and `reapVanishedWorkspaces()` (reap instances whose workspace dir is gone) — both always safe; a live instance for a real other project is never touched. After winning the lock it also `reapWorkspaceInstances(WORKSPACE, {excludePid})` to clear orphaned children a crashed prior launcher left for **this** folder.
    - **Identity-verified reaping — the registry keys by launcher pid, but a pid is only unique while alive.** Once an instance exits (its throwaway worktree removed), the OS can hand that exact pid to an UNRELATED process; a reaper that SIGTERMs `launcherPid` on the pid alone then kills the innocent reuser (the observed bug: a fresh launch killed a different healthy cellar + its live kernel). So `reapInstance` never signals a pid unless it is **provably still that instance**: `pidReapDecision(pid, recordedStart, startedAt)` → `verifyPidIdentity` compares the pid's live `processStartTime()` to the recorded `*Start` (exact, ±`START_TIME_SKEW_MS`), falling back to the `startedAt` window for legacy entries. `true` ⇒ kill; `false` (pid reused) or `null` (unverifiable / no `ps` / Windows) ⇒ **SKIP the kill, only prune the stale entry** — killing REQUIRES a positive match, so over-pruning is the safe direction. This is why lingering stale entries are harmless. Trade-off: a **pre-registry / lost-entry** same-workspace owner carries no `*Start`, so a take-over degrades to skip-and-reclaim-the-lock (documented at the launcher call site); every current-code instance registers `launcherStart`, so the normal relaunch take-over is unaffected. **Every reap decision is logged** (candidate `launcher`/`workspace`/`reason`, the identity result, KILLED vs SKIPPED/PRUNED). The launcher/`parent-watch`/app also log their **shutdown trigger** (SIGTERM received vs. orphan self-exit vs. child-exit vs. clean) so a killed kernel is distinguishable from a crash. `parent-watch` requires `CONFIRM_STRIKES` consecutive dead readings before self-exiting. Covered by `tests/unit/instances-reap.test.ts` (spawns a real process to prove an impostor pid survives).
  - **Orphan self-exit (`parent-watch.js`, started from `hooks.server.js`).** The app is passed `CELLAR_LAUNCHER_PID` and polls it every 5s; when the launcher dies uncleanly the app kills the (also-orphaned) Jupyter sidecar, unregisters, and exits — so an orphaned instance reaps itself instead of lingering. A reused launcher pid reads as "alive" (safe direction: lingers, never exits a healthy server).
  - **`cellar ls` / `cellar cleanup [--all] [-y]`** subcommands (handled at the top of `bin/cellar.js`, before normal arg parsing, never boot a server). `ls` lists registry instances (with liveness) + a POSIX `ps` scan for **untracked** cellar app processes (`scanUntrackedCellarProcesses`: matches only `node <abs>/…/cellar/build/index.js` — deliberately strict, since other agents' processes carry our prompt text mentioning "node build/index.js"/"cellar" as args). `cleanup` prunes dead registry entries and reaps **orphans** (registered zombies with a dead launcher, and untracked `ppid==1` cellar apps); `--all` also stops every **live** instance across all workspaces. Confirms unless `-y`/`--yes`/`$CI`/non-TTY. `instances.js` is in `package.json` `files` (imported by the launcher at runtime).
- **Editor edit-flush (no-run persistence).** `Cell.svelte` autosaves source on a 500ms debounce; it also `flushEdit()`s immediately on editor **blur** and window **`pagehide`**, and teardown flushes rather than cancelling - so a cell edit survives closing Cellar even inside the sub-debounce window, without a run. `LiveNotebook.editCell(id, source, {keepalive})` sets `keepalive:true` **only** from the unload flush (Cell passes `{keepalive:true}` from `pagehide`); normal page-alive autosaves stay plain `fetch` because the browser caps a keepalive body at ~64KB - routing every autosave through keepalive silently drops edits to cells >64KB (the regression to avoid). PATCH is `.catch`'d so a rejected unload flush never surfaces as an unhandled rejection.
- **Kernel run queue (`src/lib/server/run-queue.js`).** Cellar runs ONE kernel per notebook, so the queue is **sharded per notebook** (`Map<nbPath, {active, pending}>`): one cell of a notebook runs at a time, while DIFFERENT notebooks execute in parallel against isolated namespaces - a run queued in notebook B waits only behind notebook B's own cells, never behind notebook A. A run requested while that notebook's kernel is busy is **queued FIFO**, never dropped (the old behavior: the UI early-returned and MCP raced into `execute()`). The queue lives on the **server** because the kernels do - an agent's MCP run must interleave with a user's UI run on the same notebook in submission order, and no per-tab flag can see both. Entries are keyed by `(absolute notebook path, cellId)` - **cell ids repeat across documents**, so the notebook half is load-bearing. It is a *slot* primitive: it decides who runs next, never how. Both run entry points keep owning their own execution + streaming and just take a ticket first (`enqueueRun` → `await ticket.wait()` → `finally ticket.done()`); `ticket.done()` fires only **after** `run:end` is persisted + published, so the wire order stays `run:end` → next `run:start`. **Dedupe:** a cell already running or already queued is not enqueued twice; the second submission instead **refreshes the pending entry's source** (`ticket.source()` is read at dequeue), which is what makes dropping it safe - a queued cell runs what you last submitted. **Cancellation:** `clearRunQueue(nb)` drops every *pending* run **of that notebook** (another notebook's queue is untouched) and is called from `restartKernel`/`interruptKernel`/`rebindKernel`/jupyter's `autorestarting` (in `kernel.js`, **before** the restart is issued) - queued work was submitted against a namespace that is about to vanish, and running it against a fresh kernel would execute cell N+1 without cell N's definitions. The *active* run is not touched: the kernel op ends it, and its owner then releases into an empty queue. `deleteCell` calls `cancelRun` so a removed cell can't dequeue later; the `/run` route's stream `cancel()` drops its own pending entry (identity-checked via `ticket.cancel()`, **never by key**, so a duplicate request's stream closing cannot cancel the real pending run). A cancelled entry rejects with `RunCancelled`; `enqueueRun` pre-attaches a no-op `.catch` so an owner that already returned never surfaces an unhandled rejection. **Interrupt-clears-the-queue (the non-obvious client half).** `interruptKernel` calling `clearRunQueue` is necessary but NOT sufficient: with a large queue (Run All) the interrupt HTTP request could not even REACH the server. Each queued/running `/run` holds its NDJSON response open, so N of them saturate the browser's ~6-connection HTTP/1.1 pool, and the interrupt `fetch` gets no connection until a run finishes on its OWN - by which point the queue has already drained (the running cell ran to completion, `sleeper_ran` set, every marker executed). Fix is client-side and MUST stay paired with the server clear: `LiveNotebook` tracks each run's `AbortController` in `runControllers`, and the shell's `interruptKernel(path)` calls `notebookApis.get(path)?.cancelQueuedRuns()` FIRST (synchronous, no network) - it aborts every run fetch **except `runningId`'s**, which frees the held connections (so the interrupt request goes through) AND cancels the queued/browser-held runs at once (an aborted stream → the `/run` route's `cancel()` → `ticket.cancel()`; a never-sent one is dropped before the kernel). The running cell's fetch is kept so `kernel.interrupt()`'s KeyboardInterrupt still renders. An `AbortError` in `runCell`'s catch is an intentional cancel (no error output). Bumping `interruptGeneration` also stops an in-flight sequential bulk-run loop (`runCodeIds` for **runAll**/runAbove/below/stale) from advancing to the next cell after its current run is aborted - those hold only one connection at a time, so the connection-pool half doesn't bite them, but the loop-keeps-going half does. **Run all is now this same sequential loop** (`cellar-runall-kernel-wedge-z3`; see the staleness entry), so the connection-pool saturation the client-half fix guards against no longer arises from Run all - it can still arise from a user mashing Run on many cells at once, which is why `cancelQueuedRuns` stays. Covered by `tests/e2e/interrupt-clears-queue.spec.ts` (+ the server seam in `tests/unit/kernel-manager.test.ts`).
- **Queue state is a broadcast snapshot, not a delta.** Every queue change (and every run start, including the uncontended one) publishes `queue:changed` via `events.js`'s **`publishGlobal`** - it carries `{running:[…], queue:[{nb,cellId,actor,position}]}` for EVERY notebook at once (one `running` entry per busy kernel - notebooks run in parallel, so there can be several - and every notebook's pending runs, each tagged with its `nb`, which the client filters to its own), has **no `nb` and no `seq`** (it is a full snapshot, so a missed one self-heals), and is dispatched in `LiveNotebook` *before* the per-notebook `nb`/`seq` filter and *without* `originId` echo-suppression - a queue is shared state, not one tab's action. The SSE route seeds one on connect and `events-client.js` replays the latest to a late subscriber, so a tab connecting (or a notebook mounting) mid-run sees both the queue **and** the running cell; the snapshot's `running` is why `LiveNotebook` reconciles `runningId` from it (a tab that missed `run:start` would otherwise render cells "queued · 1" behind a cell it shows as idle). Reconciling `runningId` here also lets a tab that connects mid-run and is VIEWING that notebook follow the running cell - the intended behavior (it is looking at the notebook that runs); a background tab stays put via the follow effect's `active` guard. The `/run` NDJSON gains `run:start` (the initiating tab drops its own SSE echo, so this is how it learns its run actually *started* rather than merely being accepted), plus terminal `run:duplicate` / `run:cancelled` frames that close the stream without ever touching the cell's outputs. `runCell`'s `finally` clears `runningId` only when it truly `started`, so a refused duplicate can't erase a live indicator. **`runBusy` in `+page.svelte` is no longer a gate** - it is an in-flight *counter* (`runsInFlight`) feeding the kernel badge; the `busy` prop is gone from `LiveNotebook`.
- **Queued-cell affordance.** `Cell.svelte` `queuedPosition` (null | 1-based position **within this notebook's own queue**) → a static `bg-warning/50` left bar + `border-warning/30` + an amber clock and `queued · N` label, the deliberately quieter sibling of the running affordance (`bg-warning` pulsing bar + spinner), which outranks it (`isQueued = queuedPosition != null && !running`). Label text is `base-content`, **not** `warning/70`: a dimmed warning washes out to near-illegible on a light theme. Positions are per notebook - a cell only ever waits behind its OWN notebook's cells, so `queued · 3` means three of this notebook's runs are ahead of it.
- **MCP run tools + the queue.** `run_cell` **waits** its turn and then returns the real outputs annotated `queued:true` / `queue_position` / `waited_ms` - it does *not* return early as "queued". Waiting is what keeps `run_cells`/`run_all`/`run_range` meaning "these ran, here is what they printed"; they drive `runCell` sequentially and depend on its outputs. The one early return is the **dedupe** case: `{status:'queued', queue_position}` if that cell is already pending, `{status:'running'}` if it is executing right now - so an agent is told it was accepted and never that a run finished when it did not. A dropped pending run returns `{status:'cancelled', reason}` and `runCells` **stops** there (the rest would run against a namespace their predecessors never populated). `runCell` pins `nb = nbArg ?? getActiveNotebookPath()` at call time and threads it through `getCell`/`setOutputs`, since the UI may focus another notebook while the run waits. New read tool **`run_queue`** (a per-notebook map, so an agent sees each notebook's OWN queue); `INSTRUCTIONS` gained a "runs serialize only within a notebook" clause (don't interrupt/restart just because a run is slow - that drops YOUR notebook's queued runs too). **Long-run keepalive (the silent-hang fix).** Because a run tool `await`s the whole run and holds the HTTP response open, a cell longer than the MCP CLIENT's per-tool-call timeout (Claude Code default ~60s) made the client give up and DISCARD the eventual result - the agent hung and never got the output even though the cell finished server-side. Fix lives entirely in `mcp/server.ts` `withProgress(extra, fn)`: it wraps every run tool handler (`run_cell`/`add_and_run`/`run_cells`/`run_all`/`run_stale`/`run_range`) and, while `fn` is pending, emits an MCP **progress notification** every `PROGRESS_INTERVAL_MS` (15s) against the request's `_meta.progressToken` via `extra.sendNotification` - which resets the client's timeout (when it sets `resetTimeoutOnProgress`, as Claude Code does) so the FINAL result is delivered when the run completes. It's a **wall-clock heartbeat, NOT output-driven** (the canonical `time.sleep(90)` emits nothing until it ends); a client that sent no progressToken gets a no-op passthrough (never push unsolicited notifications); send errors are swallowed and the timer is `unref`'d. The tool contract is unchanged (still returns the full outputs at the end). The `httpServer.requestTimeout = 0` / `timeout = 0` in `startMcpServer` is belt-and-suspenders (Node's 300s `requestTimeout` measures request RECEIPT, not the long response, so it never actually cut a run). The stdio↔HTTP bridge relays notifications verbatim and imposes no per-request timeout. **Residual cap:** if a client sets a hard `maxTotalTimeout`, no amount of progress overrides it - that's a client config choice, not something Cellar can fix. Covered by `tests/unit/mcp-run-progress.test.ts` (fake-clock heartbeat + result delivery).
- **Databricks (`src/lib/server/databricks.js` + `src/lib/Databricks.svelte` + `src/routes/api/databricks/*`).** Native connect: pick profile → pick cluster → `spark` + `w` bound in the kernel. **Auth is the SDK's profile auth and nothing else** - `WorkspaceClient(profile=…)` / `DatabricksSession.builder.profile(…)` read `~/.databrickscfg` themselves. Never shell out to the `databricks` CLI, and **never** glob for the VS Code extension's bundled binary (the boilerplate this replaced did; it is the thing we deliberately do not do). **`resolveAuth` (`databricks.ts`) trusts that SDK profile auth: a *named* profile - with OR without a `token` - resolves to `{mode:'profile', profile, host}` and is handed to the SDK by NAME (`Config(profile=…)`), which authenticates it however the profile says (a PAT, or a `databricks-cli`/keyring/cached-OAuth credential - e.g. the captain's no-PAT `DEFAULT`, whose `auth_type=databricks-cli` auths silently, no browser).** Do NOT re-collapse a no-token profile into Cellar's own browser OAuth or discard its name (the `cellar-dbx-auth-profile-drop` bug: a no-PAT SDK-authable profile was misclassified `mode:'oauth'`, its name dropped, and gated behind a spurious `oauth_login_required`). Cellar's own external-browser OAuth (`{mode:'oauth', host}`, gated by `assertSignedIn`+`signedInHosts`) is reserved for a **bare typed host with no profile**; MOST profiles are never pre-gated - a genuine need for a fresh interactive login surfaces reactively (the SDK error → `classify()` → `oauth_login_required`), which the `login` subprocess then satisfies. **The ONE gated profile shape** (so a listing/connect can never itself pop the SDK browser): a no-token `auth_type=external-browser` profile - `resolveAuth` flags it `needsSignIn:true` and `assertSignedIn` holds it behind sign-in exactly like a bare host (tracked in the `signedInProfiles` sibling of `signedInHosts`), still authenticating by NAME (`Config(profile=…)`), just after the deliberate `login`. The gate keys on `external-browser` SPECIFICALLY - never on a merely missing `auth_type` (the captain's `DEFAULT` has no `auth_type` line and the SDK infers `databricks-cli`, so gating on "no auth_type" would re-break it). Same split in the UI (`Databricks.svelte`): a databricks-cli/PAT profile lists immediately, a bare host OR a no-token external-browser profile (`profileNeedsSignIn`) signs in first; `oauthRequired` (set only from a real `oauth_login_required`) also drives the sign-in button. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-auth-resolve.test.ts`. **An EXPIRED named profile is its own case, and must never be routed to that sign-in button (task `cellar-databricks-profile-reauth-r2`).** A `databricks-cli` (or otherwise CLI-managed) profile keeps its credential in the **databricks CLI's own store**, not in the SDK's python-local OAuth cache Cellar's browser sign-in mints into - so when its refresh token dies, "Sign in with Databricks" writes a token that profile never reads and the user is stuck forever. That is exactly what happened to the captain's `DEFAULT`: the SDK raised `ValueError: default auth: databricks-cli: cannot get access token: … the refresh token is invalid. To reauthenticate, run: $ databricks auth login --profile DEFAULT`, whose `discovery_url=…/oauth-authorization-server` matched the PROBE `classify()`'s `'oauth' in low` rule → `oauth_login_required` → the dead-end button. The fix is a distinct code **`profile_reauth_required`** (401) decided in **Node**, not python: `classify()` sees only the message, while **`reclassifyReauth(auth, result)` (`databricks.ts`)** also has the resolved `Auth`, i.e. WHICH profile was asked for - which is what lets the real name ride out on `DatabricksError.profile` so the sidebar prints a command that is never hardcoded. Applied at exactly two seams, both of which produce this failure: inside **`probe()`** (every listing/login/cluster op) and in **`connect()`** right after `CONNECT_CODE` (which hardcodes `auth_failed` for the Config/WorkspaceClient step). **The RECONNECT ladder must preserve that verdict, and preserving it needs its own mechanism - `ConnState.reconnectError`.** The likeliest way a user meets an expired profile is a session that was already WORKING, so what they touch is the sidebar Reconnect button / `databricks_reconnect`, not a fresh connect - and both rungs that rebuild a session are driven fire-and-forget by callers that must never throw, so each swallows its error (rung 2 `autoReconnect` → `false`, rung 3 `reconnectAfterKernelRestart` → a string `reason`). That flattening dropped `code`/`profile` and `reconnectSession` raised the generic `reconnect_failed` ("reconnect from the Databricks sidebar section") - the exact dead-end button this feature exists to stop offering, with `throwReconnectFailure`'s `clusterState` probe unable to rescue it since it runs on the SAME expired auth and can only answer `null`. So the classified `DatabricksError` is recorded at **`reconnectTo`** - the ONE seam both rungs already funnel through, hence no second reconnect path - and `throwReconnectFailure` re-throws it **before** the cluster probe when its code is `PROFILE_REAUTH_CODE`. `reconnectSession` clears the field before walking the ladder, so a recorded verdict can only ever belong to THAT attempt. **The AUTO-HEAL status surfaces read that same field, through the one accessor `pendingReauth(s)`** - because the explicit Reconnect button is not where most users meet this: the panel polls (`getStatus`→`liveConnection`) and the agent asks (`databricks_status`→`agentStatus`), and both run the self-heal. The state that heal leaves behind is the load-bearing detail: a failed heal runs through `connect()`, whose failure path NULLS `s.connection` (without an `s.lost`), so the panel drops to the **picker card** - the one offering "Sign in with Databricks", the exact dead end - and `agentStatus` falls to its not-connected branch. So the verdict rides EVERY not-live shape (`liveConnection` attaches `reauth`, rendered by the `sessionReauthBox` snippet on the expired/lost/picker cards; `agentStatus` returns `reauthFields()` = `reauth_required`/`profile`/`reauth_command` + a note that tells the agent to relay the command and NOT to point at the sidebar), not just the brief `expired` window. Two fences keep it honest, and both are mutation-tested: it needs a still-bound notebook (`reconnectTarget`), and any successful `connect()` RETIRES it (`s.reconnectError = null` beside `s.lost = null`) - a live session is proof the credential works, so a later loss can never be blamed on a sign-in the user already fixed. One expired profile fails EVERY operation that touches it at once (the session heal, the cluster listing behind the picker, an explicit Reconnect), so the box would stack two or three identical copies on one card; the de-dupe lives in **`errorBox` itself** (`duplicatesSessionReauth`, matching on code AND the same named profile - a non-reauth failure and a *different* profile both still render, since a hidden real error is worse than a repeated one), never at the call sites, and `sessionReauthBox` renders through **`errorBody`** directly because it is the copy that survives and must not suppress itself. The renderer **fails closed**: `reauthProfile` no longer falls back to the picker's selection (a different profile than the dead session's, or `''` while connected), so with no name on the error the box explains without naming one and shows NO command row - `databricks auth login --profile ` with an empty tail is worse than no command, since the copy button hands it over verbatim. **Scoped deliberately, and this is the no-regression rule:** only `mode:'profile'`, and never a `needsSignIn` profile - a bare typed **host** and a no-token `auth_type=external-browser` profile are precisely the two shapes whose token Cellar's OWN sign-in re-mints (`hasCellarCachedOAuth`), so for them `oauth_login_required` stays correct; redirecting those to the CLI would be the same dead end with the sign flipped. The pure rule + the copy live in the browser-safe **`src/lib/databricksReauth.ts`** (shared by server and sidebar so they cannot drift): `isProfileReauthError` matches a dead refresh token (`refresh token is invalid|expired|not set`, `invalid_refresh_token`) OR the databricks-cli strategy failing *together with* the CLI's own `databricks auth login` remedy sentence - each signal alone is too loose - and `reauthMessage()` puts Cellar's actionable head in front of the SDK's own text (so logs and MCP results are actionable too) with `reauthDetail()` splitting it back off, so the sidebar's detail row never repeats the remedy the box above already spells out. **Cellar deliberately does NOT run `databricks auth login` for the user** (and there is no MCP tool for it): it is interactive, opens a browser, can prompt for a workspace choice on a TTY Cellar does not own, and with `auth_storage = secure` writes through the OS Keychain - a hung subprocess and a half-written credential are the failure modes, so showing the exact copyable command is the reliable remedy. The sidebar renders it as a `reauthBox` snippet inside `errorBox` (`Databricks.svelte`), with the command built from the shared `REAUTH_COMMAND_HEAD`/`REAUTH_PROFILE_FLAG` constants and the flag held in a `whitespace-nowrap` span - in the ~200px box the command MUST wrap, and a browser breaks after a hyphen, which split `--profile` into `-` / `-profile`. Every Databricks route now returns its failure through the one shared **`src/routes/api/databricks/error-response.js`** (`databricksErrorResponse`) so the new `profile` field cannot be dropped by one of the nine catch blocks. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-profile-reauth.test.ts` (the REAL SDK error text committed verbatim as a fixture; both seams; the two regressions) + `tests/e2e/databricks-profile-reauth.spec.ts` (mocked routes). **The split is the design:** *listing* (profiles/clusters/catalogs/schemas/tables) runs **server-side** in a short-lived subprocess of the project venv's python (`projectPython()` = `CELLAR_PROJECT_VENV`, the same interpreter the kernel uses - so "can the kernel import the SDK" and "can our listing import the SDK" can never disagree); *the session* runs **in the kernel**, because `spark` must live where the user's cells can reach it. One python script (`PROBE`) serves every listing op, driven by a JSON command in `argv[1]`; it **always prints exactly one `__CELLAR_DBX__`-prefixed JSON line and never raises**, so a missing package / bad profile / unreachable workspace arrives as `{ok:false, code, message}` rather than a traceback we would have to guess at. `code` is the contract: the sidebar's `REMEDY` map turns it into actionable copy, and `statusFor()` into an HTTP status. Embedded as a JS string (not a sibling `.py`) because `$lib/server` is bundled into `build/` - the same reason `inspect.js` embeds its probe. Both halves scrub `DATABRICKS_*`/`SPARK_CONNECT_*` from the environment, **keeping `DATABRICKS_CONFIG_FILE`** (the one deliberate divergence from the boilerplate: it is what says where the config we just parsed lives). Profiles are parsed **in Node** so the picker renders before the SDK is installed; a section without a `host` is not a profile (the CLI writes `[__settings__]` into that same file). **Connection state is epoch-scoped**: `connect()` stamps the kernel-session epoch from `execute()`'s `kernel` event, and `connectionStatus()` reconciles against `currentSessionId()` on every read - the exact `lastRun` doctrine, for the exact reason (a restart destroys `spark`; reporting "connected" from a dead namespace hands the user a `NameError`). **The epoch cannot see a session that died WITHOUT a restart** (idle-timeout / cluster-GC expiry, or a locally CLOSED Spark Connect client), so a second, real-liveness layer sits on top: `assessLiveness()` (the ONE decision, shared by `agentStatus` heal:true / `getStatus`→`liveConnection` heal:false → live/reconnected/expired/unverified), backed by the cached `PING_CODE` probe and `autoReconnect` (rung 2, reuses `connect()` via `reconnectTo` - never add a second reconnect path). **The load-bearing signal is `spark._client.is_closed`, checked in `PING_CODE` BEFORE `SELECT 1`:** a closed client is a definitive, synchronous, no-round-trip dead-session fact, and when set the `SELECT 1` only raises `[NO_ACTIVE_SESSION]` (which the message-matching set + `isSessionClosed()` now include). The prior bug reported `connected:true, liveness_unverified:true` over a closed client because the probe swallowed `NO_ACTIVE_SESSION` as a transient error and never confirmed expiry - so `is_closed`/NO_ACTIVE_SESSION MUST classify as `expired` (→ heal), never `unverified`. `w`-only ops are unaffected (only the Spark session is probed/healed). The UI (`getStatus`) fires the heal fire-and-forget so a poll never blocks minutes on a cold-cluster reconnect. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-liveness.test.ts` (closed-client + NO_ACTIVE_SESSION cases). Connect/disconnect/preview run through `execute(..., {internal:true})`, so they bypass the run QUEUE (like `inspect.js`'s probes) and never inflate `execs_this_session`; the per-kernel exec lock (`execChain`, see kernel.ts) still serializes them behind a running cell - two `requestExecute` on one kernel must never overlap (that collision wedges `future.done`; the `cellar-runall-kernel-wedge-z3` root cause). A connect can legitimately take minutes (Databricks Connect starts a cold cluster), which is why the route just waits. `databricks-sdk`/`databricks-connect` install into the **project** venv via `venv.js`'s `installPackages` (the one place Cellar adds anything to a project venv). **`databricks-connect` MUST be ≤ the cluster's DBR** (a newer client hard-fails the session: "Unsupported combination of Databricks Runtime & Databricks Connect versions"), so it is pinned to the cluster's DBR **major.minor** line (`databricks-connect==17.3.*`, newest patch of that minor). `installDeps({version})` does the pin; `connect()` enforces it in **two layers** (pure decisions in `dbrVersion.ts`, unit-tested with no cluster). **Part A (prevention):** before building the session, `connect()` resolves the cluster's `spark_version` via the `PROBE` `cluster` op (`w.clusters.get(id).spark_version`, authoritative, not trusted from the picker), `dbrMajorMinor`→line, and `ensurePinnedConnect` reinstalls a matching client only when the installed one is provably NEWER than the DBR (numeric major.minor compare — the actual hard-fail condition; a too-old-or-equal client connects fine and an unparseable version is left untouched, so neither reinstalls nor restarts) — restarting the kernel **only if `databricks.connect` was already imported** (a fresh first-connect import picks up the new client with no restart, so the user's namespace survives). **Part B (safety net):** if a mismatch still surfaces (unresolvable/serverless DBR), `parseVersionMismatch` reads the runtime off the SDK error itself, re-pins from it and retries once (loop-safe: `ensurePinnedConnect` returns false unless it actually reinstalled); a persistent mismatch throws a dedicated **`version_mismatch`** code with an actionable, user-facing message (`versionMismatchMessage`, names both versions + the exact `==X.Y.*` pin) that the sidebar `REMEDY` map surfaces. **Serverless is not pinned** (no classic DBR; `dbrMajorMinor`→null skips the pin) and is not regressed. Covered by `tests/unit/dbr-version.test.ts` (pure) + `tests/unit/databricks-version-pin.test.ts` (the real `connect()` turning the raw error into `version_mismatch`). **Agent side:** `kernel_state` + `get_notebook_map` carry a `databricks` block, `INSTRUCTIONS` clause 9 tells the agent to use `spark`/`w` instead of writing boilerplate, and the read tools (`databricks_status`, `databricks_list_catalogs/_schemas/_tables`, `databricks_preview_table`, `databricks_list_clusters`) reuse the **same** server-side plumbing as the UI tree (`forAgent`/`listClusters`), so the two views cannot disagree. **Agent-driven reconnect/connect (task `cellar-mcp-dbx-cluster-connect-n6`):** the agent MAY restore or connect a session but never chooses compute lifecycle or drives the OAuth browser. Two write tools, both **thin orchestration over the ONE existing recovery/connect machinery - NEVER a second reconnect path** (reviewers must reject one): (1) **`databricks_reconnect`** → `reconnectSession(nb)` (`databricks.ts`) walks the ladder cheapest-first - rung 1 `refreshKernelConnection` (kernel socket, namespace-preserving), rung 2 `autoReconnect` via `agentStatus` (server-side expiry), rung 3 `reconnectAfterKernelRestart` (kernel restart) - all funnelling through `connect()`; restores the cluster the USER already chose (the stored `reconnectTarget`), never a new one, and returns `connectedPayload` + honest **`kernel_restarted`/`namespace_cleared`** flags (a version re-pin restart clears the namespace - detected by an epoch delta across the op, the same `NameError`-trap doctrine as `lastRun`); `not_connected` when there is no target. (2) **`databricks_connect`** → `connectCluster(...)` connects a CHOSEN cluster (D1) by reusing `connect()` wholesale, gated: `assertSignedIn` refuses an un-signed-in OAuth host with `oauth_login_required` (browser sign-in stays human), and a down cluster (state from the extended `cluster` PROBE op) is refused `cluster_terminated` because **compute lifecycle is human-only (D2)** - the agent may LIST/report clusters but not start/stop/restart them; same side-effect flags. Both are wrapped in `databricksTool` + `withProgress` (a re-pin reinstall can take minutes). New `DatabricksError` codes: `cluster_terminated`, `reconnect_failed` (both 409 in `statusFor`). Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-agent-reconnect.test.ts` (each rung branch + the connect auth gate). The UI's table preview appends+runs a real cell (`spark.read.table(...).limit(N).toPandas()`) because there the cell *is* the deliverable; the agent's `preview_table` reads through `spark` and touches nothing, because an agent peeking at a table must not append to the human's document. `LiveNotebook`'s `onRegisterApi` (`insertAndRunCode`) is how the sidebar reaches the active notebook - same registry shape as `onRegisterFolds`; it deliberately does **not** move `activeId` (focus must follow selection, and the user's focus is in the sidebar). **User-facing Reconnect (task `cellar-databricks-sidebar-redesign-d5`):** the bound-but-not-live states (`expired`/`lost`) in `Databricks.svelte` show a **Reconnect** button → `POST /api/databricks/reconnect` → `reconnectSession(nb)` - the SAME ladder the agent's `databricks_reconnect` and the auto-reconnect self-heal walk, so it is **NOT a second reconnect path** (reviewers must reject one). The section follows the shared sidebar-section pattern: its header carries only the DATABRICKS label + a `refreshBtn`, wired via `Databricks.svelte`'s `export function refresh()` and a `bind:this` handle in `Sidebar.svelte` (the same `bind:this` seam Environment/Checkpoints use). There is deliberately **no** header status pill in any state (expanded or folded) - the connection status lives in the panel's connected view, not the header (the earlier redesign added an `onStateChange`-fed header badge; it was removed in full, plumbing included, by `cellar-databricks-remove-pill-dp3`). The connected view is a compact dot+name+meta line (not the old tinted `<dl>` card) and the cluster picker uses the borderless kernel-row idiom (leading state dot + trailing state text); the runtime toggle stays `toggle-success` green (a deliberate Databricks cue, unlike Files' `toggle-primary`). **Redesign v2 (task `cellar-databricks-redesign-v2-k4`) - the section is now a set of clearly separated bordered cards** (`rounded-lg border border-base-300 bg-base-100 p-2.5`, the same card idiom as the "Packages missing"/expired boxes, each with a small uppercase `CLUSTER`/`UPLOAD`/`RUNTIME` label - **the UPLOAD one was later split out of the Cluster card** (`uploadCard`, `data-testid=databricks-upload-card`, rendered between the two, still only while connected): it acts on workspace FILES and never on compute, and it carries two fields, their token dropdowns, a live preview and a two-step replace confirm, which nested under Switch/Disconnect read as more connection controls. Switch/Disconnect/Log out STAY in the Cluster card - those really are connection controls): a **Cluster card** (the connection identity + Switch/Disconnect, OR the connect-form picker when disconnected, OR a warning-tinted degraded card + Reconnect + pick-another when expired/lost) and a **separate Runtime card** (the `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` toggle + version + a live `● active`/`off` status), with the Unity Catalog `data` browser as a subordinate labeled region below (not a card). The connect/toggle in-progress state is guarded (`busy==='connect'` connecting card; `runtimeApplying` keeps the connected view mounted) and `settleConnection()` polls until the post-restart reconnect re-establishes the session, so the panel NEVER flashes the "session lost" card during a connect or a toggle restart - do not remove that guard/poll. **A toggle-initiated kernel restart holds "connecting" (never "lost") until the reconnect completes, and this rests on two coupled facts (task `cellar-connect-restart-connecting-state-c2`; it covered the connect-initiated restart until `cellar-databricks-runtime-default-d2` moved the restart onto the toggle) - keep BOTH:** (1) **`settleConnection` decides on its OWN fetched body, NEVER the shared `status`** - `loadStatus()` returns the body it fetched even when the `statusSeq` guard means it does not APPLY it (superseded), and `settleConnection` checks that returned body's `connection.connected`. The bug it fixes: the `$effect` that reloads on a `kernelSessionId` change fires a CONCURRENT `loadStatus` during the restart; with a live kernel that bumps `statusSeq` so a settle poll discarded its own fresh "lost" read and re-checked the shared `status`, which still held the pre-restart "connected" - so `settleConnection` released the `busy`/`restarting` gate early and the concurrent load then applied "lost", which STUCK (no further poll corrected it) - a successful connect shown as a "lost" card with a Reconnect button. Reproduces ONLY with a started kernel (a never-started kernel makes the restart a no-op), which is why `tests/e2e/databricks-connect-no-lost-flash.spec.ts` runs a cell first - and why its connect and toggle halves are ONE test: they need the same live kernel and the same fresh notebook, and the file shares one workspace. (2) **The explicit `restarting` flag** is the "an expected restart is in flight" signal: set in `applyRuntime` right before `onRestartKernel`, cleared on settle (connected) OR on the reconnect timing out, and definitively in each caller's `finally` (so an `applyRuntime` throw can't strand it). The connecting-card branch is `busy==='connect' || (expectedRestart && !runtimeApplying)` (`expectedRestart` folds in the server's own grace flag - see the next entry), so while an expected restart is in flight the lost/expired branches are unreachable - an expected restart can never be mistaken for a genuine loss, while a real loss (kernel restarted from elsewhere, idle timeout, closed client: `restarting` false) still renders the lost/expired card + Reconnect. Reusable `{#snippet}`s: `picker` (auth source + cluster list + the connect-consequence note), `runtimeCard`, `dataBrowser`. **A kernel restart never flashes the "lost" card, and what holds it back is the POSITIVE in-flight signal, NOT a wall-clock window (`restartingAfterKernelRestart` in `databricks.ts`).** The panel's own `restarting` flag only covers a restart the PANEL issued; a restart from anywhere else (the Kernels sidebar, `%restart_python`, an autorestart) went straight to the warning-toned lost card before `reconnectAfterKernelRestart` rebuilt the session. So `connectionStatus()`'s epoch-reconciliation - the one place a restart drives this transition, and it fires exactly once per restart - stamps `ConnState.restartedAt`, but ONLY when a reconnect is really coming (a kept `reconnectTarget` AND a kernel that still exists; a torn-down kernel is a real loss with nothing to wait for). `liveConnection` then returns `restarting:true` instead of a bare lost shape for as long as an attempt is genuinely running (`restartAttemptInFlight` = `s.inFlight || s.reconnecting`), and `Databricks.svelte` folds it into `expectedRestart` (`restarting || connection.restarting`) so the connecting card renders. **`RESTART_LOST_GRACE_MS` (1s) survives only as the DISPATCH FLOOR** - the reconnect is fired detached, so a status poll can observe the epoch change before `connect()` sets `inFlight`; it is emphatically not the mechanism, because a real rebuild runs the cluster DBR probe subprocess + `checkInstall` + a whole Spark Connect session build (seconds), so a fixed 1s window merely moved the flash later and added a Reconnecting → lost → connected flip. Do not re-collapse this back into a timer. **It holds the transient window, never a known outcome**: a successful `connect()` clears the stamp, a reconnect that GAVE UP clears it, a torn-down kernel clears it, and `disconnect()` clears it UP FRONT (it also sets `inFlight`, which would otherwise read as "a reconnect is running" for the length of a teardown). It is UI-only - `agentStatus` keeps reporting the plain truth. **The stamp is RETIRED the moment its outcome is known, lapsing included** (`restartingAfterKernelRestart` nulls `restartedAt` when the floor runs out with nothing in flight): a stamp kept past the fall-through to the lost card is a latch, and any later unrelated attempt on that notebook (an expiry self-heal setting `reconnecting`, a connect that then fails) would re-satisfy the in-flight branch and claim a kernel restart that never happened - the "a non-restart loss never claims to be restarting" invariant with the sign flipped. **The panel POLLS while the flag is set** (a `$effect` timeout in `Databricks.svelte`, 1200ms backing off to 5s): it has no periodic poll, and a reconnect that FAILS publishes no `databricks:changed` - so without that re-read it would sit on "Reconnecting…" forever instead of falling through to the honest lost card. **The re-check is SELF-DRIVING, and that is the load-bearing half**: a FAILED read leaves `status` untouched (`loadStatus`'s catch only writes `statusError`), so an effect re-armed solely by a status CHANGE broke its own chain on the first flaky GET and stuck on the spinner - the exact state it exists to prevent. Each tick therefore bumps a `restartRecheckTick` `$state` AFTER its read settles (so it re-arms whatever the read did, and never overlaps two reads); it converges by construction, since the first read that is not `restarting` - or a notebook switch, or unmount - arms nothing further. **The COST of that poll is bounded at the server seam, not by slowing the client down**: the window is unbounded (a cold-cluster reconnect is minutes), and `getStatus` used to spawn TWO subprocesses per read (`uv --version`, and a project-venv python that imports databricks-sdk) on the shared Node process, able to overlap the interval. `workspaceProbes()` memoizes that pair behind `WORKSPACE_PROBE_TTL_MS` (5s) with a single-flight guard, keyed on the interpreter path it measured (so a venv rebind re-measures for free); `installDeps` calls `invalidateWorkspaceProbes()` so the sidebar never serves a pre-install "packages missing" - and that invalidation bumps a **generation counter**, not just the cache, because a probe already IN FLIGHT measured the pre-install world: without it a later caller was handed that stale in-flight promise AND it wrote its result back over the fresh one when it settled, serving for a whole TTL the exact answer the invalidation exists to retire. The generation is captured alongside the in-flight entry, and a moved generation disqualifies BOTH the reuse and the cache write. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-restart-grace.test.ts` (a multi-second in-flight rebuild is never lost; the floor lapsing with NOTHING in flight IS; a long attempt that fails stops claiming to restart at once; a lapsed stamp is never revived by a later unrelated attempt) + `tests/unit/databricks-status-probes.test.ts` (one measurement per burst, no overlap, TTL + invalidation) + `tests/e2e/databricks-restart-recheck.spec.ts` (a failed read never strands the spinner; a settled panel stops polling) + `tests/e2e/databricks-connect-no-lost-flash.spec.ts`. **Connect-time behavior (task `cellar-databricks-runtime-default-d2`, a deliberate REVERSAL of the earlier connect-auto-enables design): connecting a cluster (and switching clusters) leaves the Databricks runtime OFF and does NOT restart the kernel.** A connect binds `spark`/`w` in the LIVE kernel and stops there, so the user's namespace survives it; advertising a runtime changes what EVERY library believes about its environment (mlflow's `is_in_databricks_runtime()` reads the same env var) and costs a namespace, so it is an explicit opt-in. **The Runtime toggle is the one opt-in and the ONLY thing that restarts the kernel for this setting** - `applyRuntime(on)` persists the pref server-side race-free (`$lib/uiState`'s `setUiNow`, an awaited PUT so `initKernel` re-reads the true value) then calls `onRestartKernel` (the existing per-notebook restart path, which re-injects/omits the env AND rebuilds `spark`/`w` via `reconnectAfterKernelRestart` - NOT a new restart); `connect()` no longer calls it. The default flipped with it: `databricksRuntimeEnabled` is now `storeValue === true` (**only an explicit stored true**, so a connect - which stores nothing - can never enable it, and stray junk in the store cannot either), and `Databricks.svelte` mirrors that `=== true` when seeding `runtimeOn`; the `bound` connection scope in `shouldInjectDatabricksRuntime` is KEPT on top, so a stored ON never spoofs the runtime for a notebook that is no longer connected. The picker's connect-consequence note says what connect really does now (binds `spark`/`w`, variables kept - it must NOT promise a restart) **with the variables-kept claim QUALIFIED, not absolute**: `ensurePinnedConnect` still restarts the kernel when databricks-connect has to be re-pinned to the cluster's DBR and `databricks.connect` was already imported (the common shape being a Switch to an older-DBR cluster, shown with this very note), which is the same side effect the agent tool reports as `kernel_restarted`/`namespace_cleared` - so the note names that exception rather than promising something connect can break. The Runtime card keeps its "Toggling restarts the kernel - variables are cleared" copy. **The Runtime card's state pill is LIVE-DERIVED, never the stored preference** - with the connect-time restart gone the two can honestly diverge (a stored `true` over a kernel that started while the notebook was still unbound, so the `bound` scope gate skipped the injection, or one carried over from the old build), and a pill reading "active" over a kernel whose `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` is unset is exactly the assert-more-than-was-verified defect this codebase keeps retiring. So `initKernel` - the one place that decides the injection - RECORDS what it injected on the session (`NotebookKernel.databricksRuntime`, reset by `beginSession` since the env dies with the namespace), `kernel.ts` exports `liveDatabricksRuntime(nb)`, and `getStatus` carries it as `runtime: {kernelStarted, liveVersion, envForced, versionEnvForced}` alongside `connection`. The TOGGLE keeps showing the preference (that is what the user sets); the pill and hint report reality - `active` only when the live session really carries it, else `pending` (naming the divergence: restart the kernel to apply, or it applies when the kernel starts) - and nothing forces an extra restart to reconcile them. **The `pending`-over-a-RUNNING-kernel state - the one whose copy says "restart the kernel to apply" - carries an "Apply now" button (`data-testid=databricks-runtime-apply`) and NO other state may**: `active`/`off`/`applying` have nothing pending, so a restart button there would offer a namespace wipe nobody asked for, and `pending` with no kernel yet needs none (the next start injects it). It is a third caller of **`applyRuntime`** - never a second restart path - writing back the SAME stored value it read, so the preference is untouched and the restart, the suppressed lost-flash and the `settleConnection` handling are byte-identical to the toggle's; it exists because otherwise the only route was the toggle's off-then-on DOUBLE restart, which is precisely what the deliberate no-migration stance (a carried-over stored `true`) leaves users facing on their first connect. **It may only be offered where a restart would GENUINELY apply the runtime, because every click costs the user their namespace** - so two further states withhold it, both decided by `runtimeApplicable`. (1) An **env-FORCED** decision: `CELLAR_DATABRICKS_RUNTIME` bypasses the store *and* the connection scope, so with a forced OFF over a carried-over stored `true` the card sat in `pending` permanently and every click restarted the kernel back onto `pending` - a namespace-wiping no-op loop. The client cannot read the server's env, so `runtimeStatus` reports **`envForced`** (`true`/`false`/`null`) from **`databricksRuntimeForced()`** in `ui-state.ts`, which reads the ONE exported override parser `databricksRuntimeOverride` in `databricksRuntime.ts` (the same predicate the inject decision uses - never a second env parse, or the display and the decision could disagree). When it is forced the card says the environment controls it, the toggle shows the value in force and is **disabled** (a switch that silently changes nothing is worse than one that says who holds it), and the Apply button is not rendered at all - no restart can move that decision. (2) **No active notebook** (`notebookPath` null, the shape the Sidebar passes with no notebook tab open): `applyRuntime`'s restart is guarded on the path, so the button would spin and change nothing - it renders DISABLED saying to open a notebook, rather than vanishing, since the remedy is one the user can act on. **The rule generalizes to EVERY write control on the card - never offer an action that cannot do anything, and SAY when the environment is in control** - because each of them applies through the same namespace-wiping restart, and a control that writes a preference, SKIPS the work, then is described by copy claiming the work happened is the same defect wherever it sits. So the no-notebook case is one shared derived, **`runtimeRestartable`** (`notebookPath` + `onRestartKernel`), which gates the Apply button, `toggleRuntime` AND `commitVersion` and swaps the hint's "toggling restarts the kernel - variables are cleared" clause for the open-a-notebook remedy: without it, flipping the toggle OFF there wrote the preference, skipped the restart, and left the card reading `active` beside an unchecked toggle under a false restart claim (the `pending`-first ordering was never reached, since `runtimeActive` is checked first - it is live-derived, and the live kernel genuinely still carried the runtime). And the version override gets its own flag: **`CELLAR_DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` is INDEPENDENT of `CELLAR_DATABRICKS_RUNTIME`** (either can be set alone), so `runtimeStatus` reports **`versionEnvForced`** (the forced string, or null) from `databricksRuntimeVersionForced()` → the exported `databricksRuntimeVersionOverride` - the same parser `databricksRuntimeVersion` resolves through, never a second env read - and the two are NEVER collapsed into one boolean: a forced version leaves the on/off decision (toggle, Apply now) fully the user's, while the version input shows the value actually in force and is **disabled**, since committing an edit would restart the kernel to advertise a value the override discards. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-connect-runtime-off.test.ts` (both `envForced` and `versionEnvForced` over a real `getStatus`, incl. their independence) + `tests/unit/databricks-runtime.test.ts` (the pure override parsers) + the Apply-now / no-notebook-toggle / forced-version cases in `tests/e2e/databricks-two-card-redesign.spec.ts`. **`IS_DATABRICKS` is an internal token and must stay out of user-facing copy** - the toggle's tooltip/hint say "code that checks whether it is running on Databricks" instead; the flag name lives on only in comments and the probe source. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-connect-runtime-off.test.ts` (the invariant at the kernel-start gate, over a REAL `connect()` + the REAL preference store: connect writes no preference and `injectDatabricksRuntime(true)` stays false) + the connect/toggle halves of `tests/e2e/databricks-connect-no-lost-flash.spec.ts`. **Log out ≠ Disconnect (task `cellar-databricks-logout-l5`) - the distinction IS the feature, do not collapse them.** *Disconnect* ends one notebook's Spark session and leaves you authenticated. *Log out* (`logout()` in `databricks.ts` → `POST /api/databricks/logout` → the quiet ghost button under the Cluster card's Switch/Disconnect row, `data-testid=databricks-logout`) also drops the authentication, in three steps. It is the panel's most destructive control sitting one row under its most everyday one, so it takes a **two-step inline confirm** (the `Checkpoints.svelte` restore / `Sidebar.svelte` kernel-wipe idiom): the ghost button only ARMS (`databricks-logout-confirm-box`, whose copy names the blast radius - every notebook's Spark session app-wide, minutes of cold-cluster restart to recover), and only `databricks-logout-confirm` posts; `databricks-logout-cancel` and any selection change (`resetSelection`) disarm it. **The confirm + tooltip copy AND the button's visibility are gated on `cellarSignedInAnywhere`** (`logoutConfirmCopy`/`logoutButtonTitle`/`logoutRow`), for the same honesty rule the outcome note already follows: the button is always shown while connected, so it renders over a PAT/`databricks-cli` connection with no Cellar-minted credential anywhere, and the unconditional "clears every saved sign-in" promised there a purge that cannot happen - directly contradicting the post-action note's "left untouched", in the one place the user decides whether to proceed. With no sign-in to clear it names only what happens (every notebook's Spark session, app-wide, minutes of cold-cluster restart) and says the external credentials are untouched; with one, it keeps the strong wording. **The predicate MUST be the process-wide one, never the per-selection `cellarSignedIn`** (any recorded `signedInHosts`/`signedInProfiles` entry, plus this selection's own in-flight `authed`), because `logout()` is global: keyed off the selection, the confirm says "nothing to clear" while a *different* recorded OAuth host's token is deleted (sign in to a bare host, switch the picker to a PAT profile, connect, Log out), and the visibility gate HIDES the only control that could purge that sign-in. The one genuinely per-selection clause is the "this connection authenticates through `~/.databrickscfg`" explanation, which stays on `cellarOwnsAuth`. **The outcome note/warning deliberately OUTLIVES the connected→picker card swap a log out causes (it is rendered ungated by `cellarSignedIn`), but it describes one moment in time** - so `clearLogoutFeedback()` is the ONE clear, called from `resetSelection` AND from every other "the user moved on" action - `signIn()`/`connect()`/`disconnect()`, none of which run `resetSelection` on an unchanged selection: without them a "signed out everywhere" note ends up rendered under a live connected cluster, and (since a `sessionsFailed` warning's own remedy is literally "disconnect that notebook") the warning would outlive the disconnect that resolved it. Keep the trigger visually QUIETER than Disconnect - the confirm exists to stop a misclick, not to make Log out louder. The three steps: (1) it disconnects **every** bound notebook through the ordinary `disconnect` path (not just the caller's - a leftover `reconnectTarget` elsewhere would silently rebuild `spark` on the next kernel restart, after the user was told they were signed out); (2) it purges the cached OAuth token **only where the credential is Cellar's own**, decided by the one exported predicate **`hasCellarCachedOAuth(auth)`** = `mode==='oauth' || needsSignIn`, i.e. exactly the two external-browser shapes `assertSignedIn` gates (a bare typed host, a no-token `auth_type=external-browser` profile); (3) it clears the in-process `signedInHosts`/`signedInProfiles` gate, so the next listing/connect demands a fresh `login`. **Scope is GLOBAL and that is deliberate** - every sign-in this process recorded and every bound notebook, not just the panel's current selection, for the same reconnect-intent reason as (1); do NOT narrow it, and keep the copy that says so (the button title and the confirmation both name "everywhere"). **The load-bearing invariant is that an INCOMPLETE sign-out may never be presentable as a clean one**, because a cleared gate over a SURVIVING token is the worst state of all: the next "Sign in" is then a silent cache hit with no browser prompt and the user believes they signed out. So `LogoutResult` carries `purgeFailed` (the probe could not run or could not delete: `no_python`/`sdk_missing`/timeout/`Config` error/EPERM), `purgeMissed` (the probe RAN for a selection Cellar recorded a sign-in for and found nothing to delete - a drifted cache key, not a clean purge) and `sessionsFailed`, plus the **vanished-selection** case that funnels through `purgeFailed` (a recorded sign-in whose profile has since been deleted/renamed in `~/.databrickscfg`, so `resolveAuth` throws and the purge cannot even derive WHICH cache entry is its token - it must NOT be a silent `catch { continue }`, which would clear that gate and report a clean sign-out over an untouched token). **`sessionsFailed` splits into two counters that must never be collapsed, because they are different facts with different remedies**: `sessionsBusy` = `disconnect` threw **`busy`** (a connect is in flight, so it refused before touching anything and resolves itself once that connect ends; it cannot be waited out here, a connect can take minutes, so it is reported), and `sessionsStuck` = **any other throw**, which comes out of `runInKernel(DISCONNECT_CODE)` i.e. BEFORE the assignments that clear `connection`/`reconnectTarget` - so the notebook is genuinely STILL BOUND and would rebuild `spark` on its next kernel restart. Reporting the stuck case as "a connect is still in progress" names a cause that never happened and sends the user to wait for something that will never finish; each carries its own sentence and its own remedy. All of it folds into one `incomplete`/`incompleteReason` pair the sidebar renders as a warning-toned `databricks-logout-warning` box instead of the ordinary confirmation. **Step 3 is per-selection, not a blanket `.clear()`**: a selection whose purge did not provably complete KEEPS its gate entry, which is the only thing left telling the user (and `assertSignedIn`) that a usable credential may still exist. **Every PURGE reason names the cache location (`OAUTH_CACHE_DIR` = `~/.config/databricks-sdk-py/oauth/`) and its COUNT (`for N signed-in workspace(s)`, so several failures can never read as one), and the server owns that path - the sidebar never carries a second copy of it, nor does the reason builder sniff the text it just built (each failure carries an explicit `namesCacheDir` flag, since a `String.includes` check drifts into a duplicated or a missing path on any reword)**: keeping the gate is right, but a miss that neither the derived keys nor the claim-matching scan can resolve repeats on every attempt, so without naming where the token lives the user is left with a permanent warning and no way to reach a clean state. **The warning's REMEDY is reason-specific** (`incompleteWarning` in `Databricks.svelte`, driven off the `purgeFailed`/`purgeMissed`/`sessionsBusy`/`sessionsStuck` counters, which the client type therefore carries): only a purge that failed or missed says a token may still be deletable by hand, while a session-only sign-out (the token WAS deleted, a notebook was merely mid-connect) must NOT tell the user to remove a file that is already gone - and there it splits again, `sessionsBusy` saying to disconnect that notebook once its connect finishes while `sessionsStuck` says it is still bound and to disconnect it once its kernel is reachable again - appending one fixed token-cache sentence to every reason is the same assert-more-than-was-verified defect the honesty rule exists to prevent. Same rule in the confirmation: the strong "every saved sign-in was cleared" wording is reserved for a purge that genuinely completed (`clearedTokens`), and the no-purge-verified branch says only what was done (sessions disconnected, sign-in state cleared). Step 1's teardown stays unconditional - ending a session is always safe. **What it must NEVER delete: the user's own credentials** - `~/.databrickscfg` profiles, OS keyring entries, and the databricks CLI's own token cache are managed by the CLI/SDK, not by Cellar. A PAT / `databricks-cli` profile therefore purges NOTHING and is reported as `externalSkipped` (the UI says so rather than implying a purge that never happened); that honesty is why the button is HIDDEN when there is no Cellar sign-in ANYWHERE to clear, gated client-side by `cellarSignedInAnywhere` (whose per-selection half matches the server's NORMALIZED `signedInHosts`, hence the shared `src/lib/databricksHost.ts` - a second copy of that normalization would hide Log out exactly when it matters). The purge itself is the PROBE's **`logout` op**, which deletes `~/.config/databricks-sdk-py/oauth/<hash>.json` - the SDK's own note is that this cache is "local to the Python SDK and not reused by other SDKs", which is what makes it precisely the thing Cellar's browser sign-in minted. Two sharp edges there: it is dispatched **before `build_client`** (that authenticates, so it would pop the very browser we are erasing) and its `Config` is built with a **no-op `credentials_strategy`** for the same reason; and its `retry_timeout_seconds`/`http_timeout_seconds` are cut to 1s/5s because `Config()` also runs a best-effort `/.well-known` host-metadata discovery whose DEFAULT 300s retry budget hung the whole logout for five minutes on an unreachable host - which is exactly when someone signs out. **The filename is DERIVED through the SDK's own `TokenCache`, never rehashed here**, over a cross-product of near-miss keys (with/without `offline_access`, profile-scoped and not, every plausible `redirect_url`) - every candidate hashes THIS host, so a wrong guess can only name a file that does not exist. The key has drifted across SDK versions (current builds hash host+client_id+scopes+profile; older ones folded in the redirect URL), so **when the derived names hit NOTHING the probe falls back to identifying this host's entries POSITIVELY** - reading each cache file and matching the host its own access-token JWT claims (`iss`/`aud`) name. That fallback is emphatically **NOT a sweep of `~/.config/databricks-sdk-py/oauth/`**: that directory is shared with the user's own python scripts and with other workspaces, and blind deletion would destroy credentials Cellar never minted, which is the whole point of the feature. It runs only on a derivation miss, and only deletes an entry that names the host being signed out of. There is deliberately **no MCP tool**: signing out is a human action, like the OAuth browser itself. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-logout.test.ts` (the real SDK: the right cache file dies, EVERY signed-in workspace's does, a never-signed-in one and `~/.databrickscfg` provably survive, and a derived-key miss is still caught by claim-matching - those cases `describe.skipIf` with the reason IN THE SUITE NAME where no project `.venv` carries the SDK, so a green run is never mistakable for a verified purge; the honesty rules run everywhere against a stub interpreter) + `tests/e2e/databricks-logout.spec.ts` (mocked routes - a test must never be able to sign the user out of their real account). **Upload the open notebook to the workspace (`uploadNotebook` in `databricks.ts` → `POST /api/databricks/upload` → the Upload card's button in `Databricks.svelte`).** Copies the notebook into the CONNECTED user's own folder - `/Users/<user>/<basename>`, resolved from `current_user.me()`, never guessed - as a real Databricks notebook (`workspace.import_` with **`format=JUPYTER`**; SOURCE would flatten every cell into one `.py`). **The uploaded NAME takes an optional prefix/postfix** (the two fields above the button), whose rule - assembly around the extension-less stem, the date tokens `{YYYY-MM-DD}`/`{YYYYMMDD}`/`{YYYY-MM}`/`{YYYYMM}`/`{YYYY}`/`{MM}`/`{DD}` (local date, case-sensitive, unknown braces left LITERAL), and the refusal of a separator or control character so the target can never leave `/Users/<user>/`, and the `MAX_UPLOAD_NAME_CHARS` bound on the ASSEMBLED name (it rides `argv` in the probe request - the very limit the notebook bytes are base64'd onto stdin to avoid - and a workspace object name has its own ceiling, so an unbounded pasted affix surfaced as a bare `spawn E2BIG` instead of an actionable refusal) - lives ONCE in the browser-safe `src/lib/databricksUploadName.ts` (`resolveUploadName`), read by BOTH the sidebar's live preview and `uploadNotebook`: a second copy would let the preview promise one name while the upload landed another, which is the whole point of showing it. The browser expands tokens at click time and sends literal text (the server re-expands, which is a no-op on it - that is what makes the CLIENT/SERVER hop exact, and it is the only hop idempotence covers; an affix that is PRESENT but not a string is a `bad_request` from `affixText`, never coerced to no-affix - absent/null stays the no-affix path, and a direct API caller has no preview to notice a silent repair with), the PREVIEW keeps up with the day on its own (`uploadNow`, a reactive clock the resolve reads, refreshed on a coarse tick plus window `focus`/`visibilitychange` while the section is expanded - the wake half is the load-bearing one, since a suspended laptop fires no timers - **and by the CLICK, which reads the clock ONCE and moves `uploadNow` onto that same instant**, closing the up-to-one-tick window in which an AWAKE machine's preview named yesterday while the upload sent today; the direction is always preview-catches-up-to-click, never the reverse, because someone uploading today must get today's date), the tooltip's token examples are the tokens' REAL expansions rather than stored literals (a stored one would advertise `{YYYY} -> 2026` forever), the armed replace confirm PINS the affixes it was resolved with so Replace overwrites the path it named, and both affixes empty is byte-for-byte the pre-affix upload with ONE deliberate exception - `notebookStem` now trims BEFORE matching the extension, so a file literally named `analysis.ipynb ` (a trailing space is a legal filename) uploads as `analysis` instead of keeping its whole suffix as the workspace name. Last-used values persist per project through `$lib/uiState` (`cellar-databricks-upload-prefix`/`-postfix`, the `.cellar/` store, never `localStorage`), and an EMPTY field persists as `''` rather than deleting the key - see the default below, which an absent key would let creep back. **An unrecognized brace is left literal, and that alone was NOT enough** (task `cellar-affix-date-token-fix-d9`): the reported "date tokens do not expand" was a user typing `{YYYYMM}`, which was not in the vocabulary, so the preview showed `{YYYYMM}_analysis` - which reads as a token that has not expanded YET, not one that never will. The expander was correct throughout; the defect was that a near-miss failed SILENTLY. Three things answer it and each must stay: `unknownDateTokens` (membership decided by ASKING `expandDateTokens` - a run it does not change is unknown - never a second copy of the token list) drives a WARNING (never a refusal: the name is legal and `{FOO}` may be meant) naming the run, reached through **`unknownAffixTokens`, which scans the prefix and the postfix SEPARATELY** and merges deduped - the name is `prefix + stem + postfix`, so a braced run cannot span the fields, and scanning their concatenation INVENTED one (`a{` plus `}b` warned about a `{}` present in neither); the token CONTROLS insert the exact braced form at the caret, so the vocabulary is never guessed - and **a field nobody has EDITED appends** rather than trusting the offset, because a never-focused input reports `selectionStart` 0 indistinguishably from a caret the user put at the start (the shape that reaches it is a server-rendered `value=` attribute, which Svelte's own `set_value` then leaves alone), so an affix seeded from the cross-project default was PREPENDED into; the marker is the `tokenField` action's `WeakSet` in `uploadTokenField.ts` - applied at every affix input in BOTH surfaces, and safe to omit, since an unmarked field simply appends - while a caret the user really chose at 0 still inserts at 0. **They LOOK different per surface and share everything that matters**: the Databricks sidebar gives each affix its OWN dropdown beside its input (`data-testid=databricks-upload-token-select`, keyed by `data-affix`, whose `<option>`s keep `data-testid=databricks-upload-token`), so the target is NAMED by the control rather than inferred from "the field last focused" - which guessed wrong for anyone reaching for a token before touching a field - and seven chips no longer wrap to three lines in a narrow sidebar ahead of the Upload button; Settings keeps its chip row (`data-testid=settings-upload-token`). Both are driven off `UPLOAD_DATE_TOKENS` with each expansion computed live, and both INSERT rather than replace, which is what keeps an affix free text: a pick lands beside what is typed and a hand-built affix is never overwritten. The sidebar's select is an ACTION, not a value - `commitUploadToken` reads the token and only THEN resets it to its placeholder (reversed, every pick inserts the empty placeholder and silently does nothing; that order is also what makes a commit IDEMPOTENT); without that reset the same token cannot be picked twice running and the closed control reads as a setting. **It commits only on an EXPLICIT pick, never on the bare `change`**: arrowing over a CLOSED select changes its value and fires `change` per press on Windows and Linux, so merely tabbing to the control and pressing Down inserted a token nobody chose - something the chip row it replaced could never do. A browser dispatches key events to the page only while the option list is CLOSED, so `onUploadTokenChange` ignores a `change` landing in the SAME TASK as a keydown of ours (`uploadTokenKeyNav`, cleared on the next task) while `onUploadTokenKeydown` commits on **Enter** - which is what keeps the closed-select flow usable - and `onblur` puts the placeholder back so a token arrowed past is never left reading as a setting. **The flag must never be held ACROSS a task**: kept sticky it swallows the genuine pick that follows the Down which OPENED the list, i.e. it makes the control unusable by keyboard, which is worse than the surprise being fixed. Deliberately NOT done via an `<option>` `click` either - Safari does not fire one for a mouse pick, and no browser fires one for a commit out of an OS-rendered list. Its `min-w-0` + explicit width are load-bearing too: daisyUI gives `.select` a 10rem min-width, which at the narrower sidebar widths won the flex row and squeezed the affix input to zero. BOTH surfaces that warn share not only the scan but the SENTENCE (`unknownTokenWarning`, whose one deliberate difference is the REMEDY - the sidebar names the dropdown beside each field, Settings names nothing, having no such control in view - a PARAMETER rather than a second function;
