the sidebar's standing token hint then YIELDS to that warning, as the warning already yields to `uploadNameError`, so the specific sentence is never restated by the general one directly under it) and the caret write (`$lib/uploadTokenField`'s `insertTokenIntoField`, the DOM glue around the pure `insertUploadToken`), so a reword, a pluralisation fix or a caret fix cannot reach only one of them; and the month stamps `{YYYYMM}`/`{YYYY-MM}` were ADDED, since they are unambiguous contractions on the same convention and are what people reach for - which is not licence to admit a spelling readable two ways (`{mm}` is still refused). `BRACED` captures a whole `{…}` group and each group is ONE lookup, so long and short spellings coexist with no longest-match hazard. **A cross-project DEFAULT** for both affixes lives in Settings (`Settings.svelte`, `data-testid=settings-upload-prefix`/`-postfix`, same buttons + same warning) and is stored in the NEW global `src/lib/server/user-settings.ts` (`~/.cellar/settings.json`, keys in the browser-safe `$lib/uploadDefaults` and re-exported server-side so the two cannot drift; `CELLAR_USER_SETTINGS` redirects it, which is the only way to test it without rewriting the runner's own file - so `tests/e2e/harness.ts`'s `bootCellar` redirects it into the throwaway workspace BY DEFAULT: every booted app READS that store on its first SSR load, so "only the specs that write it opt in" was already wrong) - the third store, and the only one tied to neither a workspace nor an origin: the per-project `.cellar/` store cannot answer for a project it is not in, and `localStorage` dies with the dynamic port. **An unusable default is REFUSED, not persisted, and the verdict is PER FIELD** (`setUploadDefault` returns on `isStorableAffix(v)` before it reaches the store): the field keeps exactly what was typed and the error says why - refuse, never repair - but nothing unusable is written, because the BLAST RADIUS is what makes this stricter than the sidebar's own field, an unusable affix there disabling the upload in the one project on screen while an unusable DEFAULT disables it in every project that never set its own, under a message naming a field the user never typed into there (and clearing that project's field opts only that project out). **The rule may only read the affix being WRITTEN, never the pane's combined `defaultsResolved`** (which is what is SHOWN): the two defaults are independent keys, so a paired verdict refused to store a perfectly good postfix while the prefix was invalid, and the write that ran once the prefix was fixed carried only the prefix - the typed postfix was lost with nothing left that would ever write it. `isStorableAffix` (in `databricksUploadName.ts`, beside the resolver, over the one `FORBIDDEN` reader they now share) is therefore the separator/control-character rule ALONE, that being the only one of the resolver's checks that is a fact about an affix; the assembled name's LENGTH is deliberately excluded, since it is measured against a placeholder stem while a cross-project default meets a different stem in every project - it would refuse a pattern that fits everywhere real and could not promise it fits anywhere. The consequence is deliberate: a previously VALID stored default is left UNTOUCHED while the field holds invalid text, so a RELOAD shows the last good value (`Settings` is mounted for the life of the shell and seeds once, so closing and reopening the modal re-reads nothing and the invalid text is still on screen) - do not "fix" that by deleting the stored default, which would discard a working setting over a half-typed character. **Its MECHANISM is shared, not mirrored, on BOTH sides of the wire** - `src/lib/server/json-store.ts` `createJsonStore(pathFn)` (which `ui-state.ts` also uses) and `src/lib/clientStore.ts` `createClientStore(endpoint)` (which `$lib/uiState` also uses): each pair differs only in WHERE the file is / WHICH endpoint it PUTs to, so lazy load, copy-on-read, `null`-deletes-key, the unref'd debounce, the synchronous exit flush, the corrupt-file-reads-empty rule, **the inode+mtime+size reload and the ATOMIC write** are one implementation server-side, while the SSR-seeded cache, the `hasOwnProperty` read guard, the 300ms debounce and the `keepalive` `pagehide` flush are one implementation browser-side - a fix to any of them reaches both stores. Only what genuinely differs stays per store: server-side the path plus its domain accessors (`addProjectRootToPath`/`injectDatabricksRuntime`/`databricksRuntimeVersion`/the `*Forced` pair, the re-exported upload keys), browser-side `uiState`'s one-time `localStorage` migration + `setUiNow` and `userSettings`' change notification. **The reload is shared, so BOTH stores re-read the file when an inode+mtime+size stamp says it moved, and the GLOBAL one is why it exists** (Cellar runs one instance per repo, so a load-once cache made a default set in one instance invisible to every instance already running, permanently) - the per-project store inherits it, which is right rather than merely harmless, its file being equally editable by hand or by a second process. The cost is one `statSync` per read, affordable because no read is in a hot loop: the SSR page load, the `/api/ui-state` GET/PUT, the project-root route, two reads per kernel start, and one per `getStatus` - the Databricks panel's status poll, which reads the stored on/off preference (`runtimeStatus` -> `databricksRuntimePreference`) so the Runtime toggle can re-seed after an agent's `databricks_runtime` writes it; that poll runs at 1.2s-to-5s only while a restart is in flight, so it is a handful of `statSync`s per restart rather than a standing cost. Our own pending change always wins (a `dirty` store is never reloaded, or a preference would revert between being set and being persisted), and a re-read that fails KEEPS the cache rather than emptying it (a first load has nothing to keep, so a corrupt file there is still an empty store; a later parse failure is most likely catching another process mid-write). **The stamp is taken BEFORE the read, and that ordering is the whole guarantee**: another instance's write can land inside the read window, and stamped AFTERWARDS it would mark content the cache does not hold as already loaded - stale for good, since nothing else moves the stamp, i.e. exactly the failure above with no test able to see it; taken first, the same race costs one redundant re-read. The `flush` stamp obeys the same rule from the other side - it is kept only while the file's size still agrees with the bytes just written, else it drops to a re-read. Both stores also **write through `src/lib/server/write-file-atomic.js`'s `writeFileAtomic`** (lifted out of `harness.js`, which imports it back, so it stays node-builtins-only and rides `package.json` `files` for `bin/cellar.js`): the global file is read and written by every running instance, and a truncated one reads as an EMPTY store, so a half-written flush would be a SILENT loss of the user's cross-project defaults rather than a visible error. **Deliberately NOT the `.ipynb` path's `atomicWriteFileSync`, and the difference is the point** - temp+rename installs a NEW inode, so a writer that does not `realpathSync` first replaces a dotfile-manager's SYMLINK with a regular file, and one that does not carry the target's MODE across reverts a `chmod 600` store to the umask default; both are live cases for `~/.cellar/settings.json`. The two atomic writers coexist on purpose for now: unifying them means touching the write path for the user's PRIMARY data, which is its own change. Last-writer-wins at whole-file granularity is unchanged and accepted. **The direction is the rule: a project with an affix of its own always wins and the default only fills in for a project never asked** - inverted, it would silently rewrite naming set deliberately per project. It stores the PATTERN unexpanded (a resolved date would stamp every later upload with the day it was set). **Every surface that puts a setting into a NAME reads it through the ONE guarded accessor `getUserSettingText`, and that is enforced by the module rather than asked of the caller - the untyped read it wraps is module-PRIVATE, so there is no bare `getUserSetting` to reach for** - the file is untyped JSON and `/api/user-settings` accepts any JSON value, so a hand-edit (or a PUT) can put a number where a prefix belongs, and it lands in `expandDateTokens`, whose `text.replace` throws inside a render-time `$derived`; nothing in this app mounts a `<svelte:boundary>`, so that throw takes the WHOLE render tree, not one field (the DataFrame duplicate-key defect's blast radius, reached from another direction). It degrades to "no affix", which is both the safe reading and the honest one - a value that is not text was never an affix - and it is one shared read precisely so the Settings seed and the panel's `storedAffix` fallback cannot drift into one guarded and one not, which is exactly how it got in (a future non-text setting exports its own guarded accessor beside that one; it does not reopen the untyped read). **The panel RE-READS it rather than seeding once** (`seedUploadAffixes`, driven by `$lib/userSettings`'s `onUserSettingsChange` subscription plus the section becoming visible): the Databricks section mounts lazily and is then kept mounted for the session, so a one-shot mount read left a default set afterwards invisible until a reload - while the Settings copy promised it applied to every project without one of its own. Re-reading is safe precisely BECAUSE of the direction: every keystroke writes the project's own answer through `setUi` (which updates the client cache synchronously), so a re-read while the user is typing returns exactly what they typed and can never clobber it. **A seed that MOVES an affix drops the upload feedback exactly as typing does** (`clearUploadFeedback`, whose reason is that the "uploaded to" note and the armed replace confirm each name a workspace path built from the OLD affixes) - so the rule holds for EVERY writer of those two fields rather than for the typed one alone; it is guarded on a value having really changed, since most seeds change nothing (reopening the section, a change to some other setting) and dismissing a still-accurate box there would be the same defect with the sign flipped. **An upload IN FLIGHT DEFERS the whole seed** (`uploadConfirmBusy`, applied by the same effect once the gate lifts): that clear bumps `uploadSeq`, so re-seeding mid-flight made `uploadToWorkspace` discard the settled reply as superseded - the replace happened, the panel said nothing about it and the box vanished, the exact silent clobber Cancel is inert for. A DEFERRED seed then keeps WHATEVER arrived while it waited, and the asymmetry with typing is the point: the note (or an error) is a past-tense record of an attempt the user confirmed and is what they are still waiting to read, and an **armed replace confirm survives too** - a `status:'exists'` reply leaves both of those null, so the box IS the entire outcome and dropping it left NOTHING on screen for an attempt that had just settled, indistinguishable from an upload that silently did nothing. That it names a path built from the OLD affixes is not a reason to drop it: `uploadExistsAffixes` PINS them, so Replace overwrites exactly the path the box names. An IMMEDIATE seed (nothing in flight, so nothing arrived) still clears everything, exactly as typing does. The listener is registered ONCE and the seed runs `untrack`ed - it reads and writes the affixes, so a tracked call re-registered it on every keystroke, an effect feeding its own dependencies. The notification is deliberately a bare "something changed" rather than a key/value, so the subscriber re-derives through this precedence rule instead of keeping a second, drifting copy of it. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-upload-name.test.ts` (the rule) + `tests/unit/databricks-upload-card.test.ts` (SOURCE guards on the three component rules e2e alone would prove - the card being rendered outside the connection card's own element while Switch/Disconnect/Log out stay inside it, the read-then-reset ORDER in `commitUploadToken`, and the pick going through `insertTokenIntoField` rather than assigning the affix, plus the explicit-pick rule in both of its directions - pinned here because e2e is absent from CI and the gate, the `upload-default-guards` precedent) + `tests/unit/upload-token-field.test.ts` (the caret rule over REAL jsdom inputs in both shapes: an unedited field appends whether its value arrived as a property or as an attribute, a chosen caret at 0 still inserts at 0, and every affix input really carries the action) + the affix cases in `tests/unit/databricks-upload-notebook.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/databricks-upload.spec.ts` (whose affix tests share ONE persisted store, so they sit in their own `describe` whose **`afterEach`** clears the fields - as each test's last statement the clear was skipped by exactly the failure that leaves an affix behind, so one real failure dragged the tests after it down with it; the cleanup is best-effort in the UI and authoritative against the SERVER store, since it also has to run after a test that broke the page) + `tests/unit/user-settings.test.ts` (the global store: durability across a relaunch, a corrupt file reading EMPTY rather than throwing during SSR, the shared keys, another instance's write being picked up, our own pending change outranking the file, and a failed re-read keeping the cache) + `tests/unit/json-store.test.ts` (the shared mechanism, over BOTH stores at once: a write driven INTO the read window - `readFileSync` is wrapped, since the race is otherwise unobservable and would survive a suite that only writes files one after another - being picked up on the next read rather than missed forever, a quiet file costing no re-read, a same-size write inside one timestamp tick still being caught because the stamp folds in the INODE, a symlinked store staying a symlink with the real file receiving the write, a `chmod 600` surviving the replacement, and the write installing a NEW inode, which is what tells temp+rename apart from a truncating in-place write) + `tests/e2e/upload-name-defaults.spec.ts` (the default across TWO REAL projects - two launchers, two workspaces, two ports, pointed at ONE `CELLAR_USER_SETTINGS` file: project A sets an affix of its own and then the default, keeps its own, and project B - never asked, and reading the store for the first time - seeds from it and uploads under it; plus a default changed with a Databricks panel ALREADY OPEN reaching it with NO reload, that change dropping an armed replace confirm built from the old affix while a no-op re-seed leaves one alone, the DEFERRED seed after a held `exists` reply KEEPING that confirm and Replace still overwriting the path it pinned, an invalid default being refused while the last valid one survives, a CLEARED field staying cleared, and the same tokens/warning/split-brace rule in Settings) + `tests/unit/upload-default-guards.test.ts` (source guards on both component rules, since e2e is absent from CI and the gate). Deliberately NOT claimed there: that a default written by one running instance reaches another that has already read the store IN THE BROWSER - the server re-reads it, but a page open at the time is not pushed to. It reuses the existing auth wholesale (`requireConnectedSel` → the same `WorkspaceClient` every listing builds), so there is **no second auth mechanism** and an expired profile keeps the same `profile_reauth_required` + its exact re-auth command. **Workspace FILES only - it must never touch compute** (no start/stop/restart; the connection is required solely to identify the workspace and the user). Bytes come from `notebook.ts`'s `notebookIpynb` (the LIVE doc through the same `serialize`+`stringify` `writeNotebook` uses, so an upload can never disagree with the `.ipynb` - and a `.py` jupytext notebook uploads as a real `.ipynb`), base64 on the probe's **stdin**, which is why `probe()` gained an optional stdin channel: a notebook with outputs is routinely megabytes and the OS refuses an argv that large (E2BIG). **No silent clobber**: without an explicit `overwrite` the probe checks first and, finding a notebook, writes NOTHING and returns `status:'exists'` so the UI arms a Replace confirm - a NON-notebook object is refused outright (`workspace_conflict`), never offered as a replace. **That check's "the path is free" case keys off the exception TYPE NAME (`NotFound`/`ResourceDoesNotExist`), NEVER `classify()`** - `classify` matches the MESSAGE before the type and a workspace not-found message embeds the path, so an ordinary notebook name (or user login) containing `oauth`/`consent`/`authorization code`/`external-browser`/`authenticate` read as `oauth_login_required`, re-raised, and failed the very FIRST upload of that name permanently with a sign-in prompt for a path that simply did not exist. `classify` is for the final MESSAGE, not for control flow; anything else still re-raises, so a real permission/auth/network failure surfaces instead of being taken for a free path and overwriting. That confirm is the reason the panel's upload is **generation-guarded** (`uploadSeq`, the `statusSeq` idiom, bumped by every clear so Cancel and the notebook-switch clear are authoritative against an in-flight request): a reply landing after the panel moved to another notebook is dropped ENTIRELY, since arming the confirm from it would name one path while Replace posts the CURRENT `notebookPath` - a clobber the user was never prompted for. The confirm box also stays MOUNTED for the whole replace (the feedback is cleared when the attempt settles, never before the `await`), so the path being overwritten is on screen while it happens - with **BOTH its buttons inert through the ONE shared `uploadConfirmBusy`, Cancel included**: the overwrite request cannot be recalled once sent, so a Cancel that merely dismissed the box (it bumps `uploadSeq`, so the reply is dropped) presented a replace that WAS happening as one the user aborted - the same silent clobber, reached from the other side. Nothing in flight and it is live again, so the box is never a dead end. **Bounded on both axes the payload grows along, because this is the one op with a LARGE request**: `MAX_UPLOAD_JSON_BYTES` (7 MiB of notebook JSON, sized under the workspace import API's ~10 MB of *base64* content) is checked BEFORE the `Buffer.from(...).toString('base64')` copy exists, refusing with its own `notebook_too_large` code (413, its own `REMEDY` entry - clear the outputs, which is what makes a notebook this big) rather than the shared `bad_request`; and the subprocess runs on `UPLOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` over the probe's `UPLOAD_HTTP_TIMEOUT` per-request window and `UPLOAD_RETRY_TIMEOUT` retry budget (`build_client` takes both, dispatched per op in `main()`, so the bounded budget also covers `Config()`'s own discovery), so a multi-megabyte PUT on a slow uplink is not cut short and mislabelled as the workspace not responding - and the three are NESTED, retries + one in-flight request staying inside the parent window, so the SDK's real message reports a wedged upload rather than the parent's SIGKILL - **the listing ops keep the tight `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`/`HTTP_TIMEOUT`, do not widen those**. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-upload-notebook.test.ts` (the Node op against a recording stub interpreter, plus the SHIPPED `PROBE` source run against a FAKE `databricks` SDK - which is what pins `/Users/<me>`, JUPYTER, the exists check gating `import_`, the size refusal happening before anything is spawned, and the upload's wider client timeout beside a listing's unchanged one) + `tests/e2e/databricks-upload.spec.ts` (mocked routes - a test must never be able to write into the user's real workspace - covering the confirm staying up through a replace, a superseded reply after a notebook switch arming nothing, and the too-large remedy).
- **Live Spark job-progress bars (`SPARK_PROGRESS_CORE_PY` + `SPARK_PROGRESS_INSTALL_PY` in `databricks.ts`).** While a Spark query runs, a per-query progress bar (overall completed/total tasks across stages, Databricks-style) renders live and clears on completion. **The API is `spark.registerProgressHandler(fn)` on the Spark Connect session** - it delivers per-stage `StageInfo` (`num_tasks`/`num_completed_tasks`/`num_bytes_read`) live during `.collect()`. **Connect-ONLY** (`@remote_only`; raises on classic PySpark), which is exactly what Cellar's Databricks integration uses - so this is a Connect-exclusive feature, not a classic one we lose. **No conf/env/reconnect needed:** it is a pure runtime client-side callback; `SPARK_CONNECT_PROGRESS_BAR_ENABLED` gates only the stdout ASCII bar, so the env scrub (`scrubEnv`/`KEEP_ENV`) is irrelevant and stays untouched. **It rides the existing ipywidgets rail with ZERO frontend work:** the handler builds an `HBox[FloatProgress, Label]` (the same composite tqdm uses) via `IPython.display`, so it streams through the widget comm pipeline and `clean.ts` strips its `widget-view` mime on save (zero git diff - confirmed by `clean-on-save.test.ts`). **Where it rides the lifecycle:** `_cellar_install_spark_progress(spark)` is called from `CONNECT_CODE` right after `_g['spark']=_spark` (best-effort; guarded on `ipywidgets` being importable - silent no-op if absent, since Cellar only guarantees `ipykernel`). `clearProgressHandlers()` first makes reconnect idempotent; `DISCONNECT_CODE` clears it; a kernel restart wipes it for free (the handler + manager live in kernel globals). The handler fires synchronously on the cell-execution thread inside `.collect()`, so its widget parents to the running cell - exactly why tqdm already works. **The callable actually registered is `_cellar_spark_progress_cb(mgr)`, NOT the bound method** - a deliberate `*args`/`**kwargs` wrapper that binds any call shape, pulls the four fields by keyword (falling back to positional index), and swallows everything. pyspark's `Progress._notify` invokes the handler with no try/except of its own, so a future `databricks-connect` signature change (a param added/renamed/reordered) would raise INSIDE the user's `.collect()` where the handler body's own try/except cannot reach it; the wrapper degrades a signature drift to "no bar" instead of a query-breaking argument-binding `TypeError`, and must itself never raise. **TWO hard-won facts (verified on the captain's real cluster, MUST NOT be regressed):** (1) the handler fires **~4-5 times per ~2s tick with IDENTICAL payloads**, so it **dedupes** by an absolute-counts `key` tuple (never increments - a `completed += n` design would double-count); (2) a query **shorter than the server `reportInterval` (~2s) shows no intermediate bar** - it delivers a single terminal `done=True` callback, which the `if s['done'] and no-bar-yet: return` guard turns into "no bar" (this also suppresses Cellar's own sub-second internal `SELECT 1` probes). Do NOT add machinery to force a bar on fast queries - that is expected, matches Databricks. The bar **completes to 100%/success and closes** on `done` (the manager pops the registry entry so nothing lingers). Pure core (`_cellar_spark_summary` projection + `_CellarSparkProgress` manager, renderer-injected) plus the `_cellar_spark_progress_cb` signature-tolerant wrapper are exercised with a fake renderer by `tests/unit/spark-progress.test.ts`, mirroring the dataflow-probe pattern.
- **User-choosable imports cell (unpinned).** ONE code cell per notebook, `metadata.cellar.role = 'imports'` (rides the `cellar` allowlist, so it round-trips clean-on-save with zero git noise), living at **any** index. The user designates any Python code cell via the per-cell **⋮ actions menu** (`Cell.svelte`, `data-testid=cell-actions` → `toggle-imports-role`): "Mark as imports cell" / "Unmark as imports cell", wired `onSetRole` → `LiveNotebook.setRole` → `PATCH /api/cells/[id] {role}` → `setCellRole` (which enforces one-per-notebook: designating a cell strips the role from any other, atomically + optimistically) → `cell:role` SSE. A pin-glyph **"imports" badge** (`data-testid=imports-badge`) is the position-independent marker. Three things fill the cell: that manual designation, the **Consolidate imports** action (navbar app menu → `POST /api/notebooks/imports` → `consolidateImports`) and **agent routing** in the MCP write tools. A human typing `import os` into cell 7 is *never* touched — that is the deliberate line. `src/lib/importsRole.ts` is the shared browser-safe identity (`isImportsCell`, `importsCellIndex`, position-independent); `clampMoveIndex` is now the **identity** function (the top-pin was removed) — kept at every move site (`moveCell`/`moveCellTo`/`moveCells` server - the bulk plan abandons the WHOLE move on a step it refuses - and `Notebook.svelte` client) as the one seam to reintroduce a positional rule if ever wanted. `ensureImportsCell` no longer hoists a designated cell; it returns it in place, and only creates/adopts a top cell as the fallback when NONE is designated. `setCellType(…,'markdown'|'sql')` demotes the role (only a Python code cell can hold it, else it would strand every future routed import).
  **Parsing is a JS tokenizer, not the kernel's `ast` and not a regex** (`src/lib/server/imports.js`, pure): the kernel would force-boot / queue behind a running cell and make a structural edit depend on the runtime, while a regex cannot see that `import evil` inside a triple-quoted string, `if TYPE_CHECKING: import pandas`, or `def f(): import json` are not module-level. `logicalLines()` understands comments, all four string forms + prefixes + escapes, bracket and backslash continuation, and reports each logical line's **indent** — only indent-0 lines whose first token is `import`/`from` are lifted. Anything that does not parse into a canonically re-renderable record is **left exactly where it is**. `mergeImportSources` returns the existing source **untouched** when nothing is new, which is what makes consolidate idempotent in the strong sense (second run: zero document change, zero kernel re-run). Ordering: `__future__`, plain `import`, absolute `from`, relative `from` — sorted by code unit, deliberately NOT isort's stdlib/third-party grouping (needs a package classification Cellar has no business guessing). **Staleness is a second consumer of this same tokenizer** (`importBindings.ts`, see the Cell staleness entry): `renderImport`'s canonical rendering is not only the dedup key, it is the IDENTITY of a binding, and `isImportsOnlyResidual` (the exported residual half of `isImportsOnly`) is what proves nothing else in a cell can shadow an import - so changing either changes what "this import did not move" means downstream.
  **Adoption:** an existing role cell wins; else a first cell that is *only* imports/comments/blanks is adopted (`isImportsOnly`); else a fresh cell is inserted at 0 (`addCellAt`, which is why `cell:added` now carries an explicit `index` — `afterId` cannot express "at the very top"). New event `cell:role`. Consolidate deletes cells its sweep emptied (never the last cell, never the imports cell) and runs the imports cell **only when something moved**.
  **Agent routing** (`service.js` `routeOne` → `finishImportRouting`): `add_cell`/`add_cells`/`edit_cell`/`add_and_run` lift module-level imports out of the submitted code into the imports cell, then run it **once** (all specs of one `add_cells` merge into one cell → one run, not one run per cell); `add_and_run` routes *before* creating the cell, so the code that needs `pd` finds it. Opt-out per call: **`route_imports: false`**. Editing the imports cell itself never routes into itself. Source that is *only* imports creates no cell (`routed_to_imports: true`). The `run_*` tools are deliberately **NOT** routed: they submit no code, and yanking imports out of an existing cell at run time would rewrite a cell a human may have authored (and `run_all` would strip the whole notebook).
- **nbdev-style cell export to a `.py` module (`src/lib/server/export-py.ts` + `src/lib/exportRole.ts`).** A per-code-cell **on/off** flag `metadata.cellar.export = true` (nbdev's `#|export`, rides the `cellar` allowlist → round-trips clean-on-save with zero git noise) plus a **notebook-level** target `notebook.metadata.cellar.export_target` (a workspace-relative `.py` path, nbdev's `#|default_exp`; also honored as a `#|default_exp <module>` directive in any cell, dotted→path). `export-py.ts` reads the marked cells IN ORDER and writes a clean module: header `# AUTOGENERATED BY CELLAR! DO NOT EDIT!` + `# Source notebook:` line, a best-effort `__all__` (top-level `def`/`class`/simple assignments via `imports.ts`'s `logicalLines`, private `_names` skipped), then the sources joined by blank lines + trailing `\n` - **deterministic + idempotent** (skips the write when on-disk bytes match, so re-export is a zero git-diff no-op). This is DISTINCT from the jupytext whole-notebook `.py` mirror (`jupytext-actions.ts`): that mirrors every cell with markers; this is a lean library module of only the marked cells, **committed to git** (NOT gitignored, per nbdev). **Two triggers:** (1) **auto-on-save** - `notebook.ts` `persist()` calls `autoExportPy(doc)` after every write (best-effort try/catch so a bad target never breaks the save, but NOT silent: the throw is recorded on the doc as `lastExportError` so the export tools can report it - see the agent-side entry below; no-op unless a target is set AND ≥1 cell marked; skipped for `.py` jpFormat docs which carry no cell metadata); (2) **manual** - `exportPy()` surfaces real errors, and REFRESHES `lastExportError` in BOTH directions for the same reason `autoExportPy` does on every persist: it writes without going through `persist`, so a stale success was still reported by the next idempotent `set_cell_export` (which skips the persist that would have cleared it), while a failure left UNRECORDED is worse - with the record null that same call emits no `module` field at all, which under the conditional contract reads as a module that WAS regenerated. Reached via the notebook's top **export bar** (`Notebook.svelte`, `data-testid=export-bar`: target input + "N cells marked" + "Export to .py" button + inline feedback, shown once a target is set OR any cell is marked) and the navbar app-menu "Export to .py" item (`+page.svelte` → `activeNotebookApi().exportPy()` → toast). **Wiring:** per-cell toggle in the ⋮ actions menu (`Cell.svelte`, `data-testid=toggle-export`) + a green **"export" badge** (`data-testid=export-badge`) → `onSetExport` → `LiveNotebook.setExport` → `PATCH /api/cells/[id] {export}` → `setCellExport` → `cell:export` SSE; target via `POST /api/notebooks/export-py {op:'set-target'|'export'}` → `setExportTarget`/`exportPy` → `notebook:export-target` SSE. `getNotebook`/`NotebookView` carry `exportTarget` so the browser seeds it on load. `setCellType(…,'markdown'|'sql')` drops the export flag (only a Python code cell can hold it). **"Python code cell" is the LOGICAL type, and `isExportCell` is where that is decided - never an nbformat `cell_type === 'code'` test.** A SQL cell IS an nbformat `code` cell tagged `cellar.language='sql'` (see `cellLanguage.ts`), so a `cell_type` test admits one and `export-py.ts` then concatenates its raw SQL into a module git TRACKS - invalid Python in a committed file, with the badge unremovable from the UI (`Cell.svelte`'s toggle is gated on `canBeImports`, which excludes SQL). The rule therefore lives in the ONE identity every surface reads (the exporter's filter, the badge, the agent map), so a stale or hand-edited flag on a SQL cell is inert everywhere rather than only at whichever setter last remembered to check. Its ELIGIBILITY half is **exported as `canExportCell`** and `isExportCell` is expressed in terms of it, so both SETTERS (`notebook.ts`'s `setCellExports`, `mcp/service.ts`'s per-cell check) call the SAME function rather than each re-deriving it from `isLogicalCellType` and agreeing only by coincidence - the drift this very fix argued against, reintroduced one layer down. **Agent side: `set_cell_export(ids, export)`** (`mcp/server.ts` → `mcp/service.ts` `setCellExport` → `notebook.ts` `setCellExports`) is the MCP equivalent of that per-cell toggle - batch, handle-addressed, all-or-nothing, Python-code-cells-only - and it pairs with `set_export_target` (see the display-settings entry). It regenerates the `.py` through the SAME `persist` → `autoExportPy` path, so there is no second export path; `setCellExports` is the BATCH form (one document write + one regeneration, since `persist` auto-exports - a loop would rewrite both files once per cell) and the singular `setCellExport` the UI drives is now that batch of one. **A `.py` TEXT notebook is refused UP FRONT** (`isPyTextNotebook` → `{ok:false, refused:'py-notebook'}`, surfaced through the ONE shared `pyNotebookRefusal(what)` message naming the cause, never a generic not-found), mirroring `exportPy`'s own throw: such a doc carries no cellar cell metadata, so the flag can never be stored AND `autoExportPy` skips it, i.e. reporting success would promise a module that can never exist while spending a blocking jupytext `spawnSync` on byte-identical output. **`set_export_target` takes the SAME refusal through the SAME predicate - and so does the UI's own target route** (`src/routes/api/notebooks/export-py/+server.js`, `op:'set-target'`, refusing with an actionable 400; `op:'export'` was already refused inside `exportPy`), because the symmetry is required rather than tidy: one half accepting a target the other half refuses to act on left an agent following INSTRUCTIONS clause 5 - or a human reading the sidebar's target input - holding a target it was told is set, for a module that can never be built (it survives neither a reload nor a regeneration). **The CLIENT half of that refusal is required too, or the fix stops one layer short** (`LiveNotebook.svelte` `setExportTargetValue`): the write is optimistic and the route emits `notebook:export-target` only on SUCCESS - which this tab would echo-suppress anyway - so a swallowed 400 left the input showing the rejected path and the export bar reading as configured over metadata holding nothing, i.e. the same shown-a-setting-that-does-nothing failure the refusal exists to prevent. It reads `res.ok`, REVERTS and sends the server's message to the shell's transient notice channel (`onNotice`, the one the delete refusals already use - never a second surface). **The target input commits ONCE PER EDIT, on its `change` (a blur after typing, or Enter) - never per keystroke, and that is what keeps the revert from fighting the typist.** Writing per keystroke was harmless only while the route could refuse nothing: with real refusals it made EVERY character of a bad path (an absolute one, a `..`, a non-`.py`) 400, revert the state-driven field, move the caret and raise another notice - and the debounce, generation guard and pending-value mirror stacked to contain that were themselves a race (a superseded-but-successful write never advanced the confirmed baseline, so a clear typed inside the window was dropped while the server kept regenerating the old target). One commit per edit removes all of it: `change` does not fire on an unmodified blur, so clicking through the field writes nothing. **`change` is not reliably delivered before UNLOAD, though**, so the field also flushes an uncommitted value on `pagehide` (`Notebook.svelte`'s `flushExportTarget`, the idiom `Cell.svelte` already uses for its debounced edit, with `keepalive` there): without it a target typed and then reloaded or tab-closed on was simply lost. **It deliberately does NOT flush on TEARDOWN, unlike its `Cell.svelte` model** - `LiveNotebook` renders `Notebook` behind an `{:else if fetching}` gate, so every `load()` refetch destroys it (an SSE reconnect, a seq gap from an agent's edit, `notebook:restored`, a refused bulk op), and a teardown commit therefore fired MID-EDIT from a background event the user never caused: a half-typed path raised a spurious refusal, or one that happened to parse was silently persisted and the module regenerated to a stray target. Losing an uncommitted value to a refetch is the accepted, far better half of that trade - do not "fix" it with new machinery to preserve it. It writes only while the input still diverges from the model, so the per-edit commit model is untouched. **THE TAB KEEPS NO COPY OF THE SERVER'S TARGET, and that is the design - do not reintroduce one.** EVERY `set-target` reply carries **`target` = the value the document HOLDS once the call is done** (which is why the route's refusals are RETURNED as a json 400 rather than thrown through `error()`, whose body is a bare `{message}`), and the field simply ADOPTS it on every outcome the server answered at all - a success (so a normalized absolute path is taken up too), a refusal (so the revert goes back to what the server really holds), and a failed WRITE (where the document already holds the new path, so the field keeps it and the two cannot diverge). This replaced two client-side mirrors - the last value the server confirmed and the last value SENT - which each needed a rule to stay agreeing with each other and with the server, and every rule had its own hole: a failed write left the two describing different paths, so retyping the previous one hit the skip-check and wrote NOTHING while the server kept the newer one (and, since `change` does not fire when the value at blur equals the value at focus, the documented retry was unreachable); and a background refetch resolving inside a commit window seeded both staler than the write in flight, so that write neither advanced them nor adopted the stored value. There is therefore **no skip-check** - a redundant identical POST is the accepted price, the setter being idempotent and byte-identical - and `load()`/the SSE event just show what they were handed. **The ONE comparison left is against the FIELD, not against any memory of server state** (`exportTarget !== next` ⇒ `superseded`, checked BEFORE anything is written back, the success path included): while a write is on the wire the value can move on - the user typing again, another tab's or an agent's `notebook:export-target`, a refetch - and writing our reply back would discard state that write knows nothing about. **`exportPy()` AWAITS the in-flight commit** (`exportTargetCommit`) before the manual "Export to .py" POST: pressing the button blurs the input, so the edit is committed, but that POST is still on the wire - without the await a freshly typed target exported against the previous one, reporting "Set a target .py path first." while the field visibly showed the new path. **A commit that did NOT LAND ABORTS the export**: that path was never (verifiably) stored, so exporting on ran against the previous/absent target and its outcome REPLACED that commit's own reason on the single nonce-keyed channel - typing `utils.txt` and pressing Export answered "Set a target .py path first", telling the user to do exactly what they had just tried. **`commitExportTarget` therefore reports FIVE outcomes, never a boolean** (`TargetCommit`), because one flag conflated three of them and each conflation was its own defect: `committed` (the field adopts the target the reply reports as stored), `refused` (the server rejected the path - adopt the target it reports still holding + say why), `unreachable` (the request landed no verdict we can READ - the server was unreachable, or something other than this route answered: a proxy 502/503, an HTML error page - so nothing is claimed and the field is left alone, since reading it as a success left the field showing a path the server may never have stored while the export ran on to report "Exported N cells → \<the OLD target\>" over it), and `superseded` (the field moved on - another tab's/an agent's `notebook:export-target`, or a refetch - so a newer value owns it: touch NOTHING, and **do not abort the export**, which turned the button into a dead control that issued no request and rendered no message). The superseded check runs BEFORE anything is written back. **`writeFailed` is the fifth, and it is the ONE failure that KEEPS the value rather than reverting it**: `setExportTarget` (both in `notebook.ts` and at the UI route) VALIDATES BEFORE IT MUTATES, so a throw after that point is the `persist` - a disk failure (EACCES/ENOSPC, a read-only checkout) over a path that was never wrong and that the live document already HOLDS and will write with its next successful save. Both surfaces therefore tell the two apart by TYPE (`InvalidExportTargetError`, thrown only where the path is checked), never by matching the message text: MCP answers `writeFailed` instead of `invalid`, and the UI route answers a **5xx carrying `writeFailed`** instead of the refusal's 400 - collapsed into that 400 the tab took its refusal branch, reverting the field and telling the user the target was not set over a change that DID take, with nothing left to correct it (the success event is never emitted, and this tab would echo-suppress it anyway). **The client decides it from that FLAG, never from the status code**: any OTHER 5xx (a proxy 502/503, an HTML error page) landed no verdict at all, so treating a status as the signal told the user the target was "accepted but not saved" over a request the server may never have received - the same false acceptance `unreachable` exists to prevent, reached through the other door. It keeps the field (adopting the `target` the reply reports, which on this path is the new one the document holds), says only that it could not be SAVED, and does **not** abort the manual export (the document holds the target, so the export runs against the path the field shows and reports its own failure if the disk is still broken). **The abort is scoped to a commit still IN FLIGHT**, which is what makes it belong to the blur-then-click interaction and nothing else: the promise DROPS ITSELF the moment it settles (and is CONSUMED by `exportPy` on top, so one refusal cannot abort two exports), because a settled refusal left in `exportTargetCommit` silently aborted the NEXT manual export - one that would have run fine against the stored target - and produced NO message at all, since that export never issued a request to report on, so a dead click read as an ordinary failure. **The manual export reports the SERVER's reason** through that same `onNotice` channel, from BOTH entry points - `Notebook.svelte` renders NO inline string for a failure and `+page.svelte`'s navbar item adds none either, since that channel is single and nonce-keyed, so a generic string posted after the reason simply REPLACES it - the refusals this path can hit (the not-a-generated-module clobber guard, a non-`.py` or escaping target) exist for their actionable message, so a bare "Export failed." was both a second surface and a less informative one. **The navbar item also does NOT pre-emptively `clearNotice()`**, for the same reason: opening the app menu BLURS the target input, so its `change` commit has usually already SETTLED and posted its refusal reason by the time the item is clicked, and clearing on click erased a message the user had not read yet (the in-flight case survived it only by accident of ordering). Nothing needs clearing - every branch there, and every failure path inside `LiveNotebook.exportPy`, ends in exactly one message that REPLACES whatever was up. The AUTO-ON-SAVE failure still has no UI home (it is recorded as `lastExportError` and read only by MCP); the manual button is the human's route to it. The per-cell UI toggle in `Cell.svelte` is the ONE surface still gated only on logical code type, deliberately deferred. `setCellExports` also guards its persist with `doc.jpFormat` like `clearOutputsForCells` (the pre-existing UI PATCH path reaches it directly), while the `cell:export` events still fire so open tabs update. **A refusal echoes the handle the AGENT supplied**, not the full UUID `resolveMany` expanded it to - an id absent from its own call reads as the tool answering about some other cell. **The regeneration is REPORTED, never promised** (`moduleWarning`), and BOTH ways it can fail to happen are covered - because `module` is CONDITIONAL, so its ABSENCE is what says the module was written. (a) The write THREW: `autoExportPy` is best-effort by design (a bad target must never break the notebook save), so a target whose parent is a file, an EACCES or an ENOSPC left the module unwritten while the result claimed nothing at all, and there is no MCP export tool through which an agent could ever learn otherwise. It is not fixed by making `persist` throw nor by re-running the export as a second path: `autoExportPy` RECORDS the failure on the doc (`lastExportError`, refreshed on every persist so a later success clears it) and `moduleFailure` reads it back, i.e. reports the attempt the persist ACTUALLY made. It is a STATE, not an attribution - `lastExportError` is doc state and an idempotent call (every cell already at the requested value) skips the persist entirely, so the failure may belong to an EARLIER write; the agent-facing `reason` is worded for that, saying what is on disk and never that this call caused it. **`setExportTarget` shares that half and ONLY that half** - it regenerates too, so a throw there must not read as a success, but "no cell is marked" is the normal state right after naming a target and warning about it would restate the caller's own step. Its path is also VALIDATED at the setter (`notebook.ts` `setExportTarget` runs the exporter's own `resolveInWorkspace`, so a target escaping the workspace is refused as `invalid` rather than stored to generate nothing on every later save). **A refused PATH and a failed WRITE are told apart by TYPE, never by matching the message** - the setter validates BEFORE it mutates, so its only later throw is the `persist`, and it throws an `InvalidExportTargetError` for every path refusal: `service.ts` answers `invalid` for that one and `writeFailed` for the rest, because a disk failure (EACCES, ENOSPC, a read-only checkout) leaves the live document HOLDING the target, so rendering the invalid-path remedy there sent the caller to fix a path that was never wrong, over a change that did take. **What that setter STORES is the workspace-RELATIVE form, whatever it was given, and normalizing at the one validate-and-store site is what makes every caller portable**: `resolveInWorkspace` accepts an ABSOLUTE path that happens to resolve inside THIS workspace, so an absolute target stored verbatim landed in the COMMITTED `.ipynb` contradicting every description of the field and, on any other checkout, threw from the best-effort `autoExportPy` - whose record has no UI home - so the module silently never regenerated again for that clone. An absolute path OUTSIDE the workspace is still refused, unchanged. **A write endpoint reports the value it PERSISTED, never the one it was handed**, so the setter RETURNS the stored form and the UI route answers with it (the MCP result already read the effective target back through `exportTargetFields`): reporting the raw request left the tab recording an absolute path as its baseline and showing it in the input while the document held the relative one, with the `notebook:export-target` event that would have corrected it echo-suppressed in that very tab. **That setter ALSO refuses a target that is not a `.py` file, and `exportNotebookToPy` refuses to OVERWRITE a file it did not generate - two halves of ONE guard, and both are load-bearing because the exporter WRITES a whole file to that path.** `set_cell_export` completed the chain an agent needs: name any workspace file as the target, mark one cell, and that file is destroyed in a single turn. The extension check rejects nothing legitimate (the field is documented everywhere as the nbdev module path) but cannot cover a `#|default_exp` directive, which `resolveTarget` turns into a target without passing any setter, nor a `.py` path that is a hand-written module - so the write site tests the file ALREADY on disk for the `HEADER` first line (`isGeneratedModule`; every generated module opens with it, so this can never reject one Cellar wrote) and throws otherwise, which auto-on-save records as `lastExportError` (never breaking the notebook save) and the manual button surfaces directly. An existing file that cannot be READ is refused too, saying only that - it was not verified as ours, which is not the same as knowing it is not. **An EMPTY file (zero bytes or whitespace only) is OVERWRITTEN, not refused**: the guard protects CONTENT and there is none, while pre-creating the module (`touch utils.py`, the explorer's "New file") before naming the target is an ordinary workflow that otherwise stopped the module regenerating on every later save - permanently and silently, since the auto-on-save failure has no UI home. **Known limitation, recorded at the write site rather than fixed there:** `resolveInWorkspace` resolves LEXICALLY (no `realpath`), so a DANGLING `*.py` symlink inside the workspace is followed and the module lands outside the root - bounded (an existing destination is still caught by the header check, so nothing is destroyed), shared by every file writer in the app, and therefore a fix belonging in that one helper (`fstree.ts`). (b) Nothing is marked: `exportNotebookToPy` returns early with `no-cells`, so unmarking the LAST marked cell neither rewrites nor removes the previously generated module and an `import` of it still resolves the symbol just unmarked. Deleting or truncating a git-tracked, nbdev-committed file is deliberately NOT done (the behavior is shared with the UI toggle), so the RESULT says so instead - a conditional `module:{regenerated:false, reason}` on that case ALONE, the `undo`-on-`clear_outputs` shape, so an ordinary call pays no tokens for it - and the tool description states the regeneration conditionally. Do not re-add an unconditional "regenerates immediately" claim: that is the one case where it is false, and that includes **INSTRUCTIONS clause 5**, which frames both descriptions and is delivered before either (a source guard in the unit test keeps it from regrowing there). **The reason states only what was VERIFIED**, which takes `export-py.ts`'s `generatedModuleExists`: the gate is "a target, and nothing marked", which is equally true when nothing was EVER marked and no module was ever generated (a target just set, or repointed to a fresh path), so claiming the file "was left on disk" there invents one and invites a manual delete of nothing - the same assert-more-than-was-verified defect with the sign flipped; a nonexistent module reads "no module was generated at <path>", and neither wording says the marks were dropped "any more" (the gate sees state, never a transition). That check is **PROVENANCE, not mere existence, through the SAME `isGeneratedModule` header test the write site refuses on** - the two halves must agree about what "the module" is: the setter only requires a `.py` path inside the workspace, so a target may name a HAND-WRITTEN module, and a bare `existsSync` reported the user's own source file as the leftover module and invited deleting it - advice that destroys work, aimed at exactly the file the sibling clobber guard exists to protect. A file present but not ours (or unreadable, hence unverified) reads as no module of ours. **(c) The call WROTE NOTHING and no module is on disk** - `lastExportError` is doc state THIS PROCESS may never have written, so an idempotent call (every addressed cell already at the requested value) skips the persist and, on a freshly opened doc whose module was since deleted or replaced outside Cellar, emitted no `module` field at all, which under the conditional contract reads as a regeneration. So `moduleWarning` takes a `wrote` flag (`setCellExports`' changed-ids count) and consults `generatedModuleExists` ONLY on the no-write path - a call that really wrote either succeeded, so the module is there, or threw, which (a) reports - deciding from what is on disk rather than from a record nobody wrote, and keeping the read off the ordinary write path. **Its remedy may only name tools the CALLER has**: there is no MCP tool that exports the module (`export_html` is a different artifact and the manual button is a human surface), so "export the notebook" sent an agent to the wrong tool or to nothing - it names editing a marked cell, or re-calling `set_export_target` with the same path, which persists and therefore regenerates. **`export_target` in the result - and in `get_notebook_map`'s `display` block - is the EFFECTIVE target** (`service.ts`'s one `exportTargetFields` → `notebook.ts` `effectiveExportTarget` → `resolveTarget`, the EXPORTER's own rule reused, never a second resolution): reading `metadata.cellar.export_target` alone reported `export_target:null` over a `#|default_exp`-targeted notebook whose marks really do build a module ("my marks land nowhere"), and, since `moduleWarning` short-circuits on a null target, it also silenced the stale-module warning in exactly the case that field exists for. A directive-derived target carries `export_target_source:'default_exp'` (conditional, so the ordinary call pays nothing) precisely so it is never read as the notebook SETTING - a directive lives in a cell, so `set_export_target(null)` cannot clear it; `getExportTarget` stays the metadata-only reader that setter writes, and both tool descriptions plus `get_notebook_map`'s name the flag, since an agent cannot otherwise tell a clearable setting from a non-clearable directive. **`resolveTarget`'s directive scan is guarded by a `includes('default_exp')` substring pre-check** (the `dataframeHtml.ts` `/dataframe/i` precedent): the map short-circuits only when a notebook-level target IS set, so the COMMON case - no export configured at all - would otherwise run a multiline regex over every code cell's full source on the most frequently called agent read tool. Known limit: `__all__` misses dynamically-created names + augmented assignments (documented in `export-py.ts`). Pure logic covered by `tests/unit/export-py.test.ts`; the agent surface by `tests/unit/mcp-set-cell-export.test.ts`; the target contract - both refusals, the UI route's 400, and a SOURCE guard on the client revert+notice (vitest runs without the SvelteKit plugin, so `LiveNotebook.svelte` cannot be mounted there) - by `tests/unit/mcp-export-target.test.ts`.
- **Cell staleness + dependency graph.** A code cell that ran this session but whose inputs changed since is **stale** - the flagship correctness signal for an agent-first notebook (cells run out of order). Three layers: (1) **dataflow** `src/lib/server/dataflow.js` runs a Python **`ast` + `symtable`** analysis in a project-venv subprocess (`projectPython()`, like `databricks.js` - NOT the kernel and NOT a regex, for the same reasons `imports.js` avoids both: no force-boot, no queue, works with no kernel; results cached by source string) to get each cell's `{defines, uses}`. **A batch the probe fails on is NEVER cached** (a false `fresh` is the one verdict staleness must not invent), which alone is a CPU trap: a notebook whose analysis genuinely exceeds `PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` (10s, `CELLAR_DATAFLOW_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`) times out, caches nothing, and every debounced pass (load/run-end/edit/structural) resubmits the IDENTICAL batch - deterministic, so it burns ~10s and is SIGKILLed again forever, pinning a core. So a **timed-out batch BACKS OFF** (`probeBatch` in `dataflow.ts`): keyed by a signature of the SET of uncached source strings, it is not re-spawned until an **exponential window** (`CELLAR_DATAFLOW_BACKOFF_BASE_MS` 30s, doubling, capped at `CELLAR_DATAFLOW_BACKOFF_MAX_MS` 5min) elapses OR the batch content changes (a real edit adds/removes/rewrites a source ⇒ new signature ⇒ immediate re-probe), so a persistently-slow notebook converges instead of storming. While backed off (and on the timeout pass itself) those cells are reported **UNAVAILABLE**, which `computeStaleness(cells, dataflow, sid, unavailable)` renders as a conservative **`stale`** (reason "dependency analysis timed out; treated as stale") - **never a false `fresh`**. **ONLY a timed-out / backed-off batch is UNAVAILABLE** (`probeBatch` returns `unavailable:true` only then): a **non-timeout failure** (missing interpreter, spawn error, probe crash/parse failure) is deliberately NOT backed off AND NOT reported unavailable - it returns fast, burns no CPU, may resolve once the venv exists, and its cells degrade to **empty dataflow that reads as `fresh`** exactly as before this backoff (no broadening; the conservative-stale path is scoped to genuine timeouts). A **single probe per signature** is in flight at once (concurrent UI+MCP passes fold into one). Covered by `tests/unit/dataflow-timeout-backoff.test.ts`. **The split is the design, do not collapse it.** *Module scope* is an ordered **`ast` load-before-store walk**: a `Load` of a name the cell has not yet bound is a use, one it already bound is not. Order is the whole question and `symtable` cannot express it - it knows only that a name is referenced *somewhere* and assigned *somewhere*, so it cannot tell `x = x + 10` (reads upstream) from `df = read(); df.head()` (reads its own). Both rules available to it are wrong: subtracting assigned names (`uses = referenced − defined`, the rule this replaced) dropped the `x = x + 10` read and left the cell falsely `fresh`, while counting every reference over-reports the `df = read(); df.head()` idiom into a **permanent, uncleanable** stale chip (`upstream_unrun` keys off the upstream's run state, and `run_stale` only re-runs cells already stale). The walk drives on **evaluation order, not field order** (`Assign._fields` is (targets, value) but the RHS evaluates first; `For` evaluates `iter` before binding `target`), which is also what reaches `count += 1` - `symtable` reports `is_referenced()==False` for an `AugAssign` target. *Nested scopes* stay **`symtable`'s**, order-INSENSITIVE (`used_nested - defines`): a def/lambda body is DEFERRED, so "bound so far" for it is "bound anywhere in the cell", which is what keeps `def f(): return pd.x` a use of `pd` while a recursive `def fact(): fact(n-1)` gets no self-edge. Keeping `symtable` there is deliberate - global vs. local vs. free vs. `nonlocal` is exactly what it already gets right. The ast walk therefore never descends into a def/lambda body, only into the parts that DO evaluate at module scope (decorators, defaults, annotations, bases). Covered by `tests/unit/dataflow-load-before-store.test.ts`, which spawns the REAL probe - its positives and negatives fail in opposite directions and are one contract; (2) the **pure rule** `src/lib/staleness.js` (`computeStaleness(cells, dataflow, sid)`) builds the preceding-definer graph (B depends on A when B uses a name A defines; nearest preceding definer wins; acyclic, so doc order = topological order) and marks each code cell `not_run`/`fresh`/`stale`/`n/a`. A cell is stale if it was edited after it last ran (`metadata.cellar.editedAt`, a NEW runtime-only stamp set in `setSource`, stripped from disk by `clean.js`'s renamed `stripRuntimeMeta` alongside `lastRun`), or a (transitive) upstream was edited / re-ran / didn't run this session, all vs the existing `lastRun.{at,session}`. Never-run reads `not_run`, distinct from stale. **An imports-cell edit stales only the cells that depend on a binding that ACTUALLY changed, never the whole downstream** (task `cellar-import-staleness-scope-i8`). The graph is per NAME but `editedAt`/`lastRun` are per CELL, so an edge used to transmit staleness whenever its upstream was touched at all - and since an imports cell defines a name almost every cell below it uses, one added import (or agent routing re-adding a line, or a plain re-run) lit the entire notebook amber, which is how "stale" stopped carrying information for most of an agent session. The fix rests on the ONE property strong enough to be safe: a name bound by a MODULE-LEVEL import is a pure function of its import statement, so re-executing it rebinds the same `sys.modules` object. `src/lib/server/importBindings.ts` (pure) identifies each binding by `renderImport`'s canonical rendering - the SAME dedup key consolidate/routing already uses, so the two cannot drift - folds each source change into a THIRD runtime-only stamp, `metadata.cellar.importBindings` (name → `{spec, at, sinceAt, removedAt?}` = its last KNOWN-GOOD statement, when that binding last changed (`at:0`/absent = unchanged), when it first APPEARED, and - once a knowable source stops providing it - when it went; stripped by `stripRuntimeMeta` alongside `lastRun`/`editedAt`, same zero-diff AND anti-forgery reason - an ABSENT stamp reads as "unchanged"; an EMPTY map is stored as absent so an ordinary code cell ships no stamp over `cell:edited`/checkpoints). **Three write points, and all three are required**: `setSource`; `newCell` (so a cell BORN with a source - paste / split / undo-delete - stamps what it introduces; without it a pasted `import polars as pd` became the nearest preceding definer of `pd` carrying no stamps, i.e. exempted the very edge it just rebound - a false `fresh`); and **`applyCellType` on the way IN to a code cell**, the other way a cell acquires Python bindings with no edit - `newCell` stamps only a CODE cell, so one born markdown/raw with an imports-only source carries none, and converting it (an agent `add_cell` with the wrong `cell_type`, a paste) would have exempted the edge it just rebound. Seeded from an EMPTY previous source exactly as the birth stamp is, and **only where there is none to preserve**: a code→code sql toggle must not re-stamp and claim a change that did not happen, and a cell that was code before carries real history a there-and-back conversion must not discard. **The fold diffs the new source against that STORED baseline, NEVER against the previous source text**: `Cell.svelte` autosaves on a 500ms debounce, so an unparseable mid-edit snapshot (a bare `import `, the instant after a select-all) really is persisted, and stamping every name whenever a side was unknowable re-stamped ALL of them - the blanket-stale this task removes. An unknowable next source therefore FREEZES the baseline (safe: `importBindingNames` is empty for it, so that cell grants no exemption at all until it parses again); only a baseline that was never knowable stamps everything. **An EMPTIED source is the mirror case and must NOT be frozen** - it is perfectly knowable (it provides nothing), so freezing would silently swallow a genuine "delete all imports", the false `fresh` the removal ledger exists to prevent. Instead the removal is recorded but kept UNDOABLE: `spec` survives it and only `removedAt` is set, so retyping the same block after a select-all-DELETE matches the baseline, clears `removedAt` and stamps nothing (overwriting `at` instead - what this used to do - meant the retype read as "this binding just changed" and staled the notebook after a round trip that ended byte-identical). `buildDefinerGraph` now also returns `edgeNames` (per edge, the names it carries) and `staleness.ts`'s `edgeCarriesChange` exempts an edge whose names are ALL unchanged import bindings. **The exemption is a WHITELIST, so every unmodelled case keeps the old conservative behavior** rather than inventing a false `fresh`: an ordinary define, a name whose import moved, `upstream_unrun` (deliberately never gated - an upstream that did not run has nothing bound to exempt), and any cell whose bindings are UNKNOWABLE. Unknowable is decided by the tokenizer, never a regex: `importBindingSpecs` (ONE tokenizer pass feeding every check - it ran six, on every 500ms autosave of every cell) returns null unless the source is imports-only (so nothing else at module scope can shadow an import - `os = shim`, `for os in …`, `del os` - a MISSED shadow being exactly the false `fresh` this forbids) and null for any `import *`. A mixed `import os` + code cell therefore stays conservative; the imports cell is imports-only by construction, which is the case that matters. **LINE MAGICS are discounted for that structure test, like comments** - `%matplotlib inline` / `%pip install …` above the import block is one of the commonest headers there is, and without this the whole map degraded to null and the reported bug stayed unfixed for most real notebooks. The vocabulary is `magics.ts`'s own (`isBareMagicLine`, the same shape the probe's `blankLineMagics` blanks), never a second regex, and the assignment form (`files = !ls`) is not one, so it still reads as ordinary code and disqualifies. **Only magics PROVEN inert are discounted, via an ALLOWLIST** (`magics.ts`'s `INERT_LINE_MAGICS`, read through the one-pass `scanMagics`: `%matplotlib`/`%config`/`%pip`/`%conda`/`%autoreload`) - "a bare magic binds no Python name" is NOT unconditionally true, since `%run script.py` executes it IN the namespace (so `setup.py` may rebind `pd`), `%store -r name` / `%load` inject names, `%pylab` is a star import, and **`%load_ext`/`%reload_ext` run an extension's own `load_ipython_extension(ip)`, which may `ip.push({...})` - so they are NOT on the list either (the bar is proof, and `%load_ext autoreload`, the common case, already disqualifies notebook-wide; `%matplotlib inline` keeps the refinement)**. Those, and any magic not on the list, DISQUALIFY the cell, so an unrecognized or future magic fails toward conservative staling rather than a false `fresh`. **Three deliberate exceptions keep the conservative rule:** a `%%cell magic` cell (its body is not module-level Python), a name-injecting/unknown line magic (above), and **`%autoreload`** - autoreload exists precisely to make re-executing an import NON-idempotent, i.e. it retires the one premise the whole exemption rests on, so exempting its edges would be a false `fresh`. **The autoreload gate is NOTEBOOK-WIDE, not per cell** (`dataflow.ts` `analyzeDataflowDetailed`: if ANY code cell satisfies `hasAutoreloadMagic`, `imports` is omitted for EVERY cell, so no edge anywhere is exempt). Arming it is a KERNEL-global act and the ubiquitous header puts `%load_ext autoreload`/`%autoreload 2` in its OWN cell - which Cellar makes the default outcome, since `ensureImportsCell` adopts a first cell only via `isImportsOnly` and so inserts a separate magic-free imports cell above it - so the per-cell check in `importBindingSpecs` (kept as the local half) would have granted the exemption in exactly the arrangement where autoreload is most commonly used. **A REMOVED import is tracked, not forgotten** (`foldImportChange` keeps the name, its spec and a `removedAt`): removal leaves no definer, hence no edge, so `computeStaleness` carries a small in-order removal ledger and stales a reader of a name nothing defines any more - closing a pre-existing false `fresh` this refinement would otherwise have widened. That ledger needs BOTH halves: the name must be absent from the cell's CURRENT `imports` **and** carry a `removedAt` (the fold actually watched it leave a knowable source), else a mid-edit cell - which lists no imports at all - would read as "every binding here was just removed" and blanket-stale the notebook on each keystroke. **Removal records are PRUNED to the names something could actually have READ** (`pruneImportBindings`, applied wherever the fold is stored, keyed off the new `sinceAt` = first appearance, which - unlike `at` - never moves on a rebinding): a debounced autosave persists PARSEABLE intermediates (`import numpy as n` on the way to `as np`), and without the prune each minted a permanent phantom entry that grew the map for the whole session, rode every `cell:edited`/checkpoint, and made the ledger report a removal that never happened for any cell using a short name like `n`. The test is deliberately "was it bound while the notebook was running", NEVER "is it in the source now" - the latter would delete exactly the records the ledger exists for, since a genuinely deleted import is also absent from the source. **The reference is the DOCUMENT's newest `lastRun.at` (`notebook.ts` `latestConsumeAt`), never the providing cell's own stamp**: `clearLastRunStamps` (the wipe-variables route) DELETES `lastRun` from cells precisely BECAUSE they ran, so dating the prune per cell read a wiped imports cell as "never bound anything", dropped its removal records, and left a reader of a since-deleted import with no definer, no ledger entry and a false `fresh`. A reader with no stamp reads `not_run` and never reaches the ledger, so the newest stamp anywhere bounds every consumption the ledger can be asked about; when NOTHING in the notebook carries one, nothing is pruned at all (an undatable record is kept - an unbounded map is a cost, a false `fresh` is a defect). The probe-timeout-backoff path is untouched: `imports` is computed in JS with its own cache (`importCache`, cleared by `__resetDataflowState`) so it survives a probe failure, and an UNAVAILABLE cell has empty `defines` ⇒ no edges ⇒ no exemption ⇒ still conservative-stale. Known limits (in `staleness.ts`): the exemption assumes imports are idempotent, so `importlib.reload` / an import with side effects is not modelled (`%autoreload`, which breaks the same premise, IS detected - notebook-wide - and disqualifies every cell); and **the ledger covers a removal only while the cell that provided the name SURVIVES** - it is read off that cell's stamps, so DELETING the imports cell outright takes them with it and its readers (now with no definer and no ledger entry) read `fresh`. That is pre-existing (a deleted definer never produced an edge either), but do not read the in-cell ledger as covering removal in general; the same family covers an edit that drops a binding and leaves the cell unanalyzable in one step. Covered by `tests/unit/import-bindings.test.ts` (pure), `tests/unit/imports-staleness-scope.test.ts` (real probe + real `setSource` + the real `routeImports` path) and `tests/e2e/imports-staleness-scope.spec.ts` (the amber chip in the real UI, both directions). (3) **Surfaces**: server helper `getNotebookStaleness(nb)` (in dataflow.js) is shared by `GET /api/notebooks/staleness?path=` (the UI fetches it, debounced, on load/run-end/edit/structural change) and the MCP layer. UI: `LiveNotebook` fetches → `Notebook` → `Cell` renders an amber "stale" chip (own `--cellar-stale` token in app.css, distinct from the `warning`-hued running/queued affordances) + a quieter "not run" hint; navbar app-menu adds Run stale / Run cells above / Run cells below (delegated to the active notebook via the existing `onRegisterApi` registry - `runAbove`/`runBelow`/`runStale`, run sequentially in doc/dependency order). **Two always-visible bulk-run buttons reuse this same machinery, never a new execution path:** a top-of-notebook **Run all** button (`Notebook.svelte`, `data-testid=run-all`, disabled when no code cell) and a per-code-cell **Run above** toolbar button (`Cell.svelte`, `data-testid=run-above`, next to the run button, disabled on the first cell - runs every code cell ABOVE that cell, exclusive, Jupyter's "Run All Above"). Both feed `LiveNotebook`'s `runCodeIds` (Run all → `runAll`; Run above → `runAboveCell(id)`, addressed by the cell's OWN id, not the active selection) - one shared **SEQUENTIAL** bulk-run path that `await`s each `runCell` before the next, so the server-side FIFO queue serializes them and the interrupt-cancels-the-queue behavior applies automatically. **Run all's reused-kernel wedge is FIXED (task `cellar-runall-kernel-wedge-z3`), at its root, in two independent layers - do not undo either.** The OLD `runAll` fired every cell's `/run` POST at once (fire-and-forget) and relied on the FIFO to serialize them; on a reused kernel that intermittently left a trivial cell stuck "running" while later cells stayed "queued", recovered only by the ~120-210s watchdog (worse with more run-to-run transitions - ~40% at 3+). Root cause was NOT the queue but **concurrent `execute()` on ONE kernel**: the sidebar variables inspector (`inspect.ts` `inspectVariables`, fired after every run) calls `execute({internal:true})` which BYPASSES the run queue, so during a bulk run its probe put a second `requestExecute` on the wire alongside the next cell's - and two concurrent executes make @jupyterlab mis-pair the interleaved iopub `status:idle`/`execute_reply`, so one run's `future.done` never resolves. **Layer 1 (the invariant): `kernel.ts` `NotebookKernel.execChain` + `acquireExecLock()`** - every `execute()` (queued OR internal) serializes on a per-kernel promise-chain lock so no two `requestExecute` are ever on the wire at once; released in `execute()`'s `finally` (abort path included, so a watchdog-killed run can't block later executes). This alone fixes the wedge and is proven by `tests/unit/kernel-status-broadcast.test.ts` ("execute serialization"). **Layer 2 (dispatch): `runAll` routes through `runCodeIds`** (sequential, one open `/run` stream at a time) instead of fire-and-forget, so Run all can never oversubscribe the browser's ~6-connection HTTP/1.1 pool. **Layer 3 (keep the probe non-blocking): `inspectVariables` short-circuits `busy`** (mirrors `kernelState`'s guard, incl. the queue's `running` truth) so the lock never makes a probe queue behind a run - it returns `{variables:[], busy:true}` and `/api/kernel/variables` forwards `busy` so the client (`+page.svelte` `refreshVariables`) KEEPS its current variables rather than clearing them mid-run (poll, don't single-read, that endpoint in tests - it reports empty while busy). **Interrupt-cancels-queue still holds** and its e2e was reworked for the sequential model (only the running cell's stream is open, so there are no queued badges during a Run all; the `interruptGeneration` bump stops the loop + the kernel interrupt ends the running cell). Covered by `tests/e2e/run-above-run-all.spec.ts` (Run all reliable on a WARM kernel, four code cells = three transitions) + `tests/e2e/interrupt-clears-queue.spec.ts`. **Target-id selection is the pure, unit-tested seam `src/lib/runTargets.ts`** (`codeIdsAll`/`codeIdsAbove` - skip non-code cells, above-exclusive; covered by `tests/unit/run-targets.test.ts` + e2e `tests/e2e/run-above-run-all.spec.ts`); `codeIdsInRange`/`runAll` delegate to it so the rule can't drift from what the UI runs. MCP: `get_notebook_map` carries `stale_state`/`stale`/`stale_reason`/`stale_upstream` per cell, `kernel_state` carries `stale_cells:[{id,reason,upstream}]`, `read_cell`/`read_cells`/`read_by_location`/`read_section`/`run_cell` carry the same (those read tools became **async** to compute it), new **`run_stale`** tool re-runs everything stale in dependency order, and `INSTRUCTIONS` clause 3 gained a staleness doctrine (re-run/distrust stale cells; editing a cell stales its dependents) - **plus the imports-cell exception**, so an agent does not read the resulting quiet as staleness being broken: because its own write tools route imports on nearly every `add_cell`, clause 3 says an imports edit stales only the readers of a binding that actually moved, that a rebound or removed import DOES still stale its readers, and that the exception is withdrawn where re-running an import is no longer harmless - notebook-wide under `%autoreload`, or for an imports cell carrying any magic not proven inert (so "do not re-run everything to be safe"). Staleness is computed fresh each time, never persisted (zero git diff). **Staleness is static analysis + timestamps, NOT a runtime check** - `lastRun` carries `{at,durationMs,actor,status,session}`, never the names a cell read or wrote, and nothing inspects the namespace. So it is a **floor**: every blind spot in the graph is a blind spot in the verdict, and there is **no backstop** - `find_symbol`/`cell_impact` are derived from the SAME graph (`symbolGraph.ts`) and under-report identically, so never document one as catching what another missed (the false claim removed in `1812cea`). `cell_impact` additionally **OVER-reports in exactly one direction, deliberately**: it is the blast radius of the WHOLE cell asked BEFORE an edit exists, so it must assume every name the cell defines may move, while `computeStaleness` runs after the edit and exempts an edge carrying only unchanged import bindings - for an imports cell, `dependents` is "everything reading any of these imports" and the cells that actually go stale are only those reading an import the edit moved (documented in `symbolGraph.ts` + the tool description). The one runtime-derived input is `lastRun.session`: a restart bumps the epoch and correctly resets everything to `not_run` - epoch tracking, not name tracking. Known limits (documented in `staleness.ts`): dynamic names (`exec`, `globals()`), forward references, a **conditional bind** (`if flag: df = load()` then `df.head()` - the sequential walk counts it bound, so no upstream edge; no worse than what shipped before), and an **`AugAssign` to a `global`-declared name inside a function** (`def g(): global c; c += 1` - a deferred body, so it is `symtable`'s half and hits its `is_referenced` blind spot; module-scope `count += 1` IS caught). **Read-then-rebind is FIXED** (the `ast` load-before-store walk) - do not re-document it as a limit, and if you are tempted to "simplify" the ast walk back into a `symtable` subtraction, read the `dataflow.ts` header first: that subtraction is the bug.
- **SQL cells: the `-- >> name` result binding (`src/lib/server/sql.ts`).** A SQL cell (`metadata.cellar.language='sql'`, see `cellLanguage.ts`) is compiled to Python at RUN time only (`run.ts`: `sqlToPython`) and executed by the ordinary Python kernel against the live `spark`; the stored source stays SQL. **The binding syntax:** a cell may open with `-- >> sales_df` to bind its result to `sales_df`. It is a plain SQL **line comment** on purpose - the cell still parses as SQL in any other tool, survives a round-trip through plain Jupyter, the CodeMirror `sql()` grammar highlights it as a comment, and an agent reads it over MCP with no new tool. Recognized **only as the first non-blank line** (never scanned for in the body, so a `-- >>` mid-query stays an ordinary comment) and **stripped** before the query reaches `spark.sql`. **The binding rule:** a named cell binds **BOTH** `<name>` **and** `_sql_df` - `_sql_df` is documented to agents (`mcp/server.ts` clause 10) as the "last SQL result" chain-off point and that contract must keep holding, so the named var is the *stable* binding and `_sql_df` stays the (last-write-wins) alias it always was. **No prefix line ⇒ byte-for-byte the legacy output** (one assignment to `_sql_df`, no alias line) - existing notebooks are unmoved; the display expression is always `<var>.limit(SQL_ROW_CAP).toPandas()` on the LAZY Spark DF, never the capped pandas one.
