The name is interpolated into generated Python, so it is **validated, never merely quoted** (strict ASCII identifier + not a Python keyword - deliberately stricter than Python's own unicode rule - **and not a name Cellar itself binds in the kernel**: `spark`/`w` are legal identifiers, so without that reserved set `-- >> spark` would compile to `spark = spark.sql(...)` and silently destroy the live session the next cell needs); an unusable name compiles to a lone `raise RuntimeError(<why>)` so the cell **fails visibly** instead of emitting broken Python or silently ignoring the line. **`parseSqlCell` is the single source of truth for both halves** and must stay so: `sqlToPython` compiles from it, and `dataflow.ts` reads its `resultVars` to give each SQL cell a **synthetic `defines`** - SQL cells stay OUT of the probe (their source is SQL; `ast`/`symtable` would misparse it), but their run really does bind names, so without the synthetic contribution a Python cell reading a SQL result gets no upstream edge and never goes stale when the query is edited. A SQL cell is a graph **source**: `uses` is always `[]` (reading table names out of SQL is lineage analysis, deliberately out of scope). Covered by `tests/unit/sql-binding.test.ts`.
- **Every `nb` argument is resolved to an ABSOLUTE path, at every layer.** `kernel.ts`'s `kernels` Map (like `notebook.ts`'s `docs` Map) is keyed by absolute path, so a workspace-relative `nb` (`notebook.ipynb` - how the BROWSER addresses everything) must go through `resolveNotebookPath` or it silently misses every entry. `kernel.ts`'s `resolveNb` used to pass its argument through verbatim, against its own docstring: `currentSessionId('notebook.ipynb')` returned null, so `getNotebookStaleness` reconciled against "no kernel session" and reported **every cell `not_run`** - the flagship staleness signal read dead in the UI for all cells while MCP (which addresses notebooks absolutely) worked fine, so nothing pointed at the cause. A relative path also became its own Map key, i.e. a second kernel for one notebook. Pinned by `tests/unit/staleness-relative-path.test.ts`; when adding a `kernel.ts` entry point taking `nb`, route it through `resolveNb` and never key off the raw argument.
- **`src/lib/server/run.js` = the one cell-execution core.** The UI `/run` route, MCP `runCell`, and `runImportsCell` all call `executeCellRun({nb, cellId, actor, source, originId, onEvent})`: execute → persist outputs → stamp the runtime-only `lastRun` (session epoch captured from `execute()`'s `kernel` event, never re-read) → publish `run:start`/`run:output`/`run:end`. It is NOT the queue: claiming the kernel stays with each caller, because each answers a refused (`duplicate`) or dropped (`cancelled`) ticket in its own shape. `onEvent` is what the UI route forwards down its NDJSON stream. A third run path can no longer drift from the run_status doctrine.
- **Streamed-output deltas (`run:output-append`) - the wire is O(size), never O(size×ticks).** A growing coalesced stream element used to re-broadcast its **whole** buffer on every ~40ms `OutputAccumulator.flush()` tick (a full `run:output` at a stable index), so the server re-`JSON.stringify`d and fanned out the entire buffer to every tab each tick - **measured 91× traffic amplification** (audit finding K1, scout `cellar-perf-scout-b5`). Fixed by emitting **deltas**: `OutputAccumulator.flush()` sends the full element on its **first** materialization (client establishes it), and every later tick emits a `StreamDelta {base, keep, chunk}` of what changed since the last emission (`onEmit(out, index, delta?)`); `run.ts`'s `emit` publishes it as `run:output-append {index, base, keep, chunk}` (SSE) / `{type:'output-append',…}` (NDJSON), otherwise the unchanged full `run:output`. Client (`LiveNotebook.applyOutputAppend`) reconstructs `prev.slice(0, keep) + chunk`. **Reconcile with the terminal reducer** (the reduced stream is NOT append-only - a `\r`-overwrite rewrites earlier bytes): the delta is a **tail-splice**, not a pure append. A **non-terminal** stream reduces to identity and only appends, so `keep === base === prevLen` with **no scan** (the O(new bytes) fast path that kills the measured 91×); a **terminal** buffer diffs to the common prefix (`keep < base`). **`base` = the length the client's element must currently have** - a mismatch (dropped establishing frame/delta, or a reconnect refetch racing a live delta) means out-of-sync, so the client resyncs with **ONE `load()` refetch** (`applyOutputAppend` returns false → the SSE path calls `load()` guarded by `!fetching`, so a burst of mismatches triggers at most one in-flight refetch; the NDJSON initiating-tab path treats false as a no-op, it renders from its own ordered stream). **The resync backstop is the `load()` refetch, made authoritative by keeping the in-memory doc CURRENT on every flush** - `run.ts`'s `emit` calls `notebook.ts`'s `setOutputsLive(cellId, acc.outputs.slice(), nb)` (in-memory only: **no persist, no event**, mirroring `clearOutputsLive`) after each publish, so `getNotebook`/`GET /api/notebooks` returns the last-flushed outputs for a running cell. The doc is therefore authoritative at ALL times - mid-stream, in the final tail after the last delta, and at `run:end` - so a mid-run `load()` returns the current text (not empty) and STOPS firing once `base` matches again (no refetch storm). This replaced an earlier periodic-full-frame-checkpoint design: the doc-current approach is simpler (no checkpoint cadence, no counter, no constant) and covers the final-tail window a checkpoint bounded only to ≤N ticks. Persist still happens **once**, at `run:end` via `setOutputs`; `outputs` (persisted `.ipynb`) always holds the full reduced text - deltas are purely a wire optimization. An idle flush (reduced === emitted) broadcasts nothing. Covered by `tests/unit/output-accumulator.test.ts` (delta emission, append-vs-tail-splice decision, one-full-frame-per-element, the O(size) wire bound, base-guard, and byte-exact replay) + `tests/unit/notebook-model.test.ts` (`setOutputsLive` updates the doc in-memory without persisting).
- **Terminal-style stream output reduction (`src/lib/server/terminal.ts`) - two tiers, zero deps.** A cell running a CLI (tqdm, spinners, `uv pip install`, any progress bar) emits hundreds of `\r`-overwrite / cursor-repaint frames + ANSI escapes; raw, a `<pre>` stacks every frame and the same pile is persisted + read by the agent. `terminal.ts` is a **pure, zero-dep** reducer with a cheap gate `isTerminalStyle(text)` (has `\r` or CSI `ESC[`) and two tiers: **(1) `reduceCheap(text)`** - strip SGR color, then collapse `\r` overwrites per physical line with **true** terminal semantics (`abcdefghij\rXYZ` → `XYZdefghij`, tail survives; a `\r` never crosses a `\n`). Fixes single-line bars/spinners; **cannot undo a VERTICAL repaint** and leaves non-SGR escapes in place. **(2) `reduceFull(text)`** - a minimal **VT screen emulator** (line-array screen + `(row,col)` cursor) that applies the **BOUNDED escape set** and collapses the multi-line cursor repaint of `uv pip install` / multi-bar tqdm to its final rendered screen. The bounded set (enumerated from a real `uv` PTY capture): `\r`, `\n` (ONLCR - resets column, like the cheap tier), `\b`, SGR `m` (stripped), erase-in-line `K` (0/1/2), erase-in-display `J` (0/1/2/3), cursor up/down `A`/`B`, forward/back `C`/`D`, column-absolute `G`, absolute position `H`/`f`. **Every OTHER CSI, private-marker CSI (`ESC[?…`), OSC (`ESC]…`), and non-CSI ESC is a defensive DROP** - never printed as garbage; an **incomplete trailing escape is held back** (dropped this pass) so a sequence split across a flush boundary resolves on the next flush's whole-buffer re-reduce. Render = join rows, each right-trimmed of trailing blanks, trailing blank rows dropped (the "final screen" a terminal-capture tool shows). A full-screen TUI / alternate-screen program is out of scope by design. **A huge cursor escape is bounded, NOT honored** - because `reduceFull` re-reduces the WHOLE raw buffer on every flush, an unclamped cursor move (`ESC[9999999B`, `ESC[1;9999999H`) would append millions of empty rows/cells per ~10-byte escape, repeatedly; so the cursor row/col and every screen allocation are clamped to `MAX_ROWS` (100k) / `MAX_COLS` (10k - tighter, because a far move followed by a write PADS the gap with real space cells while an empty row is nearly free), both orders of magnitude beyond any real capture (uv/tqdm/colored logs stay byte-identical). **The pipeline uses `reduceFull`** (it supersedes the cheap tier for stream output); `reduceCheap` stays exported + independently tested. **Hooked at the single chokepoint** - `OutputAccumulator.flush()` keeps `pending.text` RAW (coalescing + byte caps + the emulator's whole-buffer re-reduce all depend on the full reassembled text) and emits the reduced COPY, so persist (`.ipynb`), the SSE broadcast, the agent MCP read, and the render all collapse to the final screen at once; `finish()` reduces the tail via the same `flush()`. **Coordinates with the streaming-delta protocol:** a full-tier reduction can rewrite an EARLIER line, so the delta is a tail-splice from the first divergence (`commonPrefixLen` → `keep < base`), reconstructed as `prev.slice(0,keep)+chunk` and resync-guarded by `base` (the existing delta layer already handles arbitrary rewrites - no change needed). **`reduceFull` is verified byte-identical against an independent VT emulator (`pyte`, ONLCR-applied)** on a battery of cases + a genuine 18KB `uv pip install` PTY capture committed at `tests/unit/fixtures/uv-pip-install.{raw,final}.txt`. Design: scout report `cellar-terminal-output-scout-t4`. Covered by `tests/unit/terminal.test.ts` (both tiers, bounded set, graceful degradation) + the reduction + chunked-delta-replay cases in `output-accumulator.test.ts`.
- **Notebook checkpoints (snapshot / undo).** `src/lib/server/checkpoints.js` snapshots a notebook's full cells (source + outputs + metadata, deep-cloned) to a restorable checkpoint. **Storage** mirrors `ui-state.js`: a single `.cellar/checkpoints.json` (gitignored, per-project, port-independent), keyed by workspace-relative path, bounded to 25/notebook (FIFO); outputs included but a snapshot over 2 MB drops them (`outputsTruncated`). Runtime metadata (`lastRun`/`editedAt`/`importBindings`) rides in the snapshot (ephemeral `.cellar`, never the `.ipynb`) so a restore reproduces run-status **and** staleness; the epoch check keeps `ran_this_session` honest, and the restored import-binding baseline keeps an imports-cell edit scoped to its real dependents (a restored stamp predating `sinceAt` reads as 0, so its removal record is kept). **Triggers:** a **manual** "Checkpoint now" (trigger `manual`) and an **automatic** pre-action snapshot before any AGENT mutation/run (trigger `agent`), **coalesced** so a burst of agent activity yields ONE pre-burst checkpoint (a 4 s quiet gap starts a new burst) - `autoCheckpointBeforeAgentAction(nb)` is called at the top of the MCP `service.js` mutating/running tools (`addCells`/`editCell`/`removeCells`/`clearOutputs`/`moveCell`/`setType`/`consolidate`/`runCell`; `addAndRun`/`runCells`/`runAll` compose these). **Restore** replaces the live doc via `notebook.js` `replaceCells` (deep-clones, persists clean-on-save → `.ipynb` git-clean, publishes `notebook:restored` so every tab refetches via `LiveNotebook.load()`); it snapshots the pre-restore state first (trigger `restore`) so restore is itself undoable. `undoLastAgentAction` restores the newest `agent` checkpoint - the headline "undo last agent action" flow (which is why the agent-facing `create_checkpoint` tool tags its snapshots `manual`, not `agent`, so a deliberate agent save point is never what undo reverts to). **Surfaces:** routes `GET/POST /api/checkpoints` (create / restore / undo-agent); sidebar **History** section (`Checkpoints.svelte`, key `history` in `sidebarSections.js`) lists checkpoints (relative time + trigger badge) with inline-confirm Restore + an Undo button; navbar app-menu "Checkpoint now" / "Undo last agent action"; MCP `create_checkpoint`. The panel refetches on a `checkpoints:changed` global event. Restore reverts the DOCUMENT, not the kernel namespace (re-run to sync; staleness flags it).
- **Interactive DataFrame grid.** A pandas DataFrame/Series output renders as a sortable/filterable/paginated data grid instead of the static text/HTML repr. **Kernel-side** (`kernel.js` `DATAFRAME_FORMATTER_CODE`, injected by `initKernel` alongside the matplotlib magic, re-run on every start/restart): registers an IPython `BaseFormatter` (`_return_type = (dict, str)` - the default `str` rejects a dict payload) for the custom mimetype `application/vnd.cellar.dataframe+json`, emitting `{columns, dtypes, index, index_name, data, total_rows, total_cols, shown_rows, shown_cols, truncated_rows, truncated_cols}`. Bounded at MAX_ROWS=500 × MAX_COLS=100 so a huge frame never lands in the output (nor the DOM). Values come from pandas' own `to_json(orient='split', date_format='iso', default_handler=str)` - deterministic (NaN→null, dates→ISO, numpy→native), so an identical re-run yields an identical payload (zero git diff). All guarded: no pandas / old IPython = silent no-op; pandas' text/plain + text/html reprs are untouched, so a bare `df` degrades anywhere the mimetype isn't understood. Spark DataFrames are deliberately **not** auto-collected (a bare `df` must not trigger a hidden job) - use `.toPandas()` / the Databricks table preview. **Persistence:** `clean.js` `cleanOutput` **strips** the mimetype on the way to disk, so it is purely a live render - kept in the in-memory doc (open tabs show the grid, SSE renders it live across tabs) but never bloats the `.ipynb`; a reopened-not-rerun notebook shows the text/plain fallback. **Frontend:** `Cell.svelte` `renderOutput` detects the mimetype (before the image branch) → `{dataframe}`; `src/lib/DataFrameGrid.svelte` renders it (dtype headers, click-to-sort cycling none→asc→desc with a numeric-aware compare, global filter box, page-size + first/prev/next/last pagination, row/col counts, truncation banner, index column, sticky header, overflow scroll). The grid owns its own height/scroll, so `Cell.svelte` skips the outer output-scroll auto-contraction + hides its toggle when `hasDataframe` (else it double-scrolls and clips the pager). Styled with daisyUI semantic classes so both themes are clean for free. **The row `{#each}` is keyed by the row's POSITION (`row.key`, the `.map()` index), NEVER by the pandas index label (`row.idx`) - and that is a correctness rule, not a style choice.** A pandas index carries no uniqueness guarantee: `set_index()` on a column with repeated values, `groupby().apply()`, `concat`, `explode`, `melt`, and a flattened MultiIndex (whose parts `dataframeHtml.ts` joins with `' / '`) all produce duplicate labels routinely. A duplicated key throws Svelte's `each_key_duplicate` **during render**, and because **nothing in the app wraps a cell in an error boundary** (`grep -rn "svelte:boundary" src/` finds none) that uncaught error takes down the WHOLE notebook's render tree, not just this grid - it is the single root cause behind four separate-looking failures, all reproduced on a real notebook: with windowing ON the render loop dies the instant the offending cell scrolls into the window, so everything below it stays permanently blank (a deterministic "gap" in the same place every time, surviving a restart) and arrow-key navigation and the jump-to-running-cell spinner stop working with it (`ensureCellMounted` can never materialize a node, and both `scrollCellIntoView` and `revealRunning` then take a SILENT no-op branch - which is why a render crash presented as "the arrow keys are stuck"); with windowing OFF ("Render all cells") it throws at load and **nothing renders at all**. Positions are unique by construction and stable under the grid's own filter/sort, which reorder rows but never change a row's identity. Note this is emphatically NOT a virtualization bug - a 3-cell notebook reproduces it - so do not go looking in `planWindow`/the height cache; windowing only changes WHEN the bad cell mounts, and therefore where the damage shows. Covered by `tests/unit/dataframe-grid.test.ts` (the real parser proving ordinary pandas output really does carry duplicate labels, plus a source guard on the key - vitest runs without the SvelteKit plugin, so the component cannot be mounted there) + `tests/e2e/dataframe-duplicate-index.spec.ts` (both modes over a committed fixture; **its load-bearing assertion is that ZERO page/console errors are raised** - the throw's blast radius varies by where in the flush it lands, so a "the cells are visible" check alone passes against the bug on some runs).
- **Rich `text/html` output tables get comfortable defaults, at LOW specificity (`src/lib/htmlOutputStyle.ts` → `HtmlOutput.svelte` `buildSrcdoc`).** A pandas **Styler** (`id="T_…"`) and any arbitrary `IPython.display.HTML` table render inside the sandboxed output iframe, whose srcdoc wrapper Cellar owns; `app.css` cannot reach in (no `allow-same-origin`), so the browser's 1px cell padding was all a bare table got - which is why users hand-rolled a `set_table_styles` helper (7px 20px padding, right-aligned data, left-aligned row headings, a styled caption) and called it on **every** table. `OUTPUT_HTML_CSS` now supplies exactly that as the default: `7px 20px` cells, right-aligned data with `tbody th` (the index) left, hairline row separators + a header rule (from `thead th`, so only where the table declares a `<thead>` - see the known limits below), `set_caption` as a left-aligned 600-weight title, `tabular-nums`, and `border:0` so pandas' `border="1"` presentational grid is replaced rather than layered under. **Scope, stated as it really is: these are defaults for EVERY `<table>` in a rich `text/html` output, not for pandas-shaped tables alone** - the reach was measured, not overlooked. A third-party `_repr_html_` (statsmodels' `summary()` → `<table class="simpletable">`, dask's array summary, a folium/bokeh legend table) gets the same padding, the same hairline row separators, and the same inherited right-align wherever its cells hold bare inline text and so take no `:has()` escape (the known limit below). The padding is a universal scannability win and dropping the default border is precisely what declutters a bare table; every default is overridable by explicit CSS or a pandas Styler, which is what the specificity rule guarantees. `border:0` also outranks the deprecated `border=` presentational attribute (author CSS always beats a presentational hint), so a hand-written `display(HTML('<table border="1">…'))` loses that grid - restore it with explicit CSS (a `style` attribute or a stylesheet rule the output carries), and reach for explicit CSS or a Styler rather than `border=` when a specific grid is wanted. **Two narrowings are REJECTED, do not reintroduce them:** carving `border:0` back to preserve `border=` buys one legacy case for a permanently more complicated rule, and scoping the block to pandas-shaped tables needs a class or id selector, i.e. exactly the specificity problem the rule below exists to close. **The specificity rule is the whole contract, not a style choice:** every selector stays element-level (`table`, `th,td`, `thead th`, `tbody th`, `caption`), because a Styler emits `#T_xxxx td{…}` and `set_properties` emits per-cell `#T_xxxx_row0_col0` - so anything the user states explicitly still wins the cascade, reproducing their helper's `overwrite=False`. Adding one class or id here would silently start overriding user styling with nothing else failing, so `tests/unit/html-output-style.test.ts` fails the build on a `#`/`.`/`[` in any selector - and on an `!important` anywhere, which defeats the same contract by another route with every selector still element-level (it also guards the `allow-scripts`-without-`allow-same-origin` sandbox, mirroring `html-preview.test.ts`). **`text-align:right` is declared on `table`, NOT on `th,td`, precisely so a user's table-level rule wins - do not move it onto the cells.** An inherited value is used only where no declaration applies to the element itself, so a cell rule would outrank ANY value the user sets on an ancestor, and `set_table_styles([{'selector': '', …}])` - the standard Styler idiom for aligning a whole table - emits exactly that (`#T_xxxx{text-align:left}` on the table element); declared on `table` the two meet on one element, the id wins, and it inherits down. **That governs the DATA alignment and GENERALIZES through ONE mechanism: a DIRECT declaration of an INHERITED property beats an inherited value whatever the specificity, so every such declaration on a NON-`table` element sits outside the reach of the whole-table Styler idiom** (`set_table_styles([{'selector':'','props':…}])` → `#T_xxxx{…}`, which moves the data cells but not these). The complete list: `tbody th{text-align:left}` (the index reads as a label - the pandas convention), `caption{text-align:left}` (a caption reads as a title, and one that INHERITED would pick up the table's numeric right-align, which is worse than what it costs), `caption{font-weight:600}` for the same title-not-datum reason, and `th{color}`/`caption{color}` (the darker heading ink). Each stays overridable through the id-scoped escape hatch (`#T_xxxx th{color:…}` / `#T_xxxx caption`), so the user-always-wins contract holds **literally, not merely nearly** - these are documented exceptions to ONE idiom, never unbeatable rules. Keep them direct - the last direct `text-align` is the `:has()` escape below, for the same beat-the-inherited-default reason. **Two of those are NOT reach Cellar took away, so do not re-raise them as regressions:** the UA sheet already declares `th{font-weight:bold}` and `caption{text-align:center}` directly, so a table-level rule never reached either property in the first place - Cellar's `th{font-weight:600}`/`caption{text-align:left}` change what they look like, not what can override them; the genuinely new ones are `th{color}`, `caption{color}` and `caption{font-weight}`. **The table also stays a REAL table** (no `display:block`, which would wrap the rows in an anonymous `width:auto` table box and leave a user's `<table width="100%">` / `table-layout:fixed` applying but doing nothing, and no `display:block` on `caption` either, which on a `display:table` parent would get it wrapped in an anonymous cell, i.e. rendered as a row) **and carries NO `max-width`** - which is why `table` was dropped from the pre-existing `img,svg,canvas,table{max-width:100%}` rule (the `img,svg,canvas` half is unrelated and stays). It reads like an obvious safety net, and it is the ONE declaration a user could not override: their `width:1500px` is a DIFFERENT property, so no amount of id specificity beats a `max-width` and their columns are silently squeezed. It also buys nothing - auto table layout already sizes an auto-width table as `max(min-content, min(max-content, available))`, so it fits its container unaided; it cannot prevent min-content overflow anyway; and a genuinely wide table overflowing into the output document's horizontal scroll is accepted by design here. Cells therefore **wrap normally** (the pandas/Jupyter default), so a long text column reads instead of scrolling; a genuinely wide table - many numeric columns, which cannot wrap - overflows and scrolls the output iframe's OWN document. The app page must still never gain a horizontal scrollbar from an output; it cannot, since the iframe is fixed-width and clips. **That deliberate document-level overflow is why the srcdoc's height reporter is `reportedHeight()` in `src/lib/htmlOutputHeight.ts`** (a pure function serialized into the srcdoc with `toString()`, since the reporter cannot import): a bare `scrollHeight` excludes the horizontal scrollbar, so where the platform paints a classic space-taking bar (Windows/Linux) a frame sized to the content exactly loses ~15px to it and grows a VERTICAL scrollbar that clips the last row. The gutter is `window.innerHeight - documentElement.clientHeight` (the viewport including vs excluding a rendered bar), added only when `scrollWidth > clientWidth` - **never re-derived from the root's own `offsetHeight`**, which is the `<html>` box's content height, not the viewport, so the difference grows with the content and, since the parent feeds the reported height back as the frame height, makes the measurement oscillate. macOS overlay scrollbars measure 0px, so the local e2e cannot see this at all - it is guarded by unit-testing the ARITHMETIC, deliberately not an observed pixel gap. **The one non-obvious rule is `td:has(p,div,ul,…)`**: the same iframe renders arbitrary HTML where a `<table>` is sometimes two-column *layout*, and there the table's right-align inherits into each prose cell and ragged-rights a paragraph. A cell holding block-level content is a container, not a datum - a pandas cell only ever holds text - so those cells take start alignment back, deliberately as a DIRECT rule since it exists to beat the table-level default it would otherwise inherit; `:has()` takes the specificity of its most specific argument, so with only type selectors inside it stays element-level. **Known limit, accepted deliberately (do NOT add a heuristic, and do NOT grow the allowlist):** that escape is a HARDCODED ALLOWLIST of block tags, not a general block-level test, so it misfires BOTH ways - it UNDER-reaches a layout table whose prose cells use a block element not on the list (`blockquote`/`section`/`figure`/`article`/`dl`) or hold bare inline text at all (statsmodels' `summary()`, dask's array summary, pint, awkward), which read right-aligned (the alignment half of the scope note above), and it OVER-reaches a genuine data cell that wraps its value in a `div`/`pre` (xarray/dask-style nested reprs), which loses the numeric right-align this block exists to supply; both are cosmetic and fully overridable (the specificity rule guarantees an explicit user or library rule wins), so the allowlist stays as-is - adding the missing tags would only widen the under-reach half and leave the over-reach half - and widening the signal with a content-length rule was REJECTED because it misfires in both directions too (a long id wrongly left-aligned, a short label still right-aligned); right-align is the pandas numeric convention, and any library can style its own table. **Same class, same verdict: a table that declares no `<thead>`** - the HTML parser auto-inserts `<tbody>` but NEVER `<thead>`, so those header `<th>`s land in the inserted tbody, match `tbody th{text-align:left}` and read as index labels (left-aligned while the data below stays right-aligned), taking the plain `th,td` hairline instead of the darker header rule; statsmodels' `summary().as_html()` is exactly that shape, so it gets no header rule at all. Without a `<thead>` no selector tells a header row from an index column, so it is not CSS-fixable - and it is cosmetic + overridable like the rest, hence documented rather than worked around. Untouched by all of this: the native `DataFrameGrid` (a plain `_repr_html_` carries `class="dataframe"` and `dataframeHtml.ts` routes it to the grid, never to this iframe) and markdown-cell tables (`.cellar-md table` in `app.css`). Covered by `tests/unit/html-output-style.test.ts` + `tests/e2e/output-table-styling.spec.ts` (real kernel, real cascade in the real browser: bare defaults, a wide table staying a real table and overflowing the output document while the app page does not scroll, a long text column wrapping into view, a Styler's own rules still winning - both the per-cell form and the TABLE-level `text-align` that regression-tests the inheritance rule above - a layout table + a div-only output unharmed, and `_repr_html_` still reaching the grid).
- **ipywidgets support (progress bars + common INTERACTIVE widgets, two-way).** tqdm bars AND interactive controls (`IntSlider`/`FloatSlider`/range, `IntText`/`Bounded*`, `Checkbox`/`ToggleButton`/`Valid`, `Dropdown`/`RadioButtons`/`Select`/`ToggleButtons`, `Text`/`Textarea`/`Password`, `Button`, `Box`/`HBox`/`VBox`/`GridBox`, `Output`) render live, and interactive ones send changes BACK to the kernel. **Receive (kernel→FE, #86):** `kernel.ts` `registerWidgetComm` (once per connection - `restart()`/autorestart keep the connection+target, a rebind rebuilds both) does `registerCommTarget('jupyter.widget', …)` for every `comm_open`+`comm_msg` `update`. **Registering this target also SILENCES @jupyterlab's `Exception opening new comm` spam** - thrown for ANY `jupyter.widget` comm_open with no registered target - so the callback must handle ALL widget types, never throw. Server store `src/lib/server/widgets.ts` keeps `comm_id → {state}` (cleared by `beginSession()`), rebroadcasting `widget:open`/`update`/`close`/`clear` as **global** SSE (`publishGlobal` - model ids unique per session, one store spans all notebooks); the events route seeds `widget:sync` on connect. **Send (FE→kernel, the interactive core):** `kernel.ts` keeps the live `IComm`s in `widgetComms` (cleared in `beginSession`) and `sendWidgetComm(commId, data)` calls `comm.send`; route `POST /api/widgets/[commId]` forwards `{method:'update',state:{<trait>:<value>}}` (a value/`index` change) or `{method:'custom',content:{event:'click'}}` (a Button), so ipywidgets updates the Python model + fires `observe`/`interact` callbacks. The route ALSO `updateWidget`s the store (optimistic broadcast) because the kernel does NOT echo a frontend-originated change back as a plain `update` - only ipywidgets-8 `echo_update`, which the receive path **deliberately ignores** (applying it mid-drag snaps the slider back). **Client** `src/lib/widgetActions.ts` posts on interaction: continuous controls (slider drag) THROTTLE (leading+trailing, `THROTTLE_MS=80`), discrete ones flush immediately; `widgetStore.svelte.ts` `setWidgetTrait` optimistically updates locally for lag-free feedback, then reconciles via SSE. No loop: sends happen only on user `input`/`change`, never on receiving a store update. **`Output` widget / `interact`:** an Output widget does NOT sync its rendered outputs over its comm - it publishes a `msg_id` trait and every iopub output whose `parent_header.msg_id` matches is captured. `kernel.ts` `registerWidgetOutputCapture` (connects `kernel.iopubMessage` once per connection) routes those into the Output's `outputs` (`appendWidgetOutput`/`clearWidgetOutput`, honoring `clear_output(wait=True)`); `setOutputCapture` tracks the `msg_id`↔comm mapping (updated from the comm's `msg_id` trait, which lands as an earlier iopub message so ordering is safe). `execute()` SUPPRESSES a captured output from the cell (`outputCommForMsg(parentId)`) so `interact`'s result renders once, in the widget, not double. This is what makes `interact(f, x=IntSlider())` re-run `f` and update its output area live as the slider moves. **Render** `src/lib/WidgetOutput.svelte` (recursive) by `_model_name` via pure helpers in `src/lib/widgetModel.ts` (`widgetKind`, trait projections); selection widgets bind `index`+`_options_labels`; `src/lib/WidgetOutputArea.svelte` renders an Output's nbformat outputs (stream/text/html/image/nested-widget). All daisyUI semantic classes → clean in both themes; unknown types render `[unsupported widget]`, never crash. **Persistence: runtime-only** - `clean.ts` strips the `widget-view` mime + drops an empty-bundle output; `metadata.widgets` dropped by the nb allowlist; zero git-diff. A reopened notebook shows no live widget (state is volatile, by design). Covered by `tests/unit/clean-on-save.test.ts`, `widget-model.test.ts`, `widget-output-capture.test.ts`. **Adding a widget type = one `widgetKind` case in `widgetModel.ts` + one render branch in `WidgetOutput.svelte`** (that is the whole extension point; the comm rail is type-agnostic). Confirm the ipywidgets model's synced traits: a selection scalar uses `index`+`_options_labels` (`selectedIndex`/`optionLabels`), a **multi**-selection (`SelectMultiple`) uses `index` as a **tuple** (`selectedIndices`, send `{index:[...]}`), and `Combobox` uses `value`(str)+`options` (`comboOptions`, rendered as a text input + `<datalist>`). Do NOT put a `<select multiple>` on daisyUI's `.select` class - it collapses the listbox; style it as a plain bordered box so it stays a vertical multi-select.
- **Databricks-style parameter widgets (`dbutils.widgets.*`) — Phase 0 (`src/lib/server/widgetsShim.ts`).** A Cellar-**native** shim injected at kernel init (in `initKernel`'s coalesced `parts`, alongside the matplotlib magic / DataFrame formatter / `%restart_python`) binds a global `dbutils` whose `.widgets` supports `text`/`dropdown`/`combobox`/`multiselect` + `get`/`getArgument`/`getAll`/`remove`/`removeAll`, backed by ipywidgets (so it rides the existing widget rail and renders inline where the declaring cell ran). **Works on ANY kernel — no Databricks connection needed** (the databricks-sdk's own `dbutils.widgets` is itself ipywidgets, so this is a faithful native equivalent). **Return-type parity with Databricks: `.get()` returns STRINGS, `multiselect` comma-joined** (`_cellar_widget_value`), so `int(dbutils.widgets.get("n"))` works. **Guarded silent degrade** (Cellar only guarantees `ipykernel`): no ipywidgets ⇒ a **value-only** registry (stores defaults, `.get()` returns them, nothing renders) — `dbutils` is always bound so pasted Databricks code never NameErrors. **Databricks interaction:** `databricks.ts` `CONNECT_CODE` binds `spark`/`w` but **deliberately does NOT rebind `dbutils`** — the native shim owns the bare `dbutils` name everywhere; the SDK's `w.dbutils` stays reachable+untouched (no double-binding). **But binding the bare global is NOT enough, and the gap failed SILENTLY: Databricks library code written to run both on and off a cluster imports the name (`from databricks.sdk.runtime import dbutils`), which off-cluster yields the SDK's own `RemoteDbUtils` -> `IPyWidgetUtil`, whose `_register` DISCARDS the existing widget and rebuilds it at the default.** The idiomatic parser declares and reads in ONE pass, so on that path an edited value can never be observed - not on re-run, not ever - while the controls still RENDER (the SDK rides ipywidgets too), which is exactly the signal a user reads as "this works" (observed with a 27-widget parser; every edit discarded). So while a runtime is advertised the installer ALSO points `databricks.sdk.runtime.dbutils` at the SAME shim instance, so both access paths share one value-preserving registry. **Two halves, because the import can happen either side of the shim:** a module already in `sys.modules` is patched in place, and one imported LATER is caught by a `sys.meta_path` finder that claims only that name, re-resolves it through the normal machinery (a `_busy` guard, so the REAL spec is found) and wraps its loader's `exec_module` - Python has no built-in post-import hook, so wrapping the loader is the mechanism, and it must DELEGATE (the module body has to run) rather than replace. **The wrapper must be STRICTLY NO WORSE THAN NO WRAPPER**, because importlib feature-detects on the loader it is HANDED, which is now the wrapper: a method defined here unconditionally makes it take a path the inner loader may not support, the delegation raises `AttributeError` and the import FAILS outright - trading a discarded-widget-value bug (recoverable) for an unimportable module (not). So `create_module` delegates only when the inner loader has one (else `None`, i.e. default module creation), and the finder DECLINES to wrap at all when the inner loader has no `exec_module` (a legacy `load_module`-only loader, reachable behind an instrumenting import hook) - losing the rebind for that exotic case is the correct degradation. Idempotent: a re-install drops the previous finder (it closes over a stale shim) instead of stacking one. **That finder MUST stay SELF-CONTAINED - it may never read a module-level name.** It lives in `sys.meta_path`, which OUTLIVES the kernel's user namespace, so a clear that is not a process restart (`%reset -f`, the everyday one) leaves it installed with the shim's globals gone; resolving the module name or the loader class there raised `NameError` on the FIRST comparison, and the import system does not swallow a finder's exception - so EVERY import in the kernel failed, not just this one, which is far worse than the silent inertness it exists to fix. Both are captured on the instance in `__init__` (by `_cellar_bind_sdk_dbutils`, while the globals are known to exist) and the body is wrapped besides, so a broken finder can only ever DECLINE. Deliberately NOT extended to the shim's own callables: they live in the namespace like every other startup injection, so a cleared namespace takes the bare `dbutils` with them and a later call is a LOUD `NameError` a restart fixes - the finder is different in kind because it is consulted silently, for every import. **Scoped to the advertised runtime deliberately** - it reads `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` from the env rather than taking a flag from TS, because `kernel.ts` injects that var as the FIRST part of the same coalesced exec, so the env IS the decision and cannot drift from it; mutating a third-party module's attribute is defensible exactly where Cellar is already emulating the runtime that module is written for. `w.dbutils` is a different object and stays untouched. **DETECT + REPORT, because silent inertness is the worst part:** `dbutilsBindingProbeCode()` (self-contained, so it still answers when the shim install itself failed) reports whether the module is imported and whether its `dbutils` is a Cellar shim; `classifySdkDbutils` (pure) maps that to `shim`/`foreign`/`not_imported`/`unknown`, and `foreign` - the ONE warnable state - surfaces as `getStatus().runtime.sdkDbutils` in the Runtime card and as `dbutils_widgets_warning` on `databricks_status`. **The sidebar shows it REGARDLESS of the cluster connection, through one shared `sdkDbutilsWarning` snippet reused by the Runtime card AND the expired / lost / picker Cluster cards** (the `sessionReauthBox` idiom): the shim is installed on EVERY kernel and `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` is read at kernel START, so the widgets are equally dead with no live session - and `agentStatus` already folds the warning into every shape it returns, so scoping the human's copy to the connected card was exactly the human/agent divergence `dbutilsShim.ts` exists to prevent. The rule, the module name and the ONE warning sentence live in the browser-safe `src/lib/dbutilsShim.ts` (the `databricksReauth.ts` precedent) so the human and the agent cannot be told different things - and the panel reads `SdkDbutilsState` FROM that module rather than re-declaring the union inline, since a second copy of the vocabulary is the drift it exists to prevent. **`sdk_is_shim` answers EXACTLY what the warning claims** - `isinstance(<module>.dbutils, _CellarDbUtils)`, the module attribute ALONE - never a comparison against whatever the bare global holds now. Those diverge in BOTH directions: after a `%reset -f` the bare name and the class are gone while the surviving finder still binds the module to a fully working shim, and a user writing `dbutils = w.dbutils` moves the bare name without touching the import path - reporting either as `foreign` tells the user their values are being discarded when every one is preserved. With the class itself gone there is nothing left to recognize a shim BY, so the probe reports `None` and **`foreign` requires an explicit `false`, never merely the absence of a `true`** (a non-boolean reads `unknown`, and `unknown` never warns). The probe is bounded like every other Cellar probe: asked ONLY when the session really advertises a runtime (which is also what keeps it off ordinary status reads), never boots a kernel, skipped on a BUSY one - read from the run QUEUE's own `running` truth as well as jupyter's status, exactly as `kernelState`/`inspectVariables` do, since a run claims the kernel synchronously at dequeue while the idle→busy flip lands a beat later and this probe is single-flight AND awaited inside `getStatus`, so landing in that window would block the Databricks panel and `databricks_status` for the whole cell - `internal:true`, single-flight, cached per (notebook, kernel SESSION) - **a FAILING probe is cached under that same TTL too**, since the round-trip is unbounded from the caller's view (a wedged kernel settles only via the watchdog's ~90-210s probe/strike path) and single-flight collapses concurrent readers but not sequential ones, so an uncached failure made every later status read pay it again; every failure resolves to `unknown` and `dbutilsShimWarning` is wrapped besides - a diagnostic may never take down the status it decorates, and over-reporting would send a user restarting a healthy kernel. Covered by `tests/unit/dbutils-sdk-import.test.ts` (REAL shim Python against a FAKE `databricks.sdk.runtime` package on `sys.path` - a fake PACKAGE, not a `sys.modules` entry, since only the real import machinery exercises the after-the-shim half, and its `dbutils` reproduces the SDK's reset so a preserved value can only mean the shim answered) + the reporting wiring in `databricks-connect-runtime-off.test.ts`. **ipywidgets is best-effort ensured** in the project venv (`venv.js` `ensureIpywidgets`, never throws — mirrors the Spark-progress guard) at launch (`bin/cellar.js` `ensureWidgets`) and on runtime rebind (`venv-bind.ts`). The pure core is parameterized by an injected widget factory (like `SPARK_PROGRESS_CORE_PY`), so `tests/unit/widgets-shim.test.ts` proves value/return-type behavior with a FAKE ipywidgets module. E2E on a plain kernel: `tests/e2e/dbx-widgets.spec.ts`. **The `_CellarDbUtils` stub MUST expose a faithful `entry_point` (`_CellarEntryPoint`), not just `.widgets`.** Because the Databricks-runtime toggle sets `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION`, a library then believes it is on a runtime and reaches for the driver-only `dbutils.entry_point` at IMPORT time - mlflow's module-level `_init_databricks_dynamic_token_config_provider(dbutils.entry_point)` (`databricks_utils.py` v3.8.1 ~L1419) was the first casualty: the stub had no `entry_point`, the `AttributeError` propagated (mlflow only catches `_NoDbutilsError`), and `import mlflow` died before the notebook's first cell. So `entry_point` returns honest **undefined/None** everywhere: `getContext().apiUrl()/apiToken()` are Scala-`Option`-like undefined options (`.isDefined()`→False, `.get()`→None, falsy) so the probe falls through cleanly, and every other driver getter (`getLogger`/`getNonUcApiToken`/`getReplId`/`getReplLocalTempDir`/…, plus any unknown method via `__getattr__`) is a callable returning None - attribute/method access must NEVER raise. Do NOT "fix" this by dropping `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` (that weakens the emulation); commit to the emulation instead. Covered by the entry_point case in `widgets-shim.test.ts`. **Deferred to Phase 1/2 (NOT built):** pinned top panel, static declaration detection, `metadata.cellar.widgets` persistence, staleness/dataflow integration (synthetic `uses` for `.get()`), auto-rerun, SQL `:param` binding, MCP tools.
- **"Databricks runtime" toggle - advertise `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` (`src/lib/server/databricksRuntime.ts`).** Distinct from the shim above: this makes a notebook's OWN import-time gate `IS_DATABRICKS = os.getenv("DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION") is not None` read True, so pasted Databricks-notebook code takes its `from databricks.sdk.runtime import dbutils; dbutils.widgets.*` path (the SDK's own widgets, which are real ipywidgets → render on the same rail, no frontend work) instead of a local CLI (`tyro`) path. **Setting the env var is the ONLY thing needed** (proven end-to-end; design report `firstmate/data/cellar-dbx-runtime-emulation-e4/report.md`). Modeled on the Project-root toggle but with **two deliberate divergences**: (1) **it takes effect at kernel START / restart ONLY** - `IS_DATABRICKS` is evaluated at import time, so setting the env in a kernel that already imported the package does not re-flip it; but rather than leave the user a manual step, the UI **APPLIES a change by restarting the kernel automatically** - the toggle (and a version edit) restarts on change, and that is the ONLY thing that does (connecting a cluster deliberately does not; see the Databricks-section Connect-time behavior entry; `applyRuntime` → `setUiNow` + `onRestartKernel`, no live-apply route and no new restart path); (2) **default OFF, and additionally SCOPED to a Databricks-CONNECTED notebook** - advertising a runtime changes ALL libraries, not just the user's gates (notably **mlflow**'s `is_in_databricks_runtime()` reads this exact var and assumes it is on a cluster), so it is an explicit opt-in and a purely-local kernel is never spoofed. The pure module holds `databricksRuntimeEnabled` (toggle preference, **default OFF** - `storeValue === true`, so only the toggle's own opt-in enables it and a connect, which stores nothing, never can - env override `CELLAR_DATABRICKS_RUNTIME`), `shouldInjectDatabricksRuntime(store, env, bound)` (the runtime gate: env override forces either way and bypasses the scope; else an unset / false preference never injects; else a stored true injects only when `bound`), `databricksRuntimeVersion` (default `'15.4'`, env override `CELLAR_DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION`), and `databricksRuntimeEnvCode`. **`bound`** = `databricksBound(nb)` in `databricks.ts` (a live session OR a kept `reconnectTarget` - the durable "this is a Databricks notebook" signal, set on connect, survives a kernel restart, cleared only by explicit disconnect; deliberately NOT `connectionStatus().connected`, which reads false between a restart and its auto-reconnect). This is why the first-connect chicken-and-egg resolves through the TOGGLE rather than the connect: connect binds the notebook (leaving the runtime off - see the Connect-time behavior note below), and the user's opt-in restart then both re-establishes `spark`/`w` AND injects the env before the fresh imports. `ui-state.ts` exposes `injectDatabricksRuntime(bound)` / `databricksRuntimeVersion()` / `databricksRuntimeForced()` (the override's verdict, for the sidebar); `kernel.ts` `initKernel(kernel, nbPath)` injects `databricksRuntimeEnvCode(...)` as the **FIRST** startup part (before every other snippet and the user's first import), gated by `injectDatabricksRuntime(await databricksNotebookBound(nbPath))` - the bound check is a dynamic `import('./databricks')` to keep kernel.ts's dependency one-directional (no cycle). The toggle + version input live in the **Databricks sidebar section**'s Runtime card (`Databricks.svelte`, shown when connected; store keys mirrored client-side since a client component can't import `$lib/server`). Does NOT connect a cluster or provide spark/UC/Volumes (that is the Connect flow's job). **The gate keys off the DURABLE `databricksBound`, and that was necessary but NOT sufficient - `CONNECT_CODE`'s env scrub was the real restart bug.** `initKernel` correctly injects the env on a bound restart (bound survives the epoch bump), but the auto-reconnect that fires right after runs `CONNECT_CODE` in the SAME kernel, whose `DATABRICKS_*`/`SPARK_CONNECT_*` scrub (auth hygiene) POPPED the just-injected `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` - so a connected notebook read `IS_DATABRICKS` False after a restart (env None, zero `DATABRICKS_*` vars). The scrub is REQUIRED (databricks-connect refuses to build a REMOTE session while it believes it is on a runtime), so a `KEEP_ENV` entry would re-break connect; instead `CONNECT_CODE` SAVES `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION` before the scrub and RESTORES it in a `finally` after the session build (build still runs with a clean env). Only `CONNECT_CODE` needs this (the kernel is where `initKernel` injects the var); `DISCONNECT_CODE` and the listing `scrubEnv` never see it. Pure toggle logic covered by `tests/unit/databricks-runtime.test.ts`; the durable-gate + env-preservation (real Python running the generated `CONNECT_CODE`) covered by `tests/unit/databricks-runtime-env-preserve.test.ts`. **The AGENT surface for it (task `cellar-databricks-runtime-legibility`) is one READ builder and one WRITE path, both in `databricks.ts`.** `agentRuntimeBlock(nb)` -> `runtime:{advertised, version, forced_by_env}` is the ONE builder behind `databricks_status`, `kernel_state` AND `get_notebook_map`, so the three cannot describe one kernel differently - the map builds its `databricks` block from the CHEAP `connectionStatus` while the other two use `agentStatus`, which is exactly how such surfaces drift. Like the sidebar's pill it is read LIVE (`liveDatabricksRuntime`), never from the preference, and it is a Map lookup plus an env read - no boot, no probe - which is what lets the hot map read carry it; keep it that cheap or it does not belong there. It is added by wrapping `agentStatus` ONCE (its body is `agentConnection`), so none of its six shapes can omit it. `forced_by_env` is the on/off override ALONE (the independent version override is reported by the setter, per this entry's never-one-boolean rule). **`setRuntimeAdvertisement` (MCP `databricks_runtime`) is a third caller of the same policy, NOT a second mechanism** - same preference keys, same `restartKernel`, same `injectDatabricksRuntime` decision - and its **restart is CONDITIONAL**: it fires only when the version a kernel WOULD start with differs from the one it carries, so enabling what is already advertised, disabling what never was, or applying with no kernel are namespace-preserving no-ops; enabling on an UNBOUND notebook stores the preference and reports that nothing is advertised rather than restarting to change nothing (the scope gate would leave it off anyway), and an env-forced decision is refused (`runtime_env_forced`, 409). **EVERY `runtimeNote` branch is derived from what was OBSERVED - the epoch delta and the resulting `runtime` block - never from the `enable`/`bound` inputs**, which are a REQUEST and a snapshot taken before the work; both had already produced a sentence contradicting the structured result beside it. Reading the request denied a restart that really happened (a notebook connected, enabled, then DISCONNECTED still carries the advertisement, so the scope gate makes `desired` null and the restart genuinely tears it out - that path now reports the restart and says the advertisement was REMOVED), and branching on the pre-restart `bound` - which `initKernel` RE-EVALUATES during the restart - let a disconnect landing in that window claim `DATABRICKS_RUNTIME_VERSION="null" ... IS_DATABRICKS reads true` beside `advertised:false`. They survive only to explain WHY an observed outcome happened (the connection scope is the sole thing that can make an enable end up advertising nothing), which is a claim about a RULE, not about this run. **The restart's other two side effects are stated wherever it is reported** (`QUEUED_RUNS_DROPPED`/`SESSION_REBUILDING`, shared by the note and the failure): `restartKernel` clears the pending queue and force-aborts the active run BEFORE issuing the restart, so a note naming only the namespace let an agent's own queued `run_cells` batch vanish into a bare `cancelled` (worded to match `restart_kernel`'s own description, one rule across both surfaces) - and it fires the session rebuild DETACHED, so `spark`/`w` are NOT bound when this returns and "re-run your cells" alone sent a `spark.*` cell into a NameError; the note says a rebuild is under way and names `databricks_status` as what observes it, never that it will succeed. For the same reason the not-restarted FAILURE message no longer claims nothing was torn down: the queue was already dropped and the active run aborted, and only the namespace is intact. **A restart that THROWS states the same pair rather than only failing** - `restartKernel` bumps the epoch in a `finally` because the namespace is gone once the REST restart has been issued, so a failure with the reconnect rejecting has already destroyed every variable; the restart is therefore caught, `kernelRestarted` is read off the EPOCH (never off whether the call threw) and the rethrown `runtime_restart_failed` carries it as `DatabricksError.kernelRestarted` plus the underlying failure in its message, which `databricksTool` forwards as the SAME `kernel_restarted`/`namespace_cleared` pair a success reports (never a second shape). Telling an agent only that the call failed leaves it building on variables that are gone - the NameError trap the run-status doctrine exists to prevent. The preference is per WORKSPACE (one `DBX_RUNTIME_KEY` pair), so it governs every bound notebook's next kernel start while only the addressed one restarts now - the tool description says so, since the per-notebook framing was new on the agent surface. The preference is written BEFORE the restart is considered (standing instruction first, reconcile second - the `harness.js` allow-list ordering). `connectCluster`'s note carries `CONNECT_RUNTIME_CLAUSE` and INSTRUCTIONS clause 9 says connected != on Databricks, because the deliberate reversal above is precisely the assumption a model forms on connecting. **`runtimeStatus` also reports the stored `preference` and `Databricks.svelte` re-seeds `runtimeOn` from it** (guarded on nothing in flight; the `seedUploadAffixes` lesson): the panel's one-shot mount read was correct only while the toggle was the sole writer, and a stale toggle applies its NEGATION, i.e. a namespace-clearing restart that changes nothing. The version INPUT is deliberately not re-seeded (it binds a field the user may be typing in), and **what makes that residual purely cosmetic is that only a deliberate version EDIT (`commitVersion`) may WRITE the version key** - `applyRuntime` takes an opt-in `writeVersion`, so the on/off toggle and "Apply now" flip the advertisement alone and apply whatever version is stored. Unconditional, that write made the user's next toggle silently revert a version an agent had just set through `databricks_runtime` and restart the kernel onto the panel's stale mount-time snapshot. `appliedVersion` (`commitVersion`'s did-it-really-change comparand) IS re-seeded, from the version the running kernel actually carries, so it tracks every writer rather than only this panel. Covered by `tests/unit/databricks-runtime-agent.test.ts` + `tests/unit/mcp-runtime-surfaces.test.ts` (the three surfaces agreeing in BOTH directions, plus the tool over the real `registerTools`).
- **jupytext / Databricks `.py` notebooks (`src/lib/server/jupytext.js` + `jupytext-actions.js`).** A `.py` opens as a **live, kernel-attached** notebook exactly like an `.ipynb` — the `.py` ⇄ cells boundary is `jupytext.js`, called from `notebook.js` `loadDoc`/`persist` at the same seam `ipynb.js` sits at, so everything above it (cell ops, run, events, MCP, live-sync) is **format-agnostic**. The doc records `jpFormat` (the format it was opened in) and round-trips back to the SAME `.py` in that format. **Two engines:** jupytext (a Python lib) handles percent/light/etc. in a short-lived subprocess of the **project venv's** python (same split as `databricks.js` — `projectPython()`; the helper is embedded as a string because `$lib/server` is bundled into `build/`); the **Databricks source format** (`# Databricks notebook source` / `# COMMAND ----------` / `# MAGIC %md|%sql`) is a dedicated pure-text converter **inside the helper** — jupytext has NO native Databricks format (it mis-reads it as "light"), and the converter round-trips byte-for-byte with `%md`→markdown and other magics kept intact as code cells. **Text has no outputs by design** (good for git/Databricks): a `.py` persists only on SOURCE/structural changes — `setOutputs` updates the in-memory doc for live display but writes nothing (`if (!doc.jpFormat) persist`). **Persist is sync** (`spawnSync`), matching the existing sync-persist model. **Detection is JS, no python** (`detectPyNotebook`): only an explicit marker (Databricks header / `# %%` / jupytext front-matter) counts, so a plain script still opens as **text**; the frontend probes `GET /api/notebooks/jupytext?path=` once per `.py` (caches the kind, persists it in tab session memory) to pick a live-notebook vs file tab. jupytext YAML headers are suppressed on write (`notebook_metadata_filter=-all`) so a re-save from a different jupytext version is a zero git-diff. **Two human actions** (navbar app menu, `POST /api/notebooks/jupytext {op}`): **Save as .py** (`exportNotebookAsPy`, format picker defaulting to Databricks) and **Convert to .ipynb** (`convertPyToIpynb` — runs every cell through the run-queue to materialize outputs, writes `<base>.ipynb`; a cell that errors still lands its error output, convert never aborts). jupytext is auto-installed into the project venv on demand (`ensureJupytext`, uv) with a clear `jupytext_missing` message if it can't be. **Known v1 limits:** `.py` carries no cellar cell-metadata, so the pinned-imports role is lost on reopen (in-session only); light-without-header reopens as text; converting onto an already-open `.ipynb` leaves that live doc stale until reload.
- **Export to self-contained HTML (`src/lib/server/export-html.js` + `GET /api/notebooks/export`).** Navbar app menu → "Export to HTML" (`onExportHtml` in `+page.svelte` → a hidden `<a download>` hitting the route with `?path=`) downloads ONE portable `.html` of the active notebook. **Server-side re-render of the notebook MODEL, not a DOM serialization:** the route reads `listCells(path)` (persisted outputs straight from the `.ipynb` — it NEVER touches the kernel, so export reflects the last run) and `renderNotebookHtml` emits a full standalone document with all CSS inlined and images as data URIs. It deliberately mirrors `Cell.svelte` — the same markdown-it config (`html:false`, so no DOMPurify is needed server-side), the same output priority (image beats the `text/plain` repr), the same markdown-table-in-text detection, the same ANSI scrub — so it reads like Cellar. **The one deliberate divergence is math**: this module keeps its OWN markdown-it with no KaTeX plugin, so `$…$` exports as literal text (see the TeX-math entry for why). It does NOT invent renderings Cellar lacks (a DataFrame exports as its text repr, not `text/html`). Python code is highlighted by a compact self-contained tokenizer in the module (CodeMirror is browser-only; a new dep wasn't worth it) that fails safe to escaped plain text. The palette is the #37 tokens verbatim (pygments-light / One-Dark) with a baked-in light/dark toggle (`data-theme-mode` + a tiny inline script, default honors `prefers-color-scheme`). Read-only: no run controls, no editor, nothing agent-facing; all cells shown (the `hidden_from_agent` filter is MCP-only). `exportFilename` derives `<name>.html` (also strips a `.py` extension). **The route + the MCP tool share ONE render orchestration, `buildNotebookHtml({nb, hideCode})`** (in `export-html.ts`): it resolves the notebook, `listCells`, decides report-style (`hideCode` undefined → the saved `getHideAllCode`, else forced), renders, and returns `{html, filename, hideAllCode}` - so the download and the agent export are byte-identical for the same inputs. **Agent-facing `export_html` MCP tool** (`svc.exportHtml` → registered in `server.ts`): writes the HTML to a workspace file and returns its LOCATION + metadata `{path, bytes, hide_code}`, **never the HTML body** (inlining a whole document would blow the token budget). Default output is `<name>.html` alongside the notebook; an explicit `path` is workspace-relative (a `.html` extension is appended if missing). **Both paths go through `resolveInWorkspace`**, so a traversal/absolute-escape is refused - nothing is written outside the workspace. `hide_code` maps to the same report path as `?hideCode` (omit = follow the saved setting). Covered by `tests/unit/mcp-export-html.test.ts`.
- **Hide code input (report view) - per-cell + notebook-wide.** Hide a code cell's INPUT (editor) while keeping its OUTPUT visible - a clean output-only report view, display-only (source untouched, cell still runs, `.ipynb` git-clean). **Distinct from `editorCollapsed`** (the tall-editor scroll-box contraction, runtime-only): hide-code is an EXPLICIT, PERSISTED hide regardless of length. **Two flags in the allowlisted `cellar` namespace (round-trip through clean-on-save):** per-cell `metadata.cellar.hide_input` (tri-state: undefined = follow the notebook default, true = hidden, false = shown) and notebook-level `notebook.metadata.cellar.hide_all_code` (report view). **Precedence:** `effectiveHidden = hide_input ?? hide_all_code` - a per-cell explicit choice always wins; the notebook-wide toggle is the default for cells with no explicit choice, so a per-cell "show code" reveal works even under global hide. **Server** (`notebook.ts`): `setHideInput` (code cells only; emits `cell:hide-input`) + `setHideAllCode` (emits `notebook:hide-all-code`), mirroring `setOutputScrolled`/`setHeaderNumbering`; `getNotebook` carries `hideAllCode`. **API:** `PATCH /api/cells/[id] {hideInput}`; `POST /api/notebooks/hide-code {hidden}`. **Client:** `LiveNotebook` `setHideInput`/`toggleHideAllCode` (optimistic + persisted), `hideAllCode` state (loaded + kept live via SSE + published up via `onHideAllCodeChange`); both events flow through `applyStructuralEvent`. `Cell.svelte` derives `codeHidden`, hides the editor block (same `hidden` mechanism as markdown rendered-mode, so CodeMirror stays mounted) and shows a slim, discoverable **"show code" bar** (`data-testid=show-code`) that reveals in place; the ⋮ actions menu (now shown for ALL code cells incl. SQL, not just Python) gains a Hide/Show code item (`data-testid=toggle-hide-input`). The toolbar (run controls, run-status, running/queued affordances) stays visible when hidden, so a running hidden cell is legible and a run still updates the visible output. **Notebook-wide toggle** lives in the Navbar app-menu "View" section (`data-testid=toggle-hide-all-code`), state reflected from the active notebook. Markdown cells are unaffected (no code to hide). **HTML export honors hide-code (report-style export).** `renderNotebookHtml({cells, hideAllCode})` (`export-html.ts`) applies the shared `isCodeHidden` rule from `src/lib/hideInput.ts` (per-cell `hide_input ?? hideAllCode`, markdown never hides - the SAME module `Cell.svelte` and the agent map use): a hidden code cell renders **output-only**, and a hidden code cell with NO output (imports, `df = load()`) is dropped from the report entirely, so a report-view export reads as pure markdown + results. The route `GET /api/notebooks/export` couples to the saved notebook setting by default (`getHideAllCode(path)`) so a notebook read as a report in Cellar exports as one; `?hideCode=1|0` forces report style on/off for a one-off export regardless. This is a deliberate reversal of the earlier "export shows all cells" stance (the feature's whole point is a code-free report); `hidden_from_agent` stays MCP-only and orthogonal. Covered by `tests/unit/export-html.test.ts`.
- **Notebook-level display settings + their agent surface (header numbering, report view, export target).** All live in the allowlisted `cellar` namespace on the NOTEBOOK's metadata (`header_numbering` = which heading levels 1-6 auto-number, `hide_all_code` = report view, `export_target` = the nbdev-style `#|default_exp` `.py` module path) and all are readable AND settable by the agent. **`export_target` is agent-exposed** (`set_export_target` tool → `mcp/service.ts` `setExportTarget`, session-targeted like the other notebook-level setters; `path:null`/`''` clears it) - a thin wrapper over the existing `notebook.ts` `setExportTarget`, so it persists to `metadata.cellar.export_target`, regenerates the `.py` module on save (auto-export), and emits `notebook:export-target` SSE exactly like the UI's target input. `get_notebook_map`'s `display` block carries `export_target` (null when unset) alongside `header_numbering`/`report_view`. INSTRUCTIONS clause 5 covers reading/setting it. Covered by `tests/unit/mcp-export-target.test.ts`. Header numbering and report view (the other two): **Numbering is computed at render time and NEVER written into a cell's source** (`computeHeadingNumbers` in `headings.ts`, pure + hierarchical over the enabled levels; `setHeaderNumbering`/`setHideAllCode` in `notebook.ts` persist only the setting and emit `notebook:header-numbering`/`notebook:hide-all-code`). **MCP:** `set_header_numbering` / `set_report_view` tools (`server.ts`), thin session-targeted wrappers in `mcp/service.ts`; `get_notebook_map` carries a **`display`** block (`{header_numbering, report_view}`) and each numbered section its rendered `number`, and `read_section`'s header carries its `number`. `read_cell`/`read_cells`/`search_cells` deliberately do NOT - they return raw source, which is exactly where the number is not. The whole point is `INSTRUCTIONS` clause 5: agents hand-number headers (`## 1. Setup`) because nothing told them Cellar already does it. **The double-numbering hazard is by design, do NOT "fix" it:** a source `## 1. Setup` under numbering renders `1. 1. Setup`, and there is deliberately **no defensive strip** of a leading number anywhere - Cellar cannot tell an agent's stray auto-number from a title the human meant (`3. Third Attempt`), so the sanctioned fix is the instructions clause telling the agent to clean the source with judgment. Adding a strip re-introduces the bug it looks like it fixes. **Per-cell `hide_input` (which overrides `hide_all_code` in either direction) IS now agent-exposed** (`set_hide_input` tool → `mcp/service.ts` `setHideInput`, session-targeted like the notebook-level setters; tri-state `hidden:true|false|null`, code cells only, emits the same `cell:hide-input` SSE + `metadata.cellar.hide_input` persistence as the UI). `get_notebook_map` leaves carry the effective `code_hidden` (only when hidden) and the explicit `hide_input` (only when a cell overrides), so an agent enabling report view CAN now see the one cell whose code a human deliberately kept shown (it reports `hide_input:false` with no `code_hidden`) - INSTRUCTIONS clause 5 tells it not to "fix" that cell back to hidden. **The `hide_input ?? hide_all_code` precedence now lives in ONE pure browser-safe module, `src/lib/hideInput.ts`** (`isCodeHidden`/`hideInputExplicit`), shared verbatim by `Cell.svelte`, `export-html.ts`, and the agent map so the three surfaces cannot drift. Covered by `tests/unit/mcp-display-settings.test.ts` + `tests/unit/mcp-hide-input.test.ts`.
- **Not yet built (deferred by design):** MCP `get_changes_since`/delta reads + subscribable-resource map (the user-activity digest now tells an agent THAT the human changed something and roughly what; a pull-able delta READ of the content itself is still absent, and the subscribable half is dead on arrival - see that entry's push finding), an agent surface (read + set, alongside `set_header_numbering`/`set_report_view`/`set_hide_input`/`set_export_target`) for the display/output settings that still have none (all notebook-level settings - header numbering, report view, per-cell `hide_input`, `export_target` - are now agent-exposed), extract-to-`.py`, background jupytext pairing (open + save-as + convert exist; no auto-pair-on-save), Databricks **serverless** compute + SQL warehouses (cluster-only for now), git merge driver, per-output size threshold, CELL-level code roots + root-aware git decorations + a root-scoped file tree (notebook-level roots ship; see that entry's stated limitation), code-block syntax highlighting in rendered markdown (TeX math itself now ships - see the KaTeX entry; typesetting it in the HTML export stays deferred), polished editing UX, live-sync later phases (unify UI+MCP run path / run metadata).

## Application shell (navbar + sidebar)

Wraps the notebook in a JupyterLab-style shell. `src/routes/+page.svelte` is the shell: it owns tabs/sidebar/kernel-controls/theme and hosts one `LiveNotebook` per open notebook tab (default + each opened `.ipynb`), all kept mounted+hidden so editor/run state survives tab switches. It no longer owns `cells`: each `LiveNotebook` owns its own cells and reports the live array up via `onCellsChange(path, cells)` into a path-keyed `notebooksCells` map; the sidebar (outline/search/variables) reads `activeCells` for the focused notebook (falls back to the default when a plain file tab is active). One kernel per notebook → runs are serialized **within** each notebook by the **server-side FIFO run queue**, while different notebooks execute in parallel (see the Kernel run queue entry above); the shell's `runBusy` is only a derived in-flight counter for the kernel badge, no longer a gate.

- **A FOREIGN action must refresh every panel the OWN action refreshes (`+page.svelte`'s SSE handler).** The shell drives an action itself AND observes the same action arriving from an agent / another tab, and the two paths refresh the sidebar independently - so a panel one refreshes and the other does not goes stale for the rest of the session, and **no test that performs the action ITSELF can see it** (a self-run goes through `onRunEnd`, which already refreshes variables, so it masks the gap completely). Both known cases are fixed and pinned: a foreign `run:end` refreshes the kernel badge **and** the variable inspector (refreshing only the badge left the Variables panel empty for a whole agent session - measured against a live kernel - because the mount-time inspect is a one-shot that had no kernel to read yet), and the `notebook:opened` branch bumps `fsRefreshSignal` like every other new-file path here (without it an agent-created notebook never appeared in the file tree, its untracked git badge included). **What must match is the refresh SET, not the mechanism - and the two halves of that set are deliberately dispatched DIFFERENTLY, because a foreign run is not paced like the user's own.** The kernel badge stays immediate and ungated (`/api/kernel` only READS state, it never probes). The inspector is a real kernel EXECUTION (`/api/kernel/variables` → `inspectVariables` → `execute`), and a foreign `run:end` arrives once per CELL, so mirroring `onRunEnd` naively made an agent's `run_cells`/`run_all` cost one probe per cell **per open tab**, each serializing on that kernel's `execChain` and inserting latency between the agent's own cells. So it is **coalesced** (`scheduleForeignVariablesRefresh`, a trailing debounce of `FOREIGN_VARS_REFRESH_MS` = 300ms - one probe per burst, far below what a human reads as lag) and **scoped to the ACTIVE notebook** (`foreignRunTouchesActiveNotebook`): `inspectVariables` probes the ACTIVE notebook's kernel, so a run elsewhere can only ever probe the wrong one. Its pending timer is cleared by the SSE subscription's own teardown (the `onMount` now returns a cleanup that unsubscribes AND clears it), so a scheduled probe can never outlive the page that scheduled it. **The gate FAILS OPEN, and that direction is the rule**: the event's `nb` is ABSOLUTE while `activeNotebookPath` is workspace-relative, so the comparison goes through **`$lib/workspacePath`'s `toWorkspaceRel(workspace, abs)` - the ONE owner of that conversion, shared with `canonicalNotebookRel` so the two cannot drift** - and every case it cannot decide (no path on the event, no active notebook, a path outside the workspace) returns **true** and refreshes - a missed refresh is the bug this whole path exists to fix, so over-refreshing is safe and silently skipping never is. **That helper is BOUNDARY-AWARE, and it is not a tidiness point**: both sites used to be a bare `startsWith`, so a SIBLING sharing the workspace's name as a prefix (`/tmp/ws` vs `/tmp/ws2/x.ipynb`) read as INSIDE the workspace and yielded the nonsense rel `2/x.ipynb`, which the gate then DECIDED as a mismatch and skipped - the missed refresh reached through the one path that did not fail open. It requires a separator boundary and returns null otherwise, so an undecidable path falls into the fail-open branch; the caller supplies its own fallback for null (`canonicalNotebookRel` keeps the bare name). **The load-bearing undecidable case is `activeTabIsNotebook`**: the probe's real subject is the SERVER's `getActiveNotebookPath()`, which moves only when a `LiveNotebook` becomes `active`, so a plain FILE tab holding focus leaves it pointing at the last-focused notebook while `activeNotebookPath` falls back to `canonicalNotebookRel` - and comparing there answers about the wrong notebook, i.e. FALSE for a run in the very notebook the probe would read, this bug reappearing with the gate failing CLOSED. So the comparison is trusted only while the active TAB is itself the notebook it names; anything else refreshes. (The endpoint deliberately keeps taking no path - scoping it server-side is a separate change.) `refreshVariables` carries a `varsReqSeq` generation guard (the `kernelReqSeq`/`statusSeq`/`uploadSeq`/`diskSeq` convention): several probes can be in flight and responses are unordered, so an older namespace landing last would otherwise stick - which is also why **EVERY** LOCAL wipe goes through `wipeVariablesLocally()` (bump, then `variables = []`, the `markKernelStarted` shape): a restart/shutdown/venv-rebind that cleared the rows without bumping let a probe issued a moment earlier land after the wipe and repopulate the inspector with the dead session's (or dead INTERPRETER's) namespace, with nothing scheduled to correct it. The unit guard therefore asserts over the WHOLE file that the helper's own is the only raw `variables = []` - scoped per known call site, it let the venv rebind bypass the generation unseen. The helper also CLEARS `varsLoading`/`varsError`, because it issues no replacement request while `refreshVariables`' `finally` deliberately declines to clear the spinner once superseded - so without it a wipe landing on an in-flight probe left the Variables header spinning for good, a stale panel shipped inside the fix for stale panels. **The own-run `onRunEnd` path is untouched** - it still refreshes both immediately, so a single interactive run's result lands as soon as it exists. That immediacy is a deliberate choice and NOT a claim that the own path cannot burst: a human bulk run (**Run all / above / below / stale**) drives `LiveNotebook`'s sequential `runCodeIds`, whose per-cell `runCell` `finally` calls `onRunEnd`, so it DOES still probe once per cell - a known, accepted cost, softened (not bounded) by `inspectVariables` short-circuiting `busy` without executing once the next cell has claimed the kernel, which is a race rather than a guarantee. Coalescing it too is a separate product decision, since it would delay exactly the single-run feedback the immediacy exists for. **Known gap, filed as a separate follow-up and deliberately out of scope here:** nothing re-probes the inspector when a notebook an agent worked in LATER becomes the active tab (there is no `$effect` on `activeNotebookPath`), and the SSE `kernel:status` branch likewise never wipes the rows after a FOREIGN kernel restart/shutdown/wipe - both leave the panel showing another session's namespace until the next own run or reload. The mount-time inspect is deliberately UNCONDITIONAL: `inspectVariables` short-circuits a not-started kernel server-side and only ever READS the status, so gating it on a kernel this page already knows about protected nothing and encoded the false premise that WE are the ones who boot it. Covered by `tests/unit/shell-refresh-handlers.test.ts` (source guards on the call sites - the only layer CI sees, since e2e is absent from CI and the no-mistakes gate - including that the foreign branch reaches the inspector only THROUGH the debounced helper, that the helper clears its pending timer, that the gate has no `return false`, and an anchor-proof on the mount block: its `blockAt` marker's `{` is INTERIOR, so a scan starting at the marker's last character opened at depth 0 and ran on into the next `onMount`'s SSE callback, silently unscoping the guard) + `tests/e2e/foreign-action-refresh.spec.ts` (a real `cellar mcp` agent drives every assertion and the observing page never acts; it counts the page's OWN requests by EXACT pathname - `/api/kernel` is a prefix of `/api/kernel/variables` - so an 8-cell agent batch is proven to collapse to a small handful of probes. **That bound is EMPIRICAL, not a pinned debounce count** - the trailing window collapses only the cells whose `run:end` land closer together than 300ms, and each cell costs a kernel round-trip plus a synchronous fsync'd `.ipynb` persist, so under pathological pacing the count really can approach one per cell; the assertion claims only "substantially fewer probes than cells" and is set with headroom (measured: **1** with the fix, **9** against an ungated + undebounced refresh, bound **6**), which are the figures to re-tune against rather than tightening it toward the measured 1 and inviting a flake. And a run in a notebook the user is not viewing is proven to probe ZERO times while the ungated badge refresh still fires, which is what shows a zero count is the gate rather than a missing event; each test sets itself up, because a failure restarts the worker into a brand-new workspace and the tab session is server-owned `.cellar/` state that a fresh browser context still restores).
- **Navbar** (`src/lib/Navbar.svelte`): app-menu dropdown → `src/lib/Settings.svelte` modal (theme toggle `dim`↔`nord`, persisted to `localStorage['cellar-theme']`, applied via `<html data-theme>`); editor-style **wrapping** tab bar (tabs flow onto more rows via `flex-wrap`, never clip). Every tab (including the notebook) is closable and part of session memory; tabs carry file-type icons + a dirty dot; `preview` tabs render italic. **A notebook tab shows a run indicator while that notebook is executing/queueing a cell — background runs included** (a notebook running while you view another tab): the icon slot swaps to a `warning`-hued spinner (`data-testid=tab-running`) or a pulsing dot when queued (`data-testid=tab-queued`), matching the in-notebook running/queued affordances. **The indicator is a clickable button (`data-testid=tab-jump-running`, "Jump to running cell"): clicking it activates that notebook (if it isn't the viewed tab) and scrolls its running (or, failing that, first queued) cell into view** — the Navbar renders it as a *sibling* button of the tab-select button (a button cannot nest in a button) and rendered ONLY while `runState` is set, its `onclick` `stopPropagation()`s then calls `onJumpToRunningCell(tab.id)` so a spinner-click is an unambiguous jump while a click anywhere else on the tab still just selects it. The shell's `jumpToRunningCell(tabId)` (`+page.svelte`) activates the tab, `await tick()`s (a background pane has no geometry until active), then calls the notebook's imperative `revealRunning()` (`NotebookApiHandle`, `types.ts`). `revealRunning` reuses the follow-running-cell machinery (`ensureCellMounted` to unfold + mount, then `scrollElementIntoView` with `settle:'frame'` - it is a discrete target, not a streaming one) but is an EXPLICIT action, so it deliberately **bypasses the `follow` preference, the typing guard, and the imports-cell skip** the automatic follow-effect honors.
