The signal reuses the existing per-notebook run state already reported up to the shell — every mounted `LiveNotebook` publishes `onRunStateChange(path, runningId, queued)` **regardless of `active`** into `+page.svelte`'s `notebooksRunState` (keyed by the workspace-relative path = `tab.path`), so a background notebook's run is visible with no new plumbing; `+page.svelte` derives `tabRunState` (tab.id → 'running'|'queued') and passes it to the Navbar. Do NOT wire this off `runsInFlight`/`runBusy` — those are a single GLOBAL counter, not per-notebook. Covered by `tests/e2e/tab-jump-running-cell.spec.ts`.
- **File tabs** (`src/lib/FileTab.svelte`): CodeMirror editor over a workspace file, ⌘S save; syntax-highlighted by file extension (`langFor`: python / markdown / json+ipynb / yaml / toml / html, else plain text), sharing `editorTheme.js`'s one static `EDITOR_THEME` with cells. TOML has no lezer grammar, so it uses the official `@codemirror/legacy-modes` stream mode via `StreamLanguage`; its tokens land on the same highlight tags as every other language, so both themes style it with no theme-side work (`.toml`/`.yaml`/`.yml` share the `config` icon in `fileIcons.js`). HTML comes from `@codemirror/lang-html`, promoted from a transitive to a **direct** dependency because the code imports it - the same convention as `@codemirror/autocomplete`/`@codemirror/search`. Dirty state bubbles up to the tab bar. Notebook + all file tabs stay mounted (hidden via `.hidden`) so editor/run state survives tab switches.
- **Rendered previews in a file tab (markdown + HTML).** Two file kinds carry a **Source/Preview** toggle in the tab header (`data-testid=file-view-source`/`file-view-preview`), one shared mechanism in `FileTab.svelte`: `previewKind` ('markdown' | 'html' | null) picks the renderer, the mode is a session-wide preference per kind (`localStorage['cellar-md-view']` / `['cellar-html-view']`, read **synchronously at init** so an HTML file paints its preview on the first frame rather than flashing the source view), and `liveSource` carries the editor buffer into the preview so a preview always shows unsaved edits and toggling back re-renders from current content. **`liveSource` is SAMPLED, not mirrored**: it is read from the editor doc when a preview is entered (`setViewMode('preview')`) and once after the initial load, **never per keystroke** - the editor is `display:none` under a preview so the USER cannot change its document while one is on screen, and a per-keystroke `doc.toString()` on a multi-MB export was a real regression that was removed. Do not reinstate an `updateListener` write. **The one thing that DOES rewrite that hidden document is an external on-disk sync**, so that path assigns `liveSource` itself (see the external-edit entry below) - without it the preview keeps rendering pre-sync text while the editor under it holds the new content, which is half the feature missing. The editor stays mounted-but-hidden under a preview (CodeMirror state / git baseline / unsaved edits survive the toggle). **Defaults differ by kind on purpose:** markdown → Source (you open a `.md` to edit it), `.html`/`.htm` → **Preview** (you open an export to look at it), and the toggle lists the kind's default first.
- **`.html` preview = a SANDBOXED iframe (`src/lib/HtmlPreview.svelte`), the security-critical surface here.** A workspace `.html` is UNTRUSTED content (it is usually machine-generated: plotly/bokeh exports, nbconvert reports), so it renders the way rich `text/html` cell outputs do (`HtmlOutput.svelte`, the precedent — keep the two in step on the SECURITY posture, and only that; the two deliberate divergences are named below): `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-popups allow-downloads"` and **never `allow-same-origin`** (paired with `allow-scripts` that hands the file the app's origin — its DOM, cookies, `localStorage`, same-origin `fetch` — and the isolation is gone), fed by **`srcdoc`** so the app never serves workspace HTML as `text/html` from its own origin (a route that did would be a stored-XSS surface reachable outside any sandbox — the same reason `/api/fs/raw` serves images only). The content is passed **verbatim**: no wrapper document, no injected script, so a full `<!doctype html>` export renders as authored; the iframe ELEMENT paints white so a document declaring no background stays readable under a dark app theme. **That verbatim pass is the FIRST deliberate divergence, and it is a hard rule: NEVER inject `OUTPUT_HTML_CSS` (or any other stylesheet) here.** `HtmlOutput.svelte` injects it because it OWNS its srcdoc wrapper around a bare fragment the kernel emitted; a workspace `.html` is a finished export the user opened precisely to view AS AUTHORED, so restyling it - re-padding its tables, re-aligning its cells - would be a defect, not parity with the output surface. Do not read "keep the two in step" as an instruction to mirror that stylesheet. **`allow-downloads` is the second deliberate divergence from `HtmlOutput.svelte`** (a cell output is a different surface and keeps its own token set): it grants NO origin access, so the no-`allow-same-origin` invariant is untouched, and without it the browser blocks every in-page save - silently killing plotly's modebar "Download plot as PNG" on exactly the exports this preview targets. **HTML gets its own, higher size cap** (`MAX_HTML_FILE_BYTES` = 15 MB, resolved through the same `isHtmlPath`, never an ad-hoc extension test): self-contained is what makes these files big (plotly `write_html(include_plotlyjs=True)` ≈ 3.5 MB, bokeh `INLINE`, nbconvert base64 figures, Cellar's own `export_html`), so the ordinary 2 MB `MAX_FILE_BYTES` refused precisely the files the feature exists to open. It is raised, not removed, and `MAX_FILE_BYTES` still governs every other text file - both directions are pinned by unit tests. **Raising the read cap does NOT raise what git will decorate:** line-level decorations are gated at `MAX_DECORATION_BYTES` (= `MAX_FILE_BYTES`, in `git.ts`) - see the git-blame entry below. **The read cap and the WRITE cap are one pair, and live together in `src/lib/server/limits.js`** (no imports at all, but SERVER-only: it is reached exclusively through `$lib/server` (`fstree.ts`), so it rides the normal bundle into `build/` and is deliberately NOT in `package.json` `files` - the launcher does not import it). `writeWorkspaceFile` refuses a save past the SAME `maxFileBytesFor(relPath)` the reader enforces (measuring UTF-8 BYTES, and refusing *before* `writeFileSync` so the original file is untouched), because a save the reader will not reopen strands the file the moment its tab closes. **The TRANSPORT is the third limit, and Cellar deliberately does NOT raise it.** adapter-node caps a request body at **512 K** (`BODY_SIZE_LIMIT`), rejecting a save *before* the route handler runs - and that setting is read once at module load and applied upstream of EVERY route, so it cannot be relaxed for the save endpoint alone. Raising it would raise how much memory any unauthenticated request can make the app process buffer (adapter-node binds `0.0.0.0`, and that process also carries kernel streaming, SSE and the in-process MCP server), so `bin/cellar.js` leaves it alone. **Reading is untouched by this** - a file arrives in a GET *response*, so a 15 MB export still opens and previews. What changes is the SAVE direction: `src/lib/saveLimit.ts` (pure, browser-safe) computes the exact serialized size of the PUT body (`saveBodyBytes` walks the string once, allocation-free, applying JSON's own escaping rules - a raw-length guess would either refuse files that fit or admit files that 413) and `FileTab.svelte` decides from the loaded content whether the document `saveFitsTransport` - once per LOAD, plus once per external on-disk apply (see the re-decide note below), never per keystroke. The verdict is **exact but never paid for in full** - this runs on every file tab's load path, before it paints, and a 15 MB export is settled by its first ~512 KB: every UTF-16 code unit contributes AT LEAST one byte (a surrogate pair is 2 units → 4 bytes), so `content.length > limit` refuses in O(1) with no scan, and the count itself stops the moment it passes `limit`. Both shortcuts are provably conservative, so the boundary lands byte-for-byte where a full count puts it (swept across it per counter branch in the unit tests). **The ceiling it compares against is the one ACTUALLY in force, never a client-side guess** - only the server knows it, so `effectiveBodyLimit(process.env.BODY_SIZE_LIMIT, dev)` is computed in `GET /api/fs/file` and carried to the tab as `bodyLimit` (`resolveBodyLimit` reads it back; the 512 K `DEFAULT_BODY_SIZE_LIMIT` is only the fallback for a value that cannot be determined, and an unrecognised wire value falls to it rather than to "no cap"). That plumbing is load-bearing in both directions: **`cellar --dev` runs Vite, whose handler calls `getRequest()` with NO `bodySizeLimit` at all**, so under `--dev` nothing is view-only for transport reasons (a hardcoded 512 K made a 600 KB `.md` uneditable there); and an operator who sets `BODY_SIZE_LIMIT` genuinely widens the editable range, since the app-spawn env is a `process.env` spread AND the app now reports their value. `parseBodySizeLimit` mirrors adapter-node's own `parse_as_bytes` (`K`/`M`/`G` suffix, `Infinity` = uncapped). If not, the tab opens **view-only**: `EditorState.readOnly` + `EditorView.editable.of(false)` + **`EditorView.contentAttributes.of({tabindex:'0'})`**, no Save button, a `data-testid=file-view-only` chip saying why - and the preview fully available. **That tabindex is load-bearing, not decoration:** `editable.of(false)` sets `contenteditable="false"` and adds NO tabindex, so `.cm-content` - the element CodeMirror attaches its key handlers to - could not take focus at all (a click landed on `.cm-scroller`), silently costing keyboard selection and the editor's own search panel. **Cmd/Ctrl+S is owned by the TAB, not by any one view - the shell holds it, `FileTab` binds NO editor keymap.** A CodeMirror keymap fires only while `.cm-content` holds focus, which is false in every view where the editor is not the focused surface - a rendered preview hides it with `display:none`, and a `display:none` subtree can hold no focus at all - so in Preview (**the default view of every `.html`**) the keystroke reached Chrome's own "Save page as…" dialog and a dirty document went unsaved. So `+page.svelte`'s capture handler now answers for BOTH kinds: the active notebook, or the active file tab through `fileTabApis` (a `FileTabApiHandle {requestSave}` registry mirroring `notebookApis`; `activeTabIsNotebook` only picks WHICH). One owner - reintroducing the editor keymap would double-save. It saves when the document is saveable and, when it is not, pulses the view-only chip (`data-flash`) rather than adding a second copy of the same sentence, since a keystroke that appears to do nothing reads as a bug. The one case no parent handler can serve is a keystroke inside the preview's sandboxed iframe, which never reaches this document - the accepted price of the opaque origin. Deciding at LOAD is sound for TYPING precisely because a document ruled view-only is then read-only, so the user cannot grow it past the line it was measured against; a document edited past the limit after loading is caught by the backstop below. **The verdict is NOT frozen, though** - an EXTERNAL on-disk sync writes the document whatever `EditorState.readOnly` says, so the extensions live in a `Compartment` and the verdict is re-decided on every such apply, in both directions (see the external-edit entry below); it is still never re-decided per keystroke. A refused save is **visible**: `FileTab.svelte` keys oversize off the **413 status** (a front-end 413 carries no JSON message of ours, so parsing the body is what used to swallow it) into its own `saveError` state rendered in the tab header - deliberately NOT `errorMsg`/`status`, which would turn a perfectly loaded document into a load-error view - and it is cleared on the next `docChanged`, so it never keeps asserting something about content the user has already changed. The write cap's own refusal ("file too large to save (over the 15 MB limit)") is distinct from the transport 413 - both read honestly. **Known limitation, by design:** a `srcdoc` document inherits the PARENT's base URL, so a page that pulls sibling files (`<script src="report_files/x.js">`) cannot load them — the request goes to the app and 404s. `src/lib/htmlPreview.ts` `hasRelativeAssetRefs()` (pure, unit-tested) detects that case and the tab says so in one line rather than showing a mysteriously empty page. It is a quote-aware string scan (never a DOM parse) and **under-reporting is its contract** - a false notice on a genuinely self-contained export is worse than a missing one - so it skips comments, inert bodies (`<script>`/`<style>`/`<textarea>`/`<title>`/`<template>`), attribute VALUES, and a `<link>` whose `rel` is not one the browser fetches to render (`stylesheet`/`preload`/`modulepreload`; `canonical`/`alternate`/`icon`/absent do NOT convict), and an **absolute `<base href>`** (`https://cdn/x/`, or protocol-relative `//cdn/x/`) suppresses the notice outright since it rebases every relative ref - a deliberate whole-document PRE-scan (one regex), because `<base>` is legal AFTER the refs it rebases and the walk's early-exit on the first relative ref is what keeps a multi-MB export cheap; a root-relative/absent/unparseable `<base href>` does not suppress. Known misses named at the seam and deliberately not chased: `srcset`, `poster`, `url()` in CSS, and plain `<a href>`. **Do NOT "fix" it with a blob URL:** Chrome's blob-URL partitioning blocks blob fetches from an opaque origin, so that trades a 404 for a preview that does not render at all. Covered by `tests/unit/html-preview.test.ts` (incl. a source-level guard on the sandbox attribute — the isolation is one word wide) + `tests/e2e/html-file-preview.spec.ts` (the fixture probes `parent.document`/`localStorage` from inside the frame and asserts both are blocked).
- **An open file tab tracks EXTERNAL on-disk edits (`src/lib/server/fileWatch.ts` + the `FileTab.svelte` half).** A `FileTab` used to read its file exactly once, in `onMount` - no polling, no revalidation - so its content was a snapshot taken at open time forever, and a save from that stale tab wrote the snapshot back over whatever an agent had since written. The server now watches every OPEN file and publishes `file:changed` on the ordinary event bus (`events.ts` `publishGlobal` - no `nb`, no `seq`: each event is a full statement about one file, so a missed one self-heals); the tab applies it. Scoped to `file`-kind tabs, which is `.md` and every other text file, since `FileTab` is one component and the mechanism is content-type agnostic. **Notebooks are deliberately OUT** - `GET /api/fs/file` is the sole registration point and only `FileTab` calls it, so a `.ipynb`/jupytext doc (whose server-side in-memory doc, cell-id merge and per-run `persist()` make it a different problem) is untouched; the silent-clobber hazard there is real and is a separate task. **Three mechanism decisions are each forced by a MEASUREMENT** - re-measure before changing any, and `tests/unit/file-watch.test.ts` pins all three: (1) **watch the containing DIRECTORY, never the file** - `fs.watch(<file>)` binds to the inode and goes permanently silent at the first rename-replace, not only for that rename but for every later IN-PLACE write too, and rename-replace is exactly how an LLM edit tool writes (Claude Code's own `Edit`/`Write` were measured staging a sibling `<name>.tmp.<pid>.<hex>` and `rename()`ing it over the target), so a per-file watch appears to work in a one-edit hand test and then silently stops - the worst failure shape, which is why a **CONTROL test** pins that a naive per-file watch misses changes the directory watch catches; (2) **debounce (`SETTLE_MS`, 120ms of quiet) before reading** - bursts coalesce unevenly, and a NON-atomic writer (a shell redirect, a python script) is observable mid-write, so reading on the first event returned a 0-byte document; (3) **decide on a content HASH, not on the event** - macOS reports every `fs.watch(dir)` event as `rename`, including a plain overwrite, so the event name carries no information, and the same hash doubles as echo suppression (`noteKnownContent`, called by the `PUT` route BEFORE the write, since the event can arrive as soon as the bytes land) so a Cellar save never bounces back into the tab that made it. Deliberately NOT a recursive workspace watch (it fires for `.git`/`build/`/`.venv`/`node_modules` - the very `IGNORE_DIRS` `fstree.ts` exists to exclude - and every kernel run rewriting an `.ipynb` would ripple through it); watching only the directories holding open files bounds event volume by what the user has open, not by workspace size. Deliberately no `chokidar` (~15 transitive deps to replace this module; revisit only if it ever needs to become a recursive watch). **Registration is on READ, LRU-bounded** - the `GET` that opens a file IS the "someone has this open" signal (the server has no other; tabs are client state) and it already holds the content, which seeds the echo hash for free; nothing has to report a closed tab because `MAX_WATCHED_FILES` (64) evicts the coldest, so a killed browser leaks nothing. Cost is one handle per DIRECTORY, not per file. **That seed is FIRST-REGISTRATION ONLY - an EXISTING entry wins over the caller's content, and the ordering is load-bearing, not tidiness.** Every window's focus revalidation issues that same `GET`, so a read landing inside a pending settle window re-seeded the hash to the very content `settle` was about to deliver, `settle` then found no change and published NOTHING: the reading tab was fine (the content is in its own response) while every OTHER browser window silently missed the edit until its own focus - defeating precisely the multi-window live sync the no-unwatch-on-close rule exists to protect. Delivering once more to the tab that read it is the harmless direction (the client short-circuits when the content already matches). `noteKnownContent` is deliberately NOT the same case and must keep OVERWRITING: the `PUT` path is a statement that we wrote those bytes, i.e. the echo suppression itself - **but only once they LAND, which is why a failed write re-seeds through `resyncKnownContent`**: the declaration is made BEFORE the write (it has to be), so a write refused for size or failing on EACCES/ENOSPC left the hash describing bytes disk never got, and echo suppression then silently swallowed a later GENUINE external change producing exactly those bytes - the everyday agent shape, where the agent writes the very edit the user was racing to save. An explicit reseed is REQUIRED precisely because an existing entry now wins over a re-read's content, so nothing else would ever correct it. **`unwatchUnder(path)` is the one deliberate stop**, called by the explorer's delete/rename/move ops beside the `dropDocs`/`shutdownKernelsUnder` already there: no window can be watching a name that is gone, so its entry could only settle into a deletion nobody is listening for while holding an LRU slot a genuinely open file could use (and a renamed file's tab had no live sync until its next window focus). Folder-shaped like its two neighbours, since a folder op takes every file under it. **The rename/move calls sit behind the SAME `res.from && res.from !== res.path` guard the neighbouring `rekeyDocs` uses, and that guard is load-bearing**: `renameEntry` returns no `from` for a rename to the same name and `moveEntry` returns `from === path` for a same-parent move, so nothing moved on disk and the file is still open under that very name - unwatching there silently switches that tab's live sync off for good, since it never remaps, so it never remounts and never re-issues the read that is the sole registration point. **The invariant that makes the LRU a real bound is that a known-hash entry NEVER outlives the watcher behind it**, and there are two ways it can: a directory whose `fs.watch` THROWS (unsupported network mounts and container overlays really do; the entry was seeded before the attempt, so every read on such a filesystem added a permanent one the LRU could not prune - the catch deletes it), and a file past **`MAX_WATCHED_FILE_BYTES`** (= `MAX_FILE_BYTES`, 2 MB - the sibling of `git.ts`'s `MAX_DECORATION_BYTES`), which is refused at registration by a `statSync` BEFORE any state is recorded and dropped outright by `settle()` if it GROWS past the ceiling later. That ceiling is what bounds the one genuinely expensive thing here: every settled event is a synchronous whole-file `readFileSync` + sha256 on the process that also carries the kernel websockets, the ~40ms SSE fan-out and the in-process MCP server, and `readWorkspaceFile` admits `.html` up to `MAX_HTML_FILE_BYTES` (15 MB), so an agent regenerating a self-contained export in a loop charged a 15 MB read+hash per settle AND again on the refetch the inline gate forces. It is `MAX_FILE_BYTES` **uniformly, without the per-path `.html` exception**; the deliberate, accepted cost is that a >2 MB export no longer live-syncs and falls back to the tab's window-focus revalidation. Deliberately NOT a `statSync` size/mtime pre-check before each read - the 120ms settle debounce already collapses the multi-event writes that would catch, so it is cost with no bound. **The apply rule mirrors the notebook's "changed on server" banner** (`Cell.svelte`), because the hard thinking about never clobbering active typing was already done there: **clean ⇒ apply silently** (the common case and the thing being asked for), **dirty ⇒ do NOT touch the buffer**, stash it and offer Reload / Keep mine, **deleted ⇒ banner only whatever the dirty state** (the buffer is kept, a save recreates the file; clearing the editor would destroy work in response to something the user did not do here). "Keep mine" advances `savedContent` to what disk holds so the SAME change is not re-announced on every window focus, while a later DIFFERENT one still raises the banner. **A banner is a claim about what disk holds RIGHT NOW, so the "disk already equals what we believe" path RETIRES it rather than merely staying quiet** - that early return used to fire before anything could be cleared, so an agent that UNDID its own edit left a Reload button offering bytes disk no longer held (clicking it wrote them back and recorded them as saved), and a file deleted then restored with identical content kept its "deleted on disk" banner for good. Both clear `pendingDisk`/`preReadyDisk`/`diskState` first. Which is also why **`revalidateFromDisk` no longer short-circuits on equal content** and delegates to the shared `applyDiskObservation` instead: a second copy of "disk agrees" is how a stale banner survives the very backstop meant to catch it. **The pre-editor flush is the third caller and goes through the funnel too** (an event landing between the load `GET` and the `EditorView` existing): calling `applyDiskContent` straight lost both of the funnel's rules - the "disk agrees" short-circuit, so the common case where that event carries the same bytes the load returned ran a full apply including a redundant git-baseline + `git blame` pair, and the seq check, so an event whose settle-read predated the load's read reverted the editor. **That funnel is also where the ORDERING guard lives** (`diskSeq`/`claimDisk`, the `kernelReqSeq`/`statusSeq` convention this codebase already uses for unordered responses): two paths fetch asynchronously - the window-focus revalidation and the refetch for a file past the inline ceiling - so a slow GET resolving last used to REVERT the editor and then record its stale bytes as `savedContent`, which suppresses the correction, since the server's known hash is already the newer content's and no further event follows. Every path that settles what we believe disk holds claims the slot, **a synchronously-applied inline event, a save, a Reload and a Keep-mine included** - guarding only the fetching paths leaves the reported case (inline B applied, older GET A resolves and reverts) wide open. **The save's `setDirty(false)` is guarded on the buffer still matching what was written** for the same class of reason: keystrokes typed during the in-flight `PUT` are unsaved, and a false clean flag does not merely mislabel them - it routes the next external change down the SILENT-apply path instead of the Reload / Keep-mine banner, so they are overwritten without ever being offered (`savedContent` still advances either way - disk really does hold those bytes). A silent apply goes through **`src/lib/externalSync.ts` `externalEdits`** - the MINIMAL edits derived from `gitdiff.ts`'s Myers line diff, dispatched as ONE transaction carrying an `externalSync` **Annotation** (not a mutable flag, so an early return cannot leave it set) that the update listener reads to avoid reporting the tab dirty for content that came FROM disk. Whole-document replacement is the thing that rule exists to avoid: it throws the caret to the end, scrolls the view and wipes the undo history, i.e. the sync would be more disruptive than the staleness it fixes. It also assigns **`liveSource`** explicitly - that value is SAMPLED on entering a preview, never mirrored per keystroke, so without it the rendered markdown keeps showing pre-sync text while the hidden editor holds the new one (the preview is half the feature, not a bonus). Two further couplings: the event **inlines content only under `MAX_INLINE_EVENT_BYTES` (256 KB)** and otherwise announces path+hash for the tab to refetch (that SSE stream also carries kernel output deltas at ~40ms and pays per byte), with `deleted` an EXPLICIT flag so an omitted-because-large payload can never read as a deletion; and the window-`focus` handler that already re-fetched git HEAD/blame now also **revalidates content**, the backstop for an event a network/virtual filesystem drops, a file the watcher refuses for its size, or one the LRU evicted - **fired on the SSE `sse:open` frame too**, since `file:changed` is NOT replayed to a late subscriber the way `queue:changed`/`kernel:status` are, so an app-server restart (the single-instance takeover) or a transient drop otherwise left a tab permanently stale with `focus` unable to help, the window never having lost focus (`LiveNotebook` already refetches on that frame; `FileTab` was the one subscriber ignoring it). **That revalidation is STAT-FIRST** (`GET /api/fs/file/stat` -> `statWorkspaceFile`, size+mtime, no read; the content `GET` and the `PUT` return the same stat so the tab can record a baseline at the load, at an accepted observation and after a save): every file tab stays mounted, so one focus ran a read for each of them, and `readWorkspaceFile` admits an `.html` export up to 15 MB - the very file the watcher declines, so focus is its ONLY mechanism - putting a multi-MB read + `JSON.stringify` + transfer + whole-string compare on the shared event loop per alt-tab back into the browser, the exact workflow the feature exists for. **The invariant is that the stat may only ever cause an EXTRA read, never a MISSED change**, so every doubt takes the read path: no baseline recorded, a failed or unparseable stat, a file that is gone (a failure is emphatically NOT a deletion - reporting those is the watcher's job). Correctness therefore never depends on catching every path that moves what we believe disk holds; an observation carrying no stat (an inline event) CLEARS the baseline rather than keeping a stale one. Stated residual: a write leaving BOTH size and mtime identical is invisible here - a non-issue under `MAX_WATCHED_FILE_BYTES`, whose content hash the watcher checks each settle, and this fallback carries only the oversize and LRU-evicted cases. Note this is NOT a reversal of the "no `statSync` pre-check" decision on the WATCH path: there the 120ms settle debounce already collapses the redundant reads, while this path has no debounce and no change signal at all. **A sync also RE-DECIDES the view-only verdict, through a CodeMirror `Compartment`** (`viewOnlyExtensions`, reconfigured from `applyDiskContent` against the `bodyLimit` captured at load - the ceiling belongs to the running server, so it is never re-fetched): `saveTooLarge` was settled once from the loaded content on the premise that a read-only document cannot grow past the line it measured, and an external sync retires that premise - `view.dispatch` applies changes whatever `EditorState.readOnly` says. Frozen into the initial state it went stale BOTH ways: a file grown past the ceiling stayed editable and only failed as a 413 on save, and one shrunk back under it kept a now-false "too large" chip and no Save for the tab's life. Still never per keystroke - typing genuinely cannot cross the line in a read-only document. The banner takes the notebook banner's warning TINT but its copy is `base-content`, NOT `text-warning`: amber-on-amber measures 1.8:1 on that background in the light theme (far under WCAG AA), so the icon carries the signal and the words stay readable - and both buttons are equally quiet, since Reload DISCARDS unsaved edits and emphasising it would make the destructive choice the one a misclick lands on. Covered by `tests/unit/file-watch.test.ts` (real filesystem, real write patterns, no `fs` mocking - a mocked watcher would happily pass the design that is measurably broken) + `tests/unit/external-sync.test.ts` (the offset arithmetic at every document edge, checked by replaying the edits, since a wrong offset silently corrupts a file the user then saves) + `tests/e2e/md-external-sync.spec.ts` (the only level that proves the SSE hop and the CodeMirror apply: repeated temp+rename edits, burst coalescing, the RENDERED preview updating, the dirty no-clobber path in both directions, echo suppression over the real wire, caret+scroll preserved across a sync, a deletion, a banner retired when disk reverts or the deleted file comes back identical, the view-only verdict flipping in both directions as an external write crosses the transport ceiling, a stubbed-stale GET resolving after a newer inline change and changing nothing, characters typed during a held-open save surviving behind the banner, and a focus costing ONE stat and zero reads when nothing moved while a broken stat endpoint still reaches the read).
- **Sidebar** (`src/lib/Sidebar.svelte`, foldable sections; whole sidebar collapsible + **drag-resizable** via the `sidebar-resizer` handle in `+page.svelte`, width persisted to `localStorage['cellar-sidebar-width']`, bounds 180–560px): **Files** (`src/lib/FileTreeNode.svelte`, recursive), **Git** (`gitSection` -> `src/lib/GitNotebooks.svelte`, directly under Files where VS Code puts Source Control; mounted only while the section is OPEN, and it ships collapsed, so a user who never opens it pays no `git` subprocess - see the Sidebar Git section entry below), **Kernels** (`kernelsSection` in `Sidebar.svelte`: **one card per kernel** — Cellar runs one kernel PER notebook (Phase 3), so the section renders `{#each kernelCards}` (a `KernelCard[]` derived in `+page.svelte`) with the **live kernel count** in the header. The card set = every LIVE kernel (from `listKernels()` via `/api/kernel` + the `kernel:status` SSE snapshot, so a closed-tab-but-alive kernel still shows) **plus the ACTIVE notebook** even with no kernel (a just-opened notebook reads "not started"). So shutting down a **non-active** notebook's kernel drops its card, while the notebook you're looking at always has one. Each card carries its own status badge + **Interrupt / Restart (=wipe namespace) / Shut down (=free the process, drop the card)**, all disabled until that notebook has a live kernel (`card.hasKernel`); the card's name focuses its tab (`onFocusNotebook`) and the active one is dotted. Live updates arrive via the `kernel:status` global SSE snapshot (published from `kernel.ts` on start / busy-idle flip / restart / shutdown / rebind, seeded on connect + replayed to late subscribers like `queue:changed`), so two notebooks running show two busy cards with no reload. **The broadcast is deduped + has a coalescing (debounced) fast path, and a busy/idle flip fans out ONLY while a USER (non-internal) run is in flight** (`NotebookKernel.userRuns`, gated in `kernel.ts`'s `statusHandler`; `scheduleKernelStatus` coalesces a run's flip burst, `emitKernelStatus` skips a byte-identical snapshot). A flip caused solely by an internal probe (inspect/variables) or a startup injection leaves `userRuns` at 0 and is suppressed - so those never flicker a card nor fan a redundant snapshot to every tab. Do NOT restore an unconditional `publishKernelStatus()` on every flip; user runs still broadcast busy→idle because `execute()` increments `userRuns` (and schedules a broadcast at the run boundary). Seeding a fresh tab is independent (the SSE route calls `listKernels()` on connect), so a deduped-away duplicate is safe. Overridable debounce window: `CELLAR_KERNEL_STATUS_DEBOUNCE_MS` (default 80ms). Startup injections (matplotlib-inline + DataFrame formatter + `%restart_python` magic + project-root `sys.path`) are coalesced into ONE silent `initKernel` exec, not four serial round-trips. Per-card control-in-flight is tracked in `actingPaths`. The notebook list is the `openNotebooks` prop derived from the shell's open notebook tabs in `+page.svelte`, so it updates live as notebooks open/close - not the server `docs` map, which lazily materializes the default notebook on SSR and would show a phantom; its `active` flag reads the same `activeNotebookPath` the Outline/Search sections use, so every sidebar section agrees on which notebook is active. **`activeNotebookPath` is null when no notebook tab is open** - it falls back to the canonical notebook only while that notebook *has* an open tab, because `notebooksCells` is seeded with its SSR cells and an unconditional fallback would fill Outline/Search from a **closed** notebook's stale snapshot (and dot it in the Kernels list). Null means "no active notebook": those sections render empty, which is the honest answer. The status→badge mapping lives in exactly one place, `src/lib/kernelBadge.js` (`kernelBadgeClass`/`kernelStatusLabel`), imported by both `Sidebar.svelte` and the navbar badge in `Navbar.svelte` so the two cannot drift. Only `idle` may render green: `getKernelInfo()` forwards the kernel's status verbatim and the Jupyter status set also includes `restarting`/`autorestarting`/`terminating`/`unknown`, none of which mean "usable". Every card reads its badge from `card.info` through that one mapping, so a live and a not-started card can only differ where meant to. The top-level `/api/kernel` fields (+ the navbar badge / variables / Databricks panel) still track the **active** notebook's kernel via `kernelInfo`; the per-card list is separate. **Per-kernel resident memory (RSS)** rides the SAME `kernel:status` snapshot: each `KernelListEntry`/`KernelInfo` carries `memoryRss` (bytes|null), rendered compactly (`formatMemory` in `kernelBadge.ts`, e.g. `312 MB`) on each sidebar card (`data-testid=kernel-memory`) and next to the navbar badge. **Cellar has NO OS pid for a kernel** — `@jupyterlab/services` only holds the Jupyter `kernel.id`, and the kernel is a grandchild the sidecar spawns — so `src/lib/server/kernelMemory.ts` measures RSS **host-side, out-of-band**: one `ps -eo pid=,rss=,command=` scan (POSIX, KiB on macOS+Linux, the same pattern `instances.js` uses) matches each `kernel.id` in the process's `-f …/kernel-<id>.json` connection-file argument and reads its RSS. Measured from the OS, NOT via an in-kernel probe, precisely because an `execute` probe queues behind a running cell and could never report memory for a BUSY kernel (the array-allocating case the figure exists for). `kernel.ts` polls (`CELLAR_KERNEL_MEMORY_POLL_MS`, default 4s, unref'd, self-stops when no kernel remains), rounds to whole MiB (so a stable idle kernel doesn't churn the deduped broadcast), and re-publishes only on change. Main process RSS only (children excluded — Spark work runs on the remote cluster). The navbar figure is scoped to the ACTIVE notebook's OWN list entry (`activeKernelMemory` in `+page.svelte`, no fallback to `kernelInfo.memoryRss`) and gated by the badge's `started`, so it never shows a stale/other-notebook value beside a "not started" badge. Pure parse + format covered by `tests/unit/kernel-memory.test.ts`. **Kernel-status writes are generation-guarded** (`kernelReqSeq` in `+page.svelte`): `/api/kernel` responses are unordered, so a read issued before the kernel booted can resolve *after* the first run started and clobber `kernelInfo` back to not-started - the last response to *land* would win, not the last one issued. Every write bumps the generation; a response applies only if nothing newer landed while it was in flight. That guard is what lets `onRunEnd` decrement `runsInFlight` **synchronously** rather than awaiting a status round-trip (which would strand the counter if the refresh threw). `onRunStart` asserts the kernel started optimistically via `markKernelStarted()`, which also bumps the generation to supersede in-flight reads - so the card never flashes not-started while a first run boots the kernel), **Databricks** (`databricksSection` -> `src/lib/Databricks.svelte`; mounted lazily on first open, then kept mounted+hidden so collapsing does not discard the connection / cluster list / half-expanded catalog tree - and so a user who never opens it never pays for its python status probe), **Connect an agent** (`agentSection` in `Sidebar.svelte`, reframed around the zero-config flow: leads with an agent-ready banner when a project `.mcp.json` registers the `cellar` stdio server — detected server-side in `+page.server.js` load through **`harnessState('claude', …)`** - never a second copy of "is this entry ours" (its own `…cellar?.command === 'cellar'` test ignored `args`, so the banner claimed agent-ready over an entry the very next launch would rewrite), still degrading to false on any read/parse trouble rather than throwing during SSR - surfaced as `data.mcp.projectConfigured` ALONGSIDE `projectManaged` (`isHarnessAllowed('claude', …)`), because the two are INDEPENDENT and the banner may only claim the every-start repair when the harness is really managed: `cellar harness remove claude` deliberately leaves the entry in place, so a configured harness can stop being maintained, and the unconditional "checked and repaired on every start" copy then promised a self-heal that would not happen - the assert-more-than-was-verified defect. Unmanaged, it says what is true (the entry is there, Cellar is not maintaining it, `cellar harness add claude` puts it back under management), in the SAME banner shape. A THIRD state sits beside those two, `projectRepairPaused`: `--no-mcp-config` filters what THIS launch reconciles without touching the allow-list, so the harness stays managed while nothing is being checked right now - the flag is per-launch, so the page load cannot infer it from the marker and the launcher passes it through the app spawn env (`CELLAR_NO_MCP_CONFIG`, the excluded harness derived from `mcpJsonHarnessNames()`, never pinned). It is reported only for a managed harness, and its copy names the remedy that actually applies (restart without the flag, NOT `harness add`). With a `--no-mcp-config` fallback message; shows the one-time `claude mcp add cellar -- cellar mcp` command with a copy button; then an **"Another harness"** block (`data-testid=mcp-harness-command`, a copy row styled identically to the one above it) carrying `cellar harness add codex`, because the banner is a claim about Claude Code ALONE - Codex reads its own file and ignores `.mcp.json`, so the panel must not read as "every agent is ready" (see the Harness MCP setup entry); demotes the live raw `/mcp` Streamable-HTTP URL + config snippet under an "Advanced · raw endpoint" disclosure. The raw URL still comes from `data.mcp.url` reading `CELLAR_MCP_PORT`), **Outline** (one row per heading, nested by level, from the shared `headings.js` `outlineRows(cells, foldedIds, counts)` - so it lists exactly the headings the notebook draws chevrons for). **Every** row has a fold chevron (`data-testid=outline-toggle`), not just rows with nested children: a leaf `## Usage` still owns the code cells under it. Clicking it folds that section in the notebook, and a fold made in the notebook shows here - one state, `foldedIds`, reached through the `onToggleFold` prop (see the Collapsible-headings entry). A folded row is tinted and carries an `…N` hidden-cell marker. The old per-outline `localStorage['cellar-outline-collapsed']` state is gone - it was the second, diverging copy), **Variables**, **Search**. Sections render from a persisted order array (`localStorage['cellar-sidebar-order']`) and are **drag-reorderable** via a per-section grip handle (`data-testid=section-drag-<key>`, native HTML5 DnD, no library); each section's open/closed state persists to `localStorage['cellar-sidebar-open']`. The saved order is untrusted input (hand-edited, or written by an older build), so `src/lib/sidebarSections.js` owns both the canonical `DEFAULT_SECTION_ORDER` and the pure `reconcileSectionOrder()` that merges it with the defaults: unknown, non-string, and **duplicate** ids are dropped (a duplicate would otherwise reach the keyed `{#each}` and throw Svelte's `each_key_duplicate`, discarding the user's whole order), and a section absent from the saved order - i.e. one added since the user last dragged - is restored **beside its default neighbour** (after the nearest preceding default sibling that survived, else before the nearest following one), never appended to the end where a new section would sit below the user's entire layout. Every default section comes back exactly once for any input. Adding a section = one entry in `DEFAULT_SECTION_ORDER` + a `sectionBody` branch; the reconcile rule then places it for existing users. Section bodies are Svelte `{#snippet}`s dispatched by key so order is data-driven.
- **Notebook search engine (`src/lib/search.ts`) — P1-P5 of the phased find-in-page plan (COMPLETE; Search is the Ctrl/Cmd+F surface)** (design: `firstmate/data/cellar-search-findgrade-s4/report.md`). Pure `searchNotebook(cells, query, opts, cache): Match[]` over the **client model** `LiveNotebook.cells` (never the DOM), so it stays complete under virtualization. Returns **every** match in document order (not one-per-cell) so a caller can show a real `i/total`; each `Match` carries the `field` it came from (`source`/`markdown`/`output`/`id`, + `outputIndex`) so a later phase highlights the right surface. Substring, case-insensitive default (`SearchOpts` carries `caseSensitive`/`wholeWord`/`regex` — all honored: **`regex` (P5)** compiles the query once per call via `compileRegex(query,opts)` (`i` flag unless caseSensitive; `g` always), scanned by `scanFieldRegex` on the ORIGINAL text — the literal path keeps its cached-lowercase optimization untouched; an **invalid pattern returns `[]`, never throws** (the fail-safe the find-bar's invalid state reads), and a zero-length-capable match (`^`/`\b`/`a*`) nudges `lastIndex` so it can't spin). **`scope` is honored and defaults to `'all'`** (captain Q1 decision): `'all'` searches source + **rendered-markdown text** + **output text**; `'source'` narrows back to raw code/markdown source (P1 behavior). `DEFAULT_SEARCH_OPTS.scope` is `'all'` — unit tests that want the P1 source-only contract must pin `scope:'source'` explicitly. **Cell ids are a searchable field too (`field:'id'`, `scope:'all'` only), so the find bar answers a cell's HANDLE**: a cell surfaces its id in the toolbar `cell #xxxxxxxx` chip (which survives a full collapse), in multi-select and to agents, so typing it is a navigation Search should serve. The rule is the ONE exported predicate `matchesCellId` (used by the engine AND by `Cell.svelte`'s chip highlight, so a painted highlight and the count can never disagree): a **prefix** of the id — never a substring, matching the agent surface's `resolveCellId` — **floored at `ID_HANDLE_MIN` (8, kept in lockstep by value with `MIN_HANDLE` in `mcp/cellHandle.ts`, which a browser module may not import)**. That floor is the load-bearing part: a UUID is hex + dashes, so without it an ordinary short content query (`ca`, `de`, `abc`) is a valid id prefix and would silently address whichever cells happen to start with it, corrupting the `i/N` of a plain content search. **Regex mode matches no ids** (a pattern has no prefix semantics — `.*` would tag every cell), and `wholeWord` is not applied (an id is one opaque token; a boundary after the prefix would reject every prefix short of the whole UUID, i.e. the handle itself). One match per cell, emitted BEFORE that cell's content matches, so a cell matching by both counts as id + content and neither is double-counted; `dedupeMatchesForDisplay` leaves it alone. The view side is a fourth surface in `Cell.svelte`'s `applyHighlights` (`K_ID`, the `cell-id-copy` chip) which — deliberately unlike the source/output surfaces — is **NOT gated on `!cellCollapsed`**: the toolbar is exactly what a collapsed cell keeps on screen, so an id match there is the one match that stays visible (the good case). Navigation needs nothing new — `jumpToCell` mounts/reveals/flashes as for any match. The MCP `search_cells` tool is deliberately untouched (a separate implementation, and an agent already addresses a cell by handle directly). Covered by the cell-ids block in `tests/unit/search.test.ts`, the `id` case in `search-highlight.test.ts`, an id-query case in `search-perf.test.ts` (whose N=300 harness now carries real UUID ids, so every query at/past the floor really does prefix-test all 300), plus `find-bar.spec.ts` (prefix + full + below-floor, windowed-out) and `find-highlight.spec.ts` (the chip highlight, collapsed and not). **Rendered-markdown** is derived model-side by `strippedMarkdown(source)` (a light md-syntax strip — headings/emphasis/links/code/list markers — so a heading's *visible* words match; never parses HTML/DOM). **Output text** is extracted browser-safe by mirroring `Cell.svelte`'s `renderOutput` (stream text, `text/plain` repr, ANSI-stripped tracebacks, and the structured `application/vnd.cellar.dataframe+json` payload flattened to header+index+cell values); rich non-text output (image/plotly/iframe-html with no `text/plain`) contributes nothing. **The mechanical primitives (`asText`/`stripAnsi`/`DataFramePayload`) live in `$lib/outputText.ts` and are shared with `$lib/copyCell.ts`, but the PRIORITY above is deliberately NOT** - it differs in two places (see the copy-input/copy-output note in the UI entry), so changing what an output contributes as text means deciding for both surfaces, not editing one. Output scanning is **capped per cell at `SEARCH_SCAN_CAP=100_000`** (own constant, kept in lockstep with the MCP `search_cells` cap in `mcp/service.ts` — NOT imported, to keep server code out of the browser bundle) so one query can't serialize megabytes; a match past the cap in one giant output is the accepted miss. **The perf primitive is a per-cell searchable-text cache** (`SearchCache = Map<cellId, CacheEntry>`) with **independent invalidation per field**: `sourceLC`/`markdownLC` key off a cheap content **signature** (`contentSignature` = FNV-1a folded with length — an integer pass, no allocation) so a source edit rebuilds them in place (the entry object is REUSED, not replaced — do not re-assert object identity on edit), while the **outputs cache keys off the outputs' ELEMENT identities** (length + per-element ref, snapshotted in `outputElems`) — NOT the `cell.outputs` array identity, because LiveNotebook grows/rewrites that array IN PLACE during a run (each mutation replaces the element object at an index) so array identity would serve stale mid/post-stream text — so editing source never re-extracts unchanged outputs and vice-versa. A cache hit reuses the lowercased buffers and never re-folds case; lowercasing/extraction is once-per-content-change, not per-keystroke. Snippet extraction is **bounded** (`snippetFrom` reads ≤`SNIPPET_CAP`+64 chars from a line-start tracked in O(1) by `scanField`), so a pathological single 100k-char line with thousands of matches never costs O(lineLength) per match. **The cache is owned by `LiveNotebook`** (`createSearchCache()`, registered up via `onRegisterSearchCache` → `+page.svelte` `searchCaches[path]` → `activeSearchCache` → Sidebar's `searchCache` prop); the sidebar falls back to a private cache when none is registered. **Sidebar Search** (`Sidebar.svelte`) is rewired onto it: query **debounced ~120ms**, a **Source/All scope toggle** (`data-testid=search-scope`, default All), `searchNotebook` → `groupByCell` gives one row per cell (navigation to the cell, unchanged) with a per-cell count badge + a real **total match count** (`data-testid=search-count`). Match offsets/lines assume length-preserving case folding (true for realistic scripts; the pathological expand-on-lower like `İ` is a known limit). Covered by `tests/unit/search.test.ts` (positions/counts/order/unicode; output+markdown coverage; per-cell cap; per-field cache invalidation; toLowerCase-call-delta proofs) + `tests/unit/search-perf.test.ts` (N=300 single-call within a frame; N=300 with multi-MB outputs stays bounded; steady-state flat vs cold). **P3 — the floating find-bar (`src/lib/FindBar.svelte`).** A find-in-page widget over the top-right of the active notebook pane (mounted once in `+page.svelte`'s `<main>`, `data-testid=find-bar`): a query box, a live **`i / N`** count (`find-count`), prev/next chevrons, and case (`Aa`, `find-case`) / whole-word (`\b`, `find-word`) / **regex (`.*`, `find-regex`, default OFF, P5)** toggle chips. It runs the SAME `searchNotebook` engine + shared `activeSearchCache` as the sidebar (never a second engine; the sidebar list view **stays**), deduped for display so the count is visible-occurrences. Keyboard: **Enter**=next, **Shift+Enter**=prev, **F3/Shift+F3**=next/prev (a window capture listener so they work with focus anywhere), **Escape** closes and returns focus to the notebook; opening seeds the query from the current text selection (`window.getSelection()`). **Entry points (P5 makes Ctrl/Cmd+F the primary one):** the `open-find` shortcut is bound to **`Mod-f` AND `Mod-Shift-f`** (`shortcuts.svelte.ts`), handled shell-level in `+page.svelte`'s capture keydown beside `command-palette`/`save-notebook` - it **intercepts the browser's native find** (`preventDefault` + `stopPropagation`) and opens this bar instead, ONLY when a notebook is active and no modal is up (a plain file tab / a modal lets Ctrl+F fall through to the browser). A repeat Ctrl+F while the bar is **already open** re-focuses + re-seeds it (native-find behavior) rather than closing: `openFindBar()` bumps a **`reseedSignal`** the bar watches (do NOT toggle `findOpen` on a repeat). Also opens from the sidebar Search-section button (`open-find-bar`). **Regex fail-safe:** an invalid pattern shows a subtle `find-invalid` "bad regex" state (input tinted `text-error`, `aria-invalid`) and matches nothing - never a crash. **The single navigation seam is `LiveNotebook.jumpToCell(id, opts?)`** (on `NotebookApiHandle`, reached via `activeNotebookApi()`), built on **`ensureCellMounted(id)`** — reveal (unfold) + `pinScrollTarget` + `tick` + query the node — the ONE primitive that makes an off-screen cell reachable; `revealRunning`/`followCell`/keyboard selection share it, and virtualization P4 routed the sidebar outline + search rows onto the same `jumpToCell` (see the virtualization entry). **This is the virtualization cooperation:** under windowing (the default since P5) a match in a windowed-out (spacer) cell still navigates because `jumpToCell` mounts it before scrolling. `jumpToCell` centers + flashes the whole cell (P3's §5.3 fallback, still the coarse "you're here" cue); match-precise scroll + in-place highlight is P4. Covered by `tests/e2e/find-bar.spec.ts` (count, next/prev/F3 in doc order + wrap, Escape+focus, seed-from-selection, folded-section unfold, virtualization windowed-out jump, case/word toggles). **P4 — in-place highlight of every match + active-match emphasis (view-only overlay, mounted cells only).** The find-bar (sole owner of the search) publishes its query/opts/deduped matches/active index into ONE shared `$state` `SearchHighlightState` (`+page.svelte`, `searchHighlight.ts`), scoped to the searched notebook by `notebookPath === path` in `LiveNotebook` (cell ids repeat across the several mounted notebooks, so this gate is what keeps a background notebook dark). `searchHighlight.ts` (pure, unit-tested) maps the flat match list onto a per-cell payload via **`buildCellHighlights(matches, activeIndex)`** (a cell appears iff it has ≥1 match; exactly one carries the `active` descriptor `{field, outputIndex?, ordinal}` — `ordinal` = the active match's position among the cell's matches of the same field-group), and exposes **`findOccurrences`**, the literal substring/whole-word scanner the DOM highlighters reuse (mirrors the engine's `scanField`, so highlighted spans match the count). Two paint mechanisms, split by surface, **never mutating `cells`** (mirrors the virtualization render-only invariant): (1) **`domHighlight.ts`** — the **CSS Custom Highlight API** for the *rendered* surfaces (`static-code` `.cm-static-content`, `markdown-rendered`, output `outputInner`, plus the toolbar `cell-id-copy` chip - the fourth surface, added with cell-id search above, and the one that is NOT gated on `!cellCollapsed`): `buildTextRanges` walks a container's text nodes (concatenate + binary-search offset→node, so a match spanning inline syntax-highlight tokens still paints; a typeset-math subtree is substituted by its source TeX and the returned array is **POSITIONAL with `null` holes** — see the TeX-math entry, index by ordinal and never compact it) and registers two named highlights, `cellar-search` (all) + `cellar-search-active` (priority-1, distinct opaque style), rebuilt from the union of live per-surface contributions (coalesced to one microtask); keys are per-cell-INSTANCE (`allocSurfaceKey()`, never the cell id). A reversible `<mark>`-wrap fallback covers engines without the API (Chromium always has it). (2) **`cmSearchHighlight.ts`** — for a BUILT editor, a decoration ViewPlugin driven by `setCmSearch` reusing `@codemirror/search`'s `SearchQuery` matcher + the theme's existing `.cm-searchMatch`/`.cm-searchMatch-selected` classes (NOT `@codemirror/search`'s own highlighter — it paints only when its panel is open). So a code cell's source highlights on its StaticCode stand-in (CSS) until its editor is summoned, then on CM decorations — the active emphasis is a `cellar-search-active` range OR a `cm-searchMatch-selected` node accordingly. `Cell.svelte`'s `applyHighlights` (a `$effect` that re-runs on query/opts/active/editorBuilt/mode/codeHidden/**cellCollapsed**/outputs/source, applied after a `tick`; that collapse dep is load-bearing, not tidiness - see the Full-cell collapse entry) paints its visible surfaces + scrolls the active match into view (`block:'nearest'` + `EditorView.scrollIntoView`); clears on find-close (query empties) and on cell unmount. Colors: `--cellar-search-all` / `--cellar-search-active-bg`/`-fg` in `app.css` (`::highlight()` + `mark.cellar-search-mark*`), theme-aware. Both DOM highlighters (`findOccurrences`) and the CM decoration plugin (`SearchQuery({regexp})`) honor `regex`, so a regex query highlights the SAME spans the count reports. Covered by `tests/unit/search-highlight.test.ts` (findOccurrences + buildCellHighlights, incl. regex) + `tests/e2e/find-highlight.spec.ts` (all-surface highlight incl. StaticCode CSS AND built-editor CM path, single active emphasis that follows next/prev, active scrolled into view, view-only no-`<mark>` proof, clear-on-close, virtualization windowed-out cell highlights once navigated-to). **P5 (find-in-page COMPLETE):** Ctrl/Cmd+F intercept + regex toggle landed (see the P3 entry's entry-points note); **print safety** - because virtualization windows cells out of the DOM, `+page.svelte` registers `beforeprint`/`afterprint` that flip a `printing` flag and `flushSync()` so an `effectiveVirtualize = virtualizeCells && !printing` derived un-windows (mounts every cell) before the print snapshot, then restores. Covered by `tests/e2e/find-ctrlf.spec.ts` (intercept prevents native find, seed-from-selection, repeat re-seeds not closes, regex on/off/invalid, and beforeprint mounts all cells under virtualization).
- **Shell backend.** `src/lib/server/fstree.js` + `src/routes/api/fs/{tree,file,file/stat}` — workspace listing, file read/write, and the read-free `stat` (size+mtime) a file tab revalidates against, all path-guarded to the workspace root. `src/lib/server/inspect.js` + `src/routes/api/kernel/variables` — variable inspector; runs a Python probe through `execute()` (starts from IPython `user_ns` minus `user_ns_hidden`, drops modules/functions/classes + typing/magics scaffolding). `src/routes/api/kernel` + `getKernelInfo()` (added to `kernel.js`) — read-only kernel status that never boots a kernel. Variables refresh after each run - the tab's OWN run immediately, a FOREIGN one coalesced + scoped to the active notebook (see the foreign-action-refresh entry above) - and **unconditionally on mount**, since `inspectVariables` short-circuits a not-started kernel server-side and never boots one.
- **Kernel controls (per notebook).** Each kernel card has **Interrupt / Restart / Shut down** buttons (`data-testid=kernel-interrupt`/`kernel-restart`/`kernel-shutdown`), disabled until that notebook has a live kernel. They POST `src/routes/api/kernel/{interrupt,restart,shutdown}` with `{path}` (the notebook, resolved via `resolveNotebookPath`), which call `interruptKernel(nb)`/`restartKernel(nb)`/`shutdownKernel(nb)` in `kernel.ts` — restart is the same restart-over-same-session path the MCP interface uses (namespace clears, process/entry kept, doc survives = "wipe from memory"), while **shutdown** (`teardownKernel` → removes the Map entry) terminates the process and drops the card; the notebook lazily gets a fresh kernel on its next run. Other notebooks are untouched. The page drops stale inspector rows only when the **active** notebook's kernel is restarted/shut down. `listKernels()`/`shutdownKernel()` are covered by `tests/unit/kernel-manager.test.ts`.
- **Kernel lifecycle & memory (Phase 6 - completes the kernel-per-notebook migration).** With N kernels = N Python processes (100s of MB each with pandas/pyspark), three safety nets keep RAM bounded. (1) **Idle culling** leans on the sidecar's own `MappingKernelManager` culler, configured in `bin/cellar.js` `cullingArgs()`: `CELLAR_KERNEL_IDLE_TIMEOUT` (default **7200s/2h**, `0` disables) + `CELLAR_KERNEL_CULL_INTERVAL` (default `min(300, timeout)`). **`cull_connected=True` is load-bearing** - Cellar holds a persistent websocket to every kernel, so with jupyter's default (`false`) an idle kernel is "connected" and never culled. Node-side reconciliation: `kernel.ts` `getManager()` connects the `KernelManager.runningChanged` poll (~10s) to `reconcileCulledKernels()`, which tears down any Map entry whose connection vanished from the server's running list (the manager also disposes that connection itself). A culled kernel therefore drops its sidebar card (`publishKernelStatus`) and bumps its epoch, so its cells read `not_run`/`ran_this_session:false` - the run-status doctrine stays honest. (2) **Soft cap**: `src/lib/kernelCap.ts` (`parseMaxKernels`/`isOverKernelCap`, `DEFAULT_MAX_KERNELS=8`) drives a **warn-only** banner in the Kernels sidebar past `CELLAR_MAX_KERNELS` live kernels (`0` disables) - it **never blocks a run** (the Nth run still lazily starts its kernel); surfaced via SSR `data.maxKernels`. (3) **Shutdown-on-delete**: `shutdownKernelsUnder(path)` (in `kernel.ts`) frees the kernel process of a deleted notebook (or every notebook under a deleted folder), wired into `src/routes/api/fs/op/+server.js`'s `delete` case beside `dropDocs`; idempotent. All three route through **`teardownKernel`**, now idempotent (removes the Map entry synchronously up front) with an `{alreadyGone}` opt-out of the REST shutdown for an already-culled kernel, and it publishes a per-notebook **`kernel:shutdown`** event (`publish`, has `nb`+`seq`) so `LiveNotebook` refetches staleness/run-status (cells read "not run this session"); manual shutdown + `rebindKernel()` fan-out (venv change → tears down EVERY live kernel + clears each queue) emit it too. Tab close does NOT shut a kernel down (keep-alive until cull/manual), and kernels are NOT pre-warmed on tab open (both deliberate - lazy-start's memory win). Covered by `tests/unit/kernel-lifecycle.test.ts` (cull reconciliation, shutdown-on-delete, rebind fan-out) + `tests/unit/kernel-cap.test.ts`.
- **Modal keyboard + shortcut registry.** Jupyter-style command/edit modes. **`src/lib/shortcuts.svelte.js` is the single source of truth**: `DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS` declares every binding as `{id, keys, mode:'command'|'edit'|'global', description, category}`, and the `shortcuts` store merges user rebindings (`localStorage['cellar-shortcuts']`, only overridden ids stored, so a later change to a default reaches every user who never touched it) over them. Bindings are *chord* strings in CodeMirror's syntax (`Mod-Shift-ArrowUp`, `Shift-Enter`, `j`), produced from an event by `chordFromEvent` and rendered for display by `chordTokens`. A binding may also be a **space-separated chord sequence** (`d d` = delete cell): the dispatcher holds the leading chords as a pending prefix (`SEQUENCE_TIMEOUT_MS`, resolved by `resolveChord`) and fires only on the full match, so a lone `d` does nothing; the prefix is discarded the moment the mode changes, which is what keeps it from leaking into an editor. Sequence length is unconstrained, so Jupyter's `i i` / `0 0` need no new machinery. `bindingsCollide` treats a prefix-vs-exact pair (`d` vs `d d`) as a conflict, since the shorter would always win. A chord names the **unshifted** key (`Ctrl-Shift--`, as JupyterLab writes it): `chordFromEvent` reads the key token off `e.code` when Shift is held together with a real modifier (`SHIFTED_BASE_KEY`), because the browser reports `_` for that keystroke. Split-cell is the one platform-conditional entry (`Ctrl` on macOS, `Mod` elsewhere - Jupyter means the literal Ctrl key on both). **Adding a shortcut = a registry entry + an action in `LiveNotebook`'s `actions` map; nothing else may bind a notebook key** (Cell's CodeMirror deliberately carries no run/escape keymap - it was removed so there is exactly one dispatcher). **An action may return `false` to mean NOT HANDLED**, and the dispatcher then leaves the keystroke completely alone - no `preventDefault`, no `stopPropagation`, so it bubbles on. That opt-out exists because the dispatcher is a **capture-phase window** listener covering the whole app: consuming a key an action did nothing with silently kills every other listener for it (the `Escape` that `clear-selection` binds is also how the sidebar dismisses its context menu, from a plain bubble-phase window listener). Anything else - a promise from an async action included - counts as handled, so an action has to decline deliberately; `clear-selection` declines when the selection is already a single cell. `LiveNotebook.onKeydown` is that dispatcher: one window capture-phase listener, registered only while that notebook is the active tab, so it beats CodeMirror + the browser. It reads the keystroke's mode **off the DOM** (`target.closest('.cm-editor')` -> edit, else command), never off the `keyMode` state - that is what guarantees a command-mode letter (`j`, `a`) can never fire while the user is typing; it also bails on `.modal-open`, on form fields, and on targets outside this notebook's `rootEl`. `keyMode` (visuals only) is driven purely by the editor's own focus/blur, so the indicator cannot drift from the DOM. Each `Cell` hands up an imperative API via `onRegister(id, {focus, focusCell, blur, enterEdit, editorOverlayOpen, run, isMarkdown, currentSource, cursorOffset, replaceSource})` (this replaced the old `focusers` registry); `run` goes through `doRun` so a shortcut always uses the editor's *live* text, not the 500ms-debounced `cell.source`. Cut/copy (`x`/`c`), the heading keys and split-cell compose the last three primitives: `currentSource` is the live editor text, and `replaceSource` rewrites the doc through the remote-apply path (cancels the debounce, suppresses the echo) leaving the **caller** to persist - never call one without the matching `editCell`. The selected cell shows a mode-colored gutter bar + ring + `cmd`/`edit` badge (`info` = command, `success` = edit - semantic tokens, not hardcoded hues, so every theme stays coherent). A newly activated notebook tab focuses its `rootEl` (`tabindex=-1`) so command keys work without first clicking a cell. `keyMode` and the `onRegister`/`onEditorFocus`/`onEditorBlur` props must stay wired all the way down (`LiveNotebook` → `Notebook` → `Cell`): drop any one and the whole modal keyboard goes silently dead, since `cellApis` never fills and the mode never leaves `command`. Fold shortcuts (`h`/Left, `l`/Right) reuse `toggleFold`, and `j`/`k` skip cells a folded heading hides.
  **Focus must follow the selection.** Because the dispatcher reads a keystroke's mode *and* its target off the focused element, a selection the focus doesn't track is a selection the next key doesn't act on (it would reach whatever button or rendered-markdown body the user last clicked). So the cell card itself is focusable (`tabindex=-1`, `outline-none`; its ring already marks selection) and every command-mode selection change goes through `LiveNotebook.selectAndFocus` (= `selectAndAct(id, focusCell)`, which MOUNTS the target through `ensureCellMounted` before it lands - an unmounted cell has no registered API, so under windowing the focus would go nowhere and take the whole modal keyboard with it; see the virtualization entry) -> `Cell.focusCell()`: `j`/`k`, `a`/`b`, a command-mode move, Escape out of an editor, `Shift+Enter`'s advance, and a **local** delete (a remote/agent delete only moves the selection - it must not yank the caret out of a cell this user is typing in). `Cell.focus()` means "advance into this cell": its editor, or the cell itself when it is rendered markdown (that editor is `display:none` and cannot take focus). `toggleFold`/`setAllFolded` likewise re-seat the primary when a fold hides it - command mode may never act on a cell the user cannot see - but they **RE-SEAT, they do not collapse**: the primary moves to the nearest still-visible MEMBER of the selection (`cellSelection.ts`'s `reseatHiddenPrimary`), so the set the user built survives a chevron (a selection may legitimately HOLD fold-hidden cells - a Shift range fills in by document index and select-all takes the whole order). Only when the fold hides EVERY member is there nowhere inside the selection to put the primary, and only then does it collapse onto the cell holding the header that swallowed it - through `selectOnly`, never a bare `activeId` write, since the primary must stay a member or `pruneSelection`'s `kept.add(activeId)` would silently widen the set on the next refetch.
  **Cut / copy / paste / undo-delete.** Cut, copy and delete address the WHOLE selection (see the Multi-cell selection entry below); the clipboard (`src/lib/cellClipboard.ts`) is a plain module singleton shared by every notebook in the tab (copy here, paste there - Jupyter's behavior), deliberately **not** the system clipboard (the toolbar's copy-input/copy-output buttons are the separate, system-clipboard copy - see the UI entry; the two never share a buffer); an entry is `{cell_type, source, output_scrolled}` and carries **no outputs**, since a pasted output would be a saved result the live kernel never produced. Undo (`z`) is a per-notebook, **local-only** stack: it records what *this* user deleted, so it can never resurrect a cell an agent (or another tab) removed. Paste/undo/split all go through `insertCellAt`, which seeds the new cell server-side via `addCell(afterId, cellType, nb, originId, source, cellar)` - one persist, one `cell:added` event carrying the real text plus whichever durable `cellar` keys `seedCellar` allowlists (undo restores the whole namespace, split inherits `splitInheritedCellar`'s subset, a paste carries only its view choice) - and appends-then-hoists for an insert at index 0 (the add API can only insert *after* an id). `deleteCell` enforces the notebook's **≥1 cell** invariant (the same one the toolbar's delete button enforces by disabling at one cell), so `dd` and `x` are no-ops on a lone cell rather than leaving nothing to type in.
  **Escape is CodeMirror's first.** The dispatcher hands Escape back to CM whenever CM has an overlay of its own open (`Cell.editorOverlayOpen()` = `completionStatus() != null || searchPanelOpen()`), so Escape closes the completion tooltip or the search panel before it ever exits to command mode - Jupyter's behavior. Keystrokes inside a `.cm-panel` (the search bar) are CM's entirely. This is why `@codemirror/autocomplete` and `@codemirror/search` are **direct** dependencies, not just transitive ones under `codemirror`.
  **`Shift+Enter` is one path for both cell types**: `Cell.doRun(advance)` renders a markdown cell / runs a code cell, then `runAndAdvance` selects the next (creating one at the end) and moves focus there - into its editor from edit mode, onto the cell from command mode. A markdown cell never re-enters its own editor on `Shift+Enter`.
  `Settings.svelte` renders the registry grouped by category and rebinds a chord in place, warning on same-mode collisions. A chord that would **type a character** (no modifier but Shift; `typesACharacter` in the registry) bound to an `edit`/`global` shortcut makes that character untypable in every cell: Settings **warns and confirms, but never refuses** (`shortcut-hazard-confirm`, then a standing `shortcut-hazard-warning` on the row). The freedom is deliberate; the surprise is what's refused.
- **Multi-cell selection (`src/lib/cellSelection.ts` + `LiveNotebook.svelte`).** Cells select as a SET, not one at a time. `LiveNotebook.svelte` is the authoritative model and holds three deliberately separate pieces: **`activeId`** (the PRIMARY - what takes DOM focus, what a single-cell op addresses, the moving end of a range), **`anchorId`** (where a Shift range starts from; kept apart from `activeId` so a second Shift+click re-ranges from the same origin, the platform convention), and **`selectedIds`** (the selection, which ALWAYS contains `activeId` - so a plain single selection is the degenerate `{activeId}` and every bulk op is the single-cell op at size 1, which is what keeps the common case from regressing). Every rule is a pure function in `$lib/cellSelection` (`rangeIds`/`toggled`/`applyGesture`/`extendSelection`/`moveSelectionPlan`/`selectionAfterRemoval`), unit-tested in `tests/unit/cell-selection.test.ts`. **Gestures:** plain click collapses; **Shift**+click ranges contiguously from the anchor; **Cmd/Ctrl**+click toggles one cell (non-contiguous); **Shift+J/K** and **Shift+Arrows** extend; **Mod+A** selects all; Escape / a plain click / entering an editor collapses. **`selectAllCells` takes the FULL `cellOrder`, fold-hidden cells INCLUDED** - one rule for what a selection may contain, and it is the one `rangeIds` already follows. Selecting only the visible cells made a bulk move destructive: with a section collapsed it selected the heading but not its body, so `moveSelectionPlan` swapped each heading past the first cell of its own section, shuffling headings into the sections they title. The deliberate asymmetry that REMAINS is correct: `extendSelection`'s HEAD walks the SELECTABLE list, so Shift+J/K skips fold-hidden cells exactly like plain `j`/`k`, while the resulting RANGE fills in by full-document index. Select-all also leaves the two ENDS spanning the document (anchor at the first cell, head at the last) - `extendSelection` rebuilds the selection as `rangeIds(anchor, head)`, so a head left wherever the primary happened to be made the very next Shift+J/K discard everything past it. **A SELECTION CHANGE IS NOT A NAVIGATION, and the seam that states that is `mountAndFocus`** - `ensureCellMounted`'s opt-in **mount-only mode (`{reveal:false}`)** plus `focusCell` (already `{preventScroll:true}`), shared by `selectAllCells` and `activateCell`'s modifier-gesture branch; there is deliberately ONE such path, not one per caller. The mount is not optional because `activeId` is ALSO the PRIMARY every single-cell action addresses through `apiOf(activeId)`: leaving the head unmounted made `run-cell`, `edit-mode`, `command-mode` and `split-cell` silently no-op right after Cmd/Ctrl+A on any notebook long enough to window the last cell out. But `scrollCellIntoView` is the wrong seam for both, for the two side effects nobody asked for - the viewport chasing the primary somewhere the user did not ask to go, and, because `ensureCellMounted` REVEALS by default, a collapsed section silently unfolding with `saveFolds()` PERSISTING it. That hit Cmd/Ctrl+A (which expanded whichever section happened to hide the last cell, and the change survived a reload) and, once a selection could legitimately hold fold-hidden cells, the Cmd/Ctrl DESELECT too: `rangeIds` fills in by document index and select-all takes the whole order, so a mere deselect could permanently open a section the user collapsed. Keep `reveal:false` opt-in: every path that genuinely NAVIGATES (`selectAndAct`, `jumpToCell`, follow-running, the outline/search jumps) must keep unfolding, since scrolling to a `display:none` cell shows nothing. A fold-hidden head therefore neither mounts nor takes focus (a `display:none` cell cannot), so focus stays where the user was - the accepted cost of not writing fold state. It also means the head may be a fold-hidden cell, so **BOTH keyboard moves resolve a head absent from the walk by DOCUMENT position through the ONE exported `stepFromUnwalkableHead`** (the nearest walkable cell in the direction of travel): `extendSelection` for Shift+J/K and `selectRelative` for plain `j`/`k`, which took a bare `findIndex` miss as "restart at `walk[0]`" and so flung the selection to the TOP of the notebook on the very next keystroke after Cmd/Ctrl+A whenever the tail section was collapsed. Only a head gone from the document order too has no position to step from. **Toggling a cell OUT moves the primary ONLY when that cell WAS the primary** (`nearestSelected`'s contract is the primary's REPLACEMENT, and `applyGesture` used to apply it to any toggled-out id): deselecting some other member would otherwise silently relocate DOM focus and re-seat the origin of the next Shift range, neither of which a deselect asked for. A modifier click is handled in `Cell.svelte`'s `onCardPointerDown`, which **`preventDefault`s so the press never opens the editor** (JupyterLab does the same - a cell is mostly editor, so without it Shift+click would drop into edit mode and collapse the range it was asked for) and exempts a control, an editor that ALREADY has the caret (`view.hasFocus`, not `keyMode`, which lags a focus change by an event), and the two READING surfaces - rendered markdown and the output block - where Shift+click must stay the browser's own text-selection gesture (grabbing part of a traceback or a printed table), since cancelling `pointerdown` suppresses the compatibility mouse events that drive it. **An exempted MODIFIER press then PRESERVES the selection rather than falling through to the plain activation**, for the same reason it was exempted at all: the press belongs to the browser / CodeMirror / the native text gesture, so it is not a cell-selection gesture and must not COLLAPSE a five-cell selection on its way past (Shift+dragging across part of a traceback used to discard one). Preserved the same way the non-primary branch preserves it - by not collapsing, never by cancelling - so the `focusin` that follows settles it: it promotes this cell to primary when the selection already holds it, and collapses to it when it does not, exactly as an unmodified click on the same spot does. `LiveNotebook` then focuses the resulting primary itself, since the dispatcher reads a keystroke's mode+target off the focused element. The `focusin` that follows carries no modifiers, so it is tagged `fromFocus` (`CellActivation` in `types.ts`) and suppressed when it merely re-states the existing primary - otherwise it would collapse the range built one event earlier. **A SECONDARY press selects nothing** - a context-menu/auxiliary gesture, and the plain path collapses the selection to the clicked cell, so it would destroy a five-cell selection before the menu opened. **What counts as secondary is a PLATFORM question, decided in ONE pure place (`cellSelection.ts`'s `pointerIntent(e, mac)`, unit-tested for both platforms) so this file can never disagree with the toggle modifier about what Ctrl means**: a non-primary button, and on **macOS a CTRL+CLICK**, which is the platform's context-menu press and arrives as `button === 0` with `ctrlKey` set while the toggle modifier there is Cmd - so testing the button number alone read the everyday Mac right-click as a PLAIN click and collapsed the selection an instant before the menu opened. Off macOS Ctrl+click IS the toggle and must stay so. A secondary press yields no extend/toggle whatever else is held (Ctrl+Shift on macOS still opens the menu), since preserving the selection is the safe direction. Skipping `onActivate` is not the whole story (the card is `tabindex=-1`, so the browser focuses it and the `focusin` would collapse onto whatever it landed on), but the answer is NOT to `preventDefault` that press: cancelling `pointerdown` suppresses the compatibility mouse events, so a right-click inside a code cell stops focusing its editor (the native menu's Cut/Copy/Paste then act on nothing) and X11 middle-click paste stops working. **A selection is preserved by NOT collapsing it, never by cancelling the event** - so the `fromFocus` branch spares any cell the selection ALREADY holds, promoting a member to primary (anchor follows, as after a toggle) instead of collapsing. Accepted cost: a right-click on an UNSELECTED cell no longer selects it first.
  **THE WINDOWING CONTRACT (why the model is shaped this way).** Windowed rendering is ON by default, so most of a large notebook has NO DOM at all. Selection therefore lives on document-model ids resolved against the full `cells` order and is **never** computed by walking the DOM - a range whose endpoints are 200 cells apart must select every cell between them. Selected cells are deliberately **NOT** added to `pinnedCellIds`: pinning them would mount the whole range and undo windowing. A windowed-out member simply renders selected when it scrolls back in, because `selected` is a prop derived from the set (the same "a Cell instance is DISPOSABLE" rule as `rawEdits`/`editorCollapsed`). Covered end-to-end by `tests/e2e/multi-select.spec.ts`, whose assertions read the SERVER document, not the DOM.
