  **Bulk ops go through ONE batch request, `POST /api/cells/bulk` (`{op:'delete'|'move'|'type', ids, …}`) → `deleteCells`/`moveCells`/`setCellTypes` in `notebook.ts`.** Delete, move and change-type act on the whole selection; so do cut/copy (the clipboard has always held a list). **The batch is about ATOMICITY, not round trips**: a loop over the single-cell routes serializes+fsyncs+renames the whole `.ipynb` once per cell and walks the file through N-1 intermediate states, and a bulk delete would leave N undo entries where the user performed one action (so `deletedCells` is now a stack of GROUPS - `z` restores the whole batch). Each batch emits the ORDINARY per-cell events (`cell:deleted`/`cell:moved`/`cell:type`), so other tabs apply patches they already understand and no new event shape exists; the move plan is a sequence of ADJACENT SWAPS precisely so replaying those events reproduces the persisted order. Move semantics are JupyterLab's and need no special case for a scattered selection: each selected cell swaps with its unselected neighbour, so a contiguous block slides as a block and a scattered selection steps one place each, keeping relative order and gaps - a move never "gathers" a selection the user built. The move is BLOCKED entirely when the selection touches the edge it is moving toward (a partial slide would silently reorder the selection relative to itself), and a delete covering EVERY cell is refused - a notebook always keeps somewhere to type. **That invariant is SERVER-enforced, in `deleteCells` itself** (returning `{ok:false, reason:'would-empty-notebook'}`, refusing the WHOLE batch: nothing removed, nothing persisted, no events - a partial delete nobody asked for is worse than a refusal, and it is the shape the client already models), because only the server knows the real cell count: the browser compares against ITS list, which is stale for as long as an agent's `cell:deleted` events are in flight, so a selection covering everything the server still has would otherwise persist a zero-cell `.ipynb`. **`deleteCell` (singular) IS `deleteCells` of one** - one implementation, one refusal shape, so the guard holds for EVERY caller: leaving the single-cell path unguarded reopened the identical race through `DELETE /api/cells/[id]` (a `dd` on what the browser thinks is one of several removing the server's LAST cell). Living in the shared layer also covers MCP **`delete_cells`**, which never had the check at all (`removeCells` reports it as `{ok:false, refused}` so an agent is never told `count:N` over a document that did not change) - and it looks the refusal up through the shared `deleteWouldEmptyNotebook` predicate BEFORE `autoCheckpointBeforeAgentAction`, since a checkpoint is a snapshot of the state a mutation is about to leave behind: checkpointing a refused batch put an 'agent'-triggered entry in the human's History for a document that never changed AND spent the action slot the auto-checkpoint throttle owed the next real mutation (the one-checkpoint-per-batch property for a batch that DOES delete is unchanged). The bulk route surfaces it as a 400 in its existing `{ok:false, reason}` shape and the single-cell DELETE route the same way; both client guards (`deleteSelection`'s `ids.length >= cells.length`, `deleteCell`'s `cells.length <= 1`, and `cutActive`'s, since a cut that cannot delete would be half a cut) STAY as the optimistic mirror, the same client/server pairing as `clampMoveIndex` - and each SAYS so through `onNotice` -> the shell's existing transient status line (`data-testid=app-notice`) rather than returning silently: a keystroke refusal sends no request that could fail and disables no affordance, so Cmd/Ctrl+A then `dd`/`x` doing nothing at all is indistinguishable from a dead keyboard. **The RACE path says it too** (`noticeRefusal`, shared by `bulkOp` and the single-cell `deleteCell`): the optimistic guards compare against a list that is stale while an agent's `cell:deleted` events are in flight, so the server can legitimately refuse a delete the browser already rendered as gone, and the recovery - a refetch - is that same silent refusal with the cells simply reappearing. It is driven by the reason the server ACTUALLY returned (`{ok:false, reason:'would-empty-notebook'}` off the 400 body), never assumed from the failure: a refetch caused by a dropped connection or any other error must not claim a rule the server never invoked. **That status line is REPEATABLE and self-dismissing, and its dismissal rule is PER-CALL because two opposite kinds of message ride one channel** (`src/lib/notice.svelte.ts` `createNoticeChannel`, the ONE owner of both rules - `+page.svelte` reaches it only through `showNotice`/`clearNotice`, never a bare `notice = …`): each call bumps a **nonce** the markup is `{#key}`ed on - re-assigning an identical string is a reactive no-op, so a second refused `dd` while the toast is still up would otherwise change nothing on screen - and a **RESULT** (this refusal, an export/checkpoint outcome) arms a `NOTICE_TIMEOUT_MS` auto-dismiss, since a refusal the user retries must not need dismissing first. **`{sticky:true}` is the opt-out and PROGRESS needs it**: a message posted BEFORE a long operation must stay up until the outcome REPLACES it - a blanket timeout took `doConvertToIpynb`'s "Converting: running all cells…" off screen mid-convert (it runs every cell, minutes), leaving the user only the app-menu spinner inside a `:focus-within` dropdown they have usually clicked away from. So classify a new caller by WHEN it posts, not by assuming they are all one-shot (that assumption is what caused the regression); the ✕ preempts either kind. Covered by `tests/unit/notice-channel.test.ts`. (The refusal rule itself is unchanged - Cellar deliberately does NOT adopt JupyterLab's delete-all-and-leave-one-empty-cell behavior), and the client's single delete now pushes its undo record only once the server confirms (a refusal refetches, so a phantom group would make `z` duplicate a cell that never left). Optimistic client halves mirror the server's `clampMoveIndex` guard the way `moveCell` already does; a failed bulk request **refetches** rather than swallowing, since the events that would correct a divergence are the ones it never published. **A REFUSED batch is not an HTTP failure** - `moveCells` returns `[]` when its recomputed plan is blocked and `setCellTypes` `[]` when it matched nothing, both under `{ok:true}` - so `bulkOp` compares the COUNT the server reports acting on (`removed`/`moved`/`changed`) against what the caller rendered and refetches on a mismatch. That check is load-bearing rather than belt-and-braces: these events carry the initiating tab's own `originId`, so that tab suppresses its echo and the very events that would correct the divergence are the ones it will never apply. Its `applied` count is derived through the SAME rules the server uses (which is why `setCellTypes`' "already this type" test is the shared `isLogicalCellType`, not a second copy), so a batch the server legitimately skipped is never mistaken for a refusal. **That shared predicate is deliberately stricter than `logicalCellType(cell) === cellType`**: the latter maps every non-markdown cell to `code`, nbformat `raw` cells included (`ipynb.ts` passes those through untouched), so skipping on the logical type alone left a raw cell raw under a bulk retype-to-code while the single-cell `setCellType` - which has no "already" check at all - converted it, and the client predicted the same skip so nothing revealed it; `isLogicalCellType` requires the nbformat `cell_type` to match too. `raw` is still not a Cellar logical type, it is simply never "already" anything. `bulkOp` **returns whether the server confirmed**, and the delete's undo group is pushed only then (the snapshots are still TAKEN before the cells leave the model - the only moment their live source is readable): a refused batch refetches the cells back, so a phantom group would make `z` re-insert copies of cells that are still there. **The restore is symmetric and SINGLE-FLIGHT**: `undoDelete` leaves the group on the stack until every insert has landed (an insert can reject, and discarding it first would half-restore the notebook with the only record of what was deleted already thrown away), which is exactly why a second `z` - or plain key auto-repeat - inside that multi-fetch window would otherwise read the SAME group and persist the cells twice, so an `undoInFlight` guard admits one restore at a time. Each record is dropped from the group the instant ITS cell is back (the group is sorted ascending and shifted, which is also the order that lands every cell at its recorded index), so what stays on the stack after a rejected insert describes exactly what is still MISSING - a retry resumes instead of replaying, which would insert the cells that already landed a second time; the rejection is caught rather than escaping, since an unhandled one also skipped the selection restore. **The undo record has its OWN shape, deliberately NOT the clipboard's** (`DeletedCell`: index + type + live source + the WHOLE `cellar` namespace): `ClipboardCell` carries only what a cross-notebook paste should, so undo - the one path where a user expects EXACT restoration - dropped `language`, and ten deleted SQL cells came back as ten plain Python ones, along with the imports `role`, the nbdev `export` flag and the `hide_input` report-view choice; bulk delete is what turned that from a one-cell annoyance into a ten-cell one. It is applied in the SAME add rather than by a PATCH per key - `addCell` takes an optional `cellar` and `notebook.ts`'s `seedCellar` merges it - so a restore stays ONE persist and ONE `cell:added`. **The seed is an ALLOWLIST of the DURABLE `cellar` keys, and the RUNTIME-only records are stripped server-side through the same `stripRuntimeMeta` the disk write uses** - `clean.ts` keeps the `cellar` namespace WHOLE through a save, so assigning the client's object wholesale would make this route a path from arbitrary request JSON into the user's persisted `.ipynb`, and the strip alone cannot stop a key nobody declared. Neither can a client-supplied snapshot become a forgery route: `lastRun` is the sole evidence a cell ran against the LIVE namespace and may only originate from an in-process run, and `importBindings` was just recomputed by `newCell` for the source the cell is born with. The cross-notebook clipboard deliberately keeps its narrower shape (a separate follow-up). **SPLIT-CELL is the other caller of that seed, through its own rule `src/lib/splitCell.ts`** (`splitInheritedCellar`): the lower half is the same cell's second half, so it inherits what says how that cell is READ and DISPLAYED - `language` (without it a split SQL cell minted a plain Python lower half that compiles through the Python path, silently), `hide_input`, `output_scrolled`, and `hidden_from_agent` (not inheriting it would DISCLOSE the code the user hid) - but NOT the imports `role` (one per notebook; the upper half keeps it) nor the nbdev `export` flag (a designation about the ORIGINAL cell, so inheriting it doubles what the module writes out). A durable key is not inheritable merely because it is durable, hence an explicit list rather than forwarding the namespace. Covered by `tests/unit/split-cell.test.ts` (the rule + the real document write) and the split case in `tests/e2e/insert-cell.spec.ts` (the wiring). The route **validates** `dir`/`cellType` against their vocabulary rather than coercing them (400 `bad-dir`/`bad-cell-type`, like `no-ids`/`unknown-op`) - both ops rewrite and persist the user's `.ipynb`, so a malformed request must fail loudly instead of reordering or retyping cells in a direction nobody asked for. Covered by `tests/unit/bulk-cell-ops.test.ts`, which pins the one-write property, the raw-cell parity and the route's vocabulary, plus the refused-delete-leaves-no-undo-group case in `tests/e2e/multi-select.spec.ts`.
  **Affordance:** the primary keeps its existing `border-primary/50 ring-1 ring-primary/40` + solid left rail; a selected non-primary cell gets the same rail at reduced voice (`bg-primary/55`) + `border-primary/30` and **no ring** - the ring is what makes a card pop, so reserving it is what keeps "the cell I am acting through" distinct from "also in the set". `data-selected` marks every member. The COUNT lives in the shell **footer** (`activeSelectionCount`, fed by `onSelectionChange`), never inside the notebook: a selection reaches past the viewport, so anything rendered in the notebook scrolls away from it. **Scope: the selection-wide ops are the KEYBOARD and PALETTE ones; a PER-CELL affordance stays single-cell** - `Cell.svelte`'s toolbar trash/move arrows and `Notebook.svelte`'s drag grip all address the ONE cell they are drawn on (`deleteCell(id)`/`moveCell(id,dir)`/`onMoveToIndex`, not `deleteSelection`/`moveSelection`), because a control attached to a specific cell naming a different set of cells is the surprise. Making the drag grip carry the selection is filed as a follow-up, deliberately not done here.
- **Notebook surfaces are three tokens, not per-component classes.** `--cellar-surface-page` / `--cellar-surface-cell` / `--cellar-surface-output` (+ `--cellar-cm-bg` for the editor interior) carry them, applied as `bg-(--cellar-surface-*)` on `Notebook.svelte`'s page wrapper, the `Cell.svelte` card, and its output block. Each token's dark side resolves to the daisyUI surface that was already in use (`base-200` / `base-100`), so dark is untouched by construction. Change a surface here, never with a `bg-base-*` override on a component. **In light themes the page is the greyest plane and every plane above it gets lighter**: grey page → white cell card → faintly-tinted editor → white output. The page grey and the editor grey are the way round they are on purpose; inverting them (the innermost surface darkest, as the first cut of this palette had it) makes a cell read as a hole punched in the page rather than a card resting on it.
- **The cell editor is flush to the card's content edge.** `Cell.svelte`'s `editorEl` wrapper carries no horizontal padding, so `.cm-gutters` starts at the card's inner edge and the input area spans its full width, Jupyter-style. The editor's vertical rhythm is `.cm-content`'s `padding: '10px 0'`; a side inset belongs in neither place.
- **Fluid notebook width.** `Notebook.svelte` content column is `max-w-[clamp(48rem,92%,88rem)]` — grows with the viewport up to a readable 88rem cap (not full-bleed on ultrawide).
- **Cell virtualization (windowed rendering) - ON BY DEFAULT since P5 (P2 windowing + P3 pinning + P4 jump paths).** **Triage before you read any of this: a blank region that starts at the SAME cell every time, arrow-keys/jump-to-running going dead with it, or a notebook that renders nothing under "Render all cells", is NOT a windowing bug** - an uncaught throw during render (there is no error boundary anywhere, so one cell's throw takes the whole render tree; the DataFrame-grid duplicate-key defect is the known instance - see that entry) does exactly that, and windowing only changes WHEN it fires. Check the browser console first. Every cell of a notebook is fully mounted when it is turned OFF; baseline ~137 nodes/cell at N=50/150/300, captured by `scripts/gen-large-notebook.js` + `scripts/virtualization-baseline.md`. The plan (`data/cellar-perf-cell-virtualization-a2/report.md`, §3 controller / §4.1 heights / §4.2 scroll stability) is a hand-rolled, flow-preserving **spacer window** — NOT a library — driven by a pure controller. **`src/lib/virtualization.ts`** = pure, DOM-free `planWindow()` (mounted cells + **coalesced** spacers; a pin splits a spacer) + `estimateHeight(cell, collapsed)` (line-count + coarse output allowance, short-circuiting to the header-only `COLLAPSED_CELL_PX` when the caller says the cell is fully collapsed - the verdict is passed IN, never read off the cell, since this module may not read the document model), unit-tested (`tests/unit/virtualization.test.ts`). **What P2 added (windowing active when ON):** `Notebook.svelte` renders TWO branches from ONE shared `{#snippet cellRow(cell,i)}` — flag OFF iterates `cells` and mounts every one (byte-identical to the old eager `{#each}`), flag ON iterates the render **plan** (mounted rows via `cellRow`, one inert `aria-hidden` `data-testid=cell-spacer` per collapsed off-screen run). Only cells overlapping `[scrollTop − overscan, scrollTop + clientHeight + overscan]` (overscan = `max(800, 1.5×viewportHeight)`) plus the `pinned` set mount. **Fold-hidden cells are excluded from the window `order` (`visibleOrder`)** so they contribute 0 height and never mount (a folded cell is absent from the DOM under ON, vs `display:none`-mounted under OFF). **Scroll stability is the browser's, NOT an explicit compensation:** the scroll pane keeps its **native `overflow-anchor`** (default `auto`, unchanged in both flag states — there is deliberately NO `overflow-anchor:none` and NO `scrollCompensation`/`offsetOf`; PATH-2 replaced the earlier explicit-delta design after it proved unable to reconcile its parallel pixel model with the real layout). Native anchoring re-anchors the viewport when an off-screen cell mounts (spacer→cell) or an above-fold cell resizes. For native anchoring to track the layout, **`planWindow` accounts for the `space-y-4` inter-cell gap** (`ROW_GAP_PX = 16`, passed as `gapPx`): the running offset advances by `h + gap` between cells (cell k's model top = Σheights + k·gap) so the window tracks the real `scrollTop` and never blanks the viewport, and a coalesced spacer's px = Σ(its cells' heights) + (K−1)·gap so it reproduces the exact flow space its run occupied (the outer gaps to its neighbors are drawn by `space-y-4` around the spacer div). A `heights: Map<id,px>` cache fed by `Cell.svelte`'s `onMeasure` (ResizeObserver on `cardEl`, `borderBoxSize` — margin-excluded, hence the explicit `gapPx`) replaces estimates as cells mount; `recordHeight` only writes the cache + bumps `heightsVersion` to re-plan. The scroll-pane metrics `$effect` also attaches a **`ResizeObserver` on the pane itself**, so a notebook that loaded on a hidden (`display:none`) tab refreshes its viewport height on the `display:none`→visible activation (and on sidebar-drag / other container resizes) - otherwise it would window against a zero-height viewport and blank until the first scroll/resize. **Pinning (P3, complete): the pinned set is `{runningId} ∪ queued heads ∪ {activeId} ∪ {focused cell} ∪ scrollPins`, and its union + the REASON each member is in it live in ONE pure, unit-tested place, `virtualization.ts`'s `pinnedCellIds`** (`Notebook.svelte`'s `pinned` derived is only the wiring - do not re-inline the union there). Each reason: **running** - a streaming cell keeps a live node so its measured height GROWS with its output and the scroll height tracks the run instead of freezing at a stale spacer (correctness never depended on it - `applyOutput` writes the MODEL, which is live whether or not the cell is mounted - so P3 needed **no** buffer/replay machinery and left the streaming-delta protocol and terminal reducer untouched); **queued heads** - the next cells to run, capped at `QUEUED_PIN_LIMIT` (3) by `queuedHeadIds` because `queued` is a global FIFO an agent's `run_cells` can fill with dozens of this notebook's cells, and pinning all of them would mount most of the notebook exactly when the machine is busiest; **active** - the selection must not vanish mid-series (j/k, a/b, dd); **focused** - the cell holding DOM focus, tracked OFF THE DOM in `LiveNotebook` (a `focusin`/`focusout` listener on `rootEl`, attached only while windowing is on) and deliberately distinct from `activeId`, because an unmounted editor loses its CodeMirror cursor + undo (never its text - `Cell` flushes on blur AND on destroy, and `cell.source` is authoritative). The pin is added EAGERLY on `focusin` but dropped LAZILY - `focusout` only schedules a re-read of the settled `document.activeElement`, since focus moves as blur→focus within one task and acting on the intermediate state would unpin a cell that is about to keep focus. **We deliberately do NOT pin every cell edited this session** (report §7 Q2): that would keep every visited editor alive and defeat the memory win; a blurred, scrolled-away cell is unmount-eligible with its text already flushed. Every pin is dropped the moment its reason lapses, so a finished run costs the window nothing. **The corollary, and the rule for every future Cell feature: a Cell instance is DISPOSABLE, so any per-cell state that must outlive a scroll belongs in `LiveNotebook`, not in Cell-local `$state`** - windowing destroys and rebuilds a Cell whenever it leaves and re-enters the window, and pinning is not the answer (it exists for the handful of cells above, not as a state-preservation mechanism). Two things obey it beyond the pre-existing `editorCollapsed`/`foldedIds` (`cellCollapsed` later joined that list for the same reason - see the Full-cell collapse entry): **`rawEdits`** (which markdown cells the user opened for RAW source editing - lifted into `LiveNotebook` + `onSetRawEdit`, borrowing `editorCollapsed`'s plumbing SHAPE, because a Cell-local flag snapped a cell the user was editing back to its rendered view the moment it scrolled out of sight), and **`cell.remoteEdit`, which is a ONE-SHOT command and is CONSUMED (nulled) by `Cell.svelte`'s remote-edit effect the moment it is handled**. **`rawEdits` deliberately drops `editorCollapsed`'s PERSISTENCE half**: it is an in-session `$state` map with no UI-store key, because the notebook stays mounted for the life of its tab - which is all a windowed unmount/remount needs - while persisting it would reopen a notebook with every markdown cell the user ever edited showing raw source instead of rendered HTML, a far louder change than a collapsed editor (pinned in `virtualization-pinning.spec.ts` from BOTH directions: survives the unmount, does NOT survive a reload). Entries are dropped on delete (`setRawEdit(id, false)` - which deletes the key rather than storing a `false`, so it is the only helper - from both the local `deleteCell` and the `cell:deleted` event) so the map cannot grow for the life of the tab, and **`cell:rendered` clears the flag through the notebook's own `setRawEdit`, not only through `cellApis[id].showRendered()`** - an agent rendering a markdown cell windowing has unmounted has no Cell to call, and a mounted-only clear left that cell stuck on raw source when it scrolled back. The `remoteEdit` consumption is load-bearing, not tidiness: an unconsumed marker is REPLAYED by the fresh instance and overwrites a NEWER local edit the user has since made (`cell.source`/`savedSource` reset to the stale remote text with no re-PATCH to correct it), so it is consumed on EVERY branch - the STASH included, since the user resolving the banner (Load, or dismissing by typing on) and then editing reaches that same replay. It may only be cleared once a mounted Cell has actually processed it, since the marker is what carries the new text into a mounted cell's EDITOR; the MODEL is not left to it - `cell:edited` writes `cell.source` itself whenever no Cell owns the editor (the *Live structural sync* entry's two write points), so a windowed-out cell's source is current for every non-editor reader and the marker drives the mounted-editor safety UX only. **The mirror of that rule is the teardown hand-back**: an UNRESOLVED stash ("changed on server", never loaded nor dismissed) lives only on the Cell, so windowing the cell out would drop the agent's edit outright - the destroy handler therefore re-arms `cell.remoteEdit` from `pendingRemoteSource`, but ONLY while the user's own text has not diverged (`currentSource() === savedSource`, checked BEFORE `flushEdit` moves `savedSource`), since a restored marker is applied unconditionally by the next instance and would clobber their typing. **That hand-back is what makes the supersede rule load-bearing on EVERY local-source path, so it lives in ONE helper (`clearRemoteStash`) - never inline**: a stash left standing over a deliberate local write is re-armed on teardown and the remount adopts the PRE-write text while the server holds what that path persisted. Typing (the update listener), `replaceSource` (split / heading restamp - invisible to that listener, which skips the remote-apply path), `loadRemote`, `doRun` (the run route PERSISTS the source it is handed) and `flushEdit`'s persist branch all drop it; four of those were found one at a time, which is why a new source path must call the helper rather than re-derive the rule. There is deliberately no per-instance `appliedRemote` dedupe any more: consuming on every branch makes reference-equality dead state that only tells a misleading story. `fullImages` (the natural-size image toggle) and `remoteChanged` (the "changed on server" banner) are deliberately NOT lifted: both are transient and re-derivable with no data loss. Plus the transient **`scrollPins`** set owned by `LiveNotebook`, which forces a jump target to mount before anything queries its DOM node. **P4 (complete): EVERY scroll-to-cell / reveal / focus path in the app goes through the ONE shared seam `ensureCellMounted(id)`** (reveal/unfold + `pinScrollTarget` + `tick` + rootEl-scoped `querySelector`) - `revealRunning` (tab-spinner jump), `followCell` (follow-running), `jumpToCell` (find bar **and** the shell's outline / sidebar-search rows), and `scrollCellIntoView`/`selectAndAct` (keyboard `j`/`k`, `a`/`b`, moves, paste/undo selection, `insertAndRunCode`, plus the run/split paths `runAndAdvance`, `runAndInsertBelow` and `splitActiveCell`) - there are no unstated exceptions left. `+page.svelte`'s `scrollToCell` no longer does its own `document.querySelector` - it opens/focuses the notebook then delegates to that notebook's `jumpToCell(id, {foldKey})`, which owns the fold reveal, the mount pin, the heading-row sub-target (an outline row addresses ONE heading of a cell that may hold several) and the flash; so there is exactly one jump implementation, not two. **Two rules the paths must keep.** (1) **A select-and-focus path must mount BEFORE it lands** - an unmounted cell has no node and no registered API, so focus would silently go nowhere and the whole modal keyboard (which reads a keystroke's mode + target off the focused element) would go dead at the window edge. That rule lives in ONE seam, **`selectAndAct(id, act)`** = `setActive` then the shared mount/land/reveal/release tail **`scrollCellIntoView(id, land)`** (`ensureCellMounted` → `land(api)` → `scrollElementIntoViewNearest` → `releaseScrollPin`), whose other caller is `insertAndRunCode` (no `land` - that path deliberately never touches `activeId`, so nothing else pins its appended cell); the sites differ only in `act` - `focusCell` (plain selection, wrapped as `selectAndFocus`), `focus` (run-and-advance landing on the next cell's editor), or `enterEdit` (run-and-insert-below / split-cell, which must open the created cell in edit mode because a markdown cell with text mounts in its rendered view whose editor is `display:none`). Do not hand-roll a second mount-then-focus variant. `j`/`k` happen to survive without it today (`activeId` is in P3's pinned set, so the selection mounts itself), but that is a coincidence of the pin order, not a guarantee - stating the requirement is what keeps it true, and it is what makes `insertAndRunCode` work at all (that path deliberately never touches `activeId`, so nothing else pins its appended cell). (2) **A transient pin is released by `releaseScrollPin(pin, settled)`, never inline** - it waits for the scroll to SETTLE *and* two more frames, because `Notebook.svelte` reads the pane metrics rAF-coalesced: unpinning in the same frame re-plans against the PRE-scroll viewport and unmounts the very cell just jumped to. **The `pinSeq` ownership token is captured at PIN time** - `pinScrollTarget` RETURNS a `{id, seq}` `ScrollPin` and `ensureCellMounted` hands it back alongside the node, so a release is tested against the seq its OWN jump took. Re-reading `pinSeq.get(id)` at release time is the bug that shape exists to prevent: two jumps to the same cell interleave across `ensureCellMounted`'s `await tick()` (A pins seq 1, B pins seq 2), so A would read back 2, pass its own equality check, and unpin a target B is still scrolling to - after which B's settle loop aborts on the `isConnected` guard and lands nowhere. Map entries are deliberately never deleted (a deletion would let a stale release unpin a fresh pin). The release still runs if the scroll rejects, so pins can never accumulate (accumulated pins = virtualization undone). **The release must never depend SOLELY on rAF** - a backgrounded tab suspends it, so `nextFrames` and the tween in `tweenScrollTop` each carry a `setTimeout` backstop (whichever settles first wins); rAF stays the fast path, since the two-real-frames wait is what the re-plan timing needs, and the timeout is only a floor so a hidden tab cannot leak one pin per followed run. **That floor is gated on `document.hidden`, NOT fixed** (`FRAME_TIMEOUT_HIDDEN_MS` 50 vs `FRAME_TIMEOUT_VISIBLE_MS` 1000 per frame, re-armed by a `visibilitychange` listener in BOTH directions if the tab flips mid-wait - tight when it is backgrounded, and **widened back on return to visible**, since otherwise the tight floor armed while hidden fires once rAF has resumed but before two real frames land, which is the premature unpin the visible floor exists to prevent; each re-arm replaces the pending timer rather than stacking one): this app has long main-thread frames during a run (it drives its own tween precisely because of them), so a tight floor is reachable on a VISIBLE tab - and a timeout winning there unpins BEFORE the metrics read lands, re-planning against the pre-scroll viewport and unmounting the very cell just jumped to, i.e. exactly the failure the two-frame wait exists to prevent. `tweenScrollTop`'s own backstop snaps to the SAME destination it was heading for, so winning that race costs the animation, never the landing. **A long jump lands TWICE, by design (`scrollElementIntoView`'s bounded settle loop).** The first scroll is computed against the window's *estimated* heights for every cell it flies over; the moment it moves, those cells mount and their real heights replace the estimates, so the target slides out from under the tween - MEASURED at ~2.6k px of drift on a 64k px jump in the 300-cell harness (the target ended up four viewports below the fold and then unmounted again). So the helper re-measures and re-scrolls, bounded by `SCROLL_SETTLE_PASSES`; pass 0 tweens (the movement the user perceives) and the corrections snap. Do NOT 'simplify' it back to a single tween - that is the bug. **WHAT counts as settled is an explicit per-call `settle` mode, never a branch on the `virtualize` flag** - the real distinction is discrete-target vs continuous-follow, and keeping it in the caller is what stops a future caller inheriting the wrong behavior: `'frame'` (DISCRETE targets - `jumpToCell` for the find bar / outline / sidebar-search rows, and `revealRunning`) converges on the exact framing within `SCROLL_SETTLE_TOLERANCE_PX`, while `'visible'` (the CONTINUOUS `followCell`) stops the moment the cell is on screen, because its target is *streaming*: its own height (and, once it outgrows the viewport, its framing rule) keeps changing, so an exact-framing loop would re-snap a viewport the user is reading up to four extra times - the regression that reached the shipping flag-OFF default. Correcting only until visible is still REQUIRED under windowing (the window does not catch the mount-drift up by itself, so a single tween leaves a followed cell mounted but permanently below the fold - proved by the follow-running case in `virtualization-jump-paths.spec.ts`). Omitting `settle` is one scroll, no re-measure. When layout is static - windowing OFF - both modes settle on pass 1 (~0 delta / already visible), so the flag-OFF path is one tween exactly as before. **`scrollGen` is the per-pane supersession guard** (distinct from `pinSeq`'s per-cell pin ownership - keep both, do not collapse them): `scrollElementIntoView` bumps it on entry and both the loop and the in-flight `tweenScrollTop` return the instant they are superseded. **EVERY writer of that pane INSIDE `LiveNotebook` bumps it**, the instant `block:'nearest'` keyboard-selection scroll (`scrollElementIntoViewNearest`, that component's one native-scroll choke point - do not add a second there) included: a selection scrolling OUTSIDE the guard would be undone by an in-flight loop's next corrective pass, and - selecting the SAME cell - its newer `pinSeq` would take pin ownership and unpin that target two frames later, so the older loop abandons on its `isConnected` guard and the jump silently lands nowhere. That helper is deliberately NOT a settle-loop caller (settle is for discrete jump targets; keyboard selection and follow-running stay single-scroll). **The known exception is `Cell.svelte`'s search-highlight scrolls** (the active match's anchor `scrollIntoView` + `EditorView.scrollIntoView` for a match inside a built editor, both pre-existing from Search P4): they write the same ancestor pane WITHOUT bumping `scrollGen`, and they fire during the find-bar jump's own settle loop. Bounded, not a fight - they target the very cell that jump is scrolling to, so the two converge - but their ordering relative to the loop's passes is not guaranteed; whether they should participate in the guard is a future call, deliberately not made here. So overlapping scrolls - the find bar's Enter-repeat, rapid outline/search clicks, a follow-running scroll landing mid-jump (a Run all fires one per cell) - never fight over the same pane, and it rests where the LAST caller asked rather than wherever the slowest loop exhausted. A superseded loop still returns normally, so its caller's release path runs and the seq check keeps it from unpinning what the newer jump now owns. Because the loop spans frames of DOM churn it re-checks `el.isConnected` each pass and abandons a target deleted/moved out from under it - a detached node measures an all-zero rect, which would otherwise read as "scroll to the top" on every remaining pass. **Print escape-hatch (done, in Search P5):** `+page.svelte` registers `beforeprint`/`afterprint` handlers that flip a `printing` `$state` and `flushSync()`, and threads an `effectiveVirtualize = virtualizeCells && !printing` into both `LiveNotebook`s - so a print/PDF un-windows and mounts every cell before the browser snapshots, then restores windowing (see the search-engine entry's P5 note; the Ctrl+F escape-hatch is subsumed by cellar Search owning Ctrl/Cmd+F). **Enablement (P5): windowing is ON by default for every notebook, and the only thing left to decide is how to turn it OFF.** There is deliberately **NO cell-count threshold anywhere** (the earlier plan's "default-on ≥~60 cells"): `planWindow` already emits no spacers when every cell fits the viewport + overscan, so a small notebook renders exactly as the eager `{#each}` did and a second, drifting copy of that rule in the shell would buy nothing. The precedence is pure and unit-tested in **`src/lib/virtualizePref.ts`** (`resolveVirtualize`, `tests/unit/virtualize-pref.test.ts`): the **`?virtualize=` URL param ALWAYS wins** (`0`/`false`/`off`/`no` off, `1`/`true`/`on`/`yes` on; anything else - including the bare `?virtualize` - is NO override, never a guessed side), else the persisted viewer preference, else on. The preference lives in the **per-project UI-state store** (`$lib/uiState` `cellar-virtualize-cells`, like follow-running-cell) - **not `localStorage`**, which the dynamic port resets on every relaunch - and it is toggled from **TWO surfaces, one preference**: the navbar View menu (`data-testid=toggle-virtualize-cells`, "Render all cells" / "Render only visible cells") and the Settings pane's "Windowed rendering" row (`Settings.svelte`, `data-testid=settings-virtualize-cells`, the discoverable home). Neither keeps a copy - `+page.svelte` owns the one `virtualizeCells` `$state` + `toggleVirtualizeCells()` and threads both into `Navbar`/`Settings` as props, so flipping either updates the other in-session and exactly one `VIRTUALIZE_PREF_KEY` value is ever persisted; a second control that read the pref itself would drift the moment the other one wrote. A session a URL param owns renders BOTH controls **disabled** rather than offering a switch that would change nothing (`virtualizeForced`), the Settings one adding a note naming the param (`data-testid=virtualize-forced-note`). `+page.svelte` threads the resolved value into both `LiveNotebook`s as `effectiveVirtualize = virtualizeCells && !printing`, so **print/PDF still un-windows and mounts every cell** (`beforeprint`/`afterprint` + `flushSync`, unchanged by the flip). `Notebook`/`LiveNotebook`'s `virtualize` prop still DEFAULTS to false - the shell is what turns it on - so a standalone mount renders eagerly. **This module is render-only** — it must never read, gate on, or mutate `cells` (the document model lives above the renderer, so MCP/agents, folding, staleness, git diff, streaming all ignore what's mounted). Covered by `tests/e2e/virtualization-scroll.spec.ts` (N=300: O(viewport) mounted, ~96% fewer DOM nodes, 0px scroll drift, flag-OFF reload mounts all N with 0 spacers) + `tests/e2e/virtualization-pinning.spec.ts` (P3, flag ON on the same N=300 harness: a cell streaming with follow OFF stays mounted and GROWS while scrolled away and its output is complete afterwards; queued heads mounted while an equally-far unqueued neighbour stays a spacer; the tab spinner jumps to the live running cell; an interrupt still clears the queue; an edited cell scrolled far out NEVER re-mounts - proved by a marker attribute a re-mount would destroy - and keeps its caret + undo history), both leaning on the shared browser-side helpers in `tests/e2e/notebook-scroll.ts`. P4 adds `tests/e2e/virtualization-jump-paths.spec.ts` (outline row, sidebar-search result, `j`/`k` across the overscan boundary with focus asserted, and follow-running - each to a target that starts as a spacer - plus a flag-OFF regression of the same jumps). There is deliberately **no clickable error/traceback link** in the app (tracebacks render as plain text), so that listed jump path has nothing to make virtualization-safe. P5 adds `tests/e2e/virtualization-default-on.spec.ts` - the ONE spec that passes no `?virtualize` param, pinning the default itself (large notebook windowed, `?virtualize=0` mounts all 300, a small notebook mounts the SAME cells with zero spacers, print safety, the View-menu opt-out surviving a reload while `?virtualize=1` still overrides it, and the Settings row driving that SAME preference - flipped in either surface the other agrees, it persists across a reload, and a `?virtualize=` session shows it locked with the reason). **Only the specs whose SUBJECT is a mode state one** (`&virtualize=0|1`): the four `virtualization-*` specs, the three `find-*` specs, and `lazy-editor-scale`, which pins `virtualize=0` because it measures the lazy EDITOR at full mount, a different axis. **Every other e2e spec (~28) passes no `virtualize` param at all** and therefore runs at the shipped default - they are unaffected only because their notebooks are small enough that `planWindow` mounts every cell with zero spacers (the same small-notebook transparency that makes the no-threshold decision safe), NOT because anything pins their mode. The one that outgrew that (`output-table-styling`, whose every test persists an output until the bottom cells start as spacers) addresses cells by `data-cell-id` and scrolls them into the window via `notebook-scroll.ts` rather than opting out - the pattern to copy, since `getByTestId('cell').nth(i)` counts only MOUNTED cells and so silently addresses the wrong one. Two of them lean on that deliberately: `follow-running-cell` and `tab-jump-running-cell` exercise following/jumping AT the windowed default, since that is the ordinary user path now, so their "the seeded notebook opened" proxy reads the SERVER document instead of counting mounted cells. The report §7 calls are all resolved and shipped (intercept Ctrl+F to app Search; don't pin edit-history - focused/active only; default-on, minus the threshold). The Ctrl+F and print escape-hatches are DONE (Search P5) and P4 re-verified the print one: ONE `beforeprint`/`afterprint` pair in `+page.svelte` (no second path).
- **Sidebar Git section - which COMMIT each notebook's kernel is rooted at (`src/lib/GitNotebooks.svelte` + `gitCommitAt` in `git.ts` + `GET /api/fs/git/roots`).** One row per OPEN notebook: its name, its code root (`metadata.cellar.root`, or `workspace`), and the branch / short SHA / subject / relative date / dirty marker of the checkout that root IS. Exists because a root is normally a git WORKTREE, so its HEAD legitimately differs from the workspace's - and every other git surface is workspace-scoped by design (see the code-roots entry's stated limitation), so a notebook reviewing PR-482 was indistinguishable from one on `main`. **READ-ONLY, and that is the scope line, not a first cut**: no change list, no commit box, no stage/commit/push/pull, no branch switching. Clicking a row focuses that notebook's tab (`onFocusNotebook`, the existing path) and nothing else acts.
  - **`gitCommitAt(absDir)` answers for a DIRECTORY, where every other reader in `git.ts` answers for a file or for the workspace.** Three CONCURRENT spawns (`symbolic-ref`, `log -1 --format=%H%x00%h%x00%ct%x00%an%x00%s`, `status --porcelain`) - NUL-separated so a subject carrying a tab or a pipe survives verbatim, and the raw `%ct` epoch so no locale/timezone parsing rides to the browser. Cached per directory on the SAME `cacheSig` (its own git index mtime - `preflight`'s `--absolute-git-dir` gives a worktree's own `.git/worktrees/<name>/`, not the main repo's) but at **`STATUS_TTL_MS` (1.5s), not `CACHE_TTL_MS`**, and cleared by **`invalidateGitStatusCache`**: the `dirty` bit answers to an UNSTAGED working edit, which touches neither the index nor anything else keyed on - exactly the blind spot `gitStatus()` uses that tighter backstop and that hook for. **A positive `preflight` is memoized for the process lifetime**, so a root worktree removed since then would report a repo with no branch, no commit and no changes; `buildDirCommit` therefore returns `notARepo()` when ALL THREE calls failed, discriminating on `status` (a clean checkout answers an EMPTY string, never null). Unborn HEAD (`log -1` fails, `symbolic-ref` succeeds) is `isRepo:true` with a branch and no commit - **not** detached, which is the opposite case (no branch, but a commit). A failed `status` is NOT reported dirty: this drives a visual marker, and claiming uncommitted work nothing observed is the wrong direction. **The status is SCOPED with a `-- .` pathspec, so `dirty` uniformly means "uncommitted changes in the DIRECTORY this kernel runs in"** - the same convention the file tree in this very sidebar follows, so the two can never contradict each other. Unscoped it walked the whole repo, so a workspace that is a SUBDIRECTORY of a larger one (`cd repo/analysis && cellar`) lit the marker for changes elsewhere in that repo, including paths the tree deliberately drops: "uncommitted changes" beside a visibly clean tree. For a worktree root it is a NO-OP (a root IS its checkout), which is what makes it uniform rather than a special case. Deliberately **no `pre.prefix` strip** to go with it, unlike `runStatus()`/`parse()`: those strip because they must turn porcelain's repo-root-relative paths into workspace-relative KEYS, while this reads only EMPTINESS and never looks at a path, so once the pathspec has scoped the walk a strip would be dead code. **`gitRefAt` deliberately KEEPS its own cheap two-spawn path rather than narrowing this one** (`symbolic-ref` + `rev-parse --short`, uncached): it answers the notebook root picker and MCP `list_roots`, once per root, and neither needs the dirty bit - so narrowing it would put a whole-tree `git status` walk per root on that path, on the process carrying the kernel websockets and the SSE fan-out, for a value nothing reads. The few shared lines are the accepted cost of two questions at two prices; do not "unify" them.
  - **The route takes the open-notebook list and answers in ONE round trip** (`?path=a.ipynb&path=b.ipynb`), deduping the probe **by directory** - several notebooks reviewing one worktree cost one probe, and `gitCommitAt`'s cache collapses repeats on top. A declared-but-unusable root (missing, a file, escaping the workspace) returns the RESOLVER'S OWN refusal on `error` with no commit, because that is the state a RUN of that notebook is about to refuse - degrading to the workspace's commit would claim a checkout the notebook is not on, the exact silent degrade `notebookRoot.ts` exists to prevent. **A notebook whose DOCUMENT cannot be read at all is a DIFFERENT fact and rides its own structural flag, `unreadable`, never that `error` channel** (deleted/renamed outside Cellar with its tab still open, so `getNotebookRoot` -> `docFor` -> `loadDoc` throws): with no document there is no declaration to report, so routing it through `error` left the row rendering `root ?? 'workspace'` and thereby asserting a root nothing verified - so the client WITHHOLDS the root chip entirely and says only that the notebook could not be read. The flag carries **no message**, because the only thing that throw knows is an absolute server path, which has no business reaching the browser; the client owns the wording, and the two states stay told apart STRUCTURALLY rather than by matching message text.
  - **The panel is mounted only while the section is OPEN** (the `Checkpoints` idiom, `bind:this` for the header refresh), so a user who leaves it closed pays no `git` subprocess; it ships **collapsed**, like the other workflow-specific sections. It refetches on mount, when the open-notebook set changes (a `pathsKey` signature, not array identity), on `fsRefreshSignal`, on **any** notebook's `notebook:root` event, on `sse:open` (that event is NOT replayed to a late subscriber, so a reconnect would otherwise strand a stale commit) and on window `focus` - a commit made in a terminal notifies nothing. No timer. Generation-guarded (`seq`, the `statusSeq` convention) since focus / a root change / a tab opening can each be in flight at once - **and COALESCED on top, keyed by the query it would send** (`createCoalescedReload` in `src/lib/coalescedReload.ts`, the ONE place that rule lives - the panel keeps no private copy), because the seq guard discards a superseded REPLY without stopping its request while `gitCommitAt` writes its cache entry only after its three spawns finish, so genuinely concurrent triggers (mount racing `sse:open`; focus racing an `fsRefreshSignal` bump) all missed it and each re-spawned three `git` processes per distinct root; keyed rather than blanket, so a request whose notebook set has since changed is never handed back as this one's answer. **PLAIN single-flight is the wrong primitive here and must not come back**: handing the in-flight promise to a call that arrives DURING a run answers the new trigger with a reply issued BEFORE the change it announces, and nothing schedules another read - so a `notebook:root` landing while an unrelated window-focus read is still out left the old checkout on screen until some later, independent trigger, and the generation guard cannot help (it only decides which reply may be WRITTEN, and there is exactly one reply and it is stale). So a coalesced call marks the run superseded and fires **exactly ONE trailing re-run** once it settles, however many calls coalesced onto it; it cannot loop (the re-run starts with the flag cleared and nothing in flight), and a call for a DIFFERENT key is not coalesced at all - it already reads the newer state, so it clears the flag rather than scheduling a re-read the newer run subsumes. With **no notebook open it does not fetch at all** - the template short-circuits to `git-empty` and discards every field, so the workspace probe behind it bought nothing; it `reload.reset()`s (forgetting the in-flight run and any pending re-read, so nothing replays into the list it just emptied - a run still out settles and writes nothing, discarded by its generation guard), `seq` still advances (a reply issued while a tab WAS open cannot land afterwards and repopulate the emptied list) and `pathsKey` resumes it when a tab opens. The route is **bounded at `MAX_PATHS` (64) distinct paths, which it TRUNCATES-AND-REPORTS rather than refusing**: the fan-out is per path - a document load each, a blocking `spawnSync` jupytext conversion for a `.py` notebook, plus three `git` spawns per distinct root - on the process carrying the kernel websockets and the SSE fan-out, so it must be bounded; but **the only real caller is this panel, whose list is every open notebook tab with no clamp**, so a 400 past the cap made every fetch an error paragraph with NO rows at all, permanently - strictly worse than the truncation it was avoiding, since it drops the notebooks it COULD have read too. So the first `MAX_PATHS` are probed and the rest come back **`notRead:true`** - named, but with no root, no commit and no error - which is what keeps the truncation from being silent, and `readLimit` rides along so the panel can name the cap. **The route truncates by REQUEST order, so the panel ASKS about the ACTIVE notebook first** (`queryOrder()`, then tab order): sending tab order verbatim made the dropped rows whatever sat rightmost, which can include the one notebook the reader is looking at - the row this panel marks with a dot - while 64 they are not looking at show commits. That is query order ONLY; the list renders straight from `notebooks`, so what is on SCREEN stays in tab order, and a tab switch deliberately does not refetch (the reload effect tracks `pathsKey`, not activity), so a newly-activated row past the cap is re-prioritised at the next trigger rather than immediately. **The not-read notice counts the rows ON SCREEN** (`notebooks.filter(n => rowFor(n.path)?.notRead)`, never `rows.filter`): the list renders from the live tab set while `rows` is the PREVIOUS answer, so between closing a tab and its refetch landing the two disagree and the notice would name six rows over five, or keep claiming a cap that close just took the set back under. **Every "nothing to report" state is its own channel, deliberately never a reused flag**, because each would otherwise assert something nobody read: a row still in flight gets a neutral dimmed placeholder (no `workspace` text, no tooltip - only a LOADED row whose root is genuinely null keeps the real chip and its claim), an `unreadable` document says so and withholds the root, a `notRead` row says only "not read" with the reason (too many notebooks open) stated **ONCE below the list, never per row** since it is a fact about the request, and an **unborn HEAD** keeps its branch and says "no commits yet" where the SHA chip would go - decided off `isRepo && !commit`, never off a missing branch, because `symbolic-ref` SUCCEEDS on an unborn HEAD (only `log -1` fails), so such a row carries a real branch name and previously rendered as a bare branch indistinguishable from a half-failed load.
  - **The root chip has THREE states, and the split is a claim about what was OBSERVED.** A row that has NOT ARRIVED (`row === null`) gets a neutral dimmed placeholder - the same icon plus a fixed-width bar so the slot is occupied and the row does not jump - with no "workspace" text and NO tooltip, because nothing yet knows what this notebook declares; a LOADED row whose root is null genuinely declares none, which is worth saying, so it keeps the real chip and its "No code root declared" tooltip; an `unreadable` document gets neither. `data-testid=git-root` marks the REAL chip ONLY (the e2e asserts exact text on it); the placeholder is `git-root-pending`. Same rule in the branch slot: `refLabel` returns the word **`detached`**, never the file tree's parenthesised `(sha)` - that chip is the only place its row names a commit, while this row renders the short SHA in its own chip beside it, so borrowing the convention printed the same seven characters twice (three times counting the tooltip, which still names the SHA and is where it belongs). Covered by `tests/unit/git-notebooks-panel.test.ts` (source guards - vitest deliberately runs without the SvelteKit plugin, so the component cannot be mounted there, and each of these rules is one expression wide).
  - **`openNotebooks` is derived in `+page.svelte` from the shell's TAB set** (threaded as a Sidebar prop, typed `NotebookRef` in `$lib/types`) - never from the server `docs` map (which keeps a document after its tab closes and materializes the canonical notebook on SSR) and deliberately not from `kernelCards`, which answers a different question and so drops an open-but-never-run notebook while keeping a closed tab's live kernel.
  - **Surprising-but-correct git fact this surfaced, worth keeping in mind for any root work:** a worktree created INSIDE the workspace is an UNTRACKED DIRECTORY in the parent checkout, so the workspace row reads dirty until `roots/` is gitignored. Honest (the file tree's `U` says the same), so it is not special-cased; the tests gitignore `roots/`, which is also the real-world setup.
  - **The muted levels are deliberately NOT uniform** (documented at the component's head): the values a reader ACTS on - notebook name, declared root, branch, short SHA - clear WCAG AA on the card in BOTH themes, while pure context (subject, date, the `workspace` default) stays at the sidebar's decorative level. The light theme is the tight one: `base-content/60` measures 3.8:1 there. The unusable-root message is a `text-warning` ICON plus `base-content/70` copy for the same reason amber body text was retired from the external-change banner - `text-warning` on the light card measures ~2:1. The dirty marker is a dot at the ROW'S RIGHT EDGE (where the file tree puts its status letter, so it reads as a status rather than as punctuation) in `--cellar-git-tree-modified`, the explorer's own modified hue.
  - Covered by `tests/unit/git-commit-at.test.ts` (REAL git + REAL worktrees: the headline two-checkouts-one-repo case, per-worktree dirty, detached, unborn, non-repo, a missing directory, and the cache), `tests/unit/git-notebook-roots-route.test.ts` (the JOIN of a declared root with its commit, over the real route + real documents), `tests/unit/coalesced-reload.test.ts` (the reload primitive against REAL promises: coalescing, the single trailing re-run however many calls piled on, no loop, a different key subsuming the pending re-read, and `reset`) and `tests/e2e/git-notebook-commits.spec.ts` (what a human READS in the sidebar; every test builds its own state, since a failure restarts the worker into a brand-new workspace).
- **File tree UX (VS Code-style).** Folders start **collapsed** (`FileTreeNode` `open=false`), only root visible. Entries carry self-contained inline-SVG **file-type icons** (`src/lib/fileIcons.js`, keyed by extension, no CDN/network — offline-safe). **Git decorations**: `src/lib/server/git.js` + `src/routes/api/fs/git` return `git status --porcelain -uall` as `{isRepo, files:{relPath→letter}}`; the tree colors names + shows a status letter (M gold, A/U green, D/C red) and rolls child status up to collapsed folders (a dot). Refreshed on mount, manual refresh, window focus, after file saves, and on a foreign `notebook:opened` (an agent creating a notebook - all through the `fsRefreshSignal` bump); a non-git workspace returns `isRepo:false` → clean tree, no errors.
- **Git change decorations below the file level (line + cell).** The tree says *which files* changed; these say *which lines* (editor gutter bars) and *which cells* (notebook accent bars). **Baseline, not diff:** `gitHeadFile()` (`git.js`) + `GET /api/fs/git/head?path=[&kind=notebook]` hand the browser the file's git-HEAD text **once** (the `kind=notebook` form parses it through `ipynb.js`'s `deserialize`, so both sides of a cell diff are normalized identically and nbformat's multiline `source` arrays never read as a change); the client re-diffs the live buffer against it. That is what keeps markers live per keystroke without a `git` process per keystroke — do NOT "optimize" this into a server-side `git diff` route. `tracked:false` (not a repo / no commits / untracked / binary / unparseable) ⇒ **no decorations at all**, matching VS Code: the tree's `U`/`A` letter is already the whole story. Baselines refetch on mount, on `fsRefreshSignal`, on window `focus` (a commit made in a terminal), and after an external on-disk change is applied to a file tab (the working tree moved under the bars).
- **`src/lib/gitdiff.js`** is the shared, pure, browser-safe engine. `lineChanges()` = Myers' greedy line diff (common prefix/suffix trimmed first; bails to one whole-region hunk past `MAX_EDIT_DISTANCE`), returning per-line `add`/`mod` plus deletion seams. `notebookCellChanges()` matches cells **by id, never by index** — Cellar owns nbformat 4.5 ids, so an id survives inserts and reorders, whereas index matching would paint every cell below an insert as modified (a HEAD notebook with missing/duplicate ids falls back to index matching). It compares `source` + `cell_type` only: **outputs are deliberately excluded**, so re-running a cell to the same result never lights up a bar. A pure reorder is flagged `moved` on the *minimal* set of cells (the complement of the LIS of HEAD positions), so dragging one cell up marks one cell, not every cell it jumped. A removed cell has no cell to decorate, so its marker anchors to the next surviving HEAD cell (`removedBefore`) or falls off the end (`removedAtEnd`), rendering as a dashed seam in `Notebook.svelte`.
- **`src/lib/gitGutter.js`** is the CodeMirror half (`FileTab.svelte` only; notebook cells use the `Notebook.svelte` bars instead). Its `gutter()` is listed **after `basicSetup`** so the bar is the rightmost gutter, hard against the code. Two sharp edges: `highlightActiveLineGutter` classes the caret's line in *every* gutter, so `.cm-gitGutter .cm-gutterElement` must force `background: transparent` and the change bars must be `::before` overlays — otherwise the active line shows a phantom bar; and the deletion triangle uses `::after`, so one line can be both changed and a deletion seam.
- **Git decoration colors are NOT daisyUI semantic tokens.** Those are accent colors and their hues are not stable across themes (`dim` makes `secondary` and `error` both salmon; `nord` makes `info` pink), so added/modified/removed would stop reading as added/modified/removed. They are fixed palettes in `app.css`, resolved per scheme by CSS `light-dark()` against the `color-scheme` daisyUI sets on `<html data-theme>` — the same source of truth `editorTheme.js` uses, so every future theme is handled with no per-theme work. There are **two** sets, because the two surfaces follow different conventions: `--cellar-git-*` for the diff bars (`gitGutter.js`, `Notebook.svelte`: added green / modified **blue** / moved purple / removed red), and `--cellar-git-tree-*` for the file explorer's names and status letters (`FileTreeNode.svelte`: untracked+added green / modified **gold** / deleted red, VS Code's explorer convention). Both sides of each `light-dark()` are that scheme's own value — VS Code's *dark* decoration hues sit near 1.5:1 on a light background, which is what made the tree's green unreadable.
- **Git blame in the bottom status bar (GitLens-style).** For the active **file tab's** cursor line, the shell footer (`+page.svelte`) shows "author, relative date" with the commit short-sha + summary on hover; uncommitted lines read "You, uncommitted" (hover "Not committed yet"). `gitBlameFile()` (`git.js`) runs `git blame --line-porcelain -w` and parses one record per line (`{commit, shortSha, author, authorTime, summary, notCommitted}`; the all-zero SHA means `notCommitted`); route `GET /api/fs/git/blame?path=` returns `{isRepo, tracked, lines}`, `tracked:false` (untracked/binary/non-repo) hides the bar. **Whole-file cache, not per-keystroke git:** `FileTab.svelte` fetches the blame array once (on mount, `gitRefresh` bump, window focus, after its own save, and after an EXTERNAL on-disk apply - which moves the working tree under both the blame cache and the gutter baseline exactly as a save does) and maps the cursor line to a record on a 90ms-debounced `selectionSet`/`docChanged`, reporting up via `onBlame(path, report|null)` into `+page.svelte`'s `blameByPath` map; the footer picks an entry with **`activeBlameFor(activeFilePath, activeNotebookPath, blameByPath)`** (pure, in `src/lib/blame.ts`, unit-tested by `tests/unit/blame-status.test.ts`), which selects by **which tab is active, never by truthiness** - both keys are live at once (`activeNotebookPath` falls back to the canonical notebook while a plain file tab holds focus, and a `LiveNotebook` keeps publishing its focused cell's record under it), so an `||` chain fell through a correctly-null file report and credited that notebook cell's author to the file. A focused file tab therefore answers with its OWN record or nothing - and nothing is the COMMON case here, since the generated `report.html` this feature targets is normally gitignored or never added. **Line-level decorations (this blame AND the gutter's HEAD baseline) are size-gated at `MAX_DECORATION_BYTES`** (= the ordinary `MAX_FILE_BYTES`, so nothing openable before the HTML exception loses them; only the export-sized files that exception admits do). They are the one git surface whose cost scales with the whole FILE - a `--line-porcelain` blame of a 10 MB report measured ~2.6s and ~46 MB of stdout, then a record per line and a synchronous `JSON.stringify` of the lot, on the same thread as kernel streaming/SSE/MCP, re-run on mount + every save + every window focus. The guard is a `stat` that gates the blame/`git show` (the point is not paying for them, not discarding the result afterwards; the mtime cache is probed first, which costs two `stat`s and no spawn, so the refusal is cached like any other answer) and on the BLAME half it answers **`tooLarge:true`, never a bare `tracked:false`** - "too big to blame" and "untracked" are different facts, so `FileTab` reports `{unavailable:'too_large'}` (the `BlameReport` union in `src/lib/blame.ts`) and the footer reads "too large for blame" instead of going blank; the change bars are simply absent. **Size alone may NOT claim that fact** (`decorationRefusal`): the file this ceiling exists for is a GENERATED `report.html`, normally gitignored or never added, which has no blame for an entirely different reason - so an oversized file first costs ONE path-scoped `git ls-files --error-unmatch` (an index lookup, a different cost class from the blame, and it runs only once the size gate has tripped - an ordinary file never pays it) and only a **tracked** one reports `tooLarge`; an untracked/ignored one reads as untracked, exactly what VS Code shows. `git status --porcelain` is the wrong tool for that half - it cannot see ignored files at all. **The HEAD-baseline half deliberately does NOT pay that lookup** (`gitHeadFile` refuses on the `stat` alone and always reports `tooLarge:false`): a refused baseline and an untracked file draw the identical empty gutter, so the distinction has no surface to render on, and computing it would have cost an `ls-files` per mount / `gitRefresh` bump / window focus for a value no caller reads. Only the blame, which has a status bar that can speak, buys it. **The NOTEBOOK path opts out** (`gitHeadFile(nb,{sizeGuard:false})`): per-cell decorations cost in SOURCE, not in the outputs that make an `.ipynb` big (its blame is already `-L`-scoped), so gating there would strip the bars off an ordinary plot-heavy notebook. Covered by `tests/unit/git-decoration-limit.test.ts`, whose refusing half asserts **which git subcommands ran** (`gitSpawnLog()`) across all three states (tracked+big, untracked+big, ignored+big) - a test that only checked "no blame shown" would pass while the 2.6s still ran, and a bare spawn COUNT of zero would now forbid the cheap lookup instead of the expensive call. Relative dates use `relativeTimeLong()` in `relativeTime.js` (weeks/months/years horizon, separate from the run-badge's day-capped `relativeTime`). Footer text is a daisyUI semantic token (`text-base-content/55`), theme-agnostic by construction - unlike the diff-bar colors, blame is neutral chrome, not an add/mod/del signal.
- **File management (VS Code-style explorer).** Create/rename/delete/cut-copy-paste (move/copy) for files + folders. **Backend**: `fstree.js` exports `createEntry`/`renameEntry`/`deleteEntry`/`moveEntry`/`copyEntry` (folders recursive via `cpSync`/`rmSync`), all path-guarded through `resolveInWorkspace` + a `resolveInside` helper that also refuses the workspace root; single-segment name validation (also rejects `.`-prefixed hidden names, which the explorer never lists); moving/copying a folder into itself/a descendant is refused and a move within the same parent is a no-op; paste auto-suffixes on collision (` copy`, ` copy 2`, …), create/rename reject on collision. One dispatch route `POST /api/fs/op` (`{op, …}`) returns the affected workspace-relative path(s). Delete/rename/move also sync the live notebook doc map server-side (`+server.js` calls `notebook.js`'s `dropDocs`/`rekeyDocs`): a deleted path (or any doc nested under a deleted folder) is dropped from the `docs` Map so a later UI/MCP persist can't `writeFileSync`-resurrect the removed file, and a renamed/moved path is rekeyed to its new absolute id (folder ops rekey nested notebook docs too) so edits keep landing in the live doc; the `activePath` pointer follows (reset to null → default notebook when a dropped doc was active). **Frontend**: `Sidebar.svelte` owns the file-ops state (clipboard, inline rename/new, context menu, delete-confirm modal) and exposes it to the recursive `FileTreeNode.svelte` via the `cellarFileOps` **context** (getters forward `$state` so nodes stay reactive — avoids prop-drilling). Inline rename + new-entry use `TreeEntryInput.svelte`. Right-click any node (or empty files-body / root) → context menu; F2/Delete/⌘·Ctrl+C/X/V shortcuts; a cut entry dims until pasted; Paste only on folders/root. After every op the tree + git decorations refresh, and `onFsChange({type,from,path})` bubbles to `+page.svelte` so open tabs are closed (delete) or remapped (rename/move) — no tab left dangling at a gone path.
- **Tabs — session memory + preview.** Open-tab set is persisted per workspace to `localStorage['cellar-tabs:'+workspace]` (paths + preview flag + active id). **First-ever open** (no key) → empty state (`data-testid=empty-state`, no tabs); returning → tabs restored. Single-click a tree file → transient **preview** tab (one at a time; next single-click reuses its slot); **double-click** (tree or tab) or **editing** promotes it to permanent (VS Code semantics). All tab logic lives in `+page.svelte`.
- **Opening a workspace `.ipynb` (live).** Any `.ipynb` opened from the tree loads as a **live, kernel-attached** notebook via `LiveNotebook` (view/edit/run, edits saved back to that file through clean-on-save) — `ipynb`-kind tabs render `LiveNotebook path={tab.path}`, the canonical one uses tab id `notebook`. Preview/permanent tab semantics are unchanged (single-click preview slot, double-click/edit promotes). The old read-only `NotebookFileView` + `/api/fs/notebook` + `readWorkspaceNotebook` were removed.

## Maintaining this file

Keep this file for knowledge useful to almost every future agent session in this project.
Do not repeat what the codebase already shows; point to the authoritative file or command instead.
Prefer rewriting or pruning existing entries over appending new ones.
When updating this file, preserve this bar for all agents and keep entries concise.
